# Siqt — Planning & Progress

_Last updated: 2026-08-17_

Siqt is a ClickUp-style task manager: Next.js 16 (App Router, Turbopack) + Prisma/SQLite + Zustand. Paused for a while in favor of Huly, now being actively picked back up.

**Status:** everything documented below is implemented and Playwright-verified against the local dev server. Several sessions' worth of work (Space/Folder/List drag-and-drop and reordering, undo/redo, Activity & Comments, the Calendar/Planner view, browser back/forward, the Docs tab, the Office tab and Cozy Rooms, Command palette, Docs drag-and-drop, and @-mentions) had been sitting uncommitted in the working tree the whole time — this is the single commit that finally captures all of it and pushes to `origin/main`. No merge was involved; `git log` has always been a plain sequential history.

## What's built and working

**Hierarchy:** Workspace > Space > Folder (nested, unlimited depth, optional) > List > Task > Subtask. A List can live directly under a Space with no folder. Spaces, Folders, and Lists all support a custom color + icon via right-click context menu ("Edit appearance" / "Rename" / "Delete" where applicable — Lists have no delete yet, see Next steps).

**Organizing the tree:**
- Drag-and-drop reparenting (drag a List/Folder onto another Folder to nest it, onto a Space header to un-nest it to that Space's top level) works **across Spaces**, not just within one — including moving a Folder with nested sub-folders/lists, which cascades their `spaceId` server-side.
- Drag-to-*reorder* siblings (drop a Folder on a sibling Folder, a List on a sibling List, or a Space header on another Space header) reorders them in place instead of nesting/doing nothing. Folder-onto-folder disambiguates by relationship: dropping on an existing sibling reorders; dropping on a folder elsewhere in the tree nests, same as before.
- Folder expand/collapse state persists across reloads (`localStorage`, key `siqt.collapsedFolders`).
- Dropping a *task* onto a Space or Folder (rather than a specific List) auto-lands it in the one List found anywhere inside, recursively, if there's exactly one; if there's no List at all, a toast explains why instead of doing nothing silently; if there's more than one, a small picker (labeled with each List's folder path, e.g. "Ideer / Backend") lets you choose instead of guessing.

**Tasks (list view):**
- Sortable/resizable/reorderable columns (Name, Status, Assignee, dates, custom fields), persisted in `localStorage`.
- Custom fields, per-space statuses, assignees, archive/restore, bulk select + move/archive/delete.
- Task detail modal: subtasks table, named docs with autosave, comments/activity feed (collapsible, animated in/out), timeframe (start/due + time-of-day). Row-into-modal "magic move" expand animation.
- Sidebar: recursive Folder/List tree (`components/FolderTree.tsx`).

**Calendar view** (`components/calendar/`), labeled "Planner" in the nav rail:
- Month / Week / Day granularity, Gantt-style bars with lane assignment so a task keeps the same lane across week breaks — lane assignment and the visible-lane cap are computed **per connected overlap cluster**, not globally across the whole visible range, so a task that's no longer crowded reliably becomes visible again. Bar color follows the Space > Folder/List cascade (`taskColorOf` in `CalendarView.tsx` — a task's List color if set, else walking up its Folder chain, else the Space's color), not Status.
- Overflow past the visible-lane cap ("+N more") lives in its own reserved strip below the bars, not overlapping them, and is clickable to drill into that day.
- Day view lays out same-day timed events in side-by-side columns instead of stacking them on top of each other.
- Drag to reschedule: move (preserves duration) and resize-from-either-edge (works even on tasks that only have one of start/due set).
- Cross-week-row dragging via a live "ghost" preview. ISO week numbers, hover "+" quick-add per day, header "+ New task" button.
- Task creation is a required dialog (`components/CreateTaskModal.tsx`): Space and List must both be chosen explicitly — no "unsorted" fallback.
- **Calendar has its own sidebar mode**: multi-select filter (checkboxes, tri-state at any folder depth) instead of the Tasks tab's single-selection navigation. Same `FolderTree` component, different mode.
- **Personal `.ics` calendar feed**: each `User` has a `calendarToken` (opaque uuid, `GET /api/calendar/[token]` — public, no session needed, matching how every other external calendar subscription URL works). Feed is scoped to that person's assigned, non-archived tasks with a start/due date, as all-day events. The token is deliberately excluded from every other User-returning endpoint (team list, task assignees, comment authors — all `select` a `publicUserSelect` from `lib/prisma.ts` instead of `include: { assignees: true }`) and only ever handed out via `GET /api/users/[id]`, fetched on demand by the copy-link button (chain icon) next to the "You are: ..." selector.

**Multi-workspace** (sidebar header dropdown, `app/page.tsx`): switch between multiple `Workspace`s, each with its own membership (`Workspace.members`/`User.workspaces`) — a workspace with exactly one member is what "private" means in this app, no separate flag. Create new workspaces and add/remove members from the same dropdown. `GET /api/workspaces`/`GET /api/tasks`/`GET /api/task-docs` all filter by `?userId=` server-side, so a private workspace's content genuinely never reaches a non-member over the network (still not real auth — see the 2026-08-08 session below for the exact privacy bar). `Users` stay one global directory shared across every workspace. See that session entry for the full architecture, including the disclosed "You are: (none) now sees zero workspaces" behavior change.

**Navigation:** Huly-style narrow icon rail (Tasks / Planner / Docs / Office, plus Settings and Trash pinned at the bottom — each of the 4 view tabs individually hideable via the Settings menu, `activeView: 'board' | 'calendar' | 'docs' | 'office' | 'mytasks' | 'mypersonal' | 'profile'`) to the left of the sidebar. A persistent "Me" zone sits above the workspace switcher (avatar → profile page; "My tasks," a private cross-workspace quick-list; "My assigned tasks," scoped to the current workspace) — see the second 2026-08-08 session entry below. Clicking a Space with no List selected shows a dedicated **Space home page** (`components/SpaceHome.tsx`) instead of a flat task table: editable cover-image-URL banner + description (both inline click-to-edit, no modal), and a click-to-browse Folders/Lists card grid with breadcrumb drill-down. The old "flat table of every task in the Space" is gone entirely — union-of-tasks-across-lists is instead handled by **multi-select Lists** in the sidebar (Ctrl/Cmd-click to toggle a List in/out, Shift-click for a contiguous range in the same depth-first order the tree renders — `activeListIds: Set<string>` in the store, not a single value; scoped to one Space at a time, clicking into a different Space always starts a fresh single-selection there since a merged view can't sensibly show two Spaces' Statuses/CustomFields at once).

**Browser back/forward** works across the whole app — Space/List nav, the task-modal stack (including nested subtask modals), Planner's month/week/day drill-down and next/prev date, Docs-tab folder browsing/open-doc, and Office-tab person selection all round-trip through the URL query string on `/` (`lib/navUrl.ts` encode/decode, two `useEffect`s in `page.tsx` syncing state↔URL, each a no-op whenever the two already agree). Deep-linking works too (e.g. `/?space=...&modal=...`, `/?view=docs&space=...&doc=...`, or `/?view=office&officeUser=...` opens directly into that state), with graceful fallback to a safe default for stale/deleted ids.

**Docs** (third nav-rail tab): a standalone, folder-nested document system — `Space > DocFolder (nested) > Doc`, unified with the task modal's existing per-task Documents (same underlying `Doc` model; `taskId`/`spaceId`+`folderId` are independent, so a doc is task-scoped, Space/Folder-scoped, or **both at once**). Sidebar tree (`components/DocFolderTree.tsx`, drag-and-drop reorder/move/nest same as Folder/List — see below) and a breadcrumb card-grid browser (`components/DocsBrowser.tsx`, click-only, not a drop target, matching how `SpaceHome.tsx`'s grid isn't either) mirror the Folder/List tree's look (click-to-navigate, right-click-free — hover pencil/trash icons — "+ New folder"/"+ New doc" instead). Opening a doc shows a full-page editor (title input + `components/collab/CollabDocEditor.tsx`, live collaborative content — see "Real-time collaborative Doc editing" below) instead of a modal. Standalone docs don't participate in Activity & Comments (task-scoped concept, no task to log against).
A task's Documents panel can **link an existing standalone doc** onto the task ("Link existing" next to "+ New", scoped to standalone docs in the task's own Space) — the doc then shows up in both places at once. Linked docs get a small `Unlink` icon (next to Delete, on hover) to detach without destroying the doc; Delete stays a hard delete everywhere. The reverse also works: the standalone Doc editor (Docs tab) has its own "Link to task"/"Linked to: ..." control, scoped to Tasks in the doc's own Space. Both directions push through the same `setDocTaskLink` action and are Ctrl+Z-undoable.

**Doc export** (`components/collab/DocExportMenu.tsx`, next to the export-affected doc's title at both `CollabDocEditor` call sites): "Download PDF" (`GET /api/docs/[id]/export/pdf`, `pdfkit`) and "Export to Google Docs" (`POST /api/docs/[id]/export/google`, `googleapis` — needs a one-time per-person OAuth connect via a status control in the identity strip). Both read `Doc.ydoc` through a shared loader (`lib/collab/loadDocForExport.ts`), not the plain-text mirror, so bold/italic/headings/lists all survive. See the third 2026-08-07 session entry below for the full architecture, including the required Google Cloud setup step.

**Real-time collaborative Doc editing** ("like Google Docs" — multiple people typing in the same Doc at once, seeing each other's changes and cursors live): both Doc editors (task-modal Documents panel and the standalone Docs-tab editor) are now the same `components/collab/CollabDocEditor.tsx` (Tiptap + Yjs), replacing the old duplicated autosave-`<textarea>`+Notion-toggle blocks entirely. A separate sidecar Node process (`server/collabServer.ts`, `@hocuspocus/server`, run via `npm run dev:collab`/bundled into `npm run dev` via `concurrently` — port 1234, `COLLAB_PORT` env override) handles the live WebSocket sync, deliberately kept out of the Next server itself (`next dev`/`next start` stay untouched) to keep this app's "Zero-Cloud SQLite, one process" design change as small as possible — it's now two processes, but neither is a hosted/cloud dependency. `Doc.ydoc` (new `Bytes?` column) stores the binary Yjs snapshot; `Doc.content` (the pre-existing plain-text field) is kept as a synced mirror on every persist (`lib/collab/docJSONToPlainText.ts`) so `DocsBrowser.tsx`'s content preview keeps working unmodified — `PATCH /api/docs/[id]` no longer accepts `content` at all once this exists, closing off the old direct-write path so it can't race the sidecar's own writes. Existing plain-text docs (including any containing `@[Label](kind:id)` mention tokens) migrate lazily and exactly once, server-side inside `onLoadDocument` (`lib/collab/legacyContentToDocJSON.ts`, one `\n`-separated line → one paragraph node), the first time they're ever opened collaboratively — Hocuspocus coalesces concurrent first-loads of the same doc internally, so this can't double-run even if two people open a never-yet-converted doc at the same instant. Mentions are a custom Tiptap node (`lib/collab/mentionNode.ts`, shared by server schema + client) rendered as a live-resolving chip (`components/collab/MentionChip.tsx`, same pill styling as the plain-text `MentionText.tsx`) with its own `@`-autocomplete (`components/collab/mentionSuggestion.ts`, reusing `scoreMatch`/`resolveMentionEntity`) — this is Docs-only; Comments still use the original plain-text `MentionText`/`MentionTextarea` unchanged. Presence (`components/collab/PresenceBar.tsx` + Tiptap's `CollaborationCaret` for in-text cursors) is sourced from the same "You are: ..." self-selected identity (`useSessionStore`) every other attribution in the app already uses, with each person's existing `User.color`. Ctrl+Z inside the editor is handled by Yjs's own `Y.UndoManager` (via the `Collaboration` extension's built-in keymap) rather than the app's global history stack — verified this correctly reverts only your own last edit even when someone else's concurrent edit is interleaved with it, which a snapshot-diff undo model fundamentally can't do; the app's global Ctrl+Z listener already no-ops whenever focus is inside a `contenteditable`, so no changes were needed there. Formatting: Bold, Italic, Underline, Strikethrough, Heading (levels 1–2), Bullet list, Ordered list, text alignment, font family/size, text/highlight color, and links, via a collapsible side panel (`components/collab/DocFormatPanel.tsx`, moved from an earlier fixed toolbar row — see the 2026-08-11 "Round 4" session below) — initially shipped deliberately minimal (paragraphs + mentions only, same ceiling as the old textarea) on 2026-08-07, extended same-day to Bold/Italic/Heading/lists, then extended again 2026-08-11 to the full set above (see that session's own entry for the export-path details).

**Office** (fourth nav-rail tab, `components/OfficePage.tsx`): a Huly-style team directory, not a settings page. Sidebar shows a flat list of team members (no Space/Folder concept for people); once you pick someone, their own page: avatar, inline click-to-edit phone/title/status (`User.phone`/`title`/`status`), and every task assigned to them across every Space, with a Space/List breadcrumb per task since they're no longer confined to one. Status colors there fall back to `DEFAULT_STATUSES` rather than any one Space's custom palette — the same simplification the Calendar view already makes for cross-Space task lists. Three ways back from a person's page: the "← Back to team" link, the clickable "Office" breadcrumb segment, or clicking the Office nav-rail icon again (which always resets, even when Office is already the active tab).

**Office rooms — the "HQ Building"** (`components/OfficeRooms.tsx`, the tab's default view): a gamified base-building floor plan, replacing an earlier flat card-grid version. A message-of-the-day banner (`Workspace.messageOfTheDay`, warm amber accent, deliberately breaking from the app's usual neutral chrome) sits above a stacked building: `Room`s render as full-width connected "floors" (`FloorRoom`, sorted by descending `order` so the newest/highest-`order` room renders at the top — `POST /api/rooms` computes `(current max order) + 1` at creation time specifically so new floors land there, not tied at the schema default), and a `Lobby` (renamed from the old "Unassigned" tray, same amber accent as the banner) anchored at the bottom for team members not in any room. Team members drag between the Lobby and any floor; overlapping "collaborating" avatar clusters (negative-margin overlap + a separation ring, `PersonAvatar`/`DraggableAvatar`) replace the old individual name-pill chips. Reuses the app's single shared `DndContext` (`app/page.tsx`) via id prefixes (`person-drag:`/`room-drop:`/`room-drag:`) dispatched in the existing `handleTaskDragEnd`, rather than a second nested context — the codebase has a documented gotcha about exactly that mistake. Rooms are also drag-reorderable among themselves (reuses the same `room-drop:` droppable, disambiguated by the dragged id's prefix; the drag handle is scoped to just the floor's header row so it doesn't shadow each avatar's own independent drag underneath).

**Do Not Disturb**, at both levels: a person can be individually DND (a small clickable status-dot badge directly on their avatar — works everywhere that avatar renders — red ring + dot when active, `User.isDnd`), and a room can *independently* be marked DND too (a door-icon toggle on the floor header, e.g. "meeting in progress" even if members aren't personally DND, `Room.isDnd`). Both are plain booleans through the existing generic `updateRoom`/`updateUser` patch actions, no new store actions.

**Live presence** ("who's online right now"): a small green dot on every avatar (`components/PersonAvatar.tsx`, opposite corner from the DND dot so the two never collide), driven by genuine real-time connection detection — not a manual toggle. Reuses the existing unauthenticated "You are: ..." identity and the existing Hocuspocus collaboration sidecar (a dedicated presence-only room, `lib/collab/usePresenceConnection.ts` + `store/usePresenceStore.ts`) rather than building real login/session infrastructure, which this app still doesn't have. See the second 2026-08-07 session entry below.

**Room detail — "step inside"** (`components/RoomDetail.tsx`, reached via a floor's expand button, or a person page's "Back to team" always clears both selections so you never get stranded): editable name, a big labeled DND switch, and — moved here entirely from the old floor-header popover — the icon/color decorate controls (`ROOM_ICON_CHOICES`/`ROOM_COLOR_CHOICES`), plus a browse-only member list (bigger avatars, click through to a person's own page). `OfficePage.tsx` is now a three-way branch (`activeUserId` → person page, else `activeRoomId` → `RoomDetail`, else the HQ Building), backed by a new `activeOfficeRoomId`/`setActiveOfficeRoomId` in the store and a matching `officeRoomId` URL-sync field mirroring the existing `officeUserId` handling.

Deleting a Room still un-assigns its members (`onDelete: SetNull` on `User.roomId`) rather than deleting people; undo restores both the room and everyone's assignment. Each floor shows a live task count (assigned tasks across its members). Person chips' inline self-authored `status` text and a `title` (role/tagline) field on their own page are unchanged from before.

**Command palette** (`components/CommandPalette.tsx`) — `Ctrl+K`/`Cmd+K` anywhere in the app (deliberately *not* skipped while focus is in a text field, unlike the undo/redo shortcut — same "opens from anywhere" behavior as Linear/Notion/Slack), or the "Search..." affordance at the top of the sidebar. Searches Task titles, **every** Doc title (both standalone Docs-tab docs and task-scoped ones — the latter bulk-loaded via `GET /api/task-docs` into the store's `docs` record at startup, no longer only per-task on modal-open), People names, and Space/List names — all loaded eagerly client-side, case-insensitive substring match, scored (exact > starts-with > substring), capped per category, ↑/↓/Enter/Escape keyboard nav. Jumping reuses existing setters entirely (`setModalTaskStack`/`setNavigation`/`setDocsNavigation`/`setActiveOfficeUserId`) — no new navigation logic; a task-scoped doc result opens its owning task (no deep-link to the specific doc tab within the modal).

**@-mentions**: type `@` in the task comment box or a Doc (task-modal or Docs-tab) to autocomplete a Task, standalone Doc, or User, same scoring/data source as the Command palette. Stored as plain text (`@[Label](kind:id)`, `lib/mentions.ts`) inside the existing `Comment.body`/`Doc.content` fields — no schema change. Renders as a clickable chip (`components/MentionText.tsx`) that live-resolves the mentioned entity's current name and jumps to it; a deleted entity falls back to a muted, non-clickable chip instead of breaking. The Doc editor (which has no separate read/write mode otherwise) gained a Notion-style view/edit toggle so its mentions can render as chips: rendered view when not focused, the raw `<textarea>` (`components/MentionTextarea.tsx`) on click.

Spaces, Folders, and Lists can all be **created and deleted** from the sidebar (`+` button / right-click menu + confirm dialog), not just renamed — cascades to everything nested, all restorable via undo (see below).

**Undo/redo — Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Shift+Z** covers essentially every mutation: field edits, moves, reorders, creates, and deletes (including deletes that cascade through an entire Space→Folder→List→Task→Comment/Doc subtree, restored with the exact original ids so nothing else needs to change). Architecture: `store/useHistoryStore.ts` is a generic undo/redo stack + `transaction()` (groups N store calls from one bulk action/drag-reorder into a single undo step) + `pushCoalesced()` (merges a burst of same-key pushes — e.g. one doc-content edit session's autosave ticks — into one step instead of one per keystroke). Every mutating action in `useTaskStore.ts` captures its old value before writing and pushes `{undo: () => sameAction(...old), redo: () => sameAction(...new)}` — no separate inverse-logic to maintain. Cascading-delete undo restores a JSON snapshot (captured before delete) by replaying the same create/update actions with explicit ids (every POST route accepts an optional `id`), then a couple of safety-net refetches. **Exception**: a Doc's *content* (not its title) is no longer part of this stack — see "Real-time collaborative Doc editing" below, where Ctrl+Z inside the collaborative editor is handled by Yjs's own per-client undo instead, since a snapshot-diff model can't express "undo my last edit" correctly once someone else's concurrent edits are interleaved with it.

**Activity & Comments** (task modal, right panel) now logs real activity, not just the four original field-change types: task creation, title changes, assignee changes, Doc create/rename/edit-session/delete (one entry per edit *session*, bounded by focus→blur, not per autosave tick), and subtask add/remove — logged **one level only** on the immediate parent, never bubbling further up if that subtask itself gets a subtask. Every entry is attributed via the Comment model's existing (until now unused) `authorId` relation to whoever is picked in a new **"You are: ..." selector** at the bottom of the sidebar (`store/useSessionStore.ts`, localStorage-persisted) — a deliberate stand-in for real auth, isolated in its own store so swapping in real login later only touches that one file. Activity rows render a real Lucide icon per `Comment.activityKind` (schema field) instead of emoji, matching the rest of the app's icon language; entries logged before this existed just fall back to the old plain-dot marker.

**Visual design:** Matches Huly's look — neutral gray (`neutral-*`, not `slate-*`, which has a blue undertone) for chrome, blue reserved for buttons/selected state, sharp-ish corners, custom thin scrollbars, muted/pastel accent palette (`FIELD_COLOR_CHOICES` in `page.tsx`) for statuses/Space/Folder/List colors and user avatars.

## Today's session (2026-07-30)

**Part 1 — Gantt bars silently vanishing on crowded days** (user report: bars disappear past 4 overlapping and don't come back). Two real, separate bugs found and fixed (committed `73d83b4`):
- The "+N more" overflow indicator was rendered inside the day-number badge's own zone — the exact spot where visible bars start — so on the busiest days (the ones that most needed the indicator) it was painted over and invisible. Fixed by giving it its own reserved strip (`overflowTop` in `CalendarView.tsx`, rendered in `WeekRow.tsx`) with explicit `z-10` (needed because the day-cell's full-height button was otherwise intercepting clicks despite later DOM order — root cause not fully explained, pragmatically fixed).
- **Day view** had no lane/collision logic at all for same-day timed events — they rendered `absolute left-0 right-0` directly on top of each other, so only the last one in paint order was ever visible, no indication anything else was there. Fixed with a new `layoutDayColumns` helper (`lib/ganttLayout.ts`) that splits same-day overlapping events into side-by-side columns, grouped per connected overlap cluster.
- While re-verifying, the user found a third, deeper issue: freeing up space on a crowded day didn't reduce its "+N more" count or bring a hidden task back. Root cause: `assignLanes`'s lane numbering and cap-compaction ran **globally** across the whole visible month, so a task with real breathing room on its own day could stay stuck past the cap just because some unrelated pileup elsewhere in the month still occupied the low lane numbers. Fixed by scoping both lane assignment and compaction to each connected overlap cluster instead of the whole range. (Introduced-then-caught a same-day edge case in the process: the cluster-merge boundary used strict `<` instead of `<=`, which wrongly treated two tasks with an identical single-day range as non-overlapping.)

**Part 2 — five "smaller polish" items from the previous session's Next steps list**, all done and Playwright-verified:
1. Raised `MONTH_MAX_LANES`/`WEEK_MAX_LANES` (4→6, 8→12) now that bars are thinner, so more overlap is visible before "+N more" kicks in.
2. Folder expand/collapse now persists (`localStorage`).
3. Folder context menu gained Rename + Delete (previously only "Edit appearance").
4. Dropping a task onto a Folder row now lands it in the first List found recursively inside, instead of doing nothing.
5. Drag-to-reorder among Folder/List siblings, using a new `order` field already on both models — see "Organizing the tree" above for the reorder-vs-nest disambiguation rule.

**Part 3 — four more requests from continued use**, all done and Playwright-verified:
1. **Cross-Space drag-and-drop.** Previously, moving a List/Folder into a different Space's Folder or onto its header silently did nothing — the code only ever looked up the drop target inside the *source* Space. Fixed by searching all Spaces for the target, and by teaching the API (`/api/folders/[id]`, `/api/lists/[id]`) to accept a `spaceId` change; moving a Folder now cascades the new `spaceId` down through every nested sub-folder and List server-side. Cross-Space moves resync via a new lightweight `refetchWorkspaces` store action instead of trying to hand-patch two Space objects' arrays in place.
2. **List context menu** (Rename + Edit appearance), mirroring Folder's. `List` gained nullable `color`/`icon` columns.
3. **Space reordering**, same drag-to-reorder pattern as Folder/List, using a new `order` field on `Space`. Space headers are now both draggable (`space-drag:${id}`) and droppable (`space:${id}`, reused from the existing task-drop/cross-Space-move target — same "reuse the existing droppable, dispatch on the dragged id's prefix" trick used for List reordering, to avoid a second droppable colliding on the same rect).
4. **Toast on invalid task drop.** Dropping a task onto a Space or Folder with no List anywhere inside it (recursively) now shows a bottom-of-screen message explaining why, instead of silently doing nothing. First minimal toast system in the app (`toast` state + `showToast()` in `page.tsx`, no library).

All of today's work verified in a real browser via Playwright against the dev server, not just eyeballed — including cascading `spaceId` updates checked via direct `/api/workspaces` fetches, not just optimistic UI state.

Schema changes today: `List.color`, `List.icon`, `Space.order` (all via `prisma db push`, no migrations dir in this project).

## Today's session (2026-07-31)

Long session, five features end to end, each Playwright-verified against the dev server with server-side data checks (not just optimistic UI):

1. **Space home page** — replaced the old "clicking a Space shows a flat table of everything inside it" with a dedicated home page (cover/description/Folders-Lists browser). "See all tasks across a Space" was deliberately *not* rebuilt here — moved to multi-select instead (below).
2. **Space create/delete**, plus **`deleteList`** (previously missing entirely) and a fix for the known "Space reordering has no landing-spot feedback" friction — a live insertion-line indicator during drag. Along the way, discovered a Space's *own* drop target only covers its header row, not its whole expanded height when it has folders/lists open — dragging past a "tall" Space now correctly treats hovering anywhere in its own tree as "below this Space," not just its header.
3. **Full undo/redo** (Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Shift+Z) — see "What's built" above for the architecture. Two real bugs surfaced only under actual runtime testing, not code review: (a) a race where the undo entry for a delete became available *before* the DELETE request had actually completed, so a fast Ctrl+Z could try to recreate a row the server hadn't removed yet → unique-constraint error; fixed by deferring the history push until after the delete's fetch resolves. (b) the keystroke-coalescing helper (`pushCoalesced`) was overwriting the *whole* stored undo entry on every call instead of keeping the first call's `undo` and only advancing `redo` — meant Ctrl+Z on a doc edit only reverted the last keystroke, not the session.
4. **Activity & Comments overhaul** — attribution (new "You are" selector), task-creation/title-change/doc/subtask logging, real icons instead of emoji (new `Comment.activityKind` column). The trickiest part wasn't the logging itself but *not* double-logging: restoring a deleted task via undo replays its original activity comments verbatim from the snapshot, so the same field-setting calls used to reconstruct it (set assignees, archive, etc.) needed a way to know "this is a mechanical replay, don't also generate a fresh log entry" — a module-level `isRestoringSnapshot` flag in `useTaskStore.ts`, set only for the duration of a snapshot-restore call, threaded into the relevant PATCH bodies as `skipActivityLog`.
5. **Multi-select Lists** in the Tasks-tab sidebar (Ctrl/Cmd toggle, Shift range-select) — `activeListId: string | null` → `activeListIds: Set<string>` throughout the store/page/FolderTree. New `getOrderedListIds` helper in `lib/folderTree.ts` mirrors the sidebar's actual depth-first render order so a Shift-click range "looks right" even across a currently-collapsed folder.

Schema changes today: `Comment.activityKind` (all via `prisma db push`, no migrations dir in this project — remember to stop the dev server first, see Known bugs).

## Planned: Discord/Slack-style chat — architecture + phase roadmap (approved 2026-08-15, Phase 1 not started yet)

User wants a real chat feature — channels (optionally linked to a Space or Task), private/group DMs, a **dual reply system** (inline quotes that stay in the main feed + Slack-style side threads, independent of each other — a message can be both a thread reply and a quote-reply at once), image attachments with local previews, and emoji reactions grouped by type with counts. Explicitly requested as an **iterative, multi-session build** — the assistant acts as an ongoing architectural guide, not a one-shot implementation. This section is the full approved architecture, agreed via a plan-mode pass before any code was written, so it survives across sessions as the standing reference — update the phase checkboxes below as each one ships, and add a normal dated session entry (further down this file) recording what actually got built each time, same as every other feature.

**Two decisions already confirmed directly with the user, don't re-litigate:** channel visibility is open by default (any workspace member can see/join; private channels reuse this app's *existing* Role-based `isPrivate`/`accessJson` grant mechanism — explicitly requested, not a new bespoke system); message deletion is hard delete for v1 (no soft-delete/Trash/restore for individual messages, deliberately simpler than this app's usual Trash convention for everything else).

**Core design decision: Prisma is the sole source of truth for chat content; the existing Hocuspocus WebSocket sidecar (`server/collabServer.ts`, port 1234) is reused strictly as a live-signal transport, never as a content store.** This app already proves the "Hocuspocus room with zero persisted Y.Doc content" pattern works today for workspace presence (`lib/collab/presenceRoom.ts` — `onLoadDocument`/`onStoreDocument` both early-return, clients only broadcast/read `provider.awareness` state). Chat gets an analogous `chat:<channelId>` room following the identical shape — it carries no message content at all, only "something changed, go fetch." Messages/channels/reactions/attachments are normal Prisma/SQLite rows, keeping pagination, search, and moderation simple (a raw Yjs CRDT append-log would make all three hard).

**Schema (5 new models, planned as one migration covering every phase's fields at once — see rationale below):**
- **`ChatChannel`** — `workspaceId` (required), `type: 'channel'|'dm'|'group_dm'` (plain string + comment, matching this schema's existing no-enum convention), `name`/`topic` (both nullable), optional `taskId`/`spaceId` (**`onDelete: SetNull`**, not `Cascade` like `Doc.taskId`/`spaceId` — a channel's message history shouldn't be destroyed just because the loosely-related Task/Space it was tied to got deleted, same reasoning as `Event.spaceId`/`User.roomId`'s `SetNull`), `isPrivate`/`accessJson` (**reused verbatim** from Space/Folder/List/Task — see `lib/auth/access.ts` — only meaningful for `type='channel'`), `dmKey` (nullable+unique 1:1-DM dedupe key `` `${workspaceId}:${[userIdA,userIdB].sort().join(':')}` ``, left null for `channel`/`group_dm` rows since group DMs deliberately do **not** dedupe — every "create group" makes a new row, matching real Slack/Discord), `createdById`, `deletedAt` (channel is a container object like Space/List/Folder/Doc, gets the normal Trash pattern even though individual *messages* deliberately don't — flagged as a recommendation for explicit sign-off, not silently assumed same as the already-confirmed message-hard-delete decision).
- **`ChatChannelMember`** — `channelId`+`userId` (`@@unique`), `lastReadMessageId`/`lastReadAt` (nullable, unused until the optional Phase 8 unread-tracking work — included now so that phase doesn't need its own migration touching this row again), `joinedAt`.
- **`ChatMessage`** — `channelId`, `authorId` (`SetNull` — same "history outlives the person" precedent as `Comment.authorId`/`DocComment.authorId`), `body`. **Inline quote-reply** (orthogonal to threading): `quotedMessageId` (self-FK, `SetNull`) + `quotedBodySnapshot`/`quotedAuthorId` (point-in-time snapshots captured at reply-creation time, same idea as `DocComment.quotedText` — the quote preview keeps showing what was actually quoted even after the original is edited/hard-deleted). **Thread branch** (orthogonal to quoting — a message can be both at once): `threadRootId` (self-FK, `SetNull` — deleting a root shouldn't cascade-destroy every reply; former replies just fall back to rendering as normal main-feed messages once `threadRootId` goes null) + a denormalized `threadReplyCount` (kept in sync in the same request that creates/deletes a thread reply, avoiding a `COUNT(*)` per rendered message).
- **`ChatReaction`** — `messageId`+`userId`+`emoji` (`@@unique` triple, the toggle route creates/deletes against it), raw unicode emoji character (no custom/shortcode emoji in v1).
- **`ChatAttachment`** — `messageId`, `url`, `kind` (default `'image'`, field left open), `fileName`/`byteSize`/`width`/`height` (display-only). Unlike Docs (a bare image URL pasted into rich-text content), chat attachments get their own row FK'd to a message, since chat needs to render previews before the body is even parsed and needs a cleanup/moderation hook later.

**`canSee` vs. membership split — important, easy to get wrong later:** `ChatChannel.isPrivate`/`accessJson` reuse `canSee()`/`getAccessContext()` verbatim, but **only for `type='channel'` rows**. DM/group-DM visibility must go through `ChatChannelMember` membership directly and never touch `canSee` — its "owner/admin always sees everything" rule (correct for a private List) would incorrectly let a workspace owner see into two other members' private DM.

**Channel membership policy:** implicit for public channels (any workspace member can post/view without a separate "Join" click; a `ChatChannelMember` row is created lazily on first open/post — Discord-like browse-and-use feel, matches "open by default"). For a private channel, granting access via `accessJson` should also create the granted user's `ChatChannelMember` row at grant time, so "who's allowed" and "who's actually a member" (the latter is where `lastReadAt` lives) stay deliberately in sync rather than drifting apart.

**Real-time transport:** new `lib/collab/chatRoom.ts`, structurally identical to `presenceRoom.ts` (`chatDocumentName(channelId)` = `` `chat:${channelId}` ``, `isChatDocumentName`, `channelIdFromChatDocumentName`). `server/collabServer.ts` gets two narrow additions: `resolveWorkspaceId()` gains a branch resolving a chat room's workspace via `ChatChannel.findUnique`; `onAuthenticate` gains a chat-specific tightening after its existing generic workspace-membership check (`dm`/`group_dm` requires a real `ChatChannelMember` row, never falls back to `canSee`; private `channel` requires `canSee(channel, ctx)`; public `channel` needs nothing further); `onLoadDocument`/`onStoreDocument` early-return for chat rooms exactly like the presence-room early-return, no Y content ever persisted. **Broadcast design — "signal + REST catch-up," not "push the payload":** the client never trusts broadcast content, it only reacts to "something changed in `chat:<channelId>`" and re-fetches everything newer than the last message it already has (`GET /api/channels/[id]/messages?after=<lastId>`) — deliberately robust against Yjs awareness's own coalescing (awareness keeps only the latest state per peer, so rapid-fire signals can collapse into one; because the reaction is always "catch up to latest," a coalesced signal still produces a correct catch-up fetch, just possibly batching several messages into one).

**The one genuinely new piece of infrastructure — the cross-process bridge, needs a real spike before any UI is built on it:** the message-POST route runs in the Next.js process (port 3000); the chat room lives in the separate sidecar (port 1234) — after `prisma.chatMessage.create(...)` commits, the route needs to signal the sidecar. Two candidates to try against the actual installed `@hocuspocus/server` version: (1) the route opens a short-lived `HocuspocusProvider` connection, sets an awareness signal field, then destroys it — gated by a new shared `CHAT_BROADCAST_SECRET` env var checked before normal user-auth in `onAuthenticate` (same `.env.local` precedent as `AUTH_SECRET`), since this connection is broadcast-only and never needs a real user identity; (2) Hocuspocus's own `openDirectConnection` API, which runs *inside* the sidecar process — would need a small internal HTTP endpoint on `collabServer.ts` for the Next route to call instead. Whichever proves less fragile in the spike ships — flagged explicitly as real risk, not assumed to just work.

**API surface** (routes only, designed not yet built): `GET/POST /api/workspaces/[id]/channels` (list canSee-filtered / create), `PATCH/DELETE /api/channels/[id]` (rename/retopic/relink/privacy-toggle, manager-gated / soft-delete), `GET/POST /api/workspaces/[id]/dms` (list membership-filtered / find-or-create, 1:1 dedupe via `dmKey`, group always new), `GET/POST /api/channels/[id]/members` + `DELETE .../members/[userId]`, `GET/POST /api/channels/[id]/messages` (cursor-paginated main feed, `threadRootId: null` / send), `DELETE /api/messages/[id]` (hard delete), `GET/POST /api/messages/[id]/thread` (list / create, increments root's `threadReplyCount`), `POST /api/messages/[id]/reactions` (toggle), `POST /api/uploads/image?context=chat` (generalized existing upload route, Phase 6).

**Store:** new standalone `store/useChatStore.ts` — same isolation precedent as `useSessionStore.ts`/`usePresenceStore.ts`/`useHistoryStore.ts`, not folded into the 2700+-line `useTaskStore.ts`. Action shapes picked per this codebase's two existing established patterns: `postMessage`/`createThreadReply`/`createChannel`/`createDM` mirror `addDocComment` (fetch-then-append + `useHistoryStore` push, undo re-deletes, redo re-POSTs with the same client-supplied id); `toggleReaction` mirrors `resolveDocComment` (immediate optimistic local patch + fire-and-forget POST, **no** history push — low-stakes, instantly reversible, not the kind of action this app tracks in undo/redo); `deleteMessage` mirrors `deleteDocComment` and is also the `undo` target `postMessage`'s own history entry points to.

**Phase roadmap — schema built once up front (in Phase 1) covering every phase's fields; "iterative" here means UI/API/store scope, not repeated migrations:**
- [x] **Phase 1 — Schema + basic channel CRUD + plain message feed.** Built 2026-08-16, see session entry below. All 5 models, one migration. Channel create/list wired into `canSee`/`getAccessContext` immediately (validates reuse early) + plain message send/list. New `useChatStore.ts`. Minimal UI: channel picker, flat message list, textarea — **no websocket yet**, manual refetch only. *Done when:* a private channel correctly hides from a non-granted member and shows for a granted one (mirrors the existing private-List Playwright check pattern); messages persist across a manual reload.
- [x] **Phase 2 — Real-time delivery.** Built 2026-08-17, see session entry below. `chatRoom.ts`, the two `collabServer.ts` branches, the cross-process broadcast bridge (spiked first, in isolation, before any UI depends on it), `useChatChannelConnection.ts` (mirrors `usePresenceConnection.ts`). *Done when:* two tabs in the same channel see each other's messages within ~1s with no manual refresh, and recovering from a brief dropped connection still catches up correctly.
- [x] **Phase 3 — DMs (1:1 + group).** Built 2026-08-17, see session entry below. `dmKey` find-or-create for 1:1, always-create for group; sidebar split Channels vs. DMs. Reuses Phase 1+2 plumbing unchanged — DMs are just `ChatChannel` rows with a different `type`. *Done when:* DM-ing the same person twice reopens one conversation; two different 3-person groups with identical membership stay distinct. **Both verified** via a real scripted run against the live server. **Superseded 2026-08-17 (see "DMs go cross-workspace" session below):** DMs moved out of the per-workspace sidebar into a top-level Me-zone destination, `ChatChannel.workspaceId` went nullable, `dmKey` dropped its workspace segment, and a new `Connection`/`ConnectionInvite` mechanism now lets DMs span people who share no workspace at all. This bullet is kept as the original historical record — the schema/route/store details above no longer match current code.
- [x] **Phase 4 — Inline quote-reply.** Built 2026-08-17, see session entry below. Quote action → composer chip → snapshot captured server-side, stays in the main feed. *Done when:* hard-deleting a quoted original still shows the snapshot text in the message that quoted it. **Verified** via a real scripted run against the live server — see session entry.
- [x] **Phase 5 — Threads.** Built 2026-08-17, see session entry below. Side-panel thread view (`GET/POST /api/messages/[id]/thread`), `threadReplyCount` maintenance, "N replies" affordance on the root. *Done when:* thread replies never leak into the main feed; a single message can be a thread reply and a quote-reply simultaneously. **Both verified** via a real scripted run against the live server.
- [x] **Phase 6 — Media (image uploads).** Built 2026-08-17, see session entry below. Generalized `app/api/uploads/image/route.ts` with a `context` param (allowlisted `['docs','chat']`, never an arbitrary client path segment) → `public/uploads/<context>`; nested `ChatAttachment` row created inside the message-POST call; local blob-URL preview while composing, before any network call. *Done when:* preview shows before sending, image persists and re-renders after reload from `public/uploads/chat/<uuid>.<ext>`. **Verified** via a real scripted run against the live server.
- [x] **Phase 7 — Reactions.** Built 2026-08-17, see session entry below. Emoji picker, `POST /api/messages/[id]/reactions` toggle, client-side grouping by emoji (count + reactor-name tooltip), also fires the Phase-2 broadcast. *Done when:* two users reacting with the same emoji show one pill at count 2, live in another tab. **Verified** via a real scripted run against the live server.
- [ ] **Phase 8 (explicitly optional/deferrable) — Unread tracking.** Populate `lastReadMessageId`/`lastReadAt` on channel open; unread badges in the channel list. No existing unread/notification precedent anywhere in this app today — genuinely separable from the 7 core phases, revisit only if actually wanted.

**Critical files (for whenever a phase actually gets picked up):** `prisma/schema.prisma`; `lib/auth/access.ts` (reused verbatim, zero new access-control logic); `server/collabServer.ts`; `lib/collab/presenceRoom.ts` → template for `lib/collab/chatRoom.ts`; `lib/collab/usePresenceConnection.ts` → template for `lib/collab/useChatChannelConnection.ts`; `app/api/uploads/image/route.ts` (generalized in Phase 6, not duplicated); `store/useTaskStore.ts` (action-shape precedents only — `addDocComment`/`deleteDocComment`/`resolveDocComment`).

## Today's session (2026-08-01)

All three "small, well-scoped" items from last session, done and Playwright-verified:

1. **Task-drop-onto-Folder/Space picker.** Previously always silently landed on the first List found recursively inside — now: 0 Lists inside → toast explaining why (unchanged); exactly 1 → still auto-picked, no need to ask; 2+ → a small floating picker (`taskListPicker` state in `page.tsx`) listing every List found recursively, each labeled with its full folder path (`listPathLabel` helper), styled like the app's existing context menus.
2. **`.ics` calendar feed** (`GET /api/calendar/[token]`) — per-person, scoped to that `User`'s assigned non-archived tasks with a start/due date, rendered as all-day `VEVENT`s. Surfaced via a chain-link icon next to the "You are: ..." selector that copies the feed URL to the clipboard. Schema gained `User.calendarToken` (unique uuid) — added as optional first, backfilled for the 3 existing seed users, then made required, since SQLite can't apply a client-side `@default` to existing rows in one `db push`. **Found and fixed a real leak while verifying**: every other endpoint that returns a `User` nested inside something else (`Task.assignees`, `Comment.author`) was using `include: { assignees: true }`, which — now that `User` carries a bearer-secret token — would have shipped everyone's `calendarToken` to any client that ever fetched a task or a comment. Fixed by adding a shared `publicUserSelect` (`lib/prisma.ts`, excludes `calendarToken`) and swapping every such `include` for a `select`; the token is now only ever returned by `GET /api/users/[id]`, fetched on-demand by the copy-link button itself, never present in the general task/comment/team payloads.
3. **"+N more" overflow indicator** in the Planner month view — was plain text ambiguously placed between two day cells; now a small chip (`WeekRow.tsx`) pinned to the bottom of its own day's reserved strip, reading more like "more Gantt bars are stacked here."

Then two bigger features, each its own plan-mode pass, Playwright-verified:

4. **Browser back/forward support**, including the task-modal stack and the Planner's own month/week/day drill-down (both confirmed as in-scope, the second via an explicit plan-mode scoping question — the alternative would've kept Planner navigation as local, non-history state). This app was previously zero-routing (a single `app/page.tsx` route, all nav state in plain React/Zustand `useState`) — now the query string on `/` is the canonical encoding (`view`, `space`, `lists`, `modal`, `cal`, `date`, each omitted when it's the default), via a new `lib/navUrl.ts` (pure encode/decode) plus two `useEffect`s in `page.tsx` (state → URL via `router.push`, URL → state via the various setters), each a no-op whenever the URL and the app's nav state already agree — that mutual "already-equal" check (not a manual reentrancy flag like `isRestoringSnapshot`) is what stops the two effects from fighting each other. `page.tsx`'s default export is now a thin `Suspense` wrapper around the real component (renamed `PageContent`), required because `useSearchParams()` needs one for production builds. `calendarGranularity`/`calendarFocusDate` moved out of `CalendarView.tsx`'s local `useState` into `useTaskStore.ts` so Planner's drill-down can participate in the same URL sync as Space/List nav.
   Two real bugs found only via live back/forward testing, not code review: (a) a first draft special-cased "the first push after hydration" as a `router.replace` (meant to silently normalize a messy incoming URL) — but since that effect only runs when something actually changes, "first push after hydration" was in practice almost always the user's first real click, which then silently replaced the page's own history entry instead of adding a new one, collapsing two navigations into one back-step. Fixed by dropping the replace-vs-push distinction entirely — every post-hydration change always pushes. (b) a bare URL (no `space=`) is deliberately "no opinion, don't touch" during initial hydration (so a fresh visit to `/` keeps the existing auto-select-first-Space behavior) — but that same "don't touch" reading, applied to a *later* back-navigation landing on a bare URL, meant backing past the user's first-ever Space click did nothing visible (the current Space just stayed selected). Fixed by only treating a bare URL as "no opinion" on the very first hydration pass; every subsequent parse resolves it to the explicit `'everything'` default like every other omitted field already does.
   Deep-linking (`/?space=...&modal=...` opening directly into that state on a cold load) and graceful fallback for stale/invalid ids (deleted Space/List/task referenced by an old link falls back to Everything, no error) both fell out of the design for free and were verified too.
5. **Docs tab** — see "What's built" above. `Doc.taskId` went from required to optional; `Doc` gained `spaceId`/`folderId`; new `DocFolder` model mirrors `Folder` field-for-field. Three real bugs found via live testing: (a) the `/api/workspaces` GET route's Prisma `include` named the relation `docs` (matching the schema), but the frontend `HierarchySpace` type expected `spaceDocs` — since the parent object itself was never null, optional chaining (`currentSpace?.spaceDocs.find(...)`) couldn't catch the mismatch, so it crashed on every render past initial load. Fixed by renaming the field in the API response mapping. (b) `DocFolderRow`'s click handler *toggled* expand/collapse on every navigate — meant browsing into an already-expanded folder collapsed it, hiding its own "+ New doc" row out from under a click that was about to use it (caught because a test doc landed at the wrong nesting level). Fixed by always expanding (never toggling) on navigate; only the chevron button itself toggles. (c) `app/api/docs/[id]/route.ts`'s DELETE handler unconditionally wrote a task-activity comment using `doc.taskId` — would have thrown on any standalone (non-task) doc, since `Comment.taskId` is required. Guarded with `if (doc.taskId)`.
6. **Office tab** — see "What's built" above. `User` gained `phone`, added to `publicUserSelect` (unlike `calendarToken`, a phone number on a team directory is exactly what this feature is for, so it's fine everywhere a `User` is already visible). First `updateUser` action (previously only `addUser`/`deleteUser` existed), same old-value-capture + undo/redo pattern as every other update action. **Found and fixed two more `calendarToken` leaks while verifying** — `POST /api/users` and `PATCH /api/users/[id]` both returned the full Prisma row with no `select`, so ever since `calendarToken` was added earlier this session, creating or editing *any* user (which the Team panel and this new page both do) shipped that user's bearer token back in the HTTP response body, same class of bug as the `Task.assignees`/`Comment.author` leaks found earlier — just missed on this route at the time since the fix pass back then only looked at nested `include`s, not a route's own un-`select`ed return value. Fixed both with `select: publicUserSelect`, same as everywhere else.
   Follow-up fix after initial ship: the only way back to the team grid from a person's page was a small "← Back to team" text link in the content area — easy to miss, and clicking the "Office" nav-rail icon again while already on a person's page did nothing (`setActiveView('office')` is a no-op when it's already the active tab). Fixed by having the nav-rail icon always reset `activeOfficeUserId` to null on click regardless of current state, and by making the breadcrumb's "Office" segment itself clickable (mirrors how the Docs breadcrumb's Space segment already works) — so there are now three ways back: the text link, the breadcrumb, and the nav-rail icon.
7. **Command palette** — see "What's built" above. User-suggested follow-up once the backlog was cleared. No new store state or API routes at all — purely a new component (`CommandPalette.tsx`) plus a `Ctrl+K`/`Cmd+K` listener, entirely built on data and setters that already existed. Verified live: all four result categories (task, standalone doc, person, list) jump to the correct place with the right nav state, Escape closes it, and it reliably reopens even after a jump (no lingering keyboard-focus trap).
8. **Doc-to-Task attach UI** — see "What's built" above. Deliberately one direction only (task side links an existing standalone doc; no reverse "link to task" from the Docs tab). One new store action, `setDocTaskLink`, does double duty for attach (`taskId` set) and detach (`taskId` null) — same old-value-capture + undo/redo pattern as everything else, verified both directions round-trip through Ctrl+Z correctly. `DocTab`'s new `Unlink` icon only renders when `doc.spaceId` is set (i.e. the doc has somewhere else to keep living) — a task-only doc created fresh via "+ New" still only shows Delete.
9. **Cozy Office rooms** — see "What's built" above. User request (Norwegian: "kan vi få office til å bli mer koselig?") for real drag-and-drop "kontorer" plus a message-of-the-day banner; agreed additional scope from the conversation: room task-count stats, a self-authored `status` per person, and a `title`/role field as the "personal touch" answer — while explicitly *not* building real presence-tracking, since that would need session/login infrastructure this app doesn't have (discussed earlier the same session). New `Room` model + `User.roomId`/`title`/`status` + `Workspace.messageOfTheDay`. The drag-and-drop reuses the app's one shared `DndContext` (two new id prefixes, dispatched in the existing `handleTaskDragEnd`) rather than a second nested context, and the drag preview reuses the existing generic `activeDragEntity` overlay (widened its `kind` union to add `'person'`) instead of a parallel one. `deleteRoom`'s undo recreates the room *and* re-assigns every member who was in it, same snapshot/restore shape as cascading Folder/Space deletes elsewhere, just flat since Rooms don't nest. No real bugs this time — verification did catch the by-now-familiar "fire-and-forget PATCH raced ahead of the API check" test-script pattern (not an app bug) on both the drag-drop assignment and the undo checks, same as several earlier features this session.

Schema changes today: `User.calendarToken`, then `Doc.taskId` (required → optional), `Doc.spaceId`/`folderId`, new `DocFolder` model, then `User.phone`, then `Room` model + `User.roomId`/`title`/`status` + `Workspace.messageOfTheDay` (via `prisma db push`, no migrations dir — stop the dev server first, see Known bugs).

Playwright + direct API verification against deterministically API-seeded fixtures (not UI-created — see the grab-point bug below) covered all six scenarios from the plan: Doc→Folder move, Doc↔Doc sibling reorder, Folder↔Folder sibling reorder (same-parent → reorder, not nest), Folder→Folder nest (different-parent), cross-Space Folder move cascading `spaceId` to a nested Doc, and Ctrl+Z undo for all four action types — all passed, no console errors. Arrow/Enter/Tab/Escape are intercepted **only** while the dropdown is open; every other keystroke and every blur always falls through to the wrapped handler untouched — verified this didn't break the comment box's existing Enter-to-submit or the task-modal doc editor's blur-triggered activity logging (`captureDocEditBaseline`/`commitDocEditActivity`), both still fire exactly as before.

## Today's session (2026-08-06)

**Part 0 — crash fix.** User report: `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` in `store/useHistoryStore.ts` while dragging things around. Root cause: `commitCoalesced` called `target(coalesceEntry)` *before* clearing `coalesceEntry`/`coalesceKey` — `push()` calls `commitCoalesced` with a target of `push` itself (to flush any pending coalesce before a fresh push), so if the entry was still set when that reentrant `push` ran, it saw the same non-null entry, tried to commit it again, and recursed without bound. Fixed by clearing the pending entry *before* invoking the target. Verified with a standalone repro script (pre/post-fix) and live (doc-edit autosave → drag a list without blurring → no crash).

**Part 1 — ClickUp data migration.** Imported the user's real ClickUp workspace ("New Game Media AS") from screenshots + a printed PDF (`Idéer & konsepter`, 254 rows in ClickUp but only ~80 titles legible before the PDF's lazy-loaded rows render blank) into a one-off seed script: 5 Spaces (Bleep, Administrativt, Ekstrem Gjemsel, Kaoskassa, Bleep Show) with their Folders/Lists, 125 real tasks, 4 new Users. Reported the ~80/254 gap and every other simplification made along the way rather than silently guessing at the missing rows.

**Part 2 — Space collapse/expand.** Sidebar Space rows gained a hover chevron (ClickUp-style) that collapses/expands the Folder/List tree beneath them, state persisted in `localStorage` (`siqt.collapsedSpaces`, mirrors the existing Folder-collapse key).

**Part 3 — Archive/Trash overhaul**, the session's main feature (three sub-asks: Archive wasn't opening from inside a Space, deletes should be recoverable, and a Space-delete should cascade as one trash entry):

- **Archive-on-Space bug**: `showingSpaceHome` and the Space-with-no-List branch of `filteredTasks` (`app/page.tsx`) only handled the `activeSpaceId === 'everything'` case for showing the Archive view; fixed both to also cover a specific Space.
- **Schema**: `deletedAt DateTime?` added to `Space`/`Folder`/`List`/`Task`/`DocFolder`/`Doc` (all nullable, one clean `db push`).
- **`lib/trashCascade.ts`** (new): one pair of "walk down and stamp `deletedAt`" functions per entity — `when: Date` soft-deletes, `when: null` restores, same function both directions so delete/restore can't drift apart. Folder/DocFolder do an actual `parentId` tree walk (self-referential); List/Task/Doc are flat or one level; Space's descendants all carry `spaceId` directly so a cascade is a single filtered `updateMany`, no walk needed.
- Every entity's DELETE route now soft-deletes by default, with a `?permanent=true` escape hatch that runs the real Prisma `.delete()` (cascades via existing FK `onDelete: Cascade`); PATCH routes gained `{ restore: true }` handling. All six `GET` read paths (`/api/workspaces` nested includes, `/api/tasks`, `/api/tasks/[id]/docs`, `/api/calendar/[token]`) now filter `deletedAt: null`.
- **`GET /api/trash`** (new): unified list across all six entity types, sorted `deletedAt desc`. Only surfaces "top-level" trash entries — an item is listed only if its own immediate parent is *not* also trashed, so deleting one Space produces one trash row, not dozens.
- **Removed the entire legacy snapshot/replay undo system** (~190 lines in `useTaskStore.ts`: `TaskSnapshot`/`ListSnapshot`/etc. types, `snapshotX`/`restoreX` pairs, the `isRestoringSnapshot` reentrancy flag). It was fundamentally incompatible with soft-delete — replaying a `POST` with an explicit id to "recreate" a row that was only ever soft-deleted (never actually removed) would hit a unique-constraint violation. Replaced every delete action's `undo` with a plain `PATCH { restore: true }` call (`restoreEntity` helper).
- **`components/TrashPanel.tsx`** (new) + a new "Trash" nav-rail icon: grouped-by-day list, Restore and Delete-forever (with `ConfirmDialog`) per row.
- All of the above Playwright-verified end to end against the running dev server: task delete → Ctrl+Z undo (with a toast confirming the undo), whole-Space cascade delete/restore (single trash entry, correct nested-item count), permanent delete, and the Trash panel itself (open, see the entry, Restore, confirm it's gone from Trash and back in the task list).

**Part 4 — silent-delete-failure bug**, found from a user report *after* Part 3 shipped: deleting a task inside "Idéer & konsepter" made it vanish from the UI but it never showed up in Trash. Reproduced the exact cascade/soft-delete logic directly against that list via the API and it worked correctly — so the bug wasn't in `trashCascade.ts`. The real bug: every optimistic delete action in `useTaskStore.ts` (`optimisticDeleteTask`, `deleteSpace`, `deleteList`, `deleteFolder`, `deleteDocFolder`, `deleteSpaceDoc`, `deleteDoc`) removed the item from local state immediately and then fired the `DELETE` fetch with no error handling at all — if that request failed for any reason (dropped connection, a dev-server restart mid-request — which happened several times this session during schema pushes), the item stayed optimistically hidden forever with no server-side record and no trash entry, and no indication anything had gone wrong. Fixed by checking `res.ok` (and catching a thrown/rejected fetch) on all seven call sites: on failure, resync the affected slice of state from the server (`refetchWorkspaces()`/`refetchTasks()`/`fetchDocs(taskId)`, whichever already covers that data) and show an `alert()` so the failure is never silent. Verified live by intercepting the `DELETE` request with Playwright's route mocking to force a failure — the task correctly reappeared with the warning shown, instead of vanishing.

**Unresolved, disclosed to the user rather than left unmentioned**: while verifying Part 3/4, found that the pre-existing "Product Dev" and "Marketing" sample/seed Spaces (unrelated to the user's real ClickUp import) are completely gone from the database — not soft-deleted, not in any trash query, a genuine prior hard-delete with no trace. Investigated but couldn't pin down cause or timing: ruled out `prisma/seed.ts` re-running (Workspace row's id/createdAt unchanged) and ruled out the Part 1 import script (it only ever called `.create()`). Predates this session's Trash-verification testing. All of the user's real ClickUp-imported data is confirmed 100% intact.

Schema changes today (Parts 0-4): `deletedAt` on `Space`/`Folder`/`List`/`Task`/`DocFolder`/`Doc` (via `prisma db push`, no migrations dir — stop the dev server first, see Known bugs).

**Part 5 — cross-machine sync gap discovered, then four queued fixes + a real undo bug.** A second local working copy of this repo (this one) turned out to be genuinely behind: `git fetch` revealed two commits on `origin/main` (`f2d337a`/`346f071`, Parts 0-4 above) this copy didn't have yet — pulled cleanly (fast-forward), `prisma db push` + dev-server restart for the new `deletedAt` columns. While investigating, checked whether the "Product Dev"/"Marketing" mystery (above) was recoverable: `prisma/dev.db` turned out to have actually been git-tracked up through commit `cb22b4f` (before being gitignored) — extracted that historical snapshot and confirmed **this machine's live `dev.db` already has both Spaces intact and always did**; the "missing" ones only ever went missing in whichever environment ran the Part 1 ClickUp import. That import's 125 real tasks/5 Spaces/4 Users don't exist in this local database at all — `dev.db` is gitignored (data never travels with code) and no second copy of it exists anywhere on this machine, so that import most likely ran in an isolated (probably remote/cloud) environment whose filesystem never synced back here. Flagged to the user; decided to leave it and move on rather than re-import.

Then, four items from "Next steps," each small and well-scoped:
1. **Planner calendar bar colors now follow Space > Folder/List > Task**, replacing pure Status-coloring. New `taskColorOf(task)` in `CalendarView.tsx` (passed down to `WeekRow`/`DayTimeline`, replacing the old `statusColorOf(name)` prop) walks List's own `color` → up the Folder `parentId` chain → Space's `color`, most-specific-set wins; falls back to the old status color only if the task's List can't be found at all. No schema change (Task has no color field yet, so the chain simply bottoms out at List). Verified via three live cases (Space-only, List override, Folder override) checked by reading the bar's actual computed `background-color`, not just eyeballing.
2. **Command palette now searches task-scoped Doc titles** — the documented gap existed only because those docs previously loaded per-task on modal-open and nowhere eagerly. New `GET /api/task-docs` (bulk, excludes docs on trashed tasks) feeds `fetchInitialData`, seeding the store's `docs` record up front instead of leaving it empty until a task modal opens; `fetchDocs(taskId)` still exists as the same per-task freshness refresh it always was. `CommandPalette.tsx` gained a matching search branch (sub-label = owning task's title); clicking opens the task (no deep-link to the specific doc *tab* — landing on the task with its docs visible is enough, avoids fighting the modal's existing auto-select-first-doc effect).
3. **Doc-to-Task attach is now bidirectional** — the standalone Doc editor (Docs tab) gained a "Link to task"/"Linked to: <task>" control mirroring the task modal's existing "Link existing" doc picker, both driven by the same already-generic `setDocTaskLink` store action (no store changes needed, just new UI).
4. **Office Rooms are now drag-reorderable among themselves**, reusing the existing `room-drop:` droppable (disambiguated by the dragged id's `room-drag:` prefix, same trick as every other reorder-vs-drop-target reuse in this app) rather than adding a second droppable. `RoomCard`'s drag *listeners* are scoped to just its header row, not the whole card — the card also droppable for people, and its members section holds each `PersonChip`'s own independent draggable, so listening on the whole card would have shadowed those nested drags.

**Real bug found via #4's own verification, not in the new code**: dragging Room B onto Room A (both tied at `order: 0` alongside a third room) reordered correctly, but Ctrl+Z left the three rooms in a state that matched *neither* the original nor the dragged arrangement — a genuine undo bug, not a test artifact (caught by re-running with a network-request logger and generous wait times to rule out the fire-and-forget-timing false alarm that looked like the cause at first). Root cause: `useHistoryStore.ts`'s `transaction()` closes and pushes its combined group *synchronously* right after its callback returns — but every `reorder*Siblings` helper in `app/page.tsx` called its per-item update (`updateFolder`/`updateRoom`/etc.) from inside a plain synchronous `.forEach`, not awaited. Those update actions push their own history entry only *after* their own `await fetch(...)` resolves — asynchronously, later — so by the time `transaction()`'s synchronous `finish()` ran, the group was still empty and got discarded; each individual update's *own* later push then landed directly on the bare undo stack as its own separate entry instead of one combined step. Ctrl+Z therefore only ever reverted whichever single sub-change happened to push last, leaving the rest applied — invisible in every prior verification this session because a plain 2-item swap often has one side whose old `order` already happens to equal its new index (skipped via the `if (x.order !== index)` guard), so only one update ever actually fired and the bug had nothing to split apart. Fixed at the root: every `reorder*Siblings` helper (Folders, Lists, DocFolders, Rooms, SpaceDocs, Spaces, Statuses) now passes `transaction()` an `async` callback that `Promise.all`s its updates, so the group only closes once every push has actually landed — `bulkArchive`/`bulkMoveToList` were already safe as-is (their underlying actions push synchronously, before awaiting their fetch, unlike the reorder actions). Re-verified Rooms *and* Folders (a second, independent reorder path) both undo/redo to an exact, byte-for-byte match of the pre-drag state.

## Today's session (2026-08-07)

**Real-time collaborative Doc editing** — see "What's built" above for the full architecture. Plan-mode approved (sidecar-process architecture confirmed with the user up front — a separate `server/collabServer.ts` process, not embedded in the Next server), then built end to end and Playwright-verified with two independent browser contexts (separate `localStorage`/session identity) on the same doc simultaneously, not just single-user:

1. Live sync: Context A types, Context B's DOM reflects it within under a second, no reload.
2. Concurrent non-overlapping edits (A inserts at the start, B inserts at the end, at the same moment) converge correctly on both sides with nothing dropped or duplicated.
3. Presence: A's presence bar + in-text cursor label show B's name/color while B has the doc open; both disappear when B's context closes.
4. Ctrl+Z in A only reverts A's own last edit, leaving B's concurrent edit intact — the CRDT-undo-under-concurrency case a snapshot-diff undo can't express.
5. Legacy doc migration: a doc with real pre-existing plain-text content (including a `@[Label](kind:id)` mention token) opened for the first time in the new editor renders the exact same visible text, with the mention as a real clickable chip.
6. Restarting the entire `npm run dev` (both processes) mid-session and reopening the doc shows the exact persisted content — no data loss across a restart.

Two real bugs found only via this live, two-client testing — neither visible in single-user manual testing, which is exactly why the two-context Playwright pass mattered:

- **Hocuspocus defaults to unloading a document from memory the instant its last client disconnects (`unloadImmediately: true`), even if a debounced `onStoreDocument` write is still pending** — closing a tab shortly after typing (well within the 2s debounce window) silently lost that edit; on reconnect, `onLoadDocument` re-read the old, pre-edit `content` from the DB since the pending write never happened. Fixed by setting `unloadImmediately: false` in `server/collabServer.ts`, which makes the server wait for the pending debounced store before actually unloading.
- **Creating the `HocuspocusProvider` (whose constructor opens a real WebSocket) inside `useMemo` is unsafe under React's Strict Mode double-invocation** — React intentionally calls `useMemo` factories twice in dev to catch exactly this class of impurity, which opened two live WebSocket connections and silently orphaned one (never explicitly closed, since React only "keeps" one memoized result). Typed edits went into whichever Y.Doc ended up bound to the discarded connection roughly as often as the kept one, so they never reached the server and the debounced persist never fired — content appeared to type correctly on-screen but silently failed to save, with no error anywhere. Fixed by moving the provider's lifecycle into `useEffect`+`useState` (create on mount, destroy on cleanup/`docId` change) instead of `useMemo` — the correct, Strict-Mode-safe pattern for any resource whose creation has a real side effect, matching React's own guidance that `useMemo` must stay pure. Cost real debugging time before being traced to this exact root cause via direct server-side `onLoadDocument`/`onStoreDocument` logging + counting WebSocket open/close events from the Playwright side, rather than assumed.

Schema change today: `Doc.ydoc Bytes?` (nullable, one clean `db push`). New sidecar process changes the Windows dev-server-restart procedure — see the expanded "Known bugs" entry below.

**Trash icon repositioned** — moved from sitting inline with the Tasks/Planner/Docs/Office tabs to the bottom of the icon rail, separated by a spacer + thin divider (`app/page.tsx`'s nav `<nav>` block). It isn't a view tab, so it shouldn't visually read as one.

**"Base Building" gamification of Office Rooms** — plan-mode approved redesign of `components/OfficeRooms.tsx` (previously a flat responsive card grid + separate "Unassigned" tray) into a stacked-floor HQ building: Rooms render as full-width connected floors (`FloorRoom`, newest/highest `order` on top — "new floors get built upward"), a Lobby anchored at the bottom for unassigned people (amber-tinted, matching the message-of-the-day banner's accent), overlapping "collaborating" avatar clusters (negative-margin overlap + separation ring, replacing the old individual name-pill chips), and a new "step inside" room detail view. Drag-and-drop mechanics (`person-drag:`/`room-drag:`/`room-drop:` id-prefix dispatch in `app/page.tsx`'s shared `DndContext`) are entirely unchanged — only the visual containers around them changed.

- **DND at both levels, resolved with the user up front**: a person can be individually Do Not Disturb (small clickable status-dot badge directly on their avatar, works everywhere that avatar renders — floor cluster, Lobby, room detail's member list) *and* a room can independently be marked DND (e.g. "meeting in progress" even if members aren't personally DND) via a door-icon toggle on the floor header. Both are plain new `Boolean @default(false)` columns (`Room.isDnd`, `User.isDnd`) reusing the existing generic `updateRoom`/`updateUser` patch actions — no new store actions needed. The avatar badge's click needs `stopPropagation()` (the avatar itself already has an `onClick` for opening the person's profile); the floor header's DND/expand/delete buttons don't — dnd-kit's pointer-sensor 5px `activationConstraint` already lets a plain click on a nested button fall through untouched, the same mechanism that's let the header's icon/name/delete buttons coexist with its drag listeners since this feature was first built, verified against the actual working code rather than assumed.
- **New `components/PersonAvatar.tsx`** (shared circle-with-initials + optional DND badge, used by the floor clusters, Lobby, and the new room detail's member list) and **`components/RoomDetail.tsx`** (new — "step inside a room": back button, editable name, the room's icon/color customize controls **moved here entirely** from the old floor-header popover — decorating a room is now something you do by going inside it, matching the user's own framing — a labeled DND switch, and a browse-only bigger-avatar member list). `OfficePage.tsx` gained a third branch (`activeRoomId` → `RoomDetail`, alongside the existing `activeUserId` → person page and the default HQ Building view), with a person page taking priority if both happen to be set — same "most specific wins" convention as every other nav field. New `activeOfficeRoomId`/`setActiveOfficeRoomId` (store) plus a matching `officeRoomId` URL-sync field (`lib/navUrl.ts`), mechanically mirroring the existing `officeUserId` handling. "Back to team" (from a person's page) and every other "reset to top level" affordance (nav-rail icon, breadcrumb) now clear both `activeOfficeUserId` and `activeOfficeRoomId`, so navigating away from a person found *inside* a room detail doesn't strand you back in that room unexpectedly.
- **Real bug found and fixed during verification**: `POST /api/rooms` never set an explicit `order` on creation, silently defaulting to the schema's `0` for every new room — meaning the new "highest order renders on top" stacking rule had no way to actually distinguish a brand-new room from an old untouched one (both tied at 0), so new floors were appearing at the *bottom* of the stack instead of the top, contradicting the whole "grows upward" premise. Fixed by computing `(max existing order in that workspace) + 1` at creation time. Confirmed via Playwright that a freshly created floor now genuinely renders above older ones.
- **A costly test-automation mistake, disclosed rather than quietly fixed**: an early verification pass used a `.first()` Playwright locator to click "the newest room's delete button," written *before* the ordering bug above was found and fixed — at that point in time the newest room was tied at `order: 0` with an existing real room ("Robs kontor") and, via `Array.sort`'s tie-preserving stability, actually rendered *first* in the DOM. `.first()` therefore deleted the user's genuine "Robs kontor" room instead of the intended disposable test room. Caught immediately by cross-checking the database rather than trusting the screenshot flow, and recreated the room (same name, matching its apparent original undecorated state) — but the original row's id is gone for good. No other data referenced that id (its one member had already been unassigned earlier in the same verification pass), so the practical impact was limited to the room's identity, not any content or relationships. Lesson for future verification passes: once list ordering itself is the thing under test, a positional locator like `.first()`/`.last()` is unsafe for a *destructive* action — scope destructive clicks to an element matched by the specific name/id being tested, never by position, especially while the exact ordering behavior is still being actively fixed in the same session.

Schema change today (second): `Room.isDnd`/`User.isDnd Boolean @default(false)`, plus the `POST /api/rooms` ordering fix (no schema change, a route-logic fix).

## Today's session (2026-08-07, continued)

Three items pulled from the "Known v1 scope cuts" list above, done end to end in one plan-mode pass (approved plan covered all three), each independently Playwright-verified against the dev server. No schema changes.

**Room undo position fix** — the exact bug already named in PLANNING.md: `deleteRoom`'s `undo` (`store/useTaskStore.ts`) replays `createRoom` → `updateRoom` → re-assigns members, but the `updateRoom` follow-up call omitted `order` even though the pre-delete value was sitting right there in the closure alongside `icon`/`color`/`isDnd`, which *were* included. Since `Room` DELETE doesn't repack remaining rooms' `order` values (a real hard delete, no gap-filling) and `POST /api/rooms` only ever computes `max+1` (never fills gaps), the deleted room's original `order` stays a genuine open gap until something claims it — so passing `order: room.order` back is a fully correct fix in the common case, not an approximation. One-line fix. Verified via three rooms (A/B/C), deleting the middle one, Ctrl+Z, and reading `order` back via a direct `/api/workspaces` fetch (not just visual position) to confirm B landed back at its exact original `order` value between A and C, not bumped to the top.

**Rich-text formatting** in the collaborative Doc editor — Bold, Italic, Heading (levels 1–2), Bullet list, Ordered list, matching exactly what was named as deferred (no blockquotes/code/tables/links, which were never in scope).
