New packages: `@tiptap/extension-bold`/`-italic`/`-heading`/`-bullet-list`/`-ordered-list`/`-list-item`/`-hard-break`, following the app's existing pattern of hand-picking individual `@tiptap/extension-*` packages rather than pulling in `starter-kit`. Added to **both** `lib/collab/schema.ts`'s `collabExtensions` (the server-side schema `server/collabServer.ts` uses for legacy-doc migration writes) and `CollabDocEditor.tsx`'s client extension list — the one place in this codebase that must stay in lockstep between server and client, per the file's own comment. New `components/collab/DocToolbar.tsx`: a small fixed row above the editor (no floating/bubble-menu precedent exists anywhere in this app, so a fixed row was the simpler, more discoverable choice), active-state styling copied from `RoomDetail.tsx`'s existing icon-picker convention (the app's one prior example of a row of toggleable icon buttons) — `ring-1 ring-blue-500` + `bg-neutral-800` when active. Buttons use `onMouseDown={e => e.preventDefault()}` so clicking a toolbar button doesn't steal focus/selection from the editor before the format toggle runs.
`lib/collab/docJSONToPlainText.ts` (the `Doc.content` plain-text mirror `DocsBrowser.tsx`'s preview reads) was hardcoded exactly two levels deep (`doc.content[i].content[j]`) and only understood `text`/`mention` leaves — any new block type would have silently rendered as a blank line in previews. Rewrote it to walk `content` fully recursively: headings get their own line, bullet/ordered lists walk each `listItem`'s nested block(s) and prefix a `- `/`1. ` marker (readable, not meant to be markdown-parseable — this is a preview, not a rendering target). `legacyContentToDocJSON.ts` (the reverse, one-time migration path) needed no change — old docs still migrate in as flat paragraphs; formatting is something you apply afterward.
Verified two-context end to end: all six formats applied in one browser context replicate live to a second context within the same second (confirmed via the rendered `<strong>`/`<em>`/`<h1>`/`<h2>`/`<ul>`/`<ol>` DOM, not just eyeballed); content survives a page reload and a **full `npm run dev` restart** (both processes) with zero data loss; a mention typed inside a bullet-list item still renders as a live-resolving chip (`MentionNode` is `group: 'inline'`, composed with the new block types with no changes needed); a brand-new legacy-style plain-text doc (created directly via `POST /api/spaces/[id]/docs` with a `@[Label](kind:id)` token, `ydoc` still null) migrates correctly into the new richer schema on first collaborative open, mention rendered as a chip, exactly like before this session; Ctrl+Z under genuine concurrent editing (two contexts both appending near the same tail position at once) still only reverts the local user's own last edit, leaving the other context's concurrent edit intact — confirmed the new node/mark types didn't change Yjs's per-client `Y.UndoManager` behavior. `yXmlFragmentToProsemirrorJSON` (used with no explicit schema argument in `onStoreDocument`) was flagged during planning as unverified against the installed `@tiptap/y-tiptap@3.0.8` for the new node/mark types — confirmed live via the restart-persistence check above that it round-trips every new type correctly with no schema argument needed.

**Live presence ("who's online")** — the third scope cut, built to the lightweight interpretation confirmed with the user up front (a question asked directly, since the two readings differ hugely in scope): this app has **zero real authentication anywhere** (confirmed again this session: no `middleware.ts`, no `app/api/auth`, no auth package in `package.json`, no session/cookie verification — `useSessionStore`'s "You are: ..." picker is a trusted, unauthenticated `userId` string in `localStorage`, always has been). Building real login/session infrastructure would be a separate, much larger project than "presence" — so this reuses the existing unauthenticated identity as-is and adds *genuine* live online/offline detection on top of it, by reusing the same Hocuspocus WebSocket sidecar already running for Doc collaboration (no new server process, no `User` schema change, no login).
New reserved Hocuspocus room name (`lib/collab/presenceRoom.ts`'s `PRESENCE_DOCUMENT_NAME`, shared by client and server so they can't drift), every open tab connects to it for the app's whole session. `server/collabServer.ts`'s `onLoadDocument`/`onStoreDocument` both got an early-return guard for this reserved name, since it carries no persisted `Doc` row, only ephemeral awareness state. New `lib/collab/usePresenceConnection.ts` (mounted once in `app/page.tsx`'s `PageContent`, right where `useSessionStore()` is already destructured): builds one `HocuspocusProvider` for the session using the same Strict-Mode-safe `useEffect`+`useState` lifecycle `CollabDocEditor.tsx` already established (constructor in an effect, not `useMemo`), broadcasts `{userId, name, color}` via `provider.setAwarenessField('user', ...)`, keeps it in sync with "You are: ..." on identity switches without reconnecting the socket, and writes the live set of online `userId`s into a new tiny `store/usePresenceStore.ts` (same standalone-store isolation pattern as `useSessionStore.ts`, so it stays swappable if real session infra is ever built later). No manual heartbeat needed — Yjs awareness already clears a peer's state the instant its WebSocket closes, the same mechanism `components/collab/PresenceBar.tsx` already relies on for per-doc "who's viewing this doc."
`components/PersonAvatar.tsx` reads `usePresenceStore` directly (no new prop — every existing call site gets the online dot for free, unlike `showDndToggle` which is opt-in because it renders a click target) and renders a small green dot at the corner **opposite** the existing DND dot (new `ONLINE_DOT_SIZE_CLASSES`, `-bottom-*-left-*` vs. the DND dot's `-bottom-*-right-*`) so the two independent signals never visually collide — confirmed via screenshot with a user simultaneously DND'd and online.
Verified with two independent browser contexts: before a second context connects, a context sees only itself as online; after the second connects as a different person, both dots appear; switching the second context's identity mid-session (no reload) correctly moves the dot to the new identity and removes it from the old one; closing the second context's tab removes its dot from the first within ~2s (real disconnect detection, no stale "online forever" state); a person who is simultaneously DND'd and online correctly shows both indicators with no overlap; per-doc `PresenceBar.tsx` still works correctly alongside the new workspace-wide connection (confirmed only after realizing the first check queried the wrong DOM scope — `PresenceBar` renders as a *sibling* of `.collab-doc-editor` in `CollabDocEditor.tsx`'s JSX, not inside it, so a test querying `.collab-doc-editor` alone will never see it; see Known bugs below).
Also found and ruled out as a non-issue: a pre-existing, unrelated quirk where `CollabDocEditor.tsx`'s `useEditor` dependency array (`[docId, provider]`) doesn't include the current-user identity, so switching "You are: ..." *after* a doc is already open doesn't retroactively update that tab's collaboration-caret label until the doc is reopened. Predates this session, out of scope for the three items being fixed, not touched.

Environment note: this session's dev database (`prisma/dev.db`) started as a fresh empty file (0 bytes, gitignored per existing convention — no real data was at risk) and needed `prisma db push` + the seed script before the app would run at all; hit the documented EPERM-on-DLL issue immediately afterward and followed the existing documented procedure (stop every project `node.exe`, `prisma generate`, restart `npm run dev`) without incident.

## Today's session (2026-08-07, third) — Doc export to PDF and Google Docs

User request: export a Doc to PDF and to a real Google Doc. Plan-mode approved, built end to end. Both exports read `Doc.ydoc` (the binary Yjs snapshot) via a new shared `lib/collab/loadDocForExport.ts` — `Y.applyUpdate` + `yXmlFragmentToProsemirrorJSON` (the exact calls `server/collabServer.ts` already makes) when present, else the existing `legacyContentToDocJSON(doc.content)` fallback for a doc never opened collaboratively — never the plain-text `Doc.content` mirror directly, which has already lost bold/italic/heading/list structure.

**PDF export** — new `pdfkit` dependency (pure Node, no headless browser; bundles the standard 14 PDF fonts, so no font asset to manage). New `lib/collab/docJSONToPdf.ts`, a third recursive-descent walker alongside `docJSONToPlainText.ts`/(now) `docJSONToGoogleRequests.ts` — same paragraph/heading/bulletList/orderedList/listItem/text/mention shape, but issues pdfkit draw calls (font/size by heading level, indent + `•`/`N.` marker per list item, font switches between the four standard PDF fonts for bold/italic runs) instead of building a string. New `GET /api/docs/[id]/export/pdf` route, modeled on `app/api/calendar/[token]/route.ts`'s existing "generate a file from app data, serve via headers" shape — 404s on a missing/trashed doc, otherwise streams a real `application/pdf` with `Content-Disposition: attachment; filename="<title>.pdf"`.
**Real bug hit and fixed**: pdfkit loads its bundled `.afm` font files via `__dirname`-relative paths at runtime — Turbopack's default bundling of route handlers breaks that path resolution (`ENOENT ... C:\ROOT\node_modules\pdfkit\js\data\Helvetica.afm`, a garbled path that isn't even this project's real root). Fixed via Next's `serverExternalPackages: ["pdfkit"]` in `next.config.ts` (checked the actual installed Next 16 docs before configuring, per this repo's own "not the Next.js you know" instruction in AGENTS.md, rather than assuming the option's still named/shaped the same as older Next versions) — this tells Next to `require()` pdfkit natively from `node_modules` instead of bundling it, the same treatment Next already gives `sharp`/`better-sqlite3` automatically for the identical class of native/fs-path-dependent package. Config changes need a dev-server restart to take effect, not just a file save.

**Google Docs export** — new `googleapis` dependency (official client, includes the OAuth2 helper). Schema: `User` gained two nullable columns, `googleRefreshToken`/`googleEmail`, following the `calendarToken` precedent exactly — `googleRefreshToken` is a bearer credential, kept **out** of `lib/prisma.ts`'s `publicUserSelect` (same leak-prevention rule protecting `calendarToken`); `googleEmail` is display-only (not sensitive, just "Connected as ..."), so it's the opposite of `calendarToken` and **is** included in `publicUserSelect`, same treatment as `phone`. New `lib/google/oauthClient.ts` centralizes the three `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`/`GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI` env reads (bare `process.env.X`, same convention `server/collabServer.ts` already uses for `COLLAB_PORT` — no config/validation layer exists in this app). Two new OAuth routes: `GET /api/google/oauth/start` (redirects to Google's consent screen, `access_type: 'offline', prompt: 'consent'` so a refresh token actually comes back, `state` carries the userId) and `GET /api/google/oauth/callback` (exchanges the code, fetches the connected email, saves both to that `User` row, redirects back to `/` with a one-shot `?googleConnect=success|retry` flag — handled by its own small, deliberately isolated `useEffect` in `app/page.tsx` that shows a toast and strips the param, **not** folded into `lib/navUrl.ts`'s persistent nav-state sync, since this is a single trigger, not app state). New `lib/collab/docJSONToGoogleRequests.ts` (fourth recursive walker) flattens the doc into one `Line` per paragraph/heading/list-item, then computes exact character-index ranges as it concatenates them into one flat text buffer, emitting a Google Docs `batchUpdate` request list: one `insertText`, then `updateParagraphStyle` (headings → `HEADING_1`/`HEADING_2`), `createParagraphBullets` (lists), `updateTextStyle` (bold/italic runs) — verified structurally correct via a standalone `tsx` fixture run (every emitted range falls exactly within the inserted text's own bounds, spot-checked by hand against the source doc). New `POST /api/docs/[id]/export/google` (body `{userId}`): 404 missing/trashed doc, 400 "Google account not connected" if that user has no refresh token, else creates the Google Doc, applies the batchUpdate, returns `{url}`; on any Google API failure the user's stored refresh token is cleared (a revoked/expired token surfaces here as `invalid_grant`) so the UI correctly falls back to "reconnect" instead of failing identically on every future attempt.
Mentions render as plain label text in both PDF and Google export (neither format has an equivalent of this app's internal clickable-entity concept) — same simplification in both new walkers, stated up front in the plan rather than discovered as a gap.

**UI**: new `components/collab/DocExportMenu.tsx` (`docId` prop, reuses `FloatingPopover` — the same component already backing "Link existing"/"Link to task" right next to where this sits), wired into both `CollabDocEditor` call sites (the standalone Docs-tab header's existing `flex items-center gap-2` row, and a new flex wrapper around the task-modal Documents panel's title input, which had no sibling row before). "Download PDF" is a plain `<a href=... download>` (no client JS needed, the server's `Content-Disposition` does the work); "Export to Google Docs" checks the current "You are: ..." user's `googleEmail` — redirects to connect if absent, otherwise POSTs and opens the result in a new tab. A small persistent "Connect Google" status control (green `Share2` icon when connected, tooltip shows the connected email; gray + clickable link to the OAuth start route when not) sits in the identity strip next to the existing calendar-feed copy-link icon (`app/page.tsx`, the same `{currentUserId && ...}`-gated spot).

**Requires a manual step from the user before Google export actually works** (does not block anything else — the PDF half needs no external setup at all): create a Google Cloud project, enable the Google Docs API, configure the OAuth consent screen (External, Testing, own account as test user — no Google review needed), create a Web-application OAuth Client ID with redirect URI `http://localhost:3000/api/google/oauth/callback`, and put `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET`/`GOOGLE_REDIRECT_URI` in a new `.env.local` (already covered by the repo's existing blanket `.env*` gitignore rule — no `.gitignore` change needed). Verified every code path that's testable without those real credentials: the full formatted-doc → PDF round trip (bold/italic/both heading levels/both list types — real selectable PDF text, not an image, confirmed by reading the rendered PDF directly), the legacy-doc fallback PDF (mention rendered as plain label), a trashed doc correctly 404ing on export, the "Google account not connected" 400 path, and the "OAuth not configured" 500 path with a clear message instead of a crash. The actual OAuth round trip and a real Google Doc's resulting formatting are **not yet live-verified** — that needs the user's own Google Cloud credentials, which weren't available in this session.

Schema change today: `User.googleRefreshToken`/`googleEmail` (nullable, via `prisma db push` — dev server stopped first per the documented EPERM procedure, no incident).

## Today's session (2026-08-08) — Multi-workspace switching, a private personal workspace, and a global "My Tasks" view

From conversation (Norwegian): the user wanted to switch between different "groups" (jobs, side projects), an overarching private zone only they can see, and a personal task list spanning all of it that also shows up correctly in Planner/calendar without leaking to others. Plan-mode approved, built end to end, Playwright + direct-API verified.

**Key finding from research before building**: `Workspace` already had real 1:N relations to `Space`/`Room`, and `GET /api/workspaces` already did a genuine `findMany` (not hardcoded to one row) — but `User` had **zero relation to `Workspace`** anywhere. Every user had implicit access to everything in the database; there was no membership concept to build on. The UI also only ever rendered `workspaces[0]` (5 call sites, no `activeWorkspaceId`, no switcher) even though the store's own reducers already treated `workspaces` as a real array — so the data-mutation layer was already multi-workspace-safe, but membership and the read/render surface both needed building from scratch.

**The privacy bar, stated to the user up front and unchanged from every other per-person feature in this app** (`calendarToken`, Google OAuth tokens): server-side filtering by whichever identity the client currently asserts (`useSessionStore`'s "You are: ..."), not cryptographic security — this app still has no real login. A real, meaningful improvement over "just hide it in the UI" (a private workspace's data genuinely never gets sent over the network to a non-member), but anyone who switches identity to you in the same browser still sees what you see.

**Workspace membership** (`prisma/schema.prisma`): `Workspace.members`/`User.workspaces`, an implicit many-to-many using the exact same pattern already in place for `Task.assignees`/`User.tasks` (`@relation("WorkspaceMembers")`) — no new join-table model needed. A workspace with exactly one member *is* what "private" means here; no separate `isPrivate` flag. One-off backfill script (run once, then deleted — no `scripts/` dir convention exists in this repo, matching how the earlier `calendarToken` migration was just documented in prose, not left as a permanent file) connected every existing User to the existing seeded Workspace, so the 3 seeded users kept seeing exactly what they already saw.

**Disclosed, deliberate behavior change**: "You are: (none)" now sees **zero** workspaces instead of everything — unavoidable, since an unset identity being able to see workspace-scoped data would make clearing your identity a trivial way to bypass anyone's privacy. `GET /api/workspaces`, `GET /api/tasks`, and `GET /api/task-docs` all gained a `?userId=` param and return `[]` when it's missing/empty, rather than falling back to "everything."

**Real privacy, not cosmetic**: `GET /api/tasks` and `GET /api/task-docs` are the two routes that make this actually mean something — both gained the same membership filter, joined up through `List → Space → Workspace → members` (a nested Prisma `where`, e.g. `list: { space: { workspace: { members: { some: { id: userId } } } } }`), so a private workspace's tasks/docs are simply never included in the response to a non-member — not filtered client-side after the fact. `fetchInitialData()` (`store/useTaskStore.ts`) now passes `useSessionStore.getState().currentUserId` as `?userId=` on all three membership-scoped fetches, and — new — the fetch effect in `app/page.tsx` re-runs whenever `currentUserId` changes, not just on mount, so switching identity actually pulls that person's own visible workspaces instead of continuing to show the previous person's. `GET /api/users` deliberately stays unfiltered — Users remain one global directory across every workspace (the user's own framing was about grouping work/projects, not hiding colleagues from each other), so no invite/signup flow was needed since Users are already pre-existing trusted rows in this app.

**Workspace CRUD + membership API**: `POST /api/workspaces` (didn't exist before — creates a workspace, connects the creator as its sole initial member), `POST/DELETE /api/workspaces/[id]/members/[userId]` (plain generic mutations, `store/useTaskStore.ts`'s `addWorkspaceMember`/`removeWorkspaceMember` — undo/redo included via the standard `push({undo, redo})` pattern, pushed only after the mutating fetch resolves per the documented race-condition rule). No `DELETE /api/workspaces` route — deliberately out of scope this session, so `createWorkspace` has no undo/redo (nothing to undo *to*), unlike every sibling create action.

**Workspace switcher UI**: the sidebar header (previously a static `<h1>{workspaces[0]?.name}</h1>`) is now a `FloatingPopover`-backed dropdown — workspace list (current one highlighted, single-member ones labeled "Private"), "+ New workspace," and a Members section for the current workspace (remove button per member if more than one remains; every non-member User listed as a one-click "+ Add") — all built directly in `app/page.tsx`, no nested `FloatingPopover`s (an early draft nested one for "Add member" and was reworked flat instead, since a `FloatingPopover`'s panel portals to `document.body` — nesting one inside another means the inner panel's DOM location isn't actually inside the outer's own `panelRef`, so the outer's click-outside handler would incorrectly treat clicking inside the inner popover as "clicked outside" and close prematurely; caught during design, not after a bug). New `activeWorkspaceId` (store) + `workspace` URL param (`lib/navUrl.ts`, mirrors `space`'s existing round-trip) — switching workspace resets Space/List selection to that workspace's own default, same "nav context changed" shape as switching Space already has. All 5 `workspaces[0]` call sites found in research replaced with a new memoized `currentWorkspace` (`workspaces.find(w => w.id === activeWorkspaceId) ?? workspaces[0]`, the `?? workspaces[0]` fallback existing only for the brief window before `activeWorkspaceId` first resolves).

**Global "My Tasks" view** (`components/MyTasksPage.tsx`, new `activeView: 'mytasks'`, 5th nav-rail icon using `UserCircle` — already imported, already means "a person" elsewhere in `CommandPalette.tsx`): a direct port of `components/OfficePage.tsx`'s existing `myTasks`/`listSpaceById` filter pattern, minus the "pick any team member" wrapper (always the current session identity here) and with a workspace name added to the breadcrumb (`{workspace} / {space} / {list}`) since tasks here can now come from more than one workspace at once. Needed no new query of its own — since `tasks` is now membership-scoped end to end, "My Tasks" spans every workspace the current person belongs to for free. The `.ics` calendar feed needed **no change at all**: it was already scoped by `assignees: { some: { id: user.id } }` with no Space/Workspace join whatsoever, confirmed in this session's research, so a personal task assigned to you already flowed into your own feed correctly before any of this was built.

**Verified** (Playwright + direct API, not just eyeballed): a brand-new private workspace's Space/List/Task is completely invisible to a second user's `/api/tasks`/`/api/workspaces` responses (checked the raw network payload, not just the UI) until explicitly added as a member, after which it appears correctly on both API and UI; "You are: (none)" shows zero workspaces; the existing seeded workspace + its 3 users kept seeing exactly what they saw before the migration; the Members panel's add-member button works via a real UI click, not just the underlying route; My Tasks correctly aggregates a task from the private workspace alongside tasks from the shared one, with the right 3-level breadcrumb; every existing nav tab (Tasks/Planner/Docs/Office) still loads with zero console errors post-change. One test-script-only false negative along the way (not an app bug): a raw `page.evaluate(() => fetch(...))` PATCH bypassing the store's own action layer correctly updated the database but left the already-loaded local `tasks` array stale, so a same-page check without a reload/refetch looked like a failure — resolved by reloading so `fetchInitialData` picked up the fresh assignment, same "test bypassed the app's real update path" category as prior sessions' fire-and-forget timing notes.

Schema change today: `Workspace.members`/`User.workspaces` implicit M2M (via `prisma db push`, dev server stopped first, clean — plus a one-off backfill script, run once and deleted, connecting every existing user to the existing workspace).

## Today's session (2026-08-08, continued) — A personal "Me" zone, cheaper modal animation, tab-visibility settings, and a cleaner sidebar tree

Six requests from conversation (Norwegian), all plan-mode approved in one pass, Playwright + direct-API verified.

**A persistent sidebar "Me" zone**, above the workspace switcher — not a nav-rail tab. Avatar + name (click → profile page), then two links that split what used to be one cross-workspace "My Tasks" nav icon (removed) into two distinct things, resolved with the user up front since they're genuinely different: **"My tasks"** — a brand-new private personal quick-list, and **"My assigned tasks"** — the old `MyTasksPage.tsx`, narrowed this session from spanning every workspace down to just the currently active one.

**Personal profile** (`User` gained `avatarUrl`/`bio`/`linkedinUrl`/`websiteUrl`, all nullable, all added to `publicUserSelect` — none are sensitive). New `components/ProfilePage.tsx` (`activeView: 'profile'`): avatar is click-to-edit-URL (the exact same "no upload, just a URL" pattern `Space.coverImageUrl` already established — this app still has no file/blob storage), bio is a multi-line click-to-edit block mirroring `SpaceHome.tsx`'s `DescriptionBlock`, LinkedIn/website reuse a newly-**exported** `EditableField` (previously private to `OfficePage.tsx` — this is now the second place that needed it, so exporting beat a third copy-paste). `components/PersonAvatar.tsx` and the sidebar identity-strip's own small avatar both gained an `avatarUrl` check (image if set, else the existing initials fallback) — deliberately **not** the other 3 independently-duplicated initials-circle copies elsewhere in the app (`OfficeRooms.tsx`/`TaskRow.tsx`/etc.), out of scope, not a regression.

**Private personal "My tasks"** lives inside the user's own single-member workspace (the "private workspace" concept from the previous session), in a hidden default Space/List the user never sees as structure — `Workspace.isPersonal` marks it so it's filtered out of the workspace switcher entirely and never becomes "the current workspace" (`fetchInitialData`'s initial pick and `app/page.tsx`'s `currentWorkspace` fallback both now skip `isPersonal` workspaces). New `components/PersonalTasksPage.tsx`: a quick-add input creates a real `Task` row directly with a fixed `listId` — deliberately bypassing `CreateTaskModal.tsx` (which always requires an explicit Space+List picker with no prop to hide them) rather than fighting its design. Clicking a personal task opens the same universal task modal as any other task, since it's a completely normal `Task` row under the hood — full comments/docs/status/dates for free, no new task-detail UI needed.

**Real bug found during verification, not anticipated in the plan**: the find-or-create route for the personal workspace (`POST /api/workspaces/personal`) originally did a plain `findFirst`-then-`create` — which raced. The sidebar's "My tasks" button called `ensurePersonalWorkspace` on click, and `PersonalTasksPage.tsx`'s own mount effect *also* called it (needed so deep-linking straight to `?view=mypersonal` works) — two calls per click, then doubled again by React Strict Mode's dev-mode effect double-invocation, meaning up to 4 near-simultaneous requests per single click. Each one's `findFirst` could see "nothing exists yet" before any of the others had committed their `create`, so multiple personal workspaces got created for the same user (caught 3 for one test user during verification, confirmed via a direct DB query showing 3 distinct `Workspace` rows all `isPersonal: true` for the same member). Fixed at the root, not papered over: added `Workspace.personalOwnerId String? @unique`, and rewrote the route around a single atomic `prisma.workspace.upsert({ where: { personalOwnerId: userId }, ... })` — a real DB-level unique constraint makes a duplicate impossible regardless of how many concurrent requests land, which a `findFirst`-then-`create` shape can never guarantee no matter how carefully it's written. Also removed the now-redundant sidebar-click call (the page's own mount effect already covers it). **Verified the fix directly**: fired 10 genuinely concurrent `POST` requests from the browser via `Promise.all` for a fresh user — all 10 converged on the exact same `workspaceId`, confirmed via a follow-up DB query showing exactly one row. Same general lesson as this project's earlier Strict-Mode/`useMemo` WebSocket bug and the reorder-transaction race from two sessions ago: **"find-or-create" needs a real unique constraint and an atomic upsert, not a check-then-act pair, and React Strict Mode's dev-mode double-invocation is a reliable way to *discover* a race that would otherwise only show up under real concurrent users.**

**Task modal: fade-in open, instant close.** The previous "row zooms into modal" effect came from the modal's inner content `motion.div` sharing a `layoutId` with `TaskRow.tsx`'s own row `motion.div` — Framer Motion interpolates position/size between `layoutId`-linked elements, which is exactly the "shared layout" magic-move mechanic. Removed `layoutId` from the **modal's** copy only (its own row `layout`/`layoutId` in `TaskRow.tsx` is completely untouched, since that also drives that row's independent list-reflow/reorder animation — an unrelated concern) and gave the modal's content its own plain `initial`/`animate` opacity fade instead. For the "snap away" close: **removed the `exit` prop entirely** from both the backdrop and content `motion.div`s — a Framer Motion component with no `exit` prop unmounts the instant it's removed from the tree, since `AnimatePresence` has nothing to animate; no new state or props needed, just deleting two props and simplifying the one motion.div that no longer needed to be one (the innermost content wrapper, previously a separately-faded `motion.div`, is now a plain `<div>`, since the outer wrapper's own fade already covers it).

**Settings menu** (`components/SettingsPanel.tsx`, structurally identical to `components/TrashPanel.tsx` — self-contained, `onClose` prop, centered overlay, deliberately **no** framer-motion, matching the modal change's "cheaper feel" direction): toggles which of the 4 view tabs (Tasks/Planner/Docs/Office) show in the nav rail. New `siqt.hiddenNavTabs` localStorage key, same "only persist the hidden minority" Set-of-ids shape as `FolderTree.tsx`'s existing `readCollapsedFolders`/`setFolderCollapsed` (defaults to "everything visible"). Trash and the new Me zone are unaffected — neither is one of the 4 toggleable tabs (Trash already wasn't a tab; the Me zone is a persistent sidebar element, not a nav-rail destination). New gear icon in the rail, next to Trash.

**FolderTree: hover-reveal chevron, deeper indentation.** The Folder row's expand/collapse chevron was previously a separate, always-visible `<button>` rendered before the folder's own decorative icon. Removed it entirely — the folder's own icon slot now shows the icon by default and morphs into a chevron (`ChevronRight`/`ChevronDown` matching current state) on hover, reusing the exact `group`/`group-hover:opacity` idiom this row's Rename/Delete buttons already used (no new hover state needed, the row div was already `className="group ..."`). Indentation (`FolderLevel`'s per-depth wrapper, previously a flat `ml-4 pl-2` at every non-zero depth) bumped to `ml-6 pl-3` — still a flat increment that compounds through natural DOM nesting rather than a depth-multiplied value, just a bigger step per level so 2+-deep nesting reads clearly at a glance. List rows inherit the wider indentation automatically since they render inside the same wrapper.

Schema changes today: `User.avatarUrl`/`bio`/`linkedinUrl`/`websiteUrl`, `Workspace.isPersonal`, then `Workspace.personalOwnerId` (unique, added after the race-condition fix above) — all via `prisma db push` (`--accept-data-loss` needed for the unique-constraint addition; safe here since the column was brand new and every existing row was `NULL`, which SQLite's `UNIQUE` allows any number of), dev server stopped first per the documented EPERM procedure each time.

## Today's session (2026-08-08, continued again) — Top bar, so the sidebar isn't carrying everything at once

User feedback (with a ClickUp screenshot for reference): the app felt "squished" with the workspace switcher, search box, and Me zone all stacked at the very top of the sidebar with no breathing room. Moved the workspace switcher and search into a new full-width top `<header>` (logo + workspace name/switcher on the left, centered search, matching ClickUp's own top-bar layout), so the icon rail and sidebar both now start naturally below it instead of carrying that weight themselves.

`app/page.tsx`'s top-level return restructured from a single `<div className="flex h-screen">` into `<div className="flex flex-col h-screen"><header>...</header><div className="flex flex-1 overflow-hidden">{nav rail, sidebar, main content}</div></div>` — the new `<header>` (`h-14`) holds the "R" logo (moved out of the icon rail, which now starts directly with the Tasks button) and the exact same `FloatingPopover` workspace-switcher block that used to live at the top of the sidebar (workspace list, "+ New workspace," Members add/remove — all unchanged, just relocated), plus the "Zero-Cloud SQLite" status line as a subtitle under the workspace name. The search button (opens the existing Command Palette) moved from the sidebar into the header's centered slot. The sidebar now starts directly with the "Me" zone, immediately followed by the Space/List tree — no separate workspace-switcher or search block above it anymore. All fixed-position overlays (toasts, `ConfirmDialog`s, `DragOverlay`, `CommandPalette`, `TrashPanel`, `SettingsPanel`) stayed siblings of the inner flex row rather than needing to move — their `fixed` positioning is relative to the viewport regardless of DOM nesting depth, so this was a pure layout move with zero behavioral changes to verify beyond "does everything still open/close correctly," which it does.

No schema or data changes. Verified via screenshots across Tasks/Docs/Planner/Office (top bar and its workspace-switcher/search both work identically to before, just relocated) and confirmed zero console errors navigating every view.

## Today's session (2026-08-08, continued a third time) — Space headers get the same hover-reveal chevron as Folders

Two more ClickUp screenshots from the user: hovering a Folder-equivalent row swaps its plain icon for a clean directional arrow (right if collapsed, down if open), reverting to the icon on hover-away. `FolderTree.tsx`'s `FolderRow` already got this treatment last session, but Space headers in `app/page.tsx` were explicitly left out of scope at the time — the user's phrasing this time ("ikonene i spaces, eller folderne") called it out directly, so this pass closes that gap.

`app/page.tsx`'s Space header row: removed the old standalone always-visible chevron `<span role="button">` plus the separately-rendered colored dot further down the same row, and replaced both with one combined `role="button"` slot — colored dot visible by default (`opacity-100 group-hover:opacity-0`), `ChevronRight`/`ChevronDown` (keyed off the existing `collapsedSpaceIds.has(space.id)` check) absolutely positioned over it and fading in on hover (`opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100`) — the exact same `group`/`group-hover:opacity` idiom as `FolderTree.tsx`, reusing the row's pre-existing `group` class rather than adding hover state.

No schema/data changes. Verified with Playwright screenshots: plain dot at rest, chevron-down on hover while expanded, chevron-right on hover after collapsing — matches the ClickUp reference in all three states.

Follow-up bug the user caught right after: the toggle only fired on the exact 12px icon box — clicking a couple pixels to either side, even while the chevron was visibly showing (hover is driven by the whole row via `group`, not by being over the tiny icon itself), fell through to the row's own `onClick` and just navigated/selected the Space instead of collapsing it. Fixed in both `app/page.tsx`'s Space header and `FolderTree.tsx`'s `FolderRow` using the standard invisible-hit-area trick: the icon/chevron toggle button keeps its visual size (an inner `relative w-3 h-3` span, unchanged), but the outer clickable element drops its fixed width and instead gets `p-1.5 -m-1.5` — padding enlarges the actual clickable box, the matching negative margin cancels it back out of the surrounding flex row's layout, so the row's spacing looks identical but the toggle now has a noticeably larger, more forgiving hit target. Verified by clicking a few px off the icon's visual center (toggled correctly) and by clicking the Space/Folder name further right (still navigates, doesn't accidentally toggle) — confirmed via `localStorage['siqt.collapsedSpaces']` before/after each click.

Second follow-up: the user wanted the whole row — including the Space's name text, not just the icon — to open a collapsed Space, matching how `FolderTree.tsx`'s `FolderRow` already behaves (its whole-row click already toggled expand/collapse, pre-dating this session). Added an `expandSpace(spaceId)` helper next to the existing `toggleSpaceCollapsed` in `app/page.tsx` — force-open only (deletes from `collapsedSpaceIds` if present, no-ops if already expanded), deliberately not a toggle, so repeatedly clicking the Space name to re-select/re-navigate it never accidentally collapses it again. Called at the top of the Space row's `onClick` before its existing navigate/calendar-toggle/docs-navigate branches; the dedicated icon slot's own click handler still calls `e.stopPropagation()` first, so icon clicks reach only the real toggle, not this new force-open path. Verified: clicking the icon on an expanded Space collapses it; clicking the Space's name text expands it again; clicking the name text a second time leaves it expanded rather than re-collapsing it.

## Today's session (2026-08-08, continued a fourth time) — Real authentication: Google OAuth + email/password login

The user wants to eventually put Siqt on a real server for real users, and asked for Google sign-in specifically. That surfaced the app's biggest remaining gap: there was **no real authentication at all** — identity was a client-asserted `userId` picked from a "You are: ..." dropdown (`store/useSessionStore.ts`, localStorage-backed), and every API route that scoped data by identity (`?userId=` query param) simply trusted whatever the caller sent. Fine for local/team use, a genuine vulnerability once real users are involved. Confirmed two scope forks with the user directly: **both** Google OAuth and email/password should work (not Google-only), and existing seeded/demo Users (Casper O., Robin, etc.) are **not** migrated — real accounts start completely fresh, seed data stays only as disconnected local dev/demo fixture data ("start helt på nytt").

**"Not the Next.js you know" fact, confirmed by reading `node_modules/next/dist/docs/.../file-conventions/proxy.md` before writing anything**: Next 16 renamed `middleware.ts` to `proxy.ts` outright — no `middleware.ts` support exists anymore — and Proxy now **defaults to the Node.js runtime** (changed in v16.0.0, previously Edge-only/experimental). That meant the usual Auth.js tutorial pattern of splitting an Edge-safe `auth.config.ts` from a full `auth.ts` was unnecessary here — one `auth.ts` works everywhere including `proxy.ts`.

**Stack**: Auth.js v5 (`next-auth@beta` + `@auth/prisma-adapter`), **JWT-only sessions** (required — the Credentials provider can't use Auth.js's database session strategy), Google + Credentials providers together (`allowDangerousEmailAccountLinking: true` so the same email works via either). `bcryptjs` for password hashing (pure-JS, no native build step — avoids adding *more* native-compile pain on top of the project's already-fussy Windows/EPERM `prisma generate` loop; `argon2` was the other option, ruled out for that reason). No `zod` — hand-rolled email-regex + length validation in the signup route, matching the project's established "skip a dependency for something this small" precedent (pdfkit over Puppeteer).

**Schema** (`prisma/schema.prisma`): `User.initials` gained a `@default("")` — `@auth/prisma-adapter`'s `createUser()` only ever supplies `id/name/email/emailVerified/image`, so a brand-new Google sign-in's very first insert would otherwise violate the old bare-required `initials` column before anything else ran; backfilled to a real value right after in `auth.ts`'s `events.createUser` hook. New nullable+unique `User.email` (same `Workspace.personalOwnerId`-style "NULL is distinct under SQLite's unique index" precedent — every seeded User keeps `email: null` with zero collisions), `emailVerified`, `password` (bcrypt hash, null for Google-only accounts — rejected explicitly in `authorize()`, not silently allowed), and `image` (the adapter's required literal field name for OAuth bookkeeping — deliberately kept separate from the existing `avatarUrl`, which is the app's own display avatar, to avoid conflating the two). New `Account` model (standard Auth.js shape, needed only for Google OAuth linking) — no `Session` model (JWT sessions never touch it) and no `VerificationToken` (no magic-link/Email provider exists).

**New files**: `auth.ts` (root — providers, JWT callbacks that attach `session.user.id`, since Auth.js doesn't do that by default; `types/next-auth.d.ts` augments the `Session` type to match), `proxy.ts` (`export { auth as proxy } from './auth'`, matcher excludes `/api/*` and `/login`), `lib/auth/session.ts` (`getCurrentUserId()` — the one helper every API route now calls; proxy/middleware-only protection is explicitly not sufficient per the Next.js docs, so every Route Handler re-verifies independently), `app/api/auth/[...nextauth]/route.ts`, `app/api/auth/signup/route.ts`, `app/login/page.tsx` (tab toggle for sign-in/create-account, email+password fields, a "Continue with Google" button — matches the app's existing neutral dark chrome), `components/SessionSync.tsx` (bridges `next-auth/react`'s `useSession()` into `useSessionStore`, so the rest of the app's 6 existing `useSessionStore` consumers needed zero changes — that file's own long-standing comment predicted exactly this: "when real login/sessions exist, this file is the only thing that needs replacing").

**`store/useSessionStore.ts`** kept its exact public shape (`currentUserId`/`setCurrentUserId`); only the internals changed — no more localStorage read/write, just a plain Zustand field populated by `SessionSync`. `app/layout.tsx` wraps `{children}` in `next-auth/react`'s `SessionProvider` (itself a Client Component, importable directly into the still-Server-Component layout). `app/page.tsx`'s old "You are: ..." `<select>` is gone, replaced by a read-only name + a Sign out button (`signOut()`); the calendar-link-copy and Google-Docs-connect icons in that same strip are unchanged.

**Every route that used to trust `?userId=`/`body.userId` now derives identity from `getCurrentUserId()` instead**: `GET/POST /api/workspaces`, `GET /api/tasks`, `GET /api/task-docs`, `POST /api/workspaces/personal` (GET routes keep the existing "no identity → `[]`" contract; POST routes 401 instead). `POST /api/workspaces/[id]/members` and `DELETE .../members/[userId]` got an *additional* fix beyond the identity-source swap — there was previously **zero check that the caller was even a member of the workspace** they were adding/removing people from; both now 403 a non-member.

**Two more instances of the identical bug, found while reading the actual route files for this plan** (folded into the same pass rather than left as a worse problem once this plan shipped):
- `/api/google/oauth/start` + `.../callback` (the unrelated Docs-export "connect your Google account" flow, `lib/google/oauthClient.ts`): `start` took `?userId=` with zero verification and threaded it through Google's OAuth `state` param; `callback` blindly wrote `googleRefreshToken`/`googleEmail` to whatever id was in `state`. Anyone who got a victim to hit `/api/google/oauth/start?userId=<victim>` could complete Google's consent with their *own* account and have the resulting refresh token attached to the victim's row instead. Both routes now derive/verify identity via `getCurrentUserId()` (the callback requires it to match `state` — the session cookie survives the external round-trip to Google and back since it's tied to the browser, not the navigation path).
- `/api/users/[id]` GET/PATCH/DELETE — the more severe one. GET did a raw `findUnique` with **no `select` and no auth check at all**, returning the entire row — including `calendarToken` and `googleRefreshToken` — to any anonymous request for any id. (The file's own PATCH comment claimed calendarToken was owner-only; the code never actually implemented that.) Once this plan added `password`, the exact same unguarded GET would have started leaking bcrypt hashes to anyone who requested a user's id — turning a pre-existing leak into a critical one via this very change. Fixed with a caller-conditional `select` (owner gets `calendarToken` too, everyone else gets the plain `publicUserSelect` — password/googleRefreshToken/email never leave this route regardless). PATCH and DELETE both gained an auth check too, but **not** a self-only one — turns out both are legitimate existing "act on someone else" features (PATCH: the Office directory's inline phone/title/status/DND editing, `app/page.tsx`'s `onUpdatePhone`/`onUpdateUserField` and `OfficeRooms.tsx`'s `onToggleDnd` all call `updateUser` with someone *else's* id; DELETE: `deleteUser` in the Team panel removes a *different* team member). Caught this by grepping actual call sites before assuming "PATCH your own profile" was the intent — a naive `callerId === id` check would have silently broken both features. This app has no admin/owner role concept yet to check against instead, so both routes only close the "a fully anonymous request can act on an arbitrary account" gap, not "which logged-in member is allowed to edit/remove which other member" — noted as a deferred follow-up, not silently glossed over.

**Deferred, named explicitly rather than silently skipped**: workspace invite-by-email (the "Add member" picker still only lists people who've already signed up — no invite flow exists); Hocuspocus sidecar auth (`server/collabServer.ts` has no `onAuthenticate` hook at all — real-time Doc collaboration/presence still trusts whatever connects); broader per-route authorization hardening (most other mutating routes — tasks, comments, rooms, docs, spaces, lists — still don't check workspace membership before allowing a mutation; this pass only fixed the routes tied to identity/privacy plus the two related vulnerabilities found along the way); actual deployment; email verification, password reset, and signup/login rate limiting.

**Google Cloud setup**: turned out no `.env.local` existed at all in this environment — the earlier Docs-export session's "needs the user's own Google Cloud OAuth credentials to fully test" note was never actually resolved, so there was no existing OAuth Client to reuse as originally planned. Generated `AUTH_SECRET` locally; the user then created a real Google Cloud OAuth Client (Web application, both `http://localhost:3000/api/auth/callback/google` and `http://localhost:3000/api/google/oauth/callback` registered as Authorized redirect URIs on the same client — login and the unrelated Docs-export connect flow share one Client ID/Secret pair) and provided the resulting Client ID/Secret, written into `.env.local` (gitignored, never touched by git).

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright**: sign up with email/password → lands in the app shell with a real session; reload → session persists; sign out → visiting `/` redirects to `/login` via `proxy.ts` (confirmed via the resulting URL); **the original vulnerability itself, proven closed**: signed in as User A, appended `?userId=<User B>` to `GET /api/workspaces`/`GET /api/tasks` — response still only ever contained User A's own data (0 results, not User B's); User B genuinely cannot see User A's private workspace via the normal `GET /api/workspaces`; unauthenticated `POST /api/workspaces` → 401; unauthenticated `PATCH /api/users/[id]` → 401; unauthenticated `GET /api/users/[id]` → no `calendarToken` in the response; a non-member's `POST`/`DELETE` on workspace members → 403 both ways; unauthenticated `GET /api/google/oauth/start` → 401. Direct DB check confirmed stored passwords are real bcrypt hashes (`$2b$10$...`), never plaintext. The "Continue with Google" button was verified up to the handoff point once real credentials landed — intercepted the outgoing request rather than letting it actually load, confirmed it redirects to `accounts.google.com` with the correct `client_id`, `redirect_uri=http://localhost:3000/api/auth/callback/google`, minimal `scope=openid profile email` (not the separate Docs-export scopes), and `response_type=code`. Completing an actual interactive Google consent screen click-through with a real Google account is the one step that has to be done by a human in a real browser, not something scriptable. All test accounts, their auto-created personal workspaces, and the verification scripts were cleaned up afterward.

## Today's session (2026-08-08, continued a fifth time) — Calendar month/week view no longer leaves dead space when empty

User feedback with a screenshot: an empty Planner month view looked "ugly" — a bare grid at the top with a large void of plain black page background below it. Turned out to be a real layout bug, not just bare styling: `components/calendar/CalendarView.tsx`'s `rowHeight` was purely content-driven (`DAY_NUM_H + laneCount * (BAR_H + BAR_GAP)`, minimum 1 lane even with zero tasks) — with nothing scheduled, 6 weeks × ~42px ≈ 250px of actual grid content sat inside a `flex-1` container that filled the full remaining viewport height, leaving everything below that content as plain unstyled space.

Fixed by measuring the grid container's real height (`ResizeObserver` on the existing `gridContainerRef`, new `containerHeight` state) and stretching `rowHeight` to `Math.max(contentDrivenHeight, containerHeight / weeks.length)` — rows now always fill the available viewport (matching how Google Calendar/Notion's month view behaves) when there's little content, while months with enough tasks to need the natural larger content height (or the "+N more" overflow strip) are unaffected, since `Math.max` only ever grows the value. `overflowTop` (where the "+N more" strip starts) deliberately stays anchored to the *content* height, not the stretched one, so it doesn't drift down into the newly-added blank space. Verified this didn't regress task-bar positioning or the day-hit-test math in `dayOffsetFromPoint` (both already used the single `rowHeight` number uniformly, so feeding it a larger value when empty just moves everything consistently) by creating a real task and confirming its bar still renders at the correct row/day.

Also added, per the "make it look more modern" ask: subtle weekend-column shading (`bg-white/[0.025]`) and a soft full-cell tint on today (`bg-blue-500/[0.06]`) in `components/calendar/WeekRow.tsx` — same priority order as the existing day-number badge already implied (out-of-month dimmest, today strongest, weekend a faint in-between), so the three signals never visually collide. Deliberately did *not* add an explicit "no tasks yet" empty-state message — a bare grid is the expected, normal look for a calendar with nothing scheduled, unlike a list view.

## Today's session (2026-08-08, continued a sixth time) — Fixing the orphaned-session crash led straight to the "Team" panel's real problem

The previous session's `getCurrentUserId()` hardening fixed the *crash*, but the user asked the sharper follow-up: why did their real account's User row vanish from the DB in the first place, and shouldn't deleting an account be harder than that? Tracing it back landed on `app/page.tsx`'s "Team" modal (`teamOpen`) — a leftover from the pre-auth shared-identity model that (a) listed **every** `User` row in the entire database via the fully unscoped `GET /api/users`, not just people in the current workspace, and (b) gave each one a trash-can button that called `deleteUser(id)` — a full, permanent account deletion — with **zero confirmation**, reachable for literally any registered user by anyone who opened the panel. That's almost certainly how the account disappeared: a stray click while exploring the panel.

Three changes, matching what was actually wrong:

1. **`GET /api/users` and the Team modal are now workspace-scoped.** The modal reads `currentWorkspace?.members` (already in memory from `GET /api/workspaces`, no new fetch) instead of the global `users` array. `GET /api/users` itself stays as the existing app-wide directory (Office, assignees, @-mentions, Command palette still need a broader "everyone" list — deliberately *not* touched, that's a bigger, separate architecture question about whether those should also narrow to workspace membership, named but not solved here) — only this one modal's data source changed.
2. **"Delete" became "remove from workspace," behind a real confirmation.** The Team modal's trash icon now calls the already-existing, already membership-checked `removeWorkspaceMember` (built two sessions ago) via a new `ConfirmDialog` (`memberToRemove` state, matching the same `xToDelete`-confirm-dialog pattern already used everywhere else in this file) — explicitly labeled "Remove," not "Delete," and the dialog body says outright that the account itself isn't touched. Hidden entirely for your own row (removing yourself from your own current workspace via this panel isn't part of what was asked). The old "+ Add user" quick-create-a-fake-person form is gone too — spawning a credential-less phantom person doesn't fit the real-account model, and it was the only remaining caller of `addUser`/`initialsFromName`, both removed as dead code.
3. **`DELETE /api/users/[id]` (full account deletion) is now genuinely self-service only, with re-proof of identity.** Re-tightened to `callerId === id` (safe now that nothing calls it with someone *else's* id anymore) and requires either the account's password (bcrypt-compared server-side) or, for Google-only accounts with no password, typing the exact email address — a new `hasPassword: boolean` flag merged onto the caller's own entry in `GET /api/users` (never added to `publicUserSelect` itself, so it can never leak onto anyone else's entry) drives which confirmation UI `components/ProfilePage.tsx`'s new "Danger zone" section shows. Successful deletion signs the browser out and redirects to `/login`.

Verified end-to-end: Team modal shows exactly the two real members of a test workspace, not the unrelated leftover seed user; removing someone drops them from workspace membership while their account (confirmed via a follow-up `GET /api/users/[id]`) still exists; a wrong password on self-delete → 403; one account attempting to delete a *different* account directly via the API → 403 even though both are legitimately authenticated; the correct password → the account is actually gone (404 afterward) and the browser lands on `/login`.

## Today's session (2026-08-09) — Profile dropdown menu (ClickUp-style), collapsed Danger zone, and "Workspace Settings" naming

User shared a ClickUp screenshot showing their own account dropdown (avatar/name → "My Profile," "Settings," etc., with a separate workspace-level settings entry elsewhere) and asked for the same shape here, plus wanted the Danger zone hidden behind an expand step for extra friction before the destructive UI is even visible.

**Danger zone** (`components/ProfilePage.tsx`): now collapsed by default behind a plain, neutral "Danger zone ›" row — no red, no warning text, nothing destructive-looking until it's explicitly expanded. Expanding reveals exactly the same red warning + confirm flow as before (unchanged); collapsing (via a new X) resets the inner confirm step too, so re-expanding always starts fresh. This is on top of the confirm-with-password/email step already there — now two separate deliberate actions before any deletion can happen at all, not one.

**Profile dropdown** (`app/page.tsx`): the sidebar's existing "Me zone" avatar+name button (previously a direct link straight to the Profile page, labeled "View profile") is now a `FloatingPopover` dropdown trigger instead — reuses the exact same `FloatingPopover` component the workspace switcher already uses, so it's consistent with the app's other menus (click-outside/Escape-to-close, viewport-aware flip/clamp positioning). Panel contents: a header showing the larger avatar + name + email, then **My Profile** (routes to the existing Profile page, unchanged), **Settings** (new, see below), a divider, and **Sign out**. Initially built a *second*, separate dropdown at the bottom of the sidebar (where the old flat identity strip used to be) before realizing that duplicated the top one — consolidated into just the top "Me zone" avatar, which is the more prominent, natural click target; the bottom sidebar area now only holds the Team button and the Siqt branding row, nothing identity-related.

**New `components/AccountSettingsPanel.tsx`** — this is what "Settings" in the new dropdown opens. Deliberately distinct from the pre-existing nav-rail "Settings" (nav-tab visibility, workspace-wide) — that one is now explicitly labeled **"Workspace Settings"** everywhere (modal header text, and the nav-rail button's hover tooltip — the visible under-icon label stays the short "Settings" since the icon rail is too narrow for the full phrase) so the two are never confused. `AccountSettingsPanel` currently houses the two small utility actions that used to live as cramped, unlabeled icon buttons in the old identity strip — "Copy personal calendar feed link" and "Connect Google account for Doc export" (or "Google Docs connected as X" once connected) — now real labeled menu items with a proper home, and a natural place for any future personal/account-level settings to land without needing yet another new panel.

No schema/data changes. Verified via Playwright: exactly one identity element renders in the sidebar (confirming the earlier duplicate-dropdown mistake was fully removed, not just hidden); opening the dropdown shows My Profile/Settings/Sign out; clicking Settings opens the new Account Settings panel (not the nav-rail's Workspace Settings, which sits nearby in the DOM and was worth explicitly re-checking with a scoped selector); Danger zone renders collapsed by default and expands to the full red confirm flow on click.

**Deferred, discussed but not built**: the user floated a Discord-style role system — custom roles with per-project access, plus the ability to make individual Spaces/Tasks/Folders private and grant access to specific users or roles. Real, sizeable feature (a proper permissions/ACL model, which this app has never had — every existing "access control" so far has just been binary workspace membership). Not started; flagged as a good candidate for its own dedicated planning session before any implementation, given how much it would touch (schema, every existing route's authorization checks, and the "broader per-route authorization hardening" already logged as deferred from the real-auth session).

## Today's session (2026-08-09, continued) — Discord-style roles + independent per-level private access

The user floated this idea two sessions ago (right after real auth landed) and it was deliberately deferred for its own planning pass, given the size. Today: "ta det videre" (take it further). Planned via `EnterPlanMode`/`ExitPlanMode` with the user confirming three scope forks up front, all of which shaped the design directly:

1. **Visibility/access only** — a role either can see a private Space/Folder/List/Task or it can't. No Discord-style permission bits (can-edit, can-delete, can-manage-members). Kept this a genuinely scoped feature instead of a full permissions engine touching every mutating route.
2. **Each level independent** — Space, Folder, List, and Task can each be marked private on their own, not just at the Space level. A hidden parent still hides its children automatically (see the folder-chain bug below), but an open Space can contain one locked Folder without the rest of it being restricted.
3. **Workspace ownership**: the creator becomes a permanent **Owner** (one per workspace, the only one who can delete the workspace itself). Owners can grant **Admin** to any number of members — Admins get every Owner capability except deleting the workspace, per the user's own framing ("får eier egenskaper, men ikke mulighet til å slette det").

**Schema**: `Workspace.members` (a plain implicit M2M, built two sessions ago for multi-workspace) had to become an explicit `WorkspaceMembership` join model — Prisma's implicit M2M tables can't carry an extra column, and a role tier (owner/admin/member) is exactly that. New `Role` model (workspace-scoped, named, colored, plain M2M to `User` — no extra columns needed on *that* relation, unlike membership). `isPrivate Boolean` + `accessJson String` (a JSON array of `{type:'user'|'role', id}`) added identically to Space/Folder/List/Task — chose a JSON column over four new relational ACL tables deliberately: this schema already has a working precedent for exactly this shape (`CustomField.options`), every one of these four resource types already tolerates dangling references gracefully elsewhere (`@mentions`), and it avoids four near-identical join tables plus an extra join on every single hierarchy read. Applying the migration found one real casualty: the old implicit `_WorkspaceMembers` table had exactly one non-test row — the user's actual "CRRM Media" workspace membership (visible in their own ClickUp-comparison screenshot from a few messages earlier) — caught via a pre-flight check before accepting `--accept-data-loss`, and manually re-inserted into the new `WorkspaceMembership` table as `role: 'owner'` right after the push, rather than silently losing it.

**`lib/auth/access.ts`** (new) — the shared enforcement layer. `getWorkspaceRole`/`canManageWorkspace` (owner-or-admin), `getAccessContext` (one per-workspace lookup — role tier + held Role ids — reused across every row-check in a request instead of re-querying per row), `canSee(resource, ctx)` (the one function Space/Folder/List/Task all share, since they're structurally identical: not private → visible; Owner/Admin → always visible, same as a Discord server admin bypassing channel overwrites; otherwise checked against `accessJson`). Filtering directly in a Prisma `where` against a JSON *string* column (SQLite has no native JSON column type here) was considered and rejected — substring-matching serialized JSON is fragile and can't express role-based grants correctly — so filtering happens in memory after a normal fetch instead, which is both correct and cheap at this app's scale (one workspace's tree, not a global scan).

**A real bug caught by the plan's own verification checklist, not assumed away**: `GET /api/workspaces` initially filtered each Folder/List by *only* its own `isPrivate`/`accessJson`, not its ancestor chain — so a List sitting inside a private, inaccessible Folder (but not itself marked private) still leaked through, since Prisma returns `space.folders`/`space.lists` as flat sibling arrays, not nested by `folderId`. Folders can nest arbitrarily deep, so a fixed "check one level up" shortcut wouldn't have been enough either. Fixed with `buildFolderChainVisibility(folders)` (`lib/auth/access.ts`) — loads a scope's full, unfiltered Folder set once and walks `parentId` chains in memory, reused identically by `GET /api/workspaces` (per-Space) and the already-existing `getTaskVisibilityContext` (per-user, across every workspace, for the flat `GET /api/tasks`/`GET /api/task-docs`). Caught by the verification script's own "#7" check (a 4th, ungranted member expected to see only the open List, not the one inside a private Folder) actually failing on the first run — a good example of why the plan's verification section listed concrete before/after expectations instead of just "test it manually."

**Enforcement scope, deliberately bounded**: `GET /api/workspaces` (nested Space→Folder→List filtering), `GET /api/tasks`/`GET /api/task-docs` (flat, full-ancestor-chain filtering), and each of Space/Folder/List/Task's own PATCH routes (the `isPrivate`/`accessJson` fields specifically require Owner/Admin — `canManageWorkspace` — checked only when those two fields are present in the request body; every other field on those same routes keeps working exactly as before, unauthenticated, matching the already-logged "broader per-route authorization hardening" deferral from the real-auth session rather than silently expanding this pass's scope). Comment/doc/custom-field/status routes nested under a Task/Space still don't independently re-check anything — named as an explicit, still-open gap, not solved here.

**New API surface**: `app/api/workspaces/[id]/roles/route.ts` (create), `[roleId]/route.ts` (rename/recolor/delete), `[roleId]/members/route.ts` + `[userId]/route.ts` (assign/unassign — requires the target to actually be a workspace member first). `app/api/workspaces/[id]/members/[userId]/route.ts` gained a `PATCH` (promote/demote between admin/member — never touches or produces an `owner` row). `app/api/workspaces/[id]/route.ts` gained a `DELETE` — workspace deletion didn't exist as a feature at all before this session; added now specifically so "Admin gets everything except deleting the workspace" is a real, checkable distinction rather than a rule with nothing to enforce.

**UI**: `components/TeamPanel.tsx` (new) replaces the inline "Team" modal — two tabs, Members (role badges, an Owner crown, promote/demote and remove, all Owner/Admin-gated in the UI on top of the server's own check) and Roles (create/recolor/delete, click a role to expand its member-assignment checklist). `components/AccessControlPanel.tsx` (new) is the shared "make this private, grant these people/roles access" modal — reused verbatim from Space/Folder/List's existing right-click context menus (a new "Manage access" entry, Owner/Admin-only, next to Rename/Delete) and from a new lock-icon button in the Task modal's header. Small closed-lock icon added next to the name on Space rows (`app/page.tsx`) and Folder/List rows (`components/FolderTree.tsx`) when `isPrivate` is true.

Verified via a full Playwright + direct-API suite covering every item in the plan's verification checklist: Role creation + assignment; a private Folder visible to its granted Role-holder and correctly absent (not just hidden client-side) from a non-holder's `GET /api/workspaces`; a plain member blocked (403) from toggling privacy or creating Roles; Owner promoting a member to Admin, who can then manage privacy/roles but still gets 403 deleting the workspace; the workspace creator's membership row is `'owner'` immediately, and `POST /api/workspaces/personal` still works unaffected; a private Task correctly visible only to its grantee (and Owner/Admin) and absent for everyone else; and the nested-Folder cascade bug above, re-verified as fixed after the `buildFolderChainVisibility` fix. All test accounts/workspaces/roles cleaned up afterward.

**Deferred, named explicitly** (matches the plan's own scope decisions, not new gaps): action-level/Discord-style permission bits; workspace ownership transfer (a second Owner, or reassigning Owner to someone else — today's Owner is permanent once set); full lockdown of comment/doc/custom-field/status routes nested under a private resource.

## Today's session (2026-08-10) — Workspace invite links

Continuing down the roadmap from the previous session's deferred list ("workspace invite-by-email" was #1). Two forks resolved directly with the user before building:

1. **Shareable link, not emailed invitation.** The app has no email-sending infrastructure at all today, and sending to arbitrary recipients needs a verified sending domain — which doesn't exist while this only runs locally (deploy hasn't happened yet, it's #4 on the same roadmap). The user asked what "cost" meant when I flagged this; answered directly (money is the smaller issue — most providers have a generous free tier — the real blocker is domain verification for deliverability to real recipients). Agreed: build a Discord-style shareable link now (works immediately, zero new infrastructure), revisit real automatic email as a small follow-up once there's an actual deployed domain to send from.
2. **New members join automatically** the instant they finish signing in/up from the invite flow — no separate confirmation step after that.

**Schema**: new `WorkspaceInvite` (`id` doubles as the shareable code in `/invite/[id]`, `role: 'admin' | 'member'` — never `'owner'`, that tier has no transfer mechanism, `createdById` for accountability). No `revoked` flag — "revoking" a link is just deleting the row; both the public GET and the accept route already treat a missing row as an invalid/expired invite, so a separate boolean would've been redundant. No expiry or max-uses either, matching Discord's own default reusable-link behavior — not asked for, a deliberate scope cut rather than a silently missing feature.

**API**: `POST`/`GET /api/workspaces/[id]/invites` (create/list, Owner/Admin-gated, same `getWorkspaceRole`/`canManageWorkspace` check every other membership action already uses) and `DELETE .../invites/[inviteId]` (revoke). Separately, `GET /api/invites/[code]` is **deliberately public — no auth check at all** (`app/api/invites/[code]/route.ts`, outside the `/api/workspaces` tree on purpose) since the whole point is showing "you're invited to X" to someone who doesn't have a session yet; returns only `{workspaceName, role}`, nothing a stranger shouldn't see. `POST /api/invites/[code]/accept` requires auth and upserts a `WorkspaceMembership` at the invite's role — already being a member is a harmless no-op, not an error, mirroring how the personal-workspace route already treats "already exists" as success rather than a conflict.

**`/invite/[code]` page** (new, `app/invite/[code]/page.tsx`) — added to `proxy.ts`'s matcher exclusion list right next to `/login`, for the same reason `GET /api/invites/[code]` is public: this page has to be visible before authentication, not behind it. Branches on `useSession()`: signed-out shows the workspace name + "Sign in" / "Create an account to join," both linking to `/login?callbackUrl=/invite/[code]`; signed-in shows a "Join {name}" button that calls accept and redirects to `/?workspace=[id]` (reuses the already-built `workspace` query param from the browser-back/forward session — no new nav plumbing needed).

**`app/login/page.tsx`** gained `callbackUrl`/`mode` query-param support (previously hardcoded `redirectTo: '/'` on the Google button and `router.push('/')` after Credentials sign-in) so the invite round-trip actually lands back on the invite page instead of the plain dashboard. Needed `useSearchParams()`, which — same as `app/page.tsx`'s own split from the browser-back/forward session — needs a `Suspense` boundary in this Next.js version; split into a thin default-export wrapper + the real `LoginPageContent`.

**`components/TeamPanel.tsx` gained a third "Invite" tab** (Owner/Admin only — not even shown to a plain member), listing active links with copy/revoke, plus a role picker for new links. Deliberately kept as local component state with its own `fetch` calls rather than new global store actions — this is Owner/Admin-only UI state that isn't part of the hierarchy every member already fetches, so routing it through the global `workspaces` tree would've been unnecessary plumbing for data only this one panel ever needs.

Verified with a full Playwright + direct-API suite matching every item in the plan's checklist, including the real end-to-end path: create a link as Owner → visit it fully signed out (confirmed only `{workspaceName, role}` is exposed, nothing else) → "Create an account to join" → sign up → land back on the *same* invite page via the `callbackUrl` round-trip → click Join → redirected straight to that workspace as active. Also verified: re-visiting an already-accepted invite is a no-op, not an error; a plain member is blocked (403) from creating invites; and revoking a link makes both the public GET and the page itself immediately show "invalid or has been revoked."

## Today's session (2026-08-10, continued) — Login completely dead when reached from a second machine on the LAN

User report: testing from a second physical computer ("PC 2") against the dev server running on "PC 1," none of the login page's buttons did anything at all. Traced to the dev server's own logs, which had already recorded the exact cause of both underlying problems the moment PC 2 tried:

1. **`⚠ Blocked cross-origin request to Next.js dev resource /_next/webpack-hmr from "192.168.1.51"`** — Next's dev server blocks cross-origin requests to dev-only resources (the Turbopack/HMR websocket) by default; reaching the dev server via its own printed "Network: http://192.168.1.51:3000" LAN address (instead of `localhost`) trips this, which can leave the page half-hydrated — indistinguishable from "the buttons don't work," since no click handlers ever actually attach. Fixed with `allowedDevOrigins: ["192.168.1.51"]` in `next.config.ts` (a config-file change, needs a full dev-server restart, not just Turbopack picking it up on save).
2. **`GET /login?callbackUrl=http%3A%2F%2Flocalhost%3A3000%2F`** — no `AUTH_URL`/`NEXTAUTH_URL` env var is set (this app is deliberately reachable from more than one origin during dev), and without one, Auth.js's own auto-redirect to the sign-in page built the `callbackUrl` from an inferred `localhost:3000` default rather than the request's real origin — meaning after successfully signing in, PC 2's browser would have been sent to `http://localhost:3000/`, an address that resolves to *PC 2's own* loopback, not the dev server at all. Added `trustHost: true` to `auth.ts`'s `NextAuth()` config, which fixed Auth.js's *internal* OAuth state cookie (`authjs.callback-url`) immediately — confirmed via a raw `curl -H "Host: 192.168.1.51:3000"` request — but the `/login` redirect's own `callbackUrl` *query param* still came back wrong even with `trustHost` on, apparently built through a different code path. Given a same-app redirect should never legitimately need to leave the current origin anyway, fixed it at the point of use instead: `app/login/page.tsx` now strips any `protocol://host` prefix off `callbackUrl` down to just the path+query before using it, so it resolves against whatever origin the browser is actually on regardless of what Auth.js's own redirect happened to construct.

**Still needs the user's own action for Google sign-in specifically to work from a second machine**: Google independently validates the exact `redirect_uri` against what's registered in Google Cloud Console, regardless of anything fixed above — `http://192.168.1.51:3000/api/auth/callback/google` needs to be added there too (same one-time external setup pattern as the original Google OAuth setup), or Google's own consent screen will reject the request before it ever gets back to this app. Email/password sign-in has no such external registration and is fully fixed by the two changes above alone.

Verified against the real reported scenario — a fresh Playwright browser hitting `http://192.168.1.51:3000` end to end (not `localhost`): the "Create account" button responds, sign-up completes with zero console errors, and the post-signup redirect correctly lands on `http://192.168.1.51:3000/`, not `localhost:3000`.

## Today's session (2026-08-10, continued) — Google OAuth from a second machine: nip.io + AUTH_URL

Continuing the cross-machine LAN session above: the two fixes there (`allowedDevOrigins`, `trustHost` + `callbackUrl` stripping) got email/password sign-in fully working from PC 2, but Google sign-in specifically still failed. Two more layers, found in order:

1. **Google Cloud Console rejects raw IP addresses as a redirect URI host outright** ("must end with a public top-level domain") — a hard Google-side constraint, no code fix possible. Worked around with **nip.io** (`192.168.1.51.nip.io` — free wildcard DNS resolving straight back to `192.168.1.51`, verified via `nslookup`), registered as the redirect URI instead of the bare IP, and added to `next.config.ts`'s `allowedDevOrigins` alongside the raw IP.
2. **Even after switching to the nip.io address, Google still redirected back to `localhost:3000`.** Root-caused by reading `@auth/core`'s own source directly rather than guessing: `trustHost` only gates whether Auth.js *allows* an unrecognized host — it never controls what origin Auth.js actually derives its URLs from, that always comes from the request's own `.url`. Confirmed with `curl` against the real LAN IP with **zero header spoofing** that `next dev`'s route handlers hand Auth.js a request whose `.url` stays pinned to the dev server's own "Local" origin (`http://localhost:3000`) regardless of which Host header a request actually arrives with — a `next dev`-specific limitation, not anything `trustHost`/`allowedDevOrigins` touch. The one thing that does override it: `AUTH_URL`. Set `AUTH_URL=http://192.168.1.51.nip.io:3000` in `.env.local` — this pins **every** Auth.js request's derived origin to that one address, including ones started from plain `localhost:3000` (which now finish the OAuth round-trip redirected onto the nip.io address instead — harmless, same server, just a different address in the bar than you started with).

Verified via `curl` (no browser) that the server-computed `redirect_uri` sent to Google changed from `localhost:3000` to the nip.io address once `AUTH_URL` was set, then confirmed the real end-to-end flow with the user directly. This whole class of issue goes away once the app is actually deployed behind one real fixed domain (`AUTH_URL` becomes that domain, permanently, no LAN/nip.io workaround needed) — noted as a "revisit once deployed" comment at both edit sites (`.env.local`, `next.config.ts`).

## Today's session (2026-08-10, continued) — ClickUp CSV import

User wants to test migrating away from ClickUp — asked for CSV import, then supplied a real ~1.1MB export (`Everything view → Export`) so the column mapping below was built against actual data, not guesswork. Clarifying questions up front: full Space→Folder→List hierarchy reconstruction (not a flatten-everything-into-one-List shortcut), Owner/Admin-only.
