**Column mapping** (from the real file): `Task Name` → title (row skipped if blank), `Task Content` → `description` (plain text), `Status`/`Space Name`/`List Name` → resolved-or-created by name within scope, `Folder Name/Path` (a JSON array string) → walked to resolve/create a nested Folder chain, `Priority` (`Urgent`/`High`/`Normal`/`Low`) → `Task.priority` 1–4, `Assignees` (bracket-wrapped but **not JSON** — unquoted names, `[Robin Hofset,Rune Løkås]`) → matched by exact trimmed name against the *target workspace's* current members only, unmatched names collected and reported, never auto-invited/created. `Parent ID` resolved in a second pass, only when that id is also present elsewhere in the same file — a parent outside the export is left as top-level, silently, matching this app's existing tolerance for dangling references elsewhere (stale `accessJson` entries, mention tokens).

**`POST /api/workspaces/[id]/import/clickup`** (new, Owner/Admin-gated via the same `requireManager` shape every other workspace-management route uses): bulk-fetches the workspace's existing Spaces/Folders/Lists/Statuses/members *once* up front into in-memory `Map`s (same "one batch lookup, not N+1 per row" trick `lib/auth/access.ts`'s `getAccessContext` already uses), then resolves each of the ~2000+ rows against those maps, creating new rows and updating the maps as new Spaces/Folders/Lists/Statuses appear. Seeds in-memory `order` counters from current DB counts rather than a per-row `count()` query (mirrors `app/api/statuses/route.ts`'s existing pattern) — needed because bulk-creating many siblings in one pass would otherwise leave them all tied at the schema's `order: 0` default. No `$transaction` wrapping the whole import — a single bad row shouldn't roll back a 1000-row import; best-effort per-row, skipped rows collected and reported instead.

**Client**: new "Import" tab in the Team panel (Owner/Admin only, alongside the existing Invite tab — see below, later moved into Workspace Settings during the same day's Office-redesign session). `papaparse` (new dependency) parses the file **client-side** first — needed because `Task Content` contains multi-line quoted fields with embedded commas/quotes that a hand-rolled `split(',')` would corrupt — producing a pure client-side preview (task/Space/List/Status counts, matched-vs-unmatched assignees) before anything is sent to the server, so a bad file can be inspected and abandoned with zero side effects.

**Found and fixed during Playwright verification against the real file**: the returned `statusesCreated` count was always 0 even though statuses were being created correctly — the counter increment was simply missing from `resolveStatus()`'s success path. Verified end-to-end against the real 2270-row export in a throwaway test workspace (cleaned up after): 2270 tasks, 4 Spaces, 2 Folders, 15 Lists, 8 Statuses created, 1659 tasks correctly linked to their parent, 5 real ClickUp assignee names correctly reported as unmatched (none were members of the test workspace).

## Today's session (2026-08-10, continued) — Office redesign: top-down floor plan + Settings/Team restructuring

Follow-up to the CSV import work: reviewing where the new Import tab landed (the "Team" modal) prompted a restructure. Confirmed with the user across a few exchanges: Roles/Invite/Import move into **Workspace Settings** (previously just a personal, local nav-tab-visibility toggle with zero server calls or admin gating); **Members move into Office** — Office becomes the actual place Owner/Admins manage the team (promote/demote, assign Roles, remove), not just a decorative space; and Office's look changes from a vertical "building floors" stack (rooms stacked like a cross-section, seen from the side) to a real **top-down floor plan** — confirmed **auto-grid** layout (rooms placed by the existing `order` field) rather than free x/y dragging, since that needed no schema change and still reads as a real office blueprint.

**`components/TeamPanel.tsx` deleted entirely** — its 4 tabs split: Roles/Invite/Import moved essentially unchanged into `components/SettingsPanel.tsx` (now genuinely gated: General stays open to everyone, the other three `canManage`-only), Members' actions moved into a new `components/ManageableAvatar.tsx`.

**`ManageableAvatar`** wraps the existing `PersonAvatar` (unchanged) with a small gear badge — shown only when `canManage` — that opens a `FloatingPopover` (role badge, promote/demote, per-Role assign/unassign checklist, "Remove from workspace"). Deliberately a *separate, additive* click target from the avatar's own `onClick`: normal avatar clicks still open that person's profile/tasks page exactly as before, confirmed explicitly with the user before building rather than assumed. Reused by both `OfficeRooms.tsx` (composed with its own `useDraggable` wiring, since people are still drag-and-droppable between rooms) and `RoomDetail.tsx` (browse-only, no drag).

**`OfficeRooms.tsx` layout rewrite**: confirmed via reading `app/page.tsx`'s `reorderRoomSiblings` and its `room-drag:`/`room-drop:` id handling that room reordering is purely `order`-value swapping with no assumption about which axis rooms render on — so the whole redesign was primarily a CSS/layout change (a `grid grid-cols-2 sm:grid-cols-3` container with a subtle graph-paper background, each room a bordered rectangle with a small header + a decorative door notch on the bottom wall), not a drag-and-drop logic rewrite. Lobby became one more grid cell (first position, same amber accent) instead of a fixed footer strip.

**A real bug the Playwright verification itself caught**: removing someone via the new manage popover correctly updated `workspace.members`, but they kept showing up in Office's Lobby indefinitely — because Office's rooms/Lobby were rendered from the app-wide `users` list (used elsewhere for task-assignee pickers etc.), not scoped to the current workspace's actual membership; `roomId` placement is a plain field on `User` with no workspace scoping of its own. Fixed by deriving a workspace-scoped `officeUsers` list (`users` filtered against `workspace.members`) once in `OfficePage.tsx` and using it everywhere Office renders people, instead of the raw global list.

Follow-up, same day: added a **"Sits in {room}" indicator** to a person's own profile page (`OfficePage.tsx`) — until now the only way to see which room someone sat in was to go back to the floor plan and find their avatar, since the profile page and the floor plan are two separate, mutually-exclusive views (a preexisting split, not something this session changed).

Verified with a full Playwright suite (two real accounts, an invite-link join, promote-to-Admin persisting, remove-from-workspace via the popover, a plain Member seeing neither the manage badge nor Roles/Invite/Import) plus visual confirmation of the grid layout, all cleaned up afterward.

**Deferred, named explicitly**: the sidebar's separate "TEAM" nav list (shown while in Office view, a flat list of people to jump straight to their profile) still reads from the same unscoped global `users` list as the bug above, not `workspace.members` — same class of leak, just a different call site not yet fixed.

## Today's session (2026-08-10, continued) — Date urgency colors, bulk-clear overdue, Space icons, and follow-up polish

Three requests in one message: (1) color-code start/due date badges by urgency, with a specific rule confirmed with the user — white (>24h away) → yellow (<24h away) → green for a passed start date, red for a passed due date, **with an overdue task's start badge also going red instead of green** (overdue is the more urgent signal); (2) a button to bulk-clear due dates that are in the past, confirmed scoped to **the currently visible/filtered list**, not the whole workspace; (3) real per-Space icons — until this session, "personalizing" a Space was just typing an emoji into its name field (the Edit Space modal's own label admitted this), since `Space` had no `icon` column at all unlike `Folder`/`List`/`Room`.

**`lib/dateBadgeColor.ts`** (new) — `startDateColor`/`dueDateColor` pure functions, `DatePickerPopover.tsx` gained an optional `badgeColorHex`/`tooltip` pair so the component itself stays agnostic to which date it represents. First shipped as plain colored text; the user then asked for more visible color-coding — reworked into a filled background pill (same `hex+alpha-suffix` trick the existing status pill already uses) with a hover tooltip giving the exact relative time ("Overdue by 2 days"). **The user then clarified the pill wasn't what they meant** — they'd liked the plain-text version better and the "not clear which color" complaint was actually about something else entirely (see below) — reverted to plain colored text, kept the tooltip.

**"Clear overdue"** button in the board toolbar, next to the existing "Column" button — only rendered when `overdueTasksInView` (derived from `filteredTasks`, the same set already driving what's on screen) is non-empty, shows the count in its own label. Confirms via the existing `ConfirmDialog` pattern, then loops the existing `optimisticSetDates(id, startDate, null)` action per task — no new store action or API route needed; each clear gets its own undo entry rather than one grouped undo, an accepted tradeoff matching how other multi-row actions in this app already behave.

**Space icons**: `icon String?` added to `Space` (same nullable shape as `Folder.icon`/`List.icon`), the Edit Space modal gained the exact same icon-grid block Folder/List's own edit modals already had, reusing `FOLDER_ICON_CHOICES`/`FOLDER_ICON_MAP` directly. Rendered in place of the sidebar's existing color dot when set, falling back to the plain dot when not (existing Spaces look unchanged until someone opts in).

**Follow-up requests, same session**: a header checkbox to select/deselect every visible task at once (the underlying `selectedIds`/bulk-action-bar/`clearSelection` infrastructure already existed — this was purely a missing header control); the icon set expanded from 9 to 74 choices (`FOLDER_ICON_CHOICES`/`MAP` in `components/FolderTree.tsx`, shared by Space/Folder/List); every color swatch picker in the app (`Space`/`Folder`/`List`/`Room`/`Role`/`Status`/custom-field-option) gained a free-form `<input type="color">` swatch alongside its ~8 presets, consolidated into one new shared `components/ColorSwatchPicker.tsx` (nullable `value` supported, since Folder/List colors can be `null` for "use the default look") — replacing ~9 near-identical inline `.map()` blocks that had accumulated across `app/page.tsx`, `SettingsPanel.tsx`, and `RoomDetail.tsx`.

**The actual "not clear" complaint, clarified**: not the date badges at all — the Space color dot in the sidebar was too small/weak to read at a glance. Fixed by enlarging it (2.5px→3px core, matching hit-area increase) and adding a faint white ring so it stays legible against the near-black sidebar even for darker chosen colors.

## Today's session (2026-08-10, continued) — Hocuspocus sidecar authentication

Next item off the standing roadmap. The realtime Doc-collaboration WebSocket sidecar (`server/collabServer.ts`, port 1234) had **zero authentication** — no `onAuthenticate` hook existed at all, so any client that could reach port 1234 and knew (or guessed) a Doc's UUID could read/write it. Separately, and found while researching the fix rather than reported by the user: `lib/collab/presenceRoom.ts`'s `PRESENCE_DOCUMENT_NAME` was a single **hardcoded global constant** — every workspace in the app shared one presence room, so "who's online" already leaked across workspace boundaries regardless of auth.

**Reused the existing Auth.js session cookie directly — no new token, no new endpoint.** Confirmed by reading `@hocuspocus/server`'s own source that `onAuthenticate` fires unconditionally on every new document connection and receives `requestHeaders: Headers` (the raw WebSocket-upgrade request headers, including `Cookie`) — and since cookies aren't port-scoped, the browser already sends the same `authjs.session-token` cookie to `ws://host:1234` that it sends to the app on port 3000. `getToken()` (`next-auth/jwt`, re-exporting `@auth/core/jwt`) decodes/verifies that exact cookie the same way the rest of the app already implicitly trusts it — confirmed empirically before writing the real hook (signed in via Playwright, extracted the real cookie, decoded it standalone and got back the correct `sub`/userId).

`collabServer.ts` had never loaded any environment variables at all before this (SQLite's datasource URL is hardcoded in `schema.prisma`, so it never needed any) — needed `AUTH_SECRET` now. Fixed by adding `--env-file=.env.local` to the `dev:collab`/`collab:start` npm scripts (Node 24, confirmed installed, supports this natively — no new dependency), verified directly before relying on it.

New `onAuthenticate`: decode the cookie → userId (reject if none), resolve which Workspace owns `documentName` (a presence-room name carries its workspaceId directly now — see below; a real Doc id resolves via `doc.spaceId` or `doc.taskId → list → space`), check a `WorkspaceMembership` row exists for `(userId, workspaceId)` — same compound-key shape as `lib/auth/access.ts`'s `getWorkspaceRole`, mirrored inline rather than imported since this process deliberately keeps its own separate `PrismaClient`. Throwing rejects the connection before `onLoadDocument`/`onCreateDocument` ever run, so no content reaches a rejected client. `lib/collab/presenceRoom.ts`'s constant became `presenceDocumentName(workspaceId)`; `usePresenceConnection()` gained a `workspaceId: string | null` param (no connection while null, reconnects on workspace switch); its one call site (`app/page.tsx`) now passes `activeWorkspaceId`. **Doc rooms needed zero client-side changes** — the cookie travels with the handshake automatically.

Verified with the real Hocuspocus wire protocol, not a mock: a small Node script drove the actual `HocuspocusProvider` client library (via a `WebSocketPolyfill` — the `ws` package, temporarily `npm install --no-save`'d for the test and removed after) against the real running sidecar. A workspace member syncs successfully; a signed-in user who is *not* a member of that workspace is rejected (`permission-denied`); a connection with no session cookie at all is rejected the same way; an outsider is also rejected from that workspace's presence room specifically. A separate Playwright pass confirmed the real browser Doc editor UI (typing, autosave) still works completely unchanged.

## Today's session (2026-08-11) — Environment restart fallout + Planner overflow-count bug, root-caused twice + checkbox/color styling pass

Picking the project back up after a `git pull` that brought in the whole auth/multi-workspace/collab-sidecar work from the sessions above (none of it built in this session). Three restart problems, none of them new bugs — all pre-existing gaps this was the first `git pull`-then-restart to actually hit:

1. `npm run dev:collab` failed outright (`.env.local: not found`, exit code 9) — `.env.local` is gitignored by design (see `AUTH_SECRET`/OAuth-secret handling further down), so a `git pull` alone can never bring it along. Fixed by generating a fresh local `AUTH_SECRET` and creating the file; `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` left as a documented-but-empty placeholder block rather than fabricated, since those are real third-party credentials.
2. Task creation was broken for the existing "Local Dev Workspace" after the pull. Root cause: that Workspace predates the `WorkspaceMembership` access-control model entirely, so it has zero membership rows — invisible to the new authorization checks, and with no self-service path to fix it (adding a member requires already being one). One-time direct Prisma insert (`role: 'owner'`) to bootstrap the first member; worth remembering if any other pre-migration-era workspace surfaces the same symptom.
3. Google sign-in failed with a `client_id=undefined` redirect (confirmed via live Playwright reproduction, not guessed) — simply that `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` aren't set on this machine (same root cause as #1, different symptom). Not fixed — needs the user's own Google Cloud OAuth Client credentials, offered but not actioned this session.

Then the bulk of the session: a Planner month-view bug report ("+N more" overflow chip staying stuck/wrong after dragging a task off a crowded day), which took **two full root-cause rounds** because the user caught a real regression in the first fix via a follow-up screenshot rather than accepting the first "looks fixed."

**Round 1.** `assignLanes` (`lib/ganttLayout.ts`) was being called once for the *entire visible month grid* in `CalendarView.tsx`, not per week row. It already scoped packing to "connected overlap clusters," but this workspace's real data has long chains of back-to-back vacation/leave bars that connect into one 16-member cluster spanning most of the month — so dragging a task to a different day within that same chain never actually freed anything, since the task never left the shared lane pool. Fixed by calling `assignLanes` once per week row instead of once for the month, threading a `previousLanes` hint map across rows to keep week-spanning bars visually stable between adjacent rows. Also moved the chip's markup from a standalone row-wide overlay strip into each day cell's own wrapper div, per the user's request that it read as "part of that day."

**Round 2 (user reported the bug as still present, with a screenshot showing a bar sitting one lane below a visibly free lane above it).** The `previousLanes` hint — kept from Round 1 specifically for cross-render stability — was itself the remaining bug: when a cluster's membership shrank (a task dragged away), neighboring tasks' *stale* hints kept pointing at their own old, now-non-adjacent lane numbers instead of re-compacting upward/together, leaving a visible gap and inflating the overflow count by one more than necessary. Confirmed by temporarily instrumenting `CalendarView.tsx` with `console.log`, since static reasoning about the hint logic wasn't enough to pin down. Fixed by removing the hint mechanism entirely — `assignLanes` is now pure classic first-fit-by-start-time (textbook-optimal for interval graph coloring; always produces the minimum lane count with no gaps, recomputed fresh every render). **Lesson: a "keep it stable across renders" hint and "always compact to minimum" are in direct conflict — when a user explicitly asks for tighter packing, drop the hint rather than trying to patch around it.**

Same follow-up report also surfaced two more real bugs, found only because the user attached a hand-drawn arrow pointing at exactly where the chip still looked "in between" rows:
- **Row height was reactive to whole-month overflow state** — a single `hasOverflow` boolean computed across *all* weeks combined got added to every row's height uniformly, so the whole grid visibly grew/shrank depending on whether *any* day anywhere in the month currently overflowed. Fixed by making `rowHeight` a fixed size that always reserves room for the full lane cap + overflow strip regardless of actual content, so all rows in a render are the same height. **Lesson: a shared/global layout value silently couples unrelated rows together — double-check whether a "seems fine" value is actually still computed globally even after a partial per-row fix.** (This is the second time in the same bug hunt a whole-month-shared value turned out to be the real culprit — first the lane assignment itself, then the row height.)
- **The chip's vertical position (`overflowTop`) was still computed once in `CalendarView.tsx` from the whole month's max lane usage** and passed down as a shared prop — so a quiet row's chip still got pushed down to match the busiest row in the month. Fixed by removing the prop and computing `overflowTop` locally inside `WeekRow.tsx` from that row's own segments.
- **The chip visually overflowed past its own cell's bottom edge** once nested inside a single day's reserved space (a row exactly at the lane cap has nothing left to absorb the mismatch) — direct DOM measurement (`getBoundingClientRect().height`) showed the chip actually renders at 24px tall, while `OVERFLOW_H` (the reserved space for it) was only `14`. Bumped to `26`; re-measured, now a small positive margin. **Lesson: verify visual/CSS claims via `getComputedStyle`/`getBoundingClientRect()`, not just screenshots — a reserved-height constant can silently drift from an element's actual rendered size, and it's cheap to just measure it.**

Also fixed in the same follow-up: sharper checkbox corners. First attempt (`rounded` → `rounded-sm`) was a no-op — direct measurement showed both compute to the same 4px in this project's Tailwind v4 config; `rounded-xs` (2px) is the genuinely smaller option and is what actually shipped.

**Separately, a styling pass across three files** per explicit user request, modeled partly on Google Calendar's sidebar convention (checked state shown via a colored checkbox) but deliberately diverging from it (names/icons keep their own assigned color always, rather than going "standard"/neutral like Google Calendar's labels do):
- `components/TaskRow.tsx` — bulk-select checkbox: solid `bg-blue-500 text-white` → tinted `bg-blue-500/20 border-blue-500/60 text-blue-400` (checkmark only, not a filled box); `rounded-xs` corners; task title no longer recolors on selection.
- `components/FolderTree.tsx` / `app/page.tsx` — Calendar-mode sidebar filter checkboxes (List rows and Space rows): same tinted-checkbox + `rounded-xs` treatment; row/name text no longer switches to blue when checked/active — List/Space names now always render in their own assigned `color` via inline `style`, independent of checked state.

Committed and pushed as one commit (`a67db1e`) covering `lib/ganttLayout.ts`, `components/calendar/CalendarView.tsx`, `components/calendar/WeekRow.tsx`, `components/TaskRow.tsx`, `components/FolderTree.tsx`, `app/page.tsx`.

~~**Still open, not started:** the user floated (didn't firmly request) letting a task's calendar lane/position be manually chosen rather than always auto-packed~~ — confirmed later the same day and built, see "Manual Planner lane pinning" session entry below.

## Today's session (2026-08-11, continued) — Docs rework: nested pages, sidebar-native creation, slash commands, comments

By far the largest single-day push on Docs — took the feature from "a flat list of standalone documents" to a ClickUp/Google-Docs-style page system, in four rounds of user feedback + screenshots, each root-caused/scoped via its own plan-mode pass. No Playwright/browser tool was available anywhere in this segment (confirmed early on) — every round was verified via `npx tsc --noEmit`, watching `/c/tmp/siqt-dev.log` for hot-reload/runtime errors after each change, and small throwaway Node scripts that mint a real Auth.js session JWT (`encode()` from `next-auth/jwt`, same `AUTH_SECRET`/`authjs.session-token` cookie shape `collabServer.ts` already decodes) to drive the real API routes end-to-end as a seeded QA user — the same technique used for the Hocuspocus auth verification in the 2026-08-10 session, just reused repeatedly this time. That gap is worth flagging explicitly to the user again if a future session has real browser access — several things below (BubbleMenu positioning, highlight color, panel layout feel) are still visually unverified.

**Round 1 — Doc-to-Doc nesting ("subpages").** `Doc` gained a self-referential `parentId` (`onDelete: Cascade`), an `ownerId` (set once at creation to whoever created it), and `contributorIdsJson` (JSON-array-in-String, same convention as `accessJson`; populated automatically in `collabServer.ts`'s `onAuthenticate` — best-effort/approximate, "has connected to edit this doc" not "has made a specific edit," documented as such). `lib/trashCascade.ts` gained `cascadeDoc`/`collectDocSubtreeIds` and `cascadeDocFolder` was extended to also expand into each swept doc's own child-subtree, since a subpage carries no `folderId` of its own (only reachable via the `parentId` chain — `lib/docFolderTree.ts`'s `getSpaceDocsIn` filters on `parentId === null` for exactly this reason). New `DocSubpagesPanel.tsx` (left-of-content column, between the main sidebar and the doc), a Doc-ancestor breadcrumb in `app/page.tsx`, `DocFolderTree.tsx`'s sidebar rows made recursively expandable. Verified via a from-scratch Node script that reproduced the exact "Doc > Main 1 > Sub 1/Sub 2, Main 2" book shape and confirmed cascade delete/restore covers the whole subtree, not just the root.

**Round 2 — sidebar-native creation + the "book" panel + slash commands.** Three asks bundled from one set of ClickUp screenshots: (1) a Doc creatable directly in the *main* Space sidebar (`components/FolderTree.tsx`, new `DocRow` alongside `ListRow`/`FolderRow`) as well as the existing separate Docs-tab nav (additive, user explicitly chose not to replace it) — required hoisting the doc-editor's render condition in `app/page.tsx` above the `activeView` switch (`activeStandaloneDoc && currentSpace && (activeView === 'board' || activeView === 'docs')`) so opening a Doc from the board sidebar doesn't force a tab switch; (2) `DocSubpagesPanel` rewritten from "current doc's direct children" to a full recursive tree rooted at the top-most ancestor (`getDocRoot`-equivalent walk), only rendered once the book has ≥1 page (`getChildDocs(root).length > 0`) — an empty lone doc shows an "Add page" button instead; "Add page" deliberately does **not** navigate into the new page (stays put, matches the observed ClickUp behavior of stubbing out a table of contents); (3) a `/`-triggered slash-command menu (`components/collab/slashCommandExtension.ts` + `slashCommandSuggestion.ts` + `SlashCommandList.tsx`) — architecturally a near-exact mirror of the pre-existing `@`-mention system (`@tiptap/suggestion`'s `Suggestion({char, items, command, render})`, same `ReactRenderer`/keyboard-nav shape), registered as a **second, independently-keyed** `Suggestion` plugin. **Real bug hit immediately**: both `@` and `/` defaulted to the *same* internal ProseMirror plugin key when neither specified one explicitly, crashing with `RangeError: Adding different instances of a keyed plugin` the moment both were registered together — harmless with only one `Suggestion` instance, breaks the moment a second joins. Fixed with an explicit `new PluginKey('slashCommand')`. Menu items deliberately scoped to what the editor's actual minimal schema can do (headings, lists) plus "New Subpage" (creates a child doc, inserts a clickable `mention`-node reference at the cursor — reused the *existing* mention node/chip rather than building a new one) — ClickUp's Table/Banner/Toggle/Checklist/Whiteboard items are explicitly out of scope, flagged rather than silently dropped.

**Round 3 — context menus, owner/contributor editing, and visual cleanup.** `Doc.color` added (mirrors List/Folder/Space); right-click Rename/Delete/Edit-appearance wired into all three Doc-row surfaces (`FolderTree.tsx`, `DocFolderTree.tsx`, `DocSubpagesPanel.tsx`'s `PageRow`) via the same `docMenu`/`docEditTarget` state + `ColorSwatchPicker` modal pattern `List`/`Folder` already use. Owner became click-*or*-right-click editable (single-select `FloatingPopover`, same visual convention as Task assignees); Contributors became multi-select-editable the same way (checkbox list) — both previously view-only, auto-populated. Owner label stays permanently singular ("Owner," never "Owners") per explicit user confirmation — Owner was deliberately kept single-select, not generalized to multiple. Four visual fixes from a single follow-up round: (a) the "Subpages" table's outer border/box removed per "keep it clean" feedback; (b) a real interaction bug — couldn't click immediately above/below an inserted `subpagesIndex` block to keep typing, since no `Gapcursor` plugin was registered at all (`components/collab/gapCursorExtension.ts`, a 10-line wrapper around `@tiptap/pm/gapcursor`'s `gapCursor()` rather than pulling in the separate `@tiptap/extension-gapcursor` package for the same few lines) — the slash-command's own insert also now appends a trailing empty paragraph immediately, belt-and-suspenders; (c) the "Subpages" block briefly became collapsible-to-a-heading, then **reverted to always-fixed/fully-expanded** two messages later once the user clarified they wanted ClickUp's actual always-shown-table behavior, not a collapse toggle — a reminder that "make X collapsible" and "match this specific reference image" aren't always the same ask even when they sound similar; (d) a persistent "white box" around the editor's empty-state placeholder turned out to be the *browser's own default contentEditable focus outline* (nothing in this app's CSS was drawing it) — fixed with `.collab-doc-editor .ProseMirror { outline: none; }` in `app/globals.css`.

**Round 4 — the formatting toolbar moved, and selection-anchored commenting shipped.** The old fixed `DocToolbar.tsx` row (sitting between title and content) was deleted outright and replaced by `DocFormatPanel.tsx` — a collapsed-icon-then-expand column to the *right* of the editor content, living inside `CollabDocEditor.tsx` itself (not lifted to `app/page.tsx`, since it's the editor's own UI chrome, same reasoning that kept `DocSubpagesPanel` — a Space-level navigation concern — in `page.tsx` instead). Then, the session's largest single addition: **Google-Docs-style commenting**. New `DocComment` model (separate from the Task-only `Comment` — `taskId` required there, nothing else depends on that, safe to leave alone) with a `markId` shared across a whole thread, one level of `parentId` self-relation for replies, `quotedText` snapshot, `resolved` boolean. New framework-agnostic `lib/collab/commentMark.ts` (a Tiptap **Mark**, not a Node — decorates existing text rather than replacing it, so it needed no changes to `docJSONToPlainText`/`Pdf`/`GoogleRequests`'s export walkers at all, unlike the `subpagesIndex` Node which needed an explicit case in all three: a mark is just metadata on a `text` node those walkers already handle generically). Applied directly to the current selection (`editor.chain().setTextSelection({from,to}).setMark('comment',{commentId}).run()`, Tiptap's built-in `setMark` command works for any registered mark by name, no custom command needed) via `@tiptap/extension-bubble-menu`'s `<BubbleMenu>` (already an installed dependency, imported from the v3 subpath `@tiptap/react/menus` — not exported from `@tiptap/react`'s root anymore), gated with a custom `shouldShow={({from,to}) => from !== to}` that ignores DOM focus entirely so the inline reply composer (rendered as BubbleMenu's own children) doesn't vanish the instant you click into it. Resolving a thread doesn't just flip a DB flag — it scans the whole doc for marks carrying that `commentId` and removes them (`components/collab/commentMarkView.tsx`'s `removeCommentMarkFromDoc`, a plain helper rather than a registered Tiptap command, sidesteps the TS module-augmentation ceremony for one call site), matching Google Docs' actual resolve behavior; reopening flips the flag back but does **not** restore the highlight (the original range isn't reliably recoverable once removed) — a documented, deliberate v1 limitation. `DocCommentsPanel.tsx` is a second collapsed-icon column stacked next to `DocFormatPanel`, click a highlighted mark in the text and the matching thread scrolls into view there. Verified via script: thread + reply creation, resolve round-trip, and cascade-delete of replies when a root comment is removed.

**Explicitly deferred, not built this session:** a Doc cover image; live "who's viewing this right now" presence (contributor tracking today only means "has ever connected," not "is here now"); the rest of ClickUp's Page Styles panel (font size, page width, header-section toggles for subtitle/last-modified/cover) — `DocFormatPanel` only carries the plain-text formatting buttons, deliberately scoped down from the full reference panel per an explicit user confirmation earlier in the session.

## Today's session (2026-08-11, continued) — Manual Planner lane pinning

Follow-up to the overflow-bug session above: the user confirmed (via two `AskUserQuestion` clarifications, both answered with the recommended option) they wanted a task's Planner lane to be manually choosable, not just always auto-packed by `assignLanes`. Confirmed shape: drag **vertically** to choose a lane (not a button/menu); dropping onto a lane already occupied by another task **swaps** the two tasks' lanes (not "bump to nearest free lane").

**Schema**: `Task.calendarLane Int? @map("calendar_lane")` (`prisma db push`, clean). `lib/ganttLayout.ts`'s `assignLanes(ranges, pinnedLanes?)` gained an optional `pinnedLanes: Map<string, number>` — pinned tasks claim their lane before the greedy first-fit loop runs for everything else. `store/useTaskStore.ts` gained `optimisticSetCalendarLane` (exact mirror of `optimisticSetDates`'s shape); `PATCH /api/tasks/[id]` accepts `calendarLane`. `CalendarView.tsx`: a `pinnedLanes` memo feeds every `assignLanes` call; drag handling extended with `deltaLanes`/`anchorLane` (vertical-drag-to-lane, `move` mode only) and swap-on-drop logic (finds a same-lane overlapping task via a bidirectional overlap check, swaps both tasks' `calendarLane`). `WeekRow.tsx` gained a small hover-only pin badge (lucide `Pin`) on any pinned bar, click to unpin.

**A real bug, caught only via user screenshots, not assumed away.** Dragging a later-starting task and pinning it onto a lane also used by an earlier, *non-overlapping* task incorrectly pushed the earlier task into "+1 more" overflow — visible as "the same total task count, but one moved from a visible bar into overflow" after a drag that shouldn't have touched it at all. Root cause: the pin-seeding logic reused the existing `isFree(laneEnd, startTime)` check, which is only valid when lanes are claimed in strict chronological order (true for the plain unpinned greedy loop) — a pin can claim a lane out of that order relative to the rest of the loop, silently breaking the assumption. Fixed with a real bidirectional interval-overlap check (`aStart <= bEnd && bStart <= aEnd`) tracked per-lane against every pinned interval, independent of processing order — see the matching "Known bugs" entry below. Verified twice: a standalone `.mjs` script (`npx tsx`) reproducing the exact screenshot scenario, and a full real-browser Playwright test (create the same task shape via API, drag in the actual UI, confirm the visible-bar count is unchanged before/after).

Committed together as `4cd40a6` (bundling the feature and the bug fix, since neither had reached `origin/main` yet).

## Today's session (2026-08-11, continued) — Folder-row checkbox-color bug

Explicit user request, arriving bundled with "commit and push the pending lane-pinning work first": in the Planner sidebar's calendar filter, checking a row's checkbox should turn *only the checkbox itself* blue — the row's name/icon should keep rendering in their own assigned color, never switch to blue. This exact bug had already been fixed for List and Space rows in the earlier same-day Docs-rework session (`components/FolderTree.tsx`/`app/page.tsx`, see the 2026-08-11 checkbox/color styling pass above) — but Folder rows specifically were missed. Found by direct source comparison (not by trusting PLANNING.md's own prior claim that "names now always render in their own assigned color") — Folder's row wrapper still had `activeView === 'calendar' && allChecked ? 'text-blue-400' : ...` on its className, so the name `<span>` (no color override of its own) inherited blue via CSS inheritance whenever fully checked. Fixed by removing that conditional class and adding an explicit `style={{ color: folder.color || undefined }}` to the name span, matching the already-correct List/Space pattern exactly. Verified via Playwright: a folder with a distinct custom color (`#e11d48`) rendered the identical computed color before and after checking it, never blue-400's `rgb(96, 165, 250)`. Committed as `29fe757`.

## Today's session (2026-08-11, continued) — Docs editor formatting: fonts, size, color, underline/strike, alignment, links

User request: richer text formatting in the collaborative Docs editor, "mer likt Google Docs" — until now `DocFormatPanel.tsx` only had Bold/Italic/Heading(1-2)/Bullet/Ordered list. Scope confirmed via two sequential `AskUserQuestion` clarifications (the tool caps at 4 options per question): font family, font size, text color + highlight, underline/strikethrough, text alignment, and links — all selected.

**A suspected prompt injection surfaced mid-clarification**, flagged directly rather than acted on: one `AskUserQuestion` answer came back with a large block of unrelated instructions for how *all future chat responses* should be formatted (headers, tables, blockquotes, etc.), appended after the legitimate feature selections with no connection to the actual question asked. Told the user explicitly what was found and that no part of it had been acted on; their follow-up didn't clearly confirm or deny it, so it was treated as **not** a legitimate instruction and chat responses stayed in the normal terse style. Worth remembering as a real, not hypothetical, risk class for this app's own `AskUserQuestion`-heavy workflow — see the matching "Known bugs" entry below.

**New dependencies**: `@tiptap/extension-underline`, `-strike`, `-text-align`, `-link`, `-text-style`, `-highlight` (all pinned to `^3.29.2`, matching every existing Tiptap package). Discovered mid-implementation by reading `node_modules/@tiptap/extension-text-style/dist/index.d.ts` directly (not assumed from docs) that this version already **bundles** `Color`/`FontFamily`/`FontSize`/`BackgroundColor`/`LineHeight` as named exports, all sharing one `textStyle` mark — the plan's originally-scoped separate `@tiptap/extension-font-family`/`-color` packages and a custom `fontSizeExtension.ts` turned out to be unnecessary; installed then uninstalled the two redundant packages.

**`lib/collab/schema.ts`** (shared client/server extension list — must stay in lockstep, a standing invariant in this codebase) and **`components/collab/CollabDocEditor.tsx`** both gained the same extension list: `Underline`, `Strike`, `TextAlign.configure({types:['heading','paragraph']})`, `Link.configure({openOnClick:false})`, `TextStyle`, `Color`, `FontFamily`, `FontSize`, `Highlight.configure({multicolor:true})`. The editor's existing comment-only `BubbleMenu` gained a "Link" button next to "Comment," same inline-prompt-then-apply shape the Comment button already had.

**`components/collab/DocFormatPanel.tsx`**: Underline/Strikethrough buttons alongside the existing Bold/Italic/Heading/List grid; a 4-button alignment group (Left/Center/Right/Justify); font-family and font-size `<select>`s (both a small curated set, not a free picker — see below); text-color and highlight-color buttons that open a `FloatingPopover` containing `ColorSwatchPicker.tsx` (built earlier this session, reused verbatim — genuine no-new-code reuse).

**Font-family choices are deliberately a small curated set** (Default / Serif → `Georgia, serif` / Monospace → `"Courier New", monospace`), not an open picker — chosen so both export paths stay exactly faithful with zero embedded font files: pdfkit ships Helvetica/Times/Courier as built-in standard fonts (the same constraint that already burned this project once with `pdfkit`'s `__dirname`-relative asset loading, see Known bugs), and Google Docs' `weightedFontFamily` just needs a real font-name string it already has available.

**Export walkers updated to carry the new formatting through**:
- `lib/collab/docJSONToPdf.ts` — `inlineRuns` reads `underline`/`strike` (mark presence) and `color`/`fontSize`/`fontFamily` (off the shared `textStyle` mark's attrs, same Tiptap composition pattern noted above) and `link` (off the `link` mark). `familyCategory()`/`fontFor()` map the three curated families to pdfkit's built-in Times-*/Courier-*/Helvetica-* font names. `writeLine()` gained an `align` option, applied via pdfkit's native `{underline, strike, link, align}` text options — no new pdfkit capability needed. `renderBlock()` passes `node.attrs?.textAlign` through for both heading and paragraph nodes.
- `lib/collab/docJSONToGoogleRequests.ts` — same `Run` shape extended; `updateTextStyle` requests gained `underline`, `strikethrough`, `foregroundColor`/`backgroundColor` (`hexToRgbColor()`, new — Google's API wants `{color:{rgbColor:{red,green,blue}}}` as 0–1 floats, not hex strings), `weightedFontFamily` (mapped via `googleFontFamily()`, same three-family curation), `fontSize` (`{magnitude, unit:'PT'}`), and `link:{url}`. Paragraph `textAlign` maps to `updateParagraphStyle`'s `alignment` field (`left→START, center→CENTER, right→END, justify→JUSTIFIED`), combined into the same request as `namedStyleType` when a line has both.
- `lib/collab/docJSONToPlainText.ts` — untouched, by design (already ignores all marks, new ones are just more marks it continues to ignore).

**Verified**, all in a real browser/process, no assumptions:
1. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (only the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error).
2. Playwright: applied underline/strike/center-align/Georgia/24px/text-color to real text in a real doc, reloaded, confirmed every property survived (`text-align:center`, `<u><s>`, `font-family:Georgia`, `font-size:24px`, `color:rgb(...)` all present in the reloaded DOM), zero console errors.
3. Playwright: applied a link via the bubble menu, reloaded, confirmed `href` survived.
4. Pure-function script driving `writeDocToPdf`/`docJSONToGoogleRequests` directly against a hand-built doc JSON exercising every new mark at once — PDF rendered without crash (non-zero buffer), and every expected Google Docs API field (`foregroundColor`, `backgroundColor`, `weightedFontFamily`, `fontSize`, `link`, both `alignment` values, `underline`, `strikethrough`) came out correct in the generated request list.

All test data (seeded user/workspace/space/doc) and throwaway `__verify_*` scripts cleaned up afterward. Committed as `066d740`.

## Today's session (2026-08-12)

**Two real bugs found and fixed**, both via live testing rather than assumed from code review:

1. **Navigation stuck on a Space/List-agnostic view.** `setNavigation()` (`store/useTaskStore.ts`) updated `activeSpaceId`/`activeListIds`/doc state but never touched `activeView` — so clicking a List or Doc in the sidebar while parked on `mytasks`/`mypersonal`/`profile`/`office` (screens that don't read `activeSpaceId`/`activeListIds` at all) silently changed nav state with zero visible effect, stranding the user on the same screen. Reported as "clicking Rob [a List] takes me to My Tasks"; confirmed via URL inspection (`view=mypersonal` never changed across clicks that should have navigated elsewhere). Fixed: `setNavigation` now bounces `activeView` back to `'board'` when leaving one of those four screens; `board`/`docs`/`calendar` are left untouched since they already handle List/Doc navigation contextually.
2. **Planner: two manually-pinned tasks colliding in the same lane.** `assignLanes` (`lib/ganttLayout.ts`) treated every `Task.calendarLane` pin as unconditionally authoritative — it checked *unpinned* tasks against pins, but never checked two *pinned* tasks against each other. "Ulrik ferie" (27–28 Aug) and "Rate 2 - 28. august 2026" (due 28 Aug) had each been dragged to `calendarLane: 5` at different times, before their dates actually conflicted — once they did, both rendered stacked in lane 5 instead of one bumping to a free lane/overflow. Fixed: within a cluster, the earlier-starting pin wins its lane; a later pin that genuinely overlaps an already-accepted pin in the same lane is dropped and falls through to the normal greedy placement, which already knows how to route around occupied pinned intervals. Verified against real task data pulled from the dev DB via a throwaway `tsx` script, not just eyeballed.

## Today's session (2026-08-12, continued) — Two real bugs: nested-Folder doc creation crash, sidebar drag autoscroll

Follow-up to the drag-and-drop verification pass above. First re-verified the three items that session had flagged as unverified (sidebar glow, cross-Space List drop, interleaved List/Doc ordering) — all confirmed correct with a real browser and screenshots, PLANNING.md updated in place at each item above. Then two fresh user reports, each root-caused via a real reproduction rather than guessed:

1. **"Prøvde å lage ny doc i Tasks fanen, skrev navn men ingenting skjedde."** Reproduced directly: creating a Doc from the "+ New doc" button while nested inside a real `Folder` (not at a Space's top level) failed with a silent 500. Root cause: `Doc.folderId` is a foreign key into the separate `DocFolder` table (used by the Docs-tab's own folder tree), never into `Folder` — but `FolderTree.tsx`'s `commitAdd` passed the current `parentId` (a real `Folder.id`, from the Tasks-tab sidebar's own Folder/List nesting) straight through as the new Doc's `folderId`. A Doc can structurally never live inside a real `Folder` (already an established invariant — see the 2026-08-11 Docs-rework session's Round 5 note), so that id never exists in `DocFolder`, and Prisma's FK constraint rejected the insert. The store's `createSpaceDoc` swallows a non-`ok` response with no toast/log (`if (!res.ok) return null`), so the failure was completely invisible client-side. Fixed at the root: the "New doc" button (`components/FolderTree.tsx`) now only renders at `parentId === null` (a Space's own top level) — offering it inside a Folder was never something that could actually work, not just a missing null-check. Verified via Playwright: the button no longer appears once nested inside a Folder; top-level creation still works exactly as before.
2. **"Funker ikke helt som det skal å trekke docs, noen ganger går det, andre ganger ikke. F.eks kan jeg ikke trekke den inni gaming spacet."** Root-caused by inspecting the real dev DB (read-only) rather than guessing: `Gaminglivet.no` sits at `order=5` of 7 Spaces, directly below `Innholdsskapelse` (`order=4`, 8 Lists + 2 Folders) — enough content above it to reliably push it below the sidebar's visible fold. Confirmed via a real Playwright drag test (holding the pointer at the scrollable sidebar container's own edge, both directions, for several seconds) that **dnd-kit's built-in `autoScroll` never actually scrolls this sidebar** — `scrollTop` stayed at `0` the entire hold, even though a control test proved basic drag/hover detection itself works correctly under the same synthetic events (a drop-target's hover ring lit up as expected). Root cause inside dnd-kit not fully chased down (its `useAutoScroller` internals depend on `overNode`/`activeNode` resolution and per-frame `rect` tracking that didn't reproduce the documented triggering conditions here) — rather than keep excavating a third-party library's internals, implemented a small manual autoscroll instead, matching this codebase's own established pattern for exactly this class of gap (see the existing "`onDragOver` only fires when the closest droppable changes" Known bugs entry below, which already solves a sibling problem the same way): a `pointermove`-driven `requestAnimationFrame` loop (`app/page.tsx`, scoped to `activeDragEntity?.kind` being one of the five sidebar-tree kinds — `space`/`folder`/`list`/`docfolder`/`spacedoc`) that scrolls the sidebar's own container (`sidebarScrollRef`) whenever the pointer is within 60px of its top/bottom edge, speed scaling linearly with proximity. Verified end-to-end: a Doc dragged from a tall Space, held at the sidebar's bottom edge, correctly auto-scrolled a target Space into reach and the drop landed it there — zero console errors.


## Today's session (2026-08-12, continued) — Docs can now live inside real Folders (Tasks tab)

Follow-up to the "New doc" crash fix above: that fix removed the ability to create a Doc while nested inside a Folder (since it was never structurally possible — offering it just crashed). The user immediately asked for the capability back: "I cannot put them in folders. I can place them around in spaces, etc. not in folders under spaces." Confirmed via a clarifying question this meant the Tasks-tab's own `Folder` tree (the one that already holds `List`s), not the separate Docs-tab tree. A full plan-mode pass, approved, then built:

**`Doc` gained a second, independent folder axis**: `boardFolderId String? @map("board_folder_id")` + relation to `Folder` (named after this app's own internal name for the Tasks tab, `activeView: 'board'`, deliberately not `taskFolderId` which would read confusingly next to the existing `taskId` field). Completely separate from the pre-existing `folderId`/`DocFolder` axis (the Docs tab's own tree) — a Doc can be in both, either, or neither, same "task-scoped AND/OR Space-scoped" independence this model already has. `Folder` gained the back-relation `docs Doc[]`.

**`lib/folderTree.ts` gained `getBoardDocsIn(space, folderId)`**, mirroring `getSpaceDocsIn` but filtering `boardFolderId` instead — `components/FolderTree.tsx`'s `FolderLevel` now reads this instead, which is what makes the existing "combine Lists+Docs into one order-sorted sequence" logic (from the earlier Docs-rework session's Round 5) correctly interleave at *every* Folder depth, not just the Space root. The "+ New doc" button's Space-root-only gate (added in the crash fix above) is gone — creating a Doc inside a Folder now works, passing `boardFolderId: parentId` (never touching the unrelated `folderId`).

**Drag-and-drop**: `handleTaskDragEnd`'s `spacedoc-drag:` branch (`app/page.tsx`) gained a `folder-drop:` case, a near-exact mirror of the existing List/Folder branch's own `folder-drop:` handling (search all Spaces for the target, so cross-Space drops work too) — calls a new store action `moveDocToBoardFolder`, itself a close copy of `moveList`'s shape.

**Cascade delete/restore**: `lib/trashCascade.ts`'s `cascadeFolder` now also sweeps Docs whose `boardFolderId` falls inside the deleted subtree (plus each doc's own subpage subtree), mirroring how `cascadeDocFolder` already sweeps the other axis — verified a Folder containing a board-foldered Doc deletes and Ctrl+Z-restores both together correctly.

**A real bug caught only by testing, not code review**: after all of the above, dragging a Doc into a Folder and creating one directly inside a Folder both silently did nothing visible — the new doc/moved doc never appeared anywhere. Traced to `GET /api/workspaces` (`app/api/workspaces/route.ts`): its nested `docs` query uses an explicit Prisma `select` (not a bare `include`) listing every field by name — a leftover from the earlier `calendarToken`-leak-avoidance pattern this codebase uses elsewhere — and it simply didn't have `boardFolderId` in that list, so the field was silently dropped from every API response even though the database had it correctly. **This is the same class of gap already logged in Known bugs** ("a `select`/`include` field name... needs updating" precedent, and the "check a route's own un-`select`ed return value" precedent) — worth re-affirming: any explicit Prisma `select` list needs to be found and updated whenever a new column is added to a model it selects from, and grepping for `include:` alone isn't enough, `select:` needs the same audit.

Verified end-to-end via Playwright with throwaway test data: drag an existing Doc onto a Folder (renders nested, interleaves correctly with a List already there); create a new Doc directly inside a Folder via "+ New doc" (no crash, correct placement); drag a Doc across Spaces into a different Folder (lands correctly, confirmed via screenshot); confirmed the Docs tab's own DocFolder-based organization is completely unaffected (same doc's `folderId` placement independent of its `boardFolderId`); Folder deletion cascades to board-foldered Docs and Ctrl+Z restores both. Zero console errors throughout. All test data and scripts cleaned up afterward.

**Immediate follow-up report, same session: "can't drag a doc directly from one folder to another, I have to place it right on the folder, not in between what's inside it."** Real gap, not user error — dropping a Doc directly onto a Folder's own header row (the `folder-drop:` case just added) worked, but dropping it "in between" the Folder's existing children (onto a List or another Doc already inside it — `list:`/`spacedoc:` targets) did not adopt the target's Folder at all. Two pre-existing helpers, never updated when `boardFolderId` was introduced, were the cause:
- `combinedSidebarSiblings` (`app/page.tsx`) filtered Docs by `folderId` (the wrong, DocFolder axis) and — worse — only ever included Docs at all when `folderId === null`, meaning a Doc could never even be considered a "sibling" inside any nested Folder.
- `moveSidebarItemRelativeTo` computed `targetFolderId`/`draggedFolderId` only for `type === 'list'`, hardcoding `null` for Docs — so a Doc dropped near another List/Doc *inside* a Folder always got treated as a top-level move, regardless of where the target actually lived. Its cross-context branch also called `moveSpaceDoc` (the DocFolder mover) with a hardcoded `null`, instead of the new `moveDocToBoardFolder` with the real target folder.
- A third, subtler wrinkle: the `spacedoc:`-target drop handler in `handleTaskDragEnd` is **shared** between `FolderTree.tsx` (Tasks tab, `boardFolderId`) and `DocFolderTree.tsx` (Docs tab, `folderId`/DocFolder) — both trees register the identical `spacedoc-drag:`/`spacedoc:` id prefixes (safe, since they're never mounted together), but the *correct* axis to update differs by tab. Branched on `activeView === 'docs'` at that one call site: the Docs tab keeps its existing `reorderSpaceDocRelativeTo` (DocFolder axis, untouched); the Tasks tab now reuses the already-fixed `moveSidebarItemRelativeTo` instead of a parallel duplicate.

Verified via Playwright: dropping a Doc onto another Doc already inside a Folder, and separately onto a List already inside a Folder, both now correctly nest the dragged Doc into that Folder (screenshots + sidebar-order dumps confirmed exact placement); re-verified the Docs tab's own same-folder doc reorder (DocFolder axis) still works unchanged, confirming the `activeView` branch didn't regress it. Zero console errors.


## Backlog — 2026-08-12 QA pass (20 items, user-reported)

User ran a testing pass (using a generated QA checklist artifact) and reported 20 issues/requests in one batch, several with screenshots. Logged verbatim here, numbering preserved for reference in conversation, so the list survives a session break — check off `[x]` as each ships.

- [x] **1. "My tasks" needs real checkboxes.** Confirmed via clarifying question: no new "done" boolean — checking a task sets its status to the list's own last status (by `order`, not by name — a Space can rename/reorder freely), unchecking sets it back to the first. Reuses `optimisticMoveTask` (the board's existing drag-between-status-columns action) directly, no new store action. `components/PersonalTasksPage.tsx` gained a checkbox per row (same `rounded-xs`/tinted-blue visual language as `TaskRow.tsx`'s bulk-select checkbox) plus a strikethrough on the title when done. Verified via Playwright: checking shows strikethrough + the status badge flips to the last status ("Done"), unchecking reverts both. **Immediate follow-up, same session**: a done task now also disappears from the list right away (still a completely normal Task row, findable everywhere else — just hidden from this quick-list), with a small "Show completed (N)"/"Hide completed" toggle at the bottom so nothing's lost from view, matching a plain to-do app's usual behavior. Verified: checking a task hides it instantly, the other task is unaffected, and the toggle round-trips correctly.
- [x] **2. Calendar: separate "Task" vs "Event" creation, ClickUp-style popup.** Reference screenshot is ClickUp's own "New" popover: tabs for Event / Task / Focus time / OOO, a single title field ("Add title, @ for people, @@ for tasks, 'space' for AI"), date range, All-day + Repeat, Add video call, Add participants, "Add ClickUp tasks and docs", Add location/room, Add description, and a bottom row of quick-toggle icons (calendar/visibility/lock-default/busy/mute). Today Planner only ever creates a `Task`. User wants an "Event" concept — happens on a given day, clickable for details like Google Calendar, but does **not** create a Task row — plus this popover-style quick-create UI instead of the current `CreateTaskModal`. See the 2026-08-13 session entry below.
- [x] **3. Independent icon color vs. text color** on Tasks etc. — today `color` is one field driving both; wants to pick each separately. See the 2026-08-13 session entry below.
- [x] **4. Planner's sidebar filter list shouldn't show Docs at all.** Fixed: `FolderTree.tsx`'s Doc rows and "New doc" button now only render when `activeView !== 'calendar'` — Planner keeps the Lists/Folders filter tree, no Docs mixed in. Today the Planner (Calendar) sidebar's List-filter tree also lists standalone Docs (e.g. "Wiki", "Tester", "NGM WIKI") alongside actual Lists; clicking one does nothing (Planner has no concept of a Doc). Ask: Docs don't belong in the Planner filter sidebar in the first place — remove them, keep it Lists/Folders/Spaces only.
- [x] **5. Local image upload for Docs, not just URL.** The image-insert modal shipped 2026-08-11/12 was URL-only ("no upload, ever" was the deliberate v1 scope cut) — user now wants to also pick a file from their own PC. See the 2026-08-14 session entry below.
- [x] **6. Can't place the cursor above/below a pasted image to type.** Real root cause: Gapcursor (registered earlier this session specifically to solve this class of problem) was already functionally active — but `prosemirror-gapcursor` ships its blinking-line marker as a *separate* stylesheet the consuming app has to import itself, which this project never did. The cursor was placeable the whole time, just **completely invisible**, indistinguishable from "doesn't work." Fixed: ported the plugin's own CSS into `app/globals.css` (`.collab-doc-editor .ProseMirror-gapcursor`), swapping its light-theme black line for `currentColor` on this dark editor. Also hardened the insertion itself: `submitImage()` (`CollabDocEditor.tsx`) now inserts `[image, paragraph]` together instead of a bare image, so a normal full-width line always exists directly below to type into, not just a thin gap-cursor target.
- [x] **7. PDF export still shows literal "[Image]" instead of the real picture.** This was a deliberate, disclosed scope-cut when images shipped (pdfkit's `doc.image()` needs a local Buffer, not a remote URL — flagged at the time as "a real 'fetch and embed' pass is a separate, larger piece of work if wanted later"). See the 2026-08-14 session entry below (also covers the explicit ask to re-test a Doc's Subpages export, not just the top-level doc).
- [x] **8. Plain-text export doesn't work at all** (separate from #7). Root cause: it never existed — `docJSONToPlainText.ts` was already used internally as a content mirror, but no route or menu item ever exposed it as a real export. Fixed: new `GET /api/docs/[id]/export/text` (same `loadDocJSONForExport` + shape as the PDF route) and a "Download plain text" item in `DocExportMenu.tsx`. Verified with a real `curl` against a seeded doc — correct `Content-Disposition`/body.
- [x] **9. Font size picker in Docs doesn't work — always shows "default"; redesign the control.** Real root cause: `DocFormatPanel.tsx` read `editor.getAttributes()`/`isActive()` directly during render with no subscription to Tiptap transactions at all — the panel only ever re-rendered as a side effect of some unrelated parent update, so a control could show a stale value until the next incidental re-render happened to catch up. Font size was the most visible casualty (the one most often checked immediately after clicking) but this was a whole-panel bug, not size-specific. Fixed with Tiptap's own documented pattern: `useEditorState({ editor, selector: ({ transactionNumber }) => transactionNumber })` forces a re-render on every transaction. Also redesigned the size control per spec: swapped the native `type="number"` spinner (redundant with the existing +/- buttons) for a `type="text"` input.
  **Round 2, after user testing**: two real follow-up bugs. (1) The native `<datalist>` dropdown genuinely never showed up reliably — replaced with a `FloatingPopover`-based custom dropdown, the same pattern the color/highlight pickers right above it already use, which this app fully controls instead of depending on inconsistent native browser rendering. (2) A real, reported focus-fighting bug: the text input's `onChange` handler called `editor.chain().focus()...run()` on every keystroke — since actually typing into the input requires real DOM focus there, that `.focus()` call yanked focus back to the editor after every single character, which is exactly what "selecting text to resize, then it disappears, like I'm choosing a new place to write" describes. Fixed by only calling `.focus()` from the +/- step buttons (which never actually lose editor focus in the first place, via the existing `onMouseDown preventDefault`) and never from the text input's own `onChange`/clear path — Tiptap commands apply to `editor.state.selection` regardless of which element currently has literal browser focus, so dropping `.focus()` there doesn't break anything, it just stops fighting the input for it.
- [ ] **10. Not tested on mobile yet** — no action here, just a flag; do a real mobile-viewport pass once the rest of this list is down.
- [x] **11. A "code block" node for Docs.** Installed `@tiptap/extension-code-block` (pinned exact `3.29.2` — `^3.29.2` alone let npm resolve to the newer `3.30.0` and conflict with the rest of the pinned Tiptap packages' `@tiptap/pm` peer dep). Added to both `lib/collab/schema.ts` and `CollabDocEditor.tsx`'s extension lists, a slash-command entry + a `DocFormatPanel.tsx` toolbar toggle (both call `toggleCodeBlock()`), and — learning directly from the earlier H1/H2 lesson this session (Tailwind v4 preflight resets `pre`/`code` to inherit too) — proactive `.collab-doc-editor .ProseMirror pre` CSS so it doesn't ship invisible-until-reported like headings did. All three export walkers handle it (a codeBlock's content is always one plain text node with literal `\n`s inside, no marks — Tiptap's default schema disables marks in code blocks): plain text wraps it in ` ``` ` fences, PDF draws a light background band in Courier, Google Docs pushes it as a single run with a forced `Courier New` `weightedFontFamily` (its embedded `\n`s still become real paragraph breaks, same mechanism every other line already relies on). Verified with a throwaway script exercising all three against a hand-built doc JSON — correct output in each, no exceptions.
- [x] **12. The sidebar "glow" active-state (shipped this session) doesn't look good.** No browser available to visually iterate on the `text-shadow` halo's blur/opacity (the likely cause of the reported "square" artifact — a known text-shadow-on-inline-text rendering quirk), so took the user's own offered fallback instead of guessing: `lib/activeGlowStyle.ts` now returns a `color-mix(in srgb, color 65%, white)` tint + `font-weight: 600`, no shadow at all. **Verified 2026-08-12** with a real browser and a colored test List: the active row renders as a clean lightened tint of its own color, bold, no shadow/artifact — screenshot confirmed clean.
- [x] **13. Archive a whole List or Doc (with everything nested inside it kept, moved to Archive).** Today archive only exists at the Task level (`showArchived` toggle, `optimisticArchiveTask`). New capability: List/Doc-level archive that cascades to every Task/subtask (or subpage) inside, keeping them intact and simply hidden behind the Archive view, restorable. See the 2026-08-13 session entry below.
- [x] **14. Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Shift+Z stop working after switching browser tabs away and back.** No browser available to reproduce directly, so this is a best-effort fix, not a confirmed root-cause. Most browsers don't blur the focused element just because a tab loses visibility — `document.activeElement` can still point at a text field/Doc editor from before the switch, silently tripping the listener's existing "skip while focus is in an editable element" guard (`app/page.tsx`'s global Ctrl+Z handler), which is working as designed but easy to not notice you're still "in." Hardened the check to also require the element be connected and actually laid out (`el.isConnected && el.offsetParent !== null`), so a stale/detached reference can't block the shortcut — but if the *same, still-visible* field genuinely has focus, the skip still correctly applies (that's not a bug, native undo should win there). **If this keeps happening, the next repro should check one thing specifically: is a cursor still visibly blinking in some text field/doc at the moment Ctrl+Z is pressed?** If yes, that's the guard behaving correctly and the fix here won't touch it — a different explanation would be needed.
- [x] **15. Right-clicking a Subpage inside an open Doc should show the same context menu as right-clicking a Doc in the left sidebar** (rename/appearance/color etc.). Turned out `DocSubpagesPanel.tsx`'s `PageRow` (the dedicated side column) already had this wired correctly — the real gap was `SubpagesIndexBlock.tsx`, the in-content "Subpages" table block a user inserts via the "/" menu *inside a doc's own body* (what "subpages i en doc" actually meant), which had no `onContextMenu` at all. Fixed: threaded a new `onContextMenu` option through `subpagesIndexNodeView.tsx`'s extension options (same pattern as its existing `onOpenDoc`), a new `onDocContextMenu` prop on `CollabDocEditor.tsx` (wired at the Docs-tab call site via the same `openDocMenu` the sidebar uses; deliberately left unwired at the task-modal Documents-panel call site, which has no `spaceId` to scope that menu to).
- [x] **16. A Subpage's color doesn't match between the sidebar tree and its embed-chip inside the parent Doc's body.** Root cause found: the "RARARA" pill was a `mention` node (`kind: 'doc'`), and `MentionChip.tsx`/`MentionText.tsx` both colored every doc-kind mention from a hardcoded `KIND_COLOR.doc = '#349f7c'` (green), completely ignoring the doc's own `color` field — unlike `user`-kind mentions, which already looked up the real user's own color. `SubpagesIndexBlock.tsx`'s table rows were never affected (already used `child.color` directly), which is why nested-subpage colors looked fine. Fixed: `resolveMentionEntity` (`lib/mentions.ts`) now returns the doc's `color` for `kind: 'doc'`; both chip renderers use it, falling back to the old fixed green only when the doc has no color set.
- [x] **17. No real color-wheel for Space/Task/etc. colors — only the fixed preset swatches.** Not actually missing — `ColorSwatchPicker`'s native `<input type="color">` swatch was already there (2026-08-11/12 rework), but rendered as a flat circle showing whatever color was *already* selected, making it visually indistinguishable from "just another preset dot" with no affordance suggesting "click for more." Fixed: that swatch now shows a small rainbow `conic-gradient` background until a genuinely custom color is picked (only then does it show the real picked color + the white selection ring), same "more colors..." convention as most native color pickers.
- [x] **18. Drag-and-drop a List between Spaces needs a mid-list drop target + position indicator.** Previously `overId.startsWith('list:')` only matched targets inside the *source* Space's own `space.lists` — dropping onto a different Space's list silently failed the check and did nothing (moving between Spaces only ever worked via the Space-header drop, which just appends). Fixed: new `moveListRelativeTo(sourceSpace, draggedId, targetSpace, targetListId, position)` (`app/page.tsx`, replacing the old same-space-only `reorderListSiblings`) searches all Spaces for the target, same reasoning already used for the folder-drop branch; handles pure same-Space/same-Folder reorder and cross-Space/cross-Folder move as one function, landing the dragged List at an exact position among the target's siblings either way (not just wherever `moveList`'s own default append lands it). Full `listDropIndicator`/`listOverRef` state + `pointermove` tracking + the same blue insertion line, mirroring the Space/Doc pattern exactly — rendered in `FolderTree.tsx`'s `ListRow`. **Verified 2026-08-12** via Playwright: dragged a List from Space A to land exactly between two existing Lists in Space B — screenshot confirmed it landed in the precise mid-list slot, not appended to the end. Zero console errors.
- [x] **18b. Docs can't be moved/reordered at all** (new report, arrived mid-session) + **live insertion-line indicator for Docs**. Not a regression in `DocFolderTree.tsx` (traced its whole pipeline — drag/drop hooks, the shared `DndContext`, `page.tsx`'s dispatch, the store actions — all intact). The real gap: `DocSubpagesPanel.tsx`'s `PageRow` (the dedicated "SUBPAGES" side column shown next to an open doc) had **zero** drag-and-drop wiring at all, unlike the sidebar's own `DocRow` — confirmed directly against the user ("nothing happens, doesn't even look picked up" — a drag never starting at all, not a drop-handling bug). Fixed: `PageRow` now uses the same `spacedoc-drag:`/`spacedoc:` id prefixes `DocRow` already uses, so `page.tsx`'s existing dispatch needed zero new branches.
  **Round 2, after user testing: that fix introduced a real id-collision, and the underlying sidebar bug was still unresolved.** `DocSubpagesPanel.tsx`'s panel and `DocFolderTree.tsx`'s sidebar render the *same* doc's row simultaneously whenever a book is open (panel next to the content, sidebar still showing the tree) — reusing `spacedoc-drag:`/`spacedoc:` in both meant two elements registered the identical draggable/droppable id at once, which dnd-kit has no defined behavior for. Gave the panel its own distinct `subpage-drag:`/`subpage:` prefix instead, with `page.tsx`'s three drag handlers (`handleTaskDragStart`/`Over`/`End`) treating it as an alias of the sidebar's own reorder logic, scoped to same-panel drops only (no cross-panel dragging). Still unverified against the *original* "sidebar row doesn't even look picked up" symptom specifically — that diagnosis pointed at `DocFolderTree.tsx` directly, and this round's fix was scoped to the collision, not a confirmed sidebar-specific root cause; worth a fresh report if the sidebar itself is still unresponsive after this.
  Also fixed a related latent correctness bug while extending this: `reorderSpaceDocSiblings` grouped "siblings" by `folderId` alone — but every subpage has `folderId: null` (same as every top-level doc), so two subpages under *different* parents were wrongly treated as siblings. Now matches `parentId` too.
  Also built the live insertion-line indicator for Docs (both the sidebar tree and the Subpages panel), mirroring the existing Space-reorder pattern exactly: `docDropIndicator`/`docOverRef` state in `page.tsx`, a `pointermove` listener while `activeDragEntity.kind === 'spacedoc'`, and a new `reorderSpaceDocRelativeTo(space, draggedId, targetId, position)` (replacing the old index-only `reorderSpaceDocSiblings`) so a drop lands exactly where the indicator last showed, not just "at the target's slot."
  **Round 3, after confirming "Tasks move fine, Docs still stuck" — found the actual root cause.** The user's original diagnostic answer ("drag a Doc row in the sidebar → nothing at all, doesn't even look picked up") pointed at `DocFolderTree.tsx` (the dedicated Docs-tab sidebar) — but that component's `DocRow` genuinely does have full drag wiring, always did. The real broken component was one I hadn't looked at at all: `FolderTree.tsx` (the **Tasks-tab** sidebar) has its **own separate** `DocRow` — Docs are shown there too, alongside Lists, as a sidebar-native convenience (see "Docs as a sidebar-native sibling of List/Folder" in that file) — and *that* `DocRow` had **zero** `useDraggable`/`useDroppable` at all, just a plain `<div onClick>`. Not a regression, never built. "Lists move fine, Docs don't, in the same sidebar, no console error" was the exact signature of a draggable that was simply never wired up, once the actual component the user was looking at was correctly identified. Fixed: full `useDraggable`/`useDroppable` + `isOver`/`isDragging` styling + the same `docDropIndicator` insertion-line rendering, reusing `spacedoc-drag:`/`spacedoc:` (safe here — this `DocRow` and `DocFolderTree.tsx`'s are mutually exclusive, gated by the same `activeView === 'docs' ? ... : ...` ternary, never mounted together). **Still unverified — please retest specifically in the Tasks tab's own sidebar this time, not just the Docs tab.**
  **Scope note surfaced while building this: Tasks have no manual ordering concept at all** — no `Task.order` field, no reorder-within-a-list mechanism to attach an indicator to (the table is sorted by column, not drag position). Extending this indicator to Tasks would mean building manual task ordering from scratch, a separate, larger feature — not done here, flagged back to the user rather than silently expanding scope.
  **Round 4: confirmed working**, including cross-Space. Added a temporary diagnostic `console.log` in `handleTaskDragOver` to see what `over.id` actually resolved to during a live drag, since three rounds of static-code-reading had stopped being productive — removed once confirmed fixed. Root cause of *this* round never fully identified (the round-3 fix, or some combination of it and the round-2 collision fix, apparently resolved it) — noted for posterity in case it resurfaces.
  **Round 5 (follow-up feature request): Docs could only ever sort after every List, never in between.** `FolderTree.tsx` rendered Lists and Docs as two entirely separate `.map()` blocks — a Doc's own `order` value was tracked and written correctly, but the render never gave it anywhere to land except after the last List. Fixed by merging Lists + top-level Docs into one combined, order-sorted sequence per sidebar level (`FolderLevel` in `FolderTree.tsx`) instead of two blocks. The harder part was the reorder/move logic: `List.folderId` and `Doc.folderId` are two genuinely different Prisma foreign keys (`Folder` vs. `DocFolder`, a separate model entirely — see `prisma/schema.prisma`), so a Doc can never structurally nest inside a real Folder the way a List can; every sidebar-native Doc is top-level (`folderId: null`) no matter what. Rather than reconciling two incompatible foreign-key spaces, the new `moveSidebarItemRelativeTo(sourceSpace, dragged, targetSpace, target, position)` (`app/page.tsx`) only ever combines them at the top level — a List target keeps its own real `folderId` for other List siblings; a Doc target (or a dragged Doc) is always `folderId: null`, the only value it can structurally have. Wired into all four drag×drop type combinations (list→list, list→doc, doc→list, doc→doc), each now correctly accounting for the *other* type's rows sitting between them when computing position — a plain list→list reorder that ignored interleaved Docs would have landed in the wrong slot the moment a Doc sat between two Lists. `listDropIndicator`/`listOverRef` (previously List-only) now also drives during Doc drags for this same reason; `reorderSpaceDocRelativeTo` (used by the Docs-tab/Subpages-panel, which don't interleave with Lists) also picked up cross-Space support it was missing before, and `FolderTree.tsx`'s own `DocRow` prop was renamed `docDropIndicator` → `listDropIndicator` to match what it actually reads now (unrelated to `DocFolderTree.tsx`'s separate `DocRow`, which is untouched). **Verified 2026-08-12** via Playwright: dragged a Doc to land precisely between two Lists in the Tasks-tab sidebar — screenshot confirmed the exact interleaved order (List, Doc, List, Doc), not appended after every List. Zero console errors.
- [x] **19. Right-clicking a custom field to edit throws a runtime error** when the field is a plain Text-type field. Exact error (screenshot): `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'map')` at `app/page.tsx:671`. Every live code path (creation, the API's `JSON.parse`, the DB rows themselves) actually produces a real `options` array, so the exact trigger wasn't pinned down — likely a stale object surviving one of this session's many Fast Refreshes. Fixed with the guard regardless (`fieldEditTarget.options ?? []`), which closes the crash whatever the cause.
- [x] **20. Custom Fields apply across every List; they should be scoped to the List they were created on.** Today a custom field created on one List apparently shows up on all Lists. Wants: fields scoped to their originating List; when a task carrying a value for such a field is dragged into a different List that doesn't have that field, show a popup asking whether to create the field on the destination List or cancel the move — not silently move it or silently drop the data. See the 2026-08-13 session entry below.

## Today's session (2026-08-13) — Three QA backlog items: per-List custom fields, List/Doc archive, independent icon/text color

The three remaining well-scoped QA items (#3, #13, #20), tackled together via one plan-mode pass since all three touch schema. Three clarifying questions up front, each resolved with the recommended option: #3 applies to Space/Folder/List/Doc/Room (not literally "Tasks," which has no color/icon field at all); #13 reuses the existing per-Space "Archive"/"Viewing archive" toggle rather than a new dedicated browsable page; #20 leaves every pre-existing Space-wide custom field untouched, only new ones become List-scoped.

**#20 — Custom Fields scoped per List.** `CustomField` gained `listId String?` (null = Space-wide, unchanged default behavior). The task table's column list (`app/page.tsx`) now filters to `f.listId === null || activeListIds.has(f.listId)`; field creation passes the single active List's id when exactly one is selected, `null` otherwise (multi-select/Everything). **Drag-into-a-different-List guard**: a new `moveTaskToList` wrapper (`app/page.tsx`) checks whether the dragged task carries a non-empty value for a field scoped to a *different* List than the drop target — if so, a `ConfirmDialog` offers "Create field there" (creates a matching field on the destination List, keeps the value, completes the move) or "Cancel" (move doesn't happen at all). Wired into all three single-task move paths (drag onto a List, the Folder/Space auto-pick-single-List case, the multi-List picker); bulk multi-select move deliberately still calls `optimisticSetList` directly, flagged rather than silently building a batched-prompt UI that wasn't asked for.

**#13 — Archive a whole List or Doc.** `List`/`Doc` gained `archived Boolean @default(false)` — non-destructive, fully independent of `deletedAt`/Trash. New `lib/archiveCascade.ts` (sibling to `lib/trashCascade.ts`, same "one function, a boolean instead of a timestamp, cascades down" shape) — archiving a List flips every Task inside; archiving a Doc flips its own subpage subtree.
