The existing per-Space `showArchived` toggle, previously task-table-only, now also drives the **sidebar tree**: `getListsIn`/`getBoardDocsIn` (`lib/folderTree.ts`) gained an `archived` parameter, hiding archived Lists/Docs normally and showing *only* them when the toggle is on (dimmed via `opacity-50`). The Docs tab's own tree (`getSpaceDocsIn`, `lib/docFolderTree.ts`) has no equivalent toggle of its own, so it simply always excludes archived docs — the Tasks-tab Archive view is the one place to see/restore one. "Archive list"/"Archive doc" (and "Restore from archive" once archived) added to the existing List/Doc right-click context menus, next to Delete.

**#3 — Independent icon color vs. text color.** `Space`/`Folder`/`List`/`Doc`/`Room` each gained `textColor String?`, falling back to the existing `color` when unset — zero visual change for anyone who never touches the new picker. Every sidebar name-text span (`FolderTree.tsx`'s List/Folder/Doc rows, `app/page.tsx`'s Space row, `DocFolderTree.tsx`/`DocSubpagesPanel.tsx`'s Doc rows, `OfficeRooms.tsx`/`RoomDetail.tsx`'s Room name) now reads `textColor || color`; the icon beside it keeps reading `color` alone, untouched. Each entity's existing edit modal gained a second "Text color" `ColorSwatchPicker` (with the same null-resets-to-Default button Folder/List's own Color picker already has) right below the existing one. **Two files initially in scope turned out to need no changes at all** — `SpaceHome.tsx`'s Folder/List browse cards and `SubpagesIndexBlock.tsx`'s Subpages table already rendered names in plain neutral text with only the icon colored, i.e. already "independent" by pre-existing design, not coupled the way the sidebar tree rows were. Room has no real icon (a plain emoji) — its `color` only ever tinted the floor's border, so `textColor` there is a genuinely new capability (coloring the name specifically) rather than a split of an existing coupling.

All three verified end-to-end via Playwright with throwaway test data (created + cleaned up after each): scoped-field column visibility and the drag-conflict picker's both branches; archiving a List with a task inside and a Doc with a subpage, confirming both the sidebar and task table correctly hide/reveal/restore; independent icon/text colors confirmed via `getComputedStyle` on List and Space (icon color unchanged, name color exactly matched the new `textColor`, accounting for the active-row glow blend where relevant) — two rounds of locator debugging along the way turned out to be **test-script targeting bugs, not app bugs**: a generic `text=X` locator matched multiple real DOM elements (a plain-styled breadcrumb/card alongside the actual colored sidebar row), so `.first()`/`.last()` sometimes grabbed the wrong one — resolved by enumerating every match and its own computed style directly rather than guessing which index was "the" element. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout (aside from the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error).

## Today's session (2026-08-13, continued) — Planner: a real "Event" concept, ClickUp-style Task/Event quick-create popover

QA backlog item #2. Until now Planner could only ever create a `Task` (`CreateTaskModal.tsx`, required Space+List). Scoped via plan-mode with three clarifying questions: **Task vs. Event tabs only** — no Focus time/OOO tabs, video call, location, recurrence, or visibility/busy/mute icons, since none of those map to anything this app can actually do today and building them now would be pure decoration; Event **optionally** links to a Space (for color-coding only, never required — that's the entire point of not being a Task); Events reuse the exact same People-picker pattern Tasks already have. One answer came back as free text rather than picking an option — user wants **two-way Google Calendar sync eventually** — resolved by treating it as an architectural principle rather than a blocker: `Event` is built as its own clean, standalone Prisma model (not a Task variant, not overloading any existing table), so a real sync feature can add its own fields additively later without a rework. No speculative "reserved for sync" fields were added now.

**New `Event` model** (`prisma/schema.prisma`): `title`, `description?`, `startDate`/`endDate` (both `DateTime`, deliberately mirrors `Task.startDate`/`dueDate`'s shape), `allDay Boolean @default(true)`, `color String?` (used only when no Space is linked), optional `spaceId`/`space` (`onDelete: SetNull` — deleting a Space un-links its Events rather than deleting them, since an Event was never "inside" a Space the way a List/Doc is), required `workspaceId`/`workspace` (`onDelete: Cascade`), `assignees User[]` (same implicit M2M shape as `Task.assignees`), `deletedAt` (soft-delete, reuses the existing Trash pattern — a leaf with nothing to cascade, no dedicated `eventCascade.ts` needed). Back-relations: `Workspace.events`, `Space.events`, `User.events @relation("EventAssignees")`.

**Explicit scope cut, flagged rather than silently dropped: no drag-to-reschedule for Events in this pass.** The existing Task drag machinery in `CalendarView.tsx` (`handleDragStart`/`Move`/`End`, `calendarLane` pin/swap, `optimisticSetDates`) is deeply Task-specific: generalizing it to a second entity type is real, separate work the user didn't ask for. An Event's dates are only settable through its own detail modal's date pickers. Event bars render fully clickable but with zero pointer-drag handlers wired up at all.

**API**: `app/api/events/route.ts` (GET/POST) and `app/api/events/[id]/route.ts` (PATCH generic field-patch + `restore`, DELETE soft/`?permanent=true` hard) — both follow the exact shape of the equivalent Task routes. **Self-caught security fix before this ever shipped**: the GET handler's first draft trusted a client-supplied `?userId=` query parameter directly — would have let any client read any other user's events just by editing the URL. Fixed to use `getCurrentUserId()` (`lib/auth/session`, cookie/session-based), matching every other real endpoint's established pattern. `app/api/trash/route.ts` gained Events in its aggregated `Promise.all` listing (no parent-trashed check needed, it's a leaf); `TrashPanel.tsx`'s `TrashItem['type']`/`TYPE_ICON`(`CalendarDays`)/`TYPE_TO_KIND` gained `'event'`.

**Store** (`useTaskStore.ts`): `Event = PrismaEvent & { assignees: AppUser[] }`, `events: Event[]` state + `refetchEvents`, four new actions (`optimisticCreateEvent`, `updateEvent`, `optimisticSetEventAssignees`, `deleteEvent`) following this codebase's standard optimistic-update-then-undo/redo-push shape exactly, no new pattern invented. One real typecheck catch: `updateEvent`'s optimistic local merge had to explicitly `new Date(patch.startDate)`/`new Date(patch.endDate)` since the patch carries ISO strings but the local `Event.startDate`/`endDate` (Prisma-generated) are real `Date` objects — same fix `optimisticSetDates` already needed for Task.

**New `components/calendar/QuickCreatePopover.tsx`** replaces `CreateTaskModal.tsx` (deleted entirely, two stale doc-comment filename references in `PersonalTasksPage.tsx`/`OfficePage.tsx` updated) at its call site in `app/page.tsx`. Task tab is byte-for-byte the old modal's fields; Event tab has title, optional Space select, Start/End `DatePickerPopover`s, an All-day toggle, description, and an inline attendee picker (`FloatingPopover` + checklist, copied from the same inline pattern the Task modal already used, not extracted into a shared component per this codebase's own "small inline pickers over premature abstraction" precedent).

**New `components/calendar/EventDetailModal.tsx`** — opens on an Event-bar click, deliberately much smaller than the Task modal: title (click-to-edit-in-place, same pattern as List/Folder/Space names), Start/End date pickers, All-day toggle, editable Space select, a `ColorSwatchPicker` (new `EVENT_COLOR_CHOICES`, shown only when no Space is linked), description, attendees, red "Delete event" button. No subtasks/comments/docs/status/custom-fields section — none of that concept applies to an Event.

**Rendering — reused the existing generic calendar primitives with zero changes to `lib/ganttLayout.ts` itself.** `TaskRange = {id, start, end}`/`assignLanes`/`clipRangeToWeek`/`layoutDayColumns` are already keyed by a generic string `id`, so Tasks and Events merge into one shared `ranges` array in `CalendarView.tsx` before lane assignment — confirmed via a real Playwright screenshot that a Task and an Event on the same day land in correctly separate, non-overlapping lanes automatically. `WeekRow.tsx`: `tasksById`/`eventsById` are two separate maps looked up per segment (`const event = task ? undefined : eventsById.get(seg.taskId)`), an Event renders as a plain non-draggable clickable `<button>` bar sharing the same `barStyle`, a Task keeps its original full draggable/resizable/pin-badge code path (`task!` non-null assertions post-refactor). `DayTimeline.tsx`: `allDayEvents`/`timedEvents` bucketed via `event.allDay` directly (unlike Task, which infers all-day from an absence of a time-of-day component); the timed-items layout calls `layoutDayColumns` **once** on a combined Task+Event array so a task and event at the same hour never overlap, producing separate `eventBlocks` (click-only) and `taskBlocks` (original draggable code).

**`app/page.tsx`**: swapped the `CreateTaskModal` import/render for `QuickCreatePopover` + a new `EventDetailModal`, added `events` + the four new actions to the store destructuring, `eventDetailId` state, `<CalendarView>` gained `events`/`onOpenEvent` props.

**Verified via Playwright + direct DB/API checks**: created an Event with a linked Space (bar color matched the Space), one without (fell back to the default event color), one with an assignee — all rendered correctly; edited title/dates/description in `EventDetailModal`, confirmed persistence and bar repositioning; created a Task via the same popover's Task tab, confirmed byte-identical behavior to the old `CreateTaskModal` (regression check); deleted an Event, confirmed it left the calendar and appeared in Trash, restored it via direct DB query + a direct `fetch()` call to the restore endpoint. **Two Playwright locator-ambiguity bugs hit during this pass, both test-script bugs not app bugs**: a generic `button:has-text("Event")` matched the workspace-switcher button instead of the Event tab because a test workspace was literally named "EventTest WS" — fixed with `getByRole('button', {name: 'Event', exact: true})` scoped to a specific container; a `text=Team Standup >> nth=0` click meant for the in-place title-edit button instead hit the calendar bar behind the modal backdrop — judged the title-rename path low-risk (an already-proven pattern used identically elsewhere) and verified the riskier parts (Space-unset, attendee-add, delete/restore) directly via network/DB instead once a Next.js dev-overlay artifact started intercepting further UI clicks. A leftover test user from an earlier failed script run *earlier in this same session* (a `status`-field crash during the #20 custom-fields work, `cfscope-1786606564607@test.local`) was also found via a final `email: {contains: 'test.local'}` sweep and cleaned up. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout (aside from the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error).

## Today's session (2026-08-14) — Local image upload for Docs (QA backlog #5)

The image-insert modal in the collaborative Doc editor was URL-only since it shipped (a deliberate v1 cut). User asked for the ability to also pick a file from their own PC — no schema change needed (the uploaded file just becomes a URL fed into the same Tiptap `image` node the URL path already inserts), so this skipped the usual plan-mode ritual reserved for schema-affecting work.

**New `POST /api/uploads/image`** (`app/api/uploads/image/route.ts`) — `getCurrentUserId()`-gated (401 if signed out, same posture as every other real route), accepts a `multipart/form-data` file, validates MIME type against an explicit allowlist (`png`/`jpeg`/`gif`/`webp` — **SVG deliberately excluded**, since it can carry inline `<script>` and this route writes straight into the public static folder with no further sanitization), rejects anything over 8MB, writes it to `public/uploads/docs/<randomUUID()>.<ext>` (never the client-supplied filename — the returned URL is the only handle to the file) via `fs/promises.mkdir(...,{recursive:true})` + `writeFile`, returns `{url}`. `public/uploads` added to `.gitignore` (machine-local content, not source — same precedent as `/prisma/dev.db`).

**`CollabDocEditor.tsx`**: the existing "Insert Image" modal's URL-submit logic was factored out into a shared `insertImageAtCursor(url)` (used by both paths, keeping the existing "always insert a following empty paragraph so Gapcursor has a real line to land in" fix intact). Added an "or" divider + an "Upload from your computer" button wired to a hidden `<input type="file" accept="image/png,image/jpeg,image/gif,image/webp">`; on selection, `submitImageFile` POSTs to the new route, shows an inline "Uploading..." state (both buttons + the URL field disabled meanwhile, backdrop/Escape/X close disabled mid-upload), and surfaces any error inline instead of failing silently. Reused unmodified by both the toolbar path and the slash-command `/image` path, since both already funnel through the same `imageUrlDraft`-driven modal.

**Verified via Playwright** (throwaway user/workspace/space/doc, cleaned up after): logged in through the real `/login` form, opened the seeded Doc, triggered the modal via `/image`, uploaded a real 1×1 PNG via `filechooser`, confirmed the resulting `<img>` tag's `src` pointed at `/uploads/docs/<uuid>.png`, that the file existed on disk, and — the real check, not just "a src attribute exists" — that the image actually rendered (`naturalWidth: 1`) and fetched `200 image/png` **through the authenticated browser context**. A first pass fetching the same URL with a bare Node-side `fetch()` (no session cookie) got redirected to `/login` (200 `text/html`) — traced to `proxy.ts`'s route matcher, which protects every non-API page route including `/uploads/*` (only `/api`, `/login`, `/invite`, and Next's own `_next/static|_next/image|favicon.ico` are excluded). Not a bug: this app has no logged-out-readable surface at all today, so gating uploaded Doc images behind login is consistent with every other page, not a new gap — confirmed correct once re-tested through the actual authenticated browser context instead of a bare fetch. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout (aside from the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error).

## Today's session (2026-08-14, continued) — PDF export now embeds real images (QA backlog #7)

Follow-up to #5 in the same session. PDF export's `image` node handling was a deliberate, disclosed v1 cut: `writeDocToPdf` (`lib/collab/docJSONToPdf.ts`) rendered a `[Image]`/`[Image: alt]` bracket placeholder instead, because pdfkit's `doc.image()` needs a local `Buffer`, not a remote URL, and the doc-JSON tree walk it's called from is otherwise fully synchronous. No schema change, so no plan-mode ritual — straight to implementation.

**New `lib/collab/resolveDocImages.ts`** — a pre-pass that walks the doc JSON once, collects every distinct `image` node's `src`, and resolves each to a `Buffer` *before* the synchronous pdfkit render starts (`Promise.all`, then handed to `writeDocToPdf` as a plain `Map<string, Buffer>`). Three source kinds: `data:` URIs (inline base64, decoded directly), same-origin paths starting with `/` (read straight off disk under `public/` — in practice always our own `/uploads/docs/...` from #5, avoids a wasteful self-referential HTTP round-trip), and `http(s)://` URLs (fetched with an 8s timeout, `content-type` must start with `image/`). Both branches cap size at 8MB; a failed/unsupported/oversized src is simply absent from the returned map, never thrown — callers fall back to the existing placeholder for exactly those.

**Real security consideration caught up front, not after the fact**: the same-origin disk-read branch takes an attacker-controlled string (the "paste an image URL" field accepts *any* text, not just real uploads — a user could type `/../../.env` deliberately) and joins it onto `public/` on disk. Guarded with a resolved-path prefix check (`diskPath.startsWith(PUBLIC_DIR)`) that rejects any `..`-traversal before ever calling `fs.readFile`, closing what would otherwise have been a real local-file-read vulnerability introduced by this exact feature.

**`docJSONToPdf.ts`**: `renderBlock`/`writeDocToPdf` gained a threaded-through `images: Map<string, Buffer>` param (reaching every recursive call site, including inside list-item recursion). The `image` case now looks up a buffer for the node's `src`; if found, computes intrinsic width/height via `doc.openImage()` (not in `@types/pdfkit` despite being the same method `image()` calls internally — bridged with a small typed cast rather than pulling in a second image-parsing dependency), fits it into a max box (own width-preserving-aspect-ratio math, mirroring pdfkit's internal `fit` option) capped at 320pt tall, **proactively page-breaks first if it wouldn't fit in the remaining space on the current page** (pdfkit's `image()` advances the cursor like `text()` does but never auto-paginates the way `text()` does), then draws it. The whole embed attempt is wrapped in try/catch — a resolved buffer that still fails to parse (corrupt file, or a non-image file smuggled through the URL-paste field despite the content-type check) falls through to the original placeholder instead of crashing the entire export.

**Real constraint surfaced, not silently papered over: pdfkit only natively supports JPEG and PNG, not GIF or WebP** (confirmed by reading pdfkit's own bundled source) — even though #5's upload endpoint accepts all four. A GIF/WebP doc image renders correctly on-screen (a normal `<img>` tag) but still falls back to the `[Image]` placeholder specifically in PDF export, caught cleanly by the try/catch above rather than crashing. Verified directly with a throwaway GIF: `resolveDocImages` successfully reads it off disk (it doesn't know or care about pdfkit's format support), but the PDF it produces has no image XObject for it and the surrounding text renders normally — confirmed via a standalone `tsx` script calling `resolveDocImages`/`writeDocToPdf` directly, not just Playwright.

**app/api/docs/[id]/export/pdf/route.ts** now calls `resolveDocImages(json)` once before building the PDF, passing the resulting map into `writeDocToPdf`.

**Verified end-to-end via Playwright** (throwaway user/workspace/space/doc + a subpage, cleaned up after): inserted one image via URL-paste (`https://placehold.co/64x48.png`, exercising the HTTP-fetch branch) and one via the #5 upload button (exercising the disk-read branch) into the root doc, confirmed both `<img>` tags rendered in the live editor; separately expanded the doc's own sidebar-tree chevron to reach its Subpage (per the user's explicit "re-test Subpages export" ask), inserted a third image there. Waited out Hocuspocus's debounced persist, confirmed `Doc.ydoc` was non-null in the DB for both, then fetched both PDFs directly and inspected the raw bytes: both contain a real `/Subtype /Image` XObject and neither contains the `[Image` placeholder text — genuine embedding confirmed, not just "didn't crash." `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout (aside from the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error).

**Known, deliberately out-of-scope wrinkle found along the way, not fixed here**: `lib/collab/docJSONToGoogleRequests.ts`'s Google Docs export already passes an image's `src` straight through as `insertInlineImage`'s `uri` — that's fine for an externally-pasted absolute URL, but a `/uploads/docs/...` relative path (from #5) isn't a URL Google's servers can fetch at all. QA item #7 named PDF specifically; flagging this as a fast-follow rather than expanding scope uninvited.

## Today's session (2026-08-14, continued again) — Doc cover image + the rest of the Page Styles panel

Closed out the last two items from the "Docs, queued next" list: a Doc cover image, and the parts of ClickUp's Page Styles panel that weren't built in the 2026-08-11 formatting pass (page width, header-section toggles for subtitle/last-modified/cover). Both landed together since the toggles and the cover feature are the same UI surface. Schema change, so the usual kill-node/`db push`/restart ritual — a mid-session Turbopack cold-compile hiccup (the already-documented "first hit to a changed route can return a dropped/truncated response" quirk) caused one early verification run to show a stale `pageWidth` in the DB despite correct on-screen behavior; a clean re-run against the warmed-up server confirmed the feature itself was correct all along.

**Four new `Doc` fields**, deliberately following two different idioms already established elsewhere in this schema rather than inventing a third: `coverImageUrl`/`subtitle` are presence-based (`null` = section not shown at all — same "no upload, just a URL, no separate on/off flag" precedent as `Space.coverImageUrl`), while `pageWidth` (`'normal' | 'full'`) and `showLastModified` (`Boolean @default(true)`) need real values since there's no natural "unset" state for either (a width always has *some* value; a timestamp always exists).

**`DocFormatPanel.tsx`** — the collapsed toggle icon and its collapse-button label were renamed from "Formatting" to "Page styles" (matching what the panel now actually covers), and gained a new "PAGE" section above the pre-existing "TEXT" formatting controls: a two-button segmented Normal/Full-width toggle, an "+ Add cover"/"Remove cover" control (the add path opens a small `FloatingPopover` with a URL input, same "paste an image URL" pattern the Doc-body image-insert modal already uses — no upload option here, this is a header banner, not body content), a presence-toggling "+ Add subtitle"/"Remove subtitle" button, and a checkbox-styled "Show last edited" toggle. The panel gained two new optional props, `doc?: TaskDoc` and `onUpdateDoc?`, both `undefined` at the task-modal Documents-panel call site (no `spaceId` there) — exactly mirroring how `onDocContextMenu` was already made optional for the same reason, so the new Page section simply doesn't render for a task-scoped embedded doc.

**`CollabDocEditor.tsx`** derives `doc` itself via a `useMemo` over the already-subscribed `workspaces` store slice (`spaceId` → Space → `spaceDocs.find(...)`) rather than accepting it as a new prop threaded down from `app/page.tsx` — consistent with how `users`/`addDocComment` are already pulled directly from the store in this same file instead of via props. `onUpdateDoc` is a thin closure over the store's existing `updateSpaceDoc(docId, spaceId, patch)`, so no new store action was needed at all — only the existing one's patch type gained the four new optional fields (plus the matching `oldPatch` reconstruction lines needed for correct undo).

**`app/page.tsx`**: the cover image renders as a `-mx-6` "breakout" banner (cancels the ancestor scroll container's own `p-6`, so it reaches the true edge on both sides) directly above the title input, with a hover-reveal "Remove cover" button; the subtitle renders as a second plain-text input directly under the title (same "click to edit, no separate save step" shape as the title itself); the existing "Last updated ..." span is now wrapped in `activeStandaloneDoc.showLastModified &&`. **Page width** required touching the *shared* outer content wrapper (`max-w-6xl mx-auto`, used by every `activeView`, not just Docs) — made its className conditional: `w-full` instead of the centered max-width, but only when a Doc is actually open (`board`/`docs` view) *and* that Doc's own `pageWidth === 'full'` — every other view keeps the fixed width regardless, and a `pageWidth: 'full'` Doc only ever affects its own screen.

Explicit scope check, not built: the `select` shape for `docs` in `GET /api/workspaces` needed the four new fields added by hand (the recurring "explicit Prisma `select` needs updating for a new column" lesson, caught before it shipped this time rather than after) — `app/api/docs/[id]/route.ts`'s PATCH already returns the full unshaped row, so no change needed there; `POST /api/spaces/[id]/docs` (doc creation) also returns the full row with no `select`, so new docs pick up the schema defaults automatically with zero code change.

Verified via Playwright + direct DB checks (throwaway user/workspace/space/doc, cleaned up after): toggled page width both directions, confirming the on-screen `max-width` computed style flips between `1152px` and `none` and the change round-trips through a real PATCH request each time (captured via Playwright's own request listener, not just trusted); set a cover image and confirmed the `<img>` renders; added a subtitle and typed into it; toggled "Show last edited" off and confirmed the metadata line disappears; a final full-page screenshot confirmed the whole header (cover banner, title, subtitle, owner/contributors row, Page Styles panel with both the new Page section and the pre-existing Text section) renders cleanly together with no visual overlap. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout (aside from the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error).

## Today's session (2026-08-14, continued a third time) — Selecting multiple lines and toggling Code block created one box per line

Reported directly: "instead of being one whole codeblock, there are 3 lines of codeblock." A quick clarifying question narrowed it to the live editor itself, not an export. Root cause found by reproducing two candidate scenarios directly rather than guessing from a code read: typing normally *inside* an already-open code block (via the `/code` slash command) worked correctly the whole time — Tiptap's own internal keymap (`newlineInCode`, baked into `@tiptap/core`, not something this app configures) already handles a plain Enter within a `code: true` node by inserting a literal `\n` in the same node, confirmed via a real Playwright DOM check (one `<pre>` containing all typed lines).

The actual bug: **selecting several *existing* plain paragraphs and clicking the Page Styles panel's "Code block" toggle**. Tiptap's plain `toggleCodeBlock()` applies `setBlockType` to every selected textblock independently — a 3-paragraph selection became 3 separate `<pre>` elements (3 separate boxed code blocks), not one block containing all 3 lines. Confirmed by reproducing it directly: typed three plain lines, selected all three, clicked "Code block," and counted 3 real `<pre>` DOM nodes.

Fixed in `DocFormatPanel.tsx`'s `codeBlock` entry in `MARK_BUTTONS`: turning code-block *off*, or toggling *on* with an empty selection (cursor with nothing selected), still goes through the plain `toggleCodeBlock()` — neither has a "multiple textblocks at once" case to merge. Toggling *on* with a real (non-empty) selection now instead reads the selected text with `state.doc.textBetween(from, to, '\n', '\n')` (joining textblock boundaries with `\n`, matching how a real code block's own multi-line content is already stored — see `docJSONToPdf.ts`'s codeBlock case), deletes the selection, and inserts a single `codeBlock` node containing that joined text — always producing exactly one block regardless of how many paragraphs were originally selected.

Verified via Playwright: re-ran the exact repro (3 plain lines → select all → toggle Code block) against the fix — now produces exactly one `<pre>` with `"alpha\nbeta\ngamma"` instead of three. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout (aside from the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error).

## Today's session (2026-08-14, continued a fourth time) — Cover image upload from disk, and numbered/bullet lists were invisible

Two more items, same session. First, a direct follow-up request: the Doc cover image (built earlier this session) was URL-only — user wanted the same local-disk upload #5 already built for body images. Reused the exact same `/api/uploads/image` endpoint with zero backend changes: `DocFormatPanel.tsx`'s cover popover gained an "or / Upload from your computer" section (hidden file input + upload button + uploading/error state), mirroring `CollabDocEditor.tsx`'s own `submitImageFile` almost line-for-line — on success it just calls `onUpdateDoc({ coverImageUrl: data.url })`, since a cover is nothing more than another `coverImageUrl` string underneath. Verified via Playwright: uploaded a real PNG, confirmed the resulting `<img>` actually rendered (`naturalWidth: 1`), the file landed on disk, and the URL persisted to the DB.

Second, reported directly: "numbered list funker ikke" (doesn't work). A quick reproduction immediately showed this was the **exact same root-cause class already hit twice this project for headings and code blocks**: Tailwind v4's preflight resets `list-style: none` and zeroes `ul`/`ol` padding/margin globally, and `.collab-doc-editor .ProseMirror` had never had its own override for lists. Confirmed via direct inspection before touching any code — the toggle command worked correctly the whole time (`<ol><li><p>first item</p></li></ol>` was the real, correct DOM structure, and the toolbar button showed its active/highlighted state), but `list-style-type: none` and `padding-left: 0` meant literally nothing distinguished it from plain text: no number, no indentation, no bullet dot either (bullet lists have the identical unstyled-in-this-app problem, just not the one specifically reported). Fixed with the same proactive-CSS pattern this project now has three instances of: `.collab-doc-editor .ProseMirror ul/ol/li` rules restoring `list-style`, padding, and margins, with a nested-list margin reset so a sub-list doesn't inherit its parent's extra vertical spacing. Verified: after the fix, the same repro renders `1. first item` with a real visible marker and indentation, confirmed both via computed `list-style-type: decimal` and a screenshot.

**Worth flagging as a standing lesson** (not just for this one bug): every rich-text block type this editor has ever added — headings, code blocks, now lists — has hit this same Tailwind-preflight-strips-the-native-tag-styling issue at least once, always discovered only after being reported as "doesn't work" rather than caught proactively. Any *future* block-level HTML element (blockquote, table, hr, etc.) should get its `.collab-doc-editor .ProseMirror <tag>` CSS rule written proactively, in the same commit that adds the extension, rather than waiting for a fourth report of the identical shape.

`npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout both fixes (aside from the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error).

## Today's session (2026-08-16) — Chat Phase 1: schema + channel CRUD + plain message feed

Built the first phase of the Chat architecture approved 2026-08-15 (see the "Planned: Discord/Slack-style chat" section above). All 5 models (`ChatChannel`, `ChatChannelMember`, `ChatMessage`, `ChatReaction`, `ChatAttachment`) added to `prisma/schema.prisma` in one pass — every later phase's fields (thread/quote/reaction/attachment columns) are already there so none of them need their own schema change, only Phase 1's own slice of behavior is wired up. Pushed via `prisma db push` (confirmed: this repo has no `prisma/migrations/` directory at all, so "one migration" in the roadmap doc means one `db push`, not `migrate dev` — every other schema change in this project's history goes through `db push` the same way).

New: `GET/POST /api/workspaces/[id]/channels` (list is `canSee`-filtered same as `app/api/workspaces/route.ts`'s Space filtering; create always makes `type: 'channel'` in this phase, creator becomes a member immediately, and a private channel's `accessEntries` of `type: 'user'` get their `ChatChannelMember` rows created eagerly at creation time, per the "canSee vs membership split" design note); `GET/POST /api/channels/[id]/messages` (flat feed, `authorId` always taken from the session server-side rather than trusted from the request body, unlike the existing `Comment`/`DocComment` routes' looser precedent; both verbs upsert the caller's own membership row, the "lazy-join-on-open/post" behavior). `store/useChatStore.ts` (new, bare Zustand store, no middleware, same shape as `useSessionStore`/`usePresenceStore`) and `components/ChatPanel.tsx` (new — channel list + flat message list + textarea, manual refresh button, no websocket). Wired into the app's existing single-file router: `'chat'` added to `NavView` (`lib/navUrl.ts`), `useTaskStore`'s `activeView` union (two separate type declarations both needed updating — easy to miss one), `NavTabId`/`NAV_TABS` in `SettingsPanel.tsx` (so Chat is hideable via Settings same as the other tabs), a nav-rail icon button, and a content branch in `app/page.tsx` right after Office (both are workspace-scoped, ignore Space/List selection — Chat was also added to the `setNavigation` "bounce back to board" list alongside Office/My Tasks/My Personal/Profile for the same reason). Deliberately **not** built this phase: channel selection isn't URL-synced (stays local `useState` in `ChatPanel` — wiring it through `page.tsx`'s two 40+-line URL-sync effects, the same way `docId`/`docFolderId` are, was judged not worth the risk for a Phase-1 "done when" bar that doesn't require it); channel rename/retopic (`PATCH`) and message delete (`DELETE`) routes don't exist yet — `PLANNING.md`'s full API surface lists them, but nothing in Phase 1's scope needs them, so `useChatStore`'s `postMessage` deliberately does **not** push a `useHistoryStore` undo/redo entry yet (there's no delete route for undo to call — a real gap to close once one exists, not something to fake with a no-op).

Verified end-to-end with two real signed-up-and-logged-in users (a scripted Auth.js credentials-flow login via `fetch`, since Playwright isn't installed in this project — captured real session cookies the same way a browser would, not a bare unauthenticated `fetch()`, which `proxy.ts` would silently redirect to `/login` per the existing "known bugs" note on `/uploads/*`). User A created a workspace, added User B as a member, created one private channel (granting only A) and one public channel, and posted in both. Confirmed: B's channel list includes the public channel but omits the private one entirely; B's direct `GET`/`POST` against the private channel's messages both 403; B can read and post in the public channel; A's refetch afterward shows both A's and B's messages — persistence and multi-user visibility both correct. Also caught live in the dev server log: the actual browser session already had `?view=chat` open and successfully listing channels through the real UI, not just the scripted API calls, while this was being verified.

## Today's session (2026-08-16, continued) — Chat channel picker moved into the main sidebar; message-feed restyle

Two follow-up UI passes the same day as Phase 1 above.

**Sidebar channel picker:** the channel list moved out of `ChatPanel.tsx`'s own left column into the app's main left `<aside>` (via a new `components/ChatChannelSidebar.tsx`), replacing the Space/Folder/List tree while `activeView === 'chat'` — same shape as how Office already swaps that tree out for its Team list. `useChatStore` gained a shared `activeChannelId`/`setActiveChannelId` so the sidebar and the message panel (now two separate components) stay in sync without prop drilling.

**Message-feed restyle:** moved away from a boxed-widget look toward ClickUp/Slack conventions — day dividers ("Today"/"Yesterday"/date), consecutive-message-from-the-same-sender grouping (avatar/name/time shown once per run of messages within a 5-minute window, not repeated on every line), and a unified pill-shaped composer. Verified the day-divider/grouping logic directly against seeded multi-day test data (a Prisma script backdating messages in an isolated test workspace) before trusting it — correctly produced "N days ago → real date," "Yesterday," and "Today" with the same-day run-breaking working as intended.

## Today's session (2026-08-17) — Chat Phase 2 (real-time), channel rename, message formatting

Three things built together at the user's request: Phase 2 from the chat roadmap (real-time delivery), plus two additions outside the original 8-phase plan — channel rename and lightweight message formatting (code/bold/italic).

**Channel rename:** `ensureChannelAccess` (previously private to `app/api/channels/[id]/messages/route.ts`) moved to a new shared `lib/auth/chatAccess.ts`, since Next.js route files can only export the recognized HTTP-verb names — a route can't just export an extra helper for a sibling route to import. New `PATCH /api/channels/[id]`, auth-gated for every caller (not just privacy-changing fields, unlike Space/List's looser precedent — every other channel route already requires membership+`canSee` for reads too, so a looser rename would've been a regression specific to this file). `useChatStore.renameChannel` + a hover-reveal pencil icon in `ChatChannelSidebar.tsx` that swaps a row into an inline input (mirrors `FolderTree.tsx`'s `ListRow` rename pattern), kept self-contained rather than lifted into `app/page.tsx`'s shared rename-id system.

**Message formatting:** new `lib/chatFormat.tsx`, a small hand-rolled renderer (confirmed via grep: no markdown library exists anywhere in this app, greenfield) — fenced ` ``` ` code blocks split out first so their contents never get inline-parsed, then one combined regex pass over what's left handles `` `code` ``, `**bold**`, `*italic*`/`_italic_`. Not a CommonMark parser, just the four token types actually asked for. Code styling reuses Docs' exact `#18181b`/`#27272a` colors for visual consistency. Verified the regex logic against edge cases (nested markers inside a code block, unmatched/dangling `*`/`` ` ``, back-to-back bold spans) with a standalone script before trusting it — all behaved correctly, no crashes. `ChatPanel.tsx`'s composer textarea also now auto-grows up to a capped height, since a fixed single row was hiding everything past the first line the moment multi-line code blocks became a real thing to type.

**Phase 2 — real-time delivery:** `lib/collab/chatRoom.ts` mirrors `presenceRoom.ts` exactly (`chat:<channelId>` document names). `server/collabServer.ts` gained a `resolveWorkspaceId` branch for chat rooms, an `onAuthenticate` addition (a narrow `CHAT_BROADCAST_SECRET`-token bypass for the server-to-server bridge connection, scoped to chat rooms only and safe when the env var is unset; plus real `canSee()`-based tightening for private channels, reusing the exact function from `lib/auth/access.ts` rather than re-implementing it inline), and `onLoadDocument`/`onStoreDocument` early-returns identical to the presence-room pattern (no Y content ever persisted for a chat room).

The broadcast bridge ended up using Hocuspocus's `sendStateless`/`onStateless`/`broadcastStateless` primitive — a discrete-event mechanism, not the awareness-state approach `PLANNING.md` originally sketched (awareness collapses to one state per peer, a poor fit for "a message was sent," which can happen repeatedly). **Spiked in isolation first, exactly as planned, and the spike caught a real gap:** a first attempt failed silently — `sendStateless()` reaches the server but does **not** auto-relay to other connections on the same document; an explicit `onStateless({ document, payload })` hook calling `document.broadcastStateless(payload)` is required, or the message is just dropped with zero error anywhere. Confirmed against the actually-installed `4.5.0`, not assumed from docs. Also hit, separately: `tsx watch` restarts `collabServer.ts` on *file* changes but does not reread `.env.local` on its own — the first spike run failed with `permission-denied` purely because the process was still running with the old environment; a full dev-server restart (not just a file save) was needed after adding `CHAT_BROADCAST_SECRET`.

New `lib/collab/broadcastChatSignal.ts` (server-only, opens a short-lived authenticated `HocuspocusProvider`, sends the stateless signal, tears down) called fire-and-forget from the messages POST route right after the Prisma write commits. New `lib/collab/useChatChannelConnection.ts` mirrors `usePresenceConnection.ts`'s exact Strict-Mode-safe lifecycle, wired into `ChatPanel.tsx` — the manual refresh button stays as a fallback for a signal missed during a brief disconnect (never queued server-side, just caught up to on the next real fetch).

Verified: a scripted two-part check — (1) the isolated stateless-broadcast spike itself (listener + sender both connected via the broadcast secret, confirmed receipt), then (2) full integration through the real authenticated REST flow (signed-up/logged-in user renames a channel via the real PATCH route, then posts a message via the real POST route while an external listener connected to that exact channel's room confirms it receives the resulting real-time signal). Channel-rename access control and the private-channel `canSee` tightening on the websocket path itself were not independently re-verified via a raw cookie-authenticated socket connection (no clean way to attach a session cookie to a bare Node-side `HocuspocusProvider`) — both reuse the exact `canSee`/`ensureChannelAccess` logic already proven correct at the REST layer in Phase 1's two-user test, so this is a reasoned trust call, not a verified gap.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued) — Clipboard crash fix, unrelated to chat

`navigator.clipboard` doesn't exist at all in an insecure context (this app is currently reached over plain HTTP via a LAN/nip.io address — see the "Deployment prep" note above, no HTTPS set up yet), not merely permission-denied — so both the Settings invite-link copy button and the calendar-feed-link copy button (`app/page.tsx`) threw `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'writeText')` instead of copying, a pre-existing bug in both spots, unnoticed until the user hit it live. Fixed with a shared `lib/copyToClipboard.ts` that falls back to the legacy hidden-textarea `execCommand('copy')` trick when the real API is unavailable, returning whether the copy actually succeeded so neither call site shows a false "copied" confirmation.

Committed and pushed to `origin/main` (commit `354a165`) — this covers everything from Chat Phase 1 through this fix in one commit, following this repo's existing convention of one commit per session even when it bundles an unrelated fix (see e.g. `7d7ccd0`'s cover-image + list-styling commit).

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued again) — Chat Phase 3: DMs, and a real security gap found + closed along the way

Session started with a `git pull` on a machine that hadn't touched this repo since 2026-08-12 — brought in five days of another session's work (all 20 QA-backlog items, the whole Chat feature through Phase 2). Local `prisma/dev.db` was stale relative to the pulled schema (chat models, `Event`, `textColor` fields, `CustomField.listId`, `Folder`/`List`/`Doc`... — none of it existed in this machine's own DB yet), surfacing as ~30 `tsc` errors ("Property 'chatChannel' does not exist on type 'PrismaClient'") that had nothing to do with any code change — `npx prisma db push` (no EPERM this time, nothing was running yet) plus a restart cleared every one of them. Worth remembering as a checklist item after any `git pull` that touches `prisma/schema.prisma` on a machine that wasn't the one that built it.

**Phase 3 — DMs (1:1 + group), per the roadmap above.** No schema change needed (`ChatChannel.type`/`dmKey`/`ChatChannelMember` were already built in Phase 1 for exactly this). New `GET/POST /api/workspaces/[id]/dms` (`app/api/workspaces/[id]/dms/route.ts`) — GET lists DMs where the caller has a real `ChatChannelMember` row (never `canSee`, see below); POST is find-or-create: 1:1 computes `dmKey` and upserts against it (reopens the same conversation both directions — messaging the same person twice, or them messaging back, both land on the identical row); 2+ members always creates fresh (`group_dm`, `dmKey: null`, deliberately never deduped, matching real Slack/Discord — two groups with identical membership stay genuinely distinct rows). Every target id is cross-checked against real `WorkspaceMembership` rows first — can't DM someone outside the workspace.

**A real, live security gap, not just a design-doc TODO.** `PLANNING.md`'s own Phase-3 design note ("canSee vs. membership split") had already flagged that DM visibility must go through `ChatChannelMember` membership directly, never `canSee` — but two places still hadn't been updated to actually do that, because DM rows never existed to exercise them until now:
1. `lib/auth/chatAccess.ts`'s `ensureChannelAccess` (guards every `/api/channels/[id]/...` route) unconditionally ran the `canSee`-based check regardless of `channel.type`. For a DM row (`isPrivate: false`, since that field is only meaningful for `type: 'channel'`), `canSee` returns `true` for *any* workspace member — meaning, before this fix, any workspace member could have read/posted into any other two members' private DM via the REST API, the moment a DM row existed to test it against.
2. `server/collabServer.ts`'s `onAuthenticate` had the exact same gap on the real-time websocket path — its own comment even said "DM/group-DM rooms don't exist yet (Phase 3)... when they land, they must check ChatChannelMember directly." They landed; this is that fix. Same failure mode: `channel.isPrivate` false for a DM meant the private-channel branch never even ran, silently granting the connection to any workspace member.

Both fixed by branching on `channel.type` before doing anything else: `'dm'`/`'group_dm'` → real `ChatChannelMember` row required, full stop, `canSee` never consulted; `'channel'` → the existing `isPrivate`/`canSee` logic, unchanged. Caught by actually building Phase 3 and re-reading the design doc's own warning against the real code, not by assumption — this is exactly the kind of gap that stays dormant and looks fine until the feature that exercises it actually ships.

**Store**: `useChatStore` gained `dmsByWorkspace` (parallel to `channelsByWorkspace`, kept as a genuinely separate fetch/slice rather than a `type` filter over one shared list, since DM visibility rules are different enough to want to keep them structurally apart), `fetchDMs`, `createOrOpenDM`. `ChatChannel`'s type gained an optional `members` field (only populated by the `/dms` route — a DM has no stored `name` at all, `null` always; it's rendered client-side from whichever *other* members are on it, the same "relative to the viewer" convention Slack/Discord use for DM titles, not one fixed string two different viewers would see identically).

**UI**: `ChatChannelSidebar.tsx` gained a second "Direct messages" section below Channels, each row showing a small stacked-avatar cluster + comma-joined other-member names, and a "+" opening a `FloatingPopover` checkbox picker (`NewDMPicker`, reusing the app's established checkbox-row visual language) over the current workspace's members minus self — picking one starts/reopens a 1:1, two or more starts a group. `ChatPanel.tsx` and `app/page.tsx`'s breadcrumb both now resolve the active conversation from *either* `channelsByWorkspace` or `dmsByWorkspace` and render a DM without the `#` prefix a real channel gets (a person's name isn't a channel name).

**Verified two ways, both against the real running server, not just eyeballed:**
1. A scripted REST-level run (minted real Auth.js session JWTs for four throwaway users the same way this project's existing PLANNING.md precedent describes, in a real throwaway workspace) confirmed every roadmap "done when" bar and the security fix at once: A messaging B twice, and B messaging A back, all three land on the identical DM id; two group-DM creates with identical membership produce two distinct ids; a workspace member who isn't a DM participant gets a real 403 on both GET and POST; a real participant gets 200; a non-workspace-member can't open the `/dms` route at all (403); DMing someone outside the workspace 400s.
2. A second script drove the actual websocket path with a real `HocuspocusProvider` client (via a `ws`-backed `WebSocketPolyfill` subclass injecting the session cookie as a real request header — same "drive the real client library against the real server" precedent as Phase 2's own broadcast-bridge spike, installed with `--no-save` and removed afterward) — confirmed the real DM participant's connection authenticates and the non-participant workspace member's connection is rejected with `permission-denied`, closing the loop on the collabServer.ts fix specifically, not just trusting it reuses already-proven REST logic.

All test fixtures (throwaway users/workspace/channels) cleaned up by both scripts after running; nothing left behind in the dev DB.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a third time) — Chat Phase 4: inline quote-reply

**Quote-reply, per the roadmap above.** No schema change (`ChatMessage.quotedMessageId`/`quotedBodySnapshot`/`quotedAuthorId` were already built in Phase 1 for exactly this). `POST /api/channels/[id]/messages` now accepts an optional `quotedMessageId`; when present, the route itself (never the client) fetches that message — scoped to the same channel, so a stale/wrong-channel id is just silently ignored rather than erroring — and snapshots its *current* `body`/`authorId` into the new message's `quotedBodySnapshot`/`quotedAuthorId` at send time. That snapshot is what every render always reads from, never a live re-lookup of `quotedMessageId` — which is exactly what makes the roadmap's "done when" bar work: `quotedMessageId` itself goes `null` the moment the original is hard-deleted (`onDelete: SetNull`), but the two snapshot columns are plain strings untouched by that relation, so a reply keeps showing what was actually quoted regardless.

**Message delete had to get built too — Phase 4's own "done when" bar requires it to test against**, even though it wasn't in that phase's own bullet description. New `DELETE /api/messages/[id]` (`app/api/messages/[id]/route.ts`): hard delete (the already-confirmed v1 decision), author-only (a caller can only delete their own message — no moderator override yet, unlike channel rename which is open to every member), broadcasts the same chat signal a new message does so other open tabs refetch and see it gone. This also closed a documented-but-deferred gap: the design doc always intended `postMessage`/`deleteMessage` to mirror `addDocComment`/`deleteDocComment`'s undo/redo shape, but `postMessage` had shipped with no history push in Phase 1 specifically because there was nothing for undo to call yet. Now wired up properly: `postMessage` pushes `{undo: deleteMessage, redo: re-postMessage with the same id}`; `deleteMessage` itself pushes nothing (mirrors `deleteDocComment` exactly — it's the undo *target*, not itself undoable via a direct delete click, same as a Doc comment).

**UI** (`ChatPanel.tsx`): every message row is now a hover target revealing Reply (everyone) and Delete (own messages only, mirroring the server-side author check) icon buttons. Reply sets a small composer chip above the textarea ("Replying to X — snippet", dismissible) that attaches `quotedMessageId` to the next send and clears itself after. A message carrying a quote snapshot renders a small left-bordered `QuotedPreview` block above its own body — quoted author name is resolved *live* against the current workspace roster (same "resolve by id, fall back if gone" convention this app's existing @-mentions already use for author display), deliberately different from the frozen body snapshot: a `User` row isn't hard-deleted the way a message is, so showing their current name is both safe and more useful than freezing that too.

**Verified against the real running server, not just eyeballed**: a scripted run (real Auth.js session JWTs for two throwaway users, same precedent as every other chat verification this project) posted an original message, posted a reply quoting it, confirmed the snapshot fields matched exactly, confirmed a non-author gets a real 403 trying to delete someone else's message, then hard-deleted the original and re-fetched — confirming the roadmap's exact "done when" wording: the reply's `quotedMessageId` went `null` (the `SetNull` relation firing correctly) while `quotedBodySnapshot`/`quotedAuthorId` were both completely unaffected and still rendered the original text/author correctly. All throwaway fixtures cleaned up after.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a fourth time) — Chat Phase 5: threads

**Threads, per the roadmap above.** No schema change (`ChatMessage.threadRootId`/`threadReplies`/`threadReplyCount` were already built in Phase 1). New `GET/POST /api/messages/[id]/thread` (`app/api/messages/[id]/thread/route.ts`) — a deliberately separate id namespace from `/api/channels/[id]/messages`, not a query param on it: the main feed route has only ever returned `threadRootId: null` rows since Phase 1, so thread replies never leaked into it *structurally*, not just by UI convention — this route is the mirror image, only ever returning `threadRootId: <id>` rows. Access still goes through `ensureChannelAccess` on the root's own channel (same DM-vs-channel membership split as everywhere else in chat) — no separate thread-level permission concept. POST wraps the reply insert and the root's `threadReplyCount: {increment: 1}` in one `$transaction`, and rejects (400) an attempt to reply-to-a-reply — this schema's `threadRootId` always points at the true root, never chains, so a second-level thread isn't a concept it can express. `DELETE /api/messages/[id]` (Phase 4) gained the matching decrement when the deleted message is itself a thread reply, also inside a `$transaction`; deleting a *root* needs no bookkeeping at all, since `threadReplies`' own `onDelete: SetNull` already lets its former replies fall back to rendering as normal main-feed messages, and the root (and its count) are simply gone together.

**A thread reply can be a quote-reply at the same time** — the roadmap's own "done when" bar for this phase — because `threadRootId` and `quotedMessageId` are fully orthogonal columns; the thread route runs the exact same server-side-only quote-snapshot logic the main feed route already does (quoting anything in the same channel, not just something inside the thread itself).

**Store**: `useChatStore` gained `threadsByRootId` (replies keyed by root id), `activeThreadRootId`/`setActiveThreadRootId` (a message's own id doubles as the thread id — no separate Thread model to reference), `fetchThread`, `postThreadReply`. `postThreadReply` mirrors `postMessage`'s undo/redo shape exactly (undo → `deleteMessage`, redo → re-`postThreadReply` with the same id). `deleteMessage` gained an optional third `threadRootId` param — passed by the caller (who already knows it, since it's rendering that specific thread) rather than waited-on from the DELETE response, so the optimistic removal from `threadsByRootId` and the cached root's `threadReplyCount` decrement both happen immediately instead of after a round-trip. `setActiveChannelId` now also clears `activeThreadRootId` — a thread panel left open for a channel you've navigated away from doesn't make sense to leave hanging.

**UI**: new `components/ChatThreadPanel.tsx`, a fixed-width side panel (`app/page.tsx`'s chat branch is now a flex row: `ChatPanel` plus this panel when a thread is open) showing the root message, a flat reply list, and its own mini-composer — deliberately reuses `ChatPanel.tsx`'s exported `timeLabel`/`QuotedPreview`/`MessageActions` rather than duplicating them, so a message looks identical whether it's rendered in the main feed or inside a thread. Every root message with `threadReplyCount > 0` gets an "N replies" affordance below its body; every message row also gained a "Reply in thread" hover-action icon (`MessagesSquare`), deliberately separate from the existing inline "Quote reply" icon — the two are orthogonal actions, not merged into one button, matching the "done when" bar's own framing. `MessageActions`'s `onOpenThread` prop is optional specifically so `ChatThreadPanel`'s own reply rows can omit it (no second-level threads, so "reply in thread" doesn't apply inside a thread). Real-time: `ChatThreadPanel` runs its own independent `useChatChannelConnection` to the same `chat:<channelId>` room `ChatPanel` is already connected to — a second simultaneous connection from the same tab, accepted deliberately rather than building shared-connection plumbing this app has nowhere else either; Hocuspocus rooms are built for many concurrent connections, this is just two from one browser tab instead of one.

**Verified against the real running server**: a scripted run posted a root message, then posted a reply that was *simultaneously* a thread reply and a quote of that same root — confirmed `threadRootId`/`quotedMessageId` both pointed at the root at once (the phase's own "done when" bar). Confirmed the reply never appeared in the main feed's response while the root's `threadReplyCount` there read `1`; confirmed the dedicated thread endpoint returned exactly that one reply. Confirmed a non-channel-member gets 403 on both thread GET and POST, and that replying to a reply (attempted second-level thread) 400s. Deleted the reply and confirmed the root's `threadReplyCount` in the main feed dropped back to `0` and the thread list emptied. All throwaway fixtures cleaned up after.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a fifth time) — DMs go cross-workspace: personal Connections + a top-level "Direct Messages" Me-zone destination

**Re-scope, not a new phase.** After seeing Phase 3 DMs live, the user asked for a real re-architecture: Direct Messages moves out of the per-workspace Chat sidebar into its own Discord-style top-level destination next to "My tasks"/"My assigned tasks" in the Me zone, sorted by most-recent activity, and — the substantial part — **DMs must work between people who share no workspace at all** ("companies talking to each other without being in the same workspace"), via an explicit "Add as connection" mechanism. Agreed via a plan-mode pass first (mirroring how the original chat architecture itself was agreed), plan approved 2026-08-17.

**Confirmed decisions (asked directly, don't re-litigate):** the connection mechanism is a personal, reusable invite link (`/connect/[code]`) mirroring the existing Workspace-invite-link pattern exactly — no in-app user search built. Sharing a workspace still auto-counts as "connected" (zero regression to the old instant-DM-any-coworker flow, no backfill needed for that half). Group chats draw from the caller's full connections list (explicit connections ∪ every coworker across every workspace), not one workspace's member list.

**Schema:** `ChatChannel.workspaceId` is now nullable (`String?`, `onDelete: SetNull`, same precedent as this model's own `taskId`/`spaceId`) — only `type: 'channel'` rows keep a real workspace; `dm`/`group_dm` rows are created with `workspaceId: null`. `dmKey` dropped the workspace segment (`${workspaceId}:${sortedIds}` → plain `sortedIds.join(':')`) so a pair lands on one conversation regardless of which (or how many, or zero) workspaces they share. New `ChatChannel.lastMessageAt`, bumped on every new top-level message or thread reply, backing the recency-sorted DM list. New `Connection` (`userAId`/`userBId`, canonical-order `@@unique` pair — one row per pair, never two) and `ConnectionInvite` (`id` itself is the `/connect/[id]` code, `createdById` `@unique` — one link per person, find-or-create via `upsert`, same `Workspace.personalOwnerId` race-avoidance precedent).

**A real bug caught during planning, not by the user:** `server/collabServer.ts`'s `onAuthenticate` resolved a chat room's workspace and unconditionally threw if that came back null — which every DM/group-DM room now does, since `workspaceId` went nullable. Would have silently broken real-time delivery for every existing DM the moment this shipped. Fixed by restructuring so the generic workspace-membership check only runs for real channels/Docs; a dm/group_dm room's own `ChatChannelMember` check (already correct, already there) is the complete gate for that case. Verified both directions with a live WebSocket test (`HocuspocusProvider` + a cookie-injecting `ws` polyfill, same technique as Phase 3's original access-control verification): a DM participant still connects, a non-participant still gets rejected, and a real channel's member/non-member gating is unaffected by the restructure.

**dmKey backfill, one-time and manual:** `npm run backfill:dm-keys` (`scripts/backfillDmKeys.ts`) rewrites every existing `type: 'dm'` row's `dmKey` to the new workspace-independent format and clears its `workspaceId`. Needed because leaving old rows in the old format would let the same pair spawn a *second*, duplicate DM the next time they message each other (old-format key never matches the new-format lookup). Ran once against the dev DB after `prisma db push` — backfilled 1 existing DM cleanly, 0 duplicates.

**API:** `/api/workspaces/[id]/dms` deleted, replaced by global `GET/POST /api/dms` (list sorted by `lastMessageAt` desc; create validates every target via a new `lib/auth/connections.ts` — `getConnectedUserIds`/`areConnected` = explicit `Connection` row **or** shared `WorkspaceMembership`, computed live, never materialized, so leaving a workspace correctly and automatically drops that coworker-connection). New `GET /api/connections` (explicit ∪ coworkers, tagged `source: 'connection'|'workspace'`), `GET/POST /api/connections/invite` (fetch-or-create / regenerate my link), public `GET /api/connect/[code]` + authed `POST /api/connect/[code]/accept` (instant, idempotent via `Connection.upsert` on the canonical pair — no accept/decline step, unlike Workspace invites). **Superseded 2026-08-17 (see "Connections: request/accept flow" session below)**: opening the link now creates a pending `ConnectionRequest` the owner explicitly accepts/declines, not an instant `Connection` — this bullet is the original historical record. `GET/POST /api/workspaces/[id]/channels` now `include`s `members`, same shape a DM row already had, so `ChatPanel`/`ChatThreadPanel` resolve quoted-author names from one identical code path for both channel types — no more per-workspace member lookup at all. `proxy.ts`'s public-route matcher gained `connect` alongside `login`/`invite`.

**Store/UI:** `useChatStore`'s `dmsByWorkspace` → flat `dms` (`fetchDMs()`/`createOrOpenDM()` dropped their `workspaceId` param); new `connections`/`connectionInvite` state. `ChatChannelSidebar.tsx` lost its entire "Direct messages" section — Channels-only now, still correctly workspace-scoped. `ChatPanel`/`ChatThreadPanel` dropped their `workspaceId` prop, resolving the active channel/thread's channel from a flattened `channelsByWorkspace` + `dms` lookup instead. New `components/DirectMessagesPage.tsx` — the actual Me-zone destination, a two-tab layout (Chats: recency-sorted DM/group list + reuses `ChatPanel`/`ChatThreadPanel` for the message view; Connections: connect-link copy/regenerate + resolved connections list tagged "Connected directly" vs. "Coworker"). New `app/connect/[code]/page.tsx`, structurally mirroring `app/invite/[code]/page.tsx` but auto-firing the accept call on an authenticated session with no manual "Join" click. New `'directMessages'` view registered in all the same 6 places `'mypersonal'` already was (`lib/navUrl.ts`'s `NavView` union + `parseNavUrl`, `useTaskStore.ts`'s `activeView` field/setter/`setNavigation`'s Space-agnostic-screens bounce-back array, `FolderTree.tsx`'s prop union) plus the new Me-zone button and content-switch branch in `app/page.tsx`.

**Verified against the real running server**, scripted: coworkers with zero explicit `Connection` row still DM successfully (zero regression); a stranger sharing no workspace gets 403 from `POST /api/dms` until they accept the other person's `/connect/[code]` link, after which the same call succeeds; accepting the same link twice is a no-op, not an error; `GET /api/connections` correctly tags both sources; `GET /api/dms` re-sorts to put a channel first after a plain message, and *again* after a thread reply lands in a different, older channel (confirming thread replies bump recency too). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error. All fixtures cleaned up after (test `Connection`/`ConnectionInvite`/DM-channel rows deleted, `ws` package removed — installed with `--no-save` purely for the WebSocket test harness).

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a sixth time) — Chat Phase 6: image uploads

**Media, per the roadmap.** Generalized `app/api/uploads/image/route.ts` (previously Docs-only) with an allowlisted `context` param (`'docs'|'chat'`, never an arbitrary client-supplied path segment — anything not on the list is rejected, not sanitized-and-allowed) → `public/uploads/<context>/<uuid>.<ext>`. Defaults to `'docs'` when the field is absent, so the original Docs caller (`CollabDocEditor.tsx`) needed zero changes. New shared `lib/chatAttachment.ts` (`validateChatAttachment`) — a client-supplied attachment is only ever trusted if its `url` starts with `/uploads/chat/`, i.e. something this app's own upload route could actually have produced; verified server-side rejects a spoofed external URL (silently dropped, not an error, same "don't trust the client" posture as the quote-snapshot logic beside it).

**Message routes** (`app/api/channels/[id]/messages/route.ts` POST, `app/api/messages/[id]/thread/route.ts` POST): accept an optional `attachment: { url, fileName?, byteSize? }`, nested-created via `attachments: { create: attachment }` in the same `chatMessage.create()` call. **An image-only message (empty body) is now valid** — Discord/Slack-style — the "reject empty message" check now only fires when there's neither real body text nor a validated attachment. GET routes and both POST responses gained `include: { attachments: true }`.

**Store**: `ChatMessage.attachments: ChatAttachment[]`, new `PendingChatAttachment` type for what the composer passes in. `postMessage`/`postThreadReply` needed no implementation changes — both already spread their whole `opts` object into the request body, so `attachment` flows through for free.

**Composer** (`ChatPanel.tsx` + `ChatThreadPanel.tsx`, mirrored in both): a picked image shows an immediate local blob-URL preview chip above the composer — same "no network call until the user commits" convention `CollabDocEditor.tsx`'s own image picker already uses — client-side type/size validation (PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP, 8MB cap) mirrors the route's own allowlist purely for a faster/friendlier failure, the route re-validates independently regardless. Actual upload (new shared `lib/uploadChatImage.ts`, `context=chat`) only happens on Send, then the returned URL rides along in the same `postMessage`/`postThreadReply` call as the text body. Blob URLs are revoked on remove/channel-switch/unmount to avoid leaking memory. New shared `AttachmentGrid` (exported from `ChatPanel.tsx`, reused by the thread panel) renders each message's images as capped `240×240` thumbnails, click-through to the full image in a new tab; a message row's body `<div>` is now conditionally rendered (`{first.body && ...}`) so an image-only message doesn't show an empty paragraph above its thumbnail.

**Verified against the real running server**: uploaded a real PNG through the generalized route with `context=chat`, confirmed the URL lands under `/uploads/chat/`; confirmed the original no-context call still defaults to `/uploads/docs/` (zero regression for Docs); posted and persisted (survives a fresh GET) a genuine image-only message; confirmed a truly empty message (no body, no attachment) still 400s; confirmed a spoofed non-`/uploads/chat/` attachment URL is silently dropped server-side rather than trusted; confirmed a thread reply can also carry an attachment. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error. Test fixtures (throwaway messages + the two placeholder PNGs actually written to disk) cleaned up after.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a seventh time) — Chat Phase 7: reactions

**Reactions, per the roadmap — the last of the 7 core Chat phases (Phase 8/unread-tracking stays explicitly optional/deferred).** New `POST /api/messages/[id]/reactions` — a single toggle endpoint (not separate POST/DELETE), keyed on the schema's existing `@@unique([messageId, userId, emoji])`: creates the row if absent, deletes it if present, returns the message's full current reaction list. Access gated through `ensureChannelAccess(message.channelId, userId)`, same DM-vs-channel membership split as every other chat route. `emoji` is only loosely validated (non-empty, ≤8 codepoints — a raw unicode sequence, not a real allowlist of specific emoji) since v1 deliberately has no custom/shortcode emoji, per the schema's own standing comment. Fires the same `broadcastChatSignal` every message-mutating route does, so a reaction lands live in another open tab.

**No emoji-picker library added** — a small fixed `QUICK_REACTIONS` palette (👍 ❤️ 😂 😮 😢 🎉 🙏 👀, Discord-style common set) lives directly in `ChatPanel.tsx`, kept intentionally minimal rather than pulling in a dependency for an open-ended picker.

**Message routes** (`app/api/channels/[id]/messages/route.ts`, `app/api/messages/[id]/thread/route.ts`) gained `include: { reactions: { include: { user } } } }` on every GET/POST response, alongside the existing `attachments` include from Phase 6.

**Store**: `ChatMessage.reactions: ChatReaction[]` (flat rows, not pre-grouped server-side — grouping-by-emoji for the pill display happens client-side, same "server sends raw rows, client shapes them" split the day/run message grouping already uses). New `toggleReaction(channelId, messageId, emoji, currentUser, threadRootId?)` — mirrors `resolveDocComment`'s exact shape from the original chat store design doc: immediate optimistic local patch (using a deterministic `optimistic:<userId>:<emoji>` placeholder id, overwritten cleanly by the next real-time-triggered refetch) + fire-and-forget POST, **no** history push — low-stakes, instantly reversible by clicking again, not the kind of action this app tracks in undo/redo. `currentUser` is the caller's own resolved profile (from the active channel's own `.members`, same source `resolveAuthorName` already uses everywhere) rather than the store reaching into `useSessionStore` itself, keeping the store's existing "receives whatever ids/objects it needs as params" convention. `threadRootId` is optional, same convention `deleteMessage` already established for "is this a reply, not a root."

**UI** (`ChatPanel.tsx` + `ChatThreadPanel.tsx`, mirrored in both, root and reply rows): new exported `ReactionBar` — one pill per distinct emoji with its count, highlighted border when the caller is one of the reactors, click toggles; a reactor-names tooltip via the pill's own `title`. `MessageActions` gained an optional `onReact` prop rendering a new smiley button that opens a `FloatingPopover` grid of the quick-react palette — self-contained per row (each `MessageActions` instance owns its own popover open-state locally, no lifted/shared state needed across rows). Omitted entirely when the caller couldn't resolve its own member profile (shouldn't happen in practice, degrades to no react button rather than a broken click).

**Verified against the real running server**: two different users each reacting with 👍 correctly grouped to one pill at count 2; a second, distinct emoji from the same user added a second pill; re-toggling an already-set reaction removed it (count back down); reactions persisted across a fresh GET; a non-participant's reaction attempt correctly 403'd (same access gate as everything else in chat); an oversized/non-emoji string correctly 400'd. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error. Test fixtures cleaned up after.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued an eighth time) — Chat: drag-and-drop + generic file uploads

**User request, not part of the original 7-phase roadmap** — Phase 6 only covered images. Two asks together: drag-and-drop for attaching (images and files both), and the ability to upload non-image files at all.

**Upload route** (`app/api/uploads/image/route.ts`, path kept as-is for caller compatibility despite now serving more than images) gained a second allowlist, `ALLOWED_FILE_TYPES` — pdf/doc(x)/xls(x)/ppt(x)/txt/csv/zip/rar/7z/mp3/wav/mp4/mov. **Deliberately an allowlist, not a denylist**: this route writes straight into `public/`, served statically with no per-file `Content-Disposition` control, so anything a browser could execute inline if opened directly (html/htm/svg/js/xml) or the OS could execute (exe/msi/bat/cmd/sh/ps1/jar) is simply never on the list — same reasoning the image allowlist's own SVG exclusion already established, extended rather than abandoned. Non-image files are **chat-only** — `CONTEXTS_ALLOWING_FILES = Set(['chat'])` — Docs' image-insert modal has no "attach a file" concept, so its own upload surface stays exactly as narrow as before (verified: a PDF posted with `context=docs` still 400s). Size cap: 8MB for images (unchanged), 20MB for files. Response now includes `kind: 'image' | 'file'`, derived server-side from the file's actual validated MIME type — never trusted from the client.

**Schema**: no change — `ChatAttachment.kind` already existed with `@default("image")` and was explicitly commented "field left open" back in Phase 1, exactly for this. `lib/chatAttachment.ts`'s `validateChatAttachment` now also validates/passes through `kind` (defaults to `'image'` if absent or not `'file'`, so any payload shaped like the pre-this-pass client still behaves identically).

**Client upload helper renamed** `lib/uploadChatImage.ts` → `lib/uploadChatFile.ts` (`uploadChatFile`), now returns the server's own `kind` alongside url/fileName/byteSize.

**Composer** (`ChatPanel.tsx` + `ChatThreadPanel.tsx`, mirrored in both): `pendingImage` state generalized to `pendingAttachment: { file, kind, previewUrl }` — `previewUrl` stays a real blob-URL thumbnail for images, `null` for a generic file (rendered as an icon + filename + size chip instead, new shared `lib/formatBytes.ts`). The file `<input>` dropped its `accept="image/*"` restriction (any file type can now be picked; validation happens in `processPickedFile`, same as before, shared between the file-input path and the new drop path — not duplicated). Composer button changed from an image-specific icon to `Paperclip` ("Attach a file or image").

**Drag-and-drop**, new: the whole panel (message list + composer) is a drop target, `onDragEnter`/`onDragLeave`/`onDragOver`/`onDrop`, with a `dragActive` overlay ("Drop to attach"). Uses a **counter, not a boolean**, for enter/leave tracking — `dragenter`/`dragleave` fire on every nested element a dragged file passes over, so a plain boolean flickers off the instant the pointer crosses into a child element instead of only when it actually leaves the whole zone (same class of gotcha as this session's earlier dnd-kit notes, different API). `e.dataTransfer.types.includes('Files')` gates every handler so an internal drag (e.g. a browser tab) doesn't light up the overlay. `AttachmentGrid` now branches on `a.kind`: image renders the existing capped thumbnail; `file` renders a small card (icon, filename, formatted size) that downloads on click instead of opening inline.

**Verified against the real running server**: a PDF uploads successfully under `context=chat` with `kind: 'file'`; the identical PDF under `context=docs` still 400s (Docs stays image-only, zero regression); an HTML file under `context=chat` is rejected (allowlist holds even for a context that now accepts "files," not just "not an image"); a 21MB file 413s against the new 20MB file cap; a message posted with a `kind: 'file'` attachment round-trips correctly through both POST and a fresh GET. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error. **Not verifiable by script, needs the user's own eyes**: the actual drag-and-drop interaction itself (no browser/Playwright tool available in this session) — the overlay's look, whether dropping over the composer vs. the message list both work, and whether the counter-based enter/leave tracking is actually flicker-free in a real browser.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a ninth time) — Connections: request/accept flow, incoming requests, total-connections count

**Supersedes the original Connections design decision.** The connect-link (`/connect/[code]`) originally connected two people *instantly* on open — explicitly decided that way ("no accept/decline step, no in-app search"). User asked for the opposite: a real send-request solution, an incoming-requests list they can confirm/deny, and a visible total-connections count. Implemented directly (no plan-mode pass — this builds on, doesn't replace, the existing Connection/ConnectionInvite/canonicalPair infrastructure from the earlier Connections session).

**Schema**: new `ConnectionRequest` (`fromUserId`, `toUserId`, `@@unique([fromUserId, toUserId])`) — directional, unlike `Connection`'s own canonical-pair storage, since "who requested whom" is exactly what the incoming/outgoing UI split needs. Accepting creates the real `Connection` row and deletes the request; declining just deletes it. `Connection` itself and `lib/auth/connections.ts`'s `getConnectedUserIds`/`areConnected`/`canonicalPair` are completely unchanged — coworker auto-connect (shared Workspace) and the DM/group access checks built on top of them were never touched by this pivot.

**`/api/connect/[code]/accept`** rewritten: opening someone's link now creates a `ConnectionRequest` (`status: 'requested'`) instead of an instant `Connection`, **except** two cases the route itself resolves before ever creating a pending row: already connected (idempotent no-op, returns `status: 'connected'`) and a **mutual request** — the link owner had separately already opened *my* link first, so their request is sitting there waiting on me. Rather than making them separately go find and accept my now-also-pending request, this auto-accepts both immediately (one `$transaction`: create `Connection`, delete their pending request) and returns `status: 'connected'` right away — mirrors how real friend-request systems treat "you both added each other" as instant friends, not a redundant double-confirm. Re-opening the same link while a request is already pending is a friendly upsert no-op, same "click twice" precedent as `WorkspaceInvite`'s own accept route.

**New `GET /api/connections/requests`** — my pending requests both directions (`incoming`/`outgoing`), each row's `user` field always the *other* person so the client never has to re-derive which side it's on. **New `POST /api/connections/requests/[id]/accept`** — recipient-only (404, not 403, for anyone else — never confirms a request id exists to someone who can't act on it), creates the Connection + deletes the request in one transaction. **New `POST /api/connections/requests/[id]/decline`** — same operation (delete the row) works for either side: the recipient declining, or the sender canceling their own still-pending outgoing request.
