**Store**: `connectionRequestsIncoming`/`connectionRequestsOutgoing` + `fetchConnectionRequests`/`acceptConnectionRequest`/`declineConnectionRequest`. Accept/decline refetch (connections + requests, or a local filter) rather than optimistically patching three pieces of state — not a hot-path/high-frequency action like a reaction toggle, so a plain re-fetch is simpler and correct by construction, no optimistic-placeholder bookkeeping needed.

**UI** (`DirectMessagesPage.tsx`'s Connections tab): connect-link copy updated ("sends you a connection request" instead of "connects instantly"). New **Requests** section — incoming requests get explicit Accept/Decline buttons (two-button confirm, not a single toggle like reactions, since this creates a real persistent row); a second, smaller **"Sent, waiting on them"** section shows my own outgoing requests with a Cancel button. A small red badge on the **Connections tab button itself** shows the incoming-request count (Discord-style), fetched eagerly on page mount (not gated on the tab being open) so it's accurate the instant the Me-zone page loads. The **total connections count** — the thing explicitly asked for — is the existing per-connection list's header, relabeled from "People (N)" to **"Connections (N)"** for clarity; it already summed explicit connections ∪ coworkers, no new counting logic needed. `app/connect/[code]/page.tsx` now branches on the accept route's `status` field: `'requested'` shows "Connection request sent to X, they'll need to accept it," `'connected'` (either already-connected or the mutual-auto-accept path) shows the original "you're now connected" copy unchanged.

**Verified against the real running server**, scripted (15 checks): opening a link creates a request, not a connection; re-opening is idempotent (exactly one row, not a duplicate); both sides see the request in the correct incoming/outgoing list; the sender cannot accept their own outgoing request (404); declining removes the request without creating a Connection; a fresh request followed by an explicit accept creates the real Connection and cleans up the request row; DMing then succeeds between the two; and — the trickiest case — two fresh users each opening the other's link (mutual request) ends in exactly one Connection row and zero leftover pending requests, confirming the auto-accept transaction. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error. All fixtures (test users, connections, requests, invite links) cleaned up after.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a tenth time) — Direct Messages: Chats/Connections moved from an internal tab strip into the real sidebar

**User observation**: Direct Messages' Chats/Connections switcher lived as a small tab bar *inside* the content pane, while the actual left `<aside>` sidebar kept showing the (meaningless, for a workspace-agnostic view) Spaces & Lists tree underneath it — an extra, redundant nested-tab layer instead of using the sidebar-driven navigation every other top-level view (`'chat'`, `'office'`) already has.

**Fix**: new `components/DirectMessagesSidebar.tsx`, swapped into the main `<aside>` for `activeView === 'directMessages'` in `app/page.tsx`'s existing sidebar-swap ternary (`'office' ? Team list : 'chat' ? ChatChannelSidebar : 'directMessages' ? DirectMessagesSidebar : Spaces & Lists tree` — exact same shape ChatChannelSidebar already established for `'chat'`). Contains two vertical nav buttons (Chats / Connections, the latter carrying the incoming-request-count badge that used to sit on the internal tab) and, only under Chats, the DM/group conversation list + "New message" picker — all moved verbatim out of `DirectMessagesPage.tsx`.

**New shared store field**: `activeDmTab: 'chats' | 'connections'` + `setActiveDmTab` in `useChatStore` — same "lives in the store so two separate components (sidebar + content pane) stay in sync without prop drilling" reasoning `activeChannelId` already uses for ChatChannelSidebar/ChatPanel.

**`DirectMessagesPage.tsx`** is now content-pane-only: no more internal tab state, no more left column. Renders `<ChatPanel />` (+ thread panel) when `activeDmTab === 'chats'`, or the connect-link/requests/connections-list content when `'connections'` — which also got a real layout upgrade now that it has the full content width instead of a cramped 240px sidebar column (a centered `max-w-xl` column, larger avatars/rows, matching this app's existing "cap the text column, not the whole page" convention from the earlier Docs full-width session). Data-fetching split the same way ChatChannelSidebar/ChatPanel already split it: the sidebar (which needs the list to render) owns `fetchDMs`/`fetchConnections`/`fetchConnectionRequests`; the content pane only fetches what it alone needs (`fetchConnectionInvite`, gated on the Connections sub-view being active).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error; a server-rendered hit to `/?view=directMessages` with a real session returns 200 with no errors in the dev log. The actual visual layout/interaction (does the sidebar toggle feel right, does the wider Connections view read well) needs the user's own eyes — no browser tool available this session.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued an eleventh time) — "My tasks" becomes a real private Space/List zone instead of a flat quick-list

**User request, same underlying complaint as the Direct-Messages sidebar fix earlier today**: "My tasks" should work like Spaces/Lists do everywhere else in the app — real organizational structure, tasks that look like any other board — just scoped to one person instead of a team.

**Key finding that shaped the approach**: Board rendering (the Spaces & Lists sidebar tree, board columns, task creation) is **not a reusable component** in this codebase — it's built directly into `app/page.tsx`, keyed on `activeView === 'board'` + `activeWorkspaceId`/`currentWorkspace`, spread across dozens of gates through a 5000+ line file. Extracting it into something reusable for a truly separate "personal zone" that never touches `activeWorkspaceId` would have been a large, risky refactor project on its own. But: the hidden personal Workspace this app already auto-creates behind "My tasks" (`Workspace.isPersonal`, `POST /api/workspaces/personal`) turned out to **already be a real, fully-formed Workspace** — its own `WorkspaceMembership` (owner), its own `Space` ("Personal"), its own `List` ("My tasks") — using the exact same models, same generic Space/Folder/List CRUD routes, same `getAccessContext`/`canSee` access control as any team workspace, with **zero personal-workspace special-casing anywhere in that path**. Confirmed via a dedicated Explore pass before touching anything.

**Decision** (confirmed with the user directly — offered "switch into it like any workspace, reusing everything" vs. "build a real standalone component first," user chose the former, while keeping the "My tasks" Me-zone button itself as the one-click, always-visible entry point it already was): clicking "My tasks" now does what picking any workspace from the switcher does — `ensurePersonalWorkspace(userId)` → `setActiveWorkspaceId(workspaceId)` → `setActiveView('board')`. This lights up the **entire existing** Space/List sidebar tree, Board columns, "New Space"/"New List" flows, and task creation for the personal workspace with **zero new UI code** — real Space/List CRUD, "as much or as little as you want," tasks rendered identically to any team workspace's board, exactly as asked.

**`'mypersonal'` retired as a distinct `activeView`/`NavView`** — it's no longer a separate screen, just a workspace switch + the real `'board'` view, so it was removed from all the same registration points it (and `'directMessages'` before it) needed when added: `lib/navUrl.ts`'s `NavView` union + `parseNavUrl`, `store/useTaskStore.ts`'s `activeView` field/setter types + the "Space/List-agnostic screens" bounce-back array in `setNavigation` (My tasks is explicitly no longer in that array — a Space/List click while viewing it should behave exactly like it does for any other workspace, not bounce to a different screen), `components/FolderTree.tsx`'s prop union. **`components/PersonalTasksPage.tsx` deleted** — its whole reason to exist (a deliberately flat, single-list quick-add UI with no Space/List picker, by its own header comment) is superseded by reusing the real Board. The Me-zone button's active-highlight condition changed from `activeView === 'mypersonal'` to `currentWorkspace?.isPersonal`.

**Privacy gaps this surfaced and closed** (real regressions, not pre-existing bugs — the personal workspace was never reachable as `currentWorkspace` via user action before this, since it was excluded from the workspace switcher's dropdown and from default auto-selection; now that "My tasks" routes through the exact same `currentWorkspace` machinery, surfaces that assumed "any `currentWorkspace` is a real team workspace" needed a look):
- The header workspace-switcher's own popover shows a "Members" list with add/remove-member buttons for `currentWorkspace` — would have let someone accidentally add other people into their private personal workspace. Guarded: hidden entirely when `currentWorkspace.isPersonal`.
- `SettingsPanel.tsx`'s "Invite" tab would generate a real shareable `/invite/[code]` join-link to the personal workspace — a genuine leak, not just clutter (unlike Roles/Import, which are harmless-if-odd for a single-member workspace, so left alone to avoid overreach). Guarded: tab hidden when `workspace.isPersonal`.

**Verified against the real running server**: `ensurePersonalWorkspace` still returns the workspace/space/list ids; `GET /api/workspaces` returns the personal workspace with a real populated Space→List hierarchy; the generic `POST /api/lists` and `POST /api/spaces` routes (the exact same ones team workspaces use) successfully create a second List and a second Space inside it, with no rejection anywhere in the path; a server-rendered hit to `/?workspace=<personalId>` returns 200 with no dev-log errors. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error. Not verifiable by script, needs the user's own eyes: the actual sidebar-tree/board visual experience while inside "My tasks," and whether seeing "Personal" surface through the header's workspace-switcher label feels right in practice.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a twelfth time) — Fixed a double-highlight bug from the "My tasks" change: Chat and My tasks stayed active at once

**User-reported, with a screenshot**: after switching into "My tasks" (which now sets `activeWorkspaceId` to the personal workspace and `activeView` to `'board'`, per the previous session), clicking the icon-rail "Chat" tab correctly switched `activeView` to `'chat'` — but "My tasks" in the Me zone *stayed* highlighted blue too, since its highlight condition only checked `currentWorkspace?.isPersonal`, which never changes back just because you navigated elsewhere while still inside the personal workspace (`activeWorkspaceId` only changes when you explicitly switch workspaces or click "My tasks" again).

**Fix, two one-line condition changes in `app/page.tsx`**: the "My tasks" Me-zone button's highlight now requires `currentWorkspace?.isPersonal && activeView === 'board'` (not just workspace identity) — so it stops being "active" the moment you navigate to Chat/Docs/Planner/Office for the personal workspace. The icon-rail "Tasks" tab's highlight now requires `activeView === 'board' && !currentWorkspace?.isPersonal` — so it never lights up for the personal board, leaving "My tasks" as the sole owner of that highlighted state instead of both showing active simultaneously (they're structurally the same `activeView === 'board'`, just different `activeWorkspaceId`). "My assigned tasks" and "Direct Messages" already used their own distinct `activeView` values (`'mytasks'`/`'directMessages'`, never equal to `'board'`/`'calendar'`/`'docs'`/`'office'`/`'chat'`), so they were never actually part of this bug — confirmed by reading every icon-rail tab's condition, not just assumed.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error; dev log clean. The actual visual behavior (only one thing highlighted at a time now) needs the user's own eyes to confirm — no browser tool available this session.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a thirteenth time) — Deployment prep: the "deliberately bounded" authorization gap, closed

User picked deployment prep as the next focus. Of its three known prerequisites, only one is actually actionable from this codebase without VPS/Pterodactyl access the assistant doesn't have (permanent `AUTH_URL` needs a real domain that doesn't exist yet; the sidecar port-mapping happens in the Pterodactyl panel itself) — so this session scoped down to that one: the "broader per-route authorization hardening" gap named (but not sized) back in the 2026-08-09 access-control session.

**A full audit (read every route under `app/api/**` scoped to Task/Space/List/Folder/Doc) found the real gap was far bigger than "comment/custom-field/status routes":** 24 route files total (23 found by the initial audit pass + `POST /api/tasks`, missed by the first pass since it isn't `[id]`-parameterized, caught by a follow-up sweep for any route never calling `getCurrentUserId()` at all) had zero access check beyond, at best, an `isPrivate`-change-only gate — including Task/Space/List/Folder's own PATCH/DELETE for every *non-privacy* field (title, status, assignees, move, rename, delete), and Doc's own PATCH/DELETE with no check whatsoever (Doc doesn't even have the privacy-gate pattern, since it has no `isPrivate` of its own). Also found, materially worse than a missing check: `app/api/docs/[id]/export/google/route.ts` trusted a client-supplied `body.userId` instead of the session and used *that* person's stored Google refresh token — any caller could pass a victim's user id and get Siqt to write into their real Google Drive. A same-shaped, lower-severity version in `app/api/spaces/[id]/docs/route.ts` (`getCurrentUserId()` called but a null result wasn't rejected). Chat's own nested routes were re-checked and confirmed already correct (`ensureChannelAccess`) — nothing to fix there.

**Fix, one new shared module — `lib/auth/resourceAccess.ts`** — same "ensureXAccess" contract as the already-proven `ensureChannelAccess` (`lib/auth/chatAccess.ts`): `ensureSpaceAccess`/`ensureFolderAccess`/`ensureListAccess`/`ensureTaskAccess`/`ensureDocAccess`/`ensureDocFolderAccess`, each authenticating then confirming the caller can actually *see* the resource by walking its full independent ancestor chain (reusing `getAccessContext`/`canSee`/`buildFolderChainVisibility` from `lib/auth/access.ts` — no new access-control logic, just applying what already existed for flat list routes to single-resource routes too). This app's access model is visibility-only, not permission bits (per the 2026-08-09 session), so one check covers every method — the existing `canManageWorkspace`-gated `isPrivate`/`accessJson`-change branches stayed untouched as a separate, correctly-scoped elevated check on top. Doc's helper is the one genuinely non-trivial case: since Doc has no `isPrivate` of its own, it checks whichever of `taskId`/`spaceId`/`boardFolderId` are actually set and passes if the caller can see it via *any one* of them — matching how a Doc is already exposed today through two independent listing endpoints, not a new stricter rule. Applied the same `getCurrentUserId()` → `ensureXAccess()` two-line pattern at the top of all 24 route files, plus `app/api/spaces/[id]/docs/route.ts` gained an extra check on `body.parentId` specifically (a caller with access to Space A could otherwise smuggle a subpage doc into a private Space B's tree just by naming one of its doc ids as `parentId`).

**A real regression caught by the plan's own verification, not assumed away**: `ensureTaskAccess`/etc. initially excluded soft-deleted rows (`deletedAt` set) from the existence check — which broke every one of Task/Doc/Folder/List/Space's own `body.restore === true` un-delete branch, since a task being restored is by definition currently soft-deleted. Fixed by decoupling the access check entirely from trash state (whether something shows in a *live list* is each route's own separate `deletedAt: null` filter; whether a caller may act on a specific known id at all doesn't change just because it's sitting in Trash). A **second** real bug caught by the verification script itself, not assumed fixed: `DELETE /api/tasks/[id]` was missed on the first pass (the check landed on PATCH but not DELETE in that one file) — a scripted test of a non-granted user deleting someone else's private task came back 200 instead of 403, caught immediately and fixed.

**Verified with a 38-check scripted pass** (two real signed-up/logged-in users, same style as every other verification this session): a plain workspace member can still fully use a *public* Space/List/Status/CustomField exactly as before (create, rename — zero regression for the common case); a non-granted member gets 403 on all 24 routes when targeting a *private* Space's Folder/List/Task/Doc/CustomField/Status/DocFolder directly by id; the Space's own owner can still do everything inside their own private Space; a logged-out request gets 401; restoring a soft-deleted Task still works; the Google-export spoof is closed (a non-authorized caller gets 403 before the route ever looks at whose Google account to use). All 38/38 passed on the final run.

**Found beyond the approved plan's scope, flagged rather than silently expanded — user asked to fix them too, same session:** `app/api/events/route.ts`+`[id]/route.ts`, `app/api/rooms/route.ts`+`[id]/route.ts`, and `app/api/trash/route.ts` had the same "no `getCurrentUserId()` at all" shape. Event/Room aren't Task/Space/List/Folder/Doc, so a plain workspace-membership check (`getAccessContext(workspaceId, userId).isMember`) is the whole fix — no `canSee`/ancestor-chain needed, since neither model has an `isPrivate` of its own. **`GET /api/trash` was the worst of the three, genuinely a global data leak**: zero `workspaceId` filtering on any of its 7 parallel queries meant *any* caller — authenticated or not — could enumerate every soft-deleted Space/Folder/List/Task/DocFolder/Doc/Event across **every workspace in the entire app**, not just their own. Fixed by scoping every query to `workspaceId: { in: <caller's memberships> }` (Doc's query needed an `OR` across its two independent parent paths — Space and Task→List→Space — same "any one path grants visibility" shape as `ensureDocAccess`). Named explicitly, not yet closed: this only fixes the *cross-workspace* leak — a trashed *private* Task/Doc a member wouldn't normally have `canSee` access to still shows in that workspace's own Trash list today, a narrower further refinement.

Verified with a second, focused 12-check scripted pass: a workspace outsider (zero shared workspaces, not merely non-granted on one private resource) is fully denied creating/editing/deleting a Room or Event and sees a genuinely empty Trash array — not a filtered one, an *empty* one — while the actual owner keeps working normally and a logged-out request gets nothing at all instead of the previous full leak. 12/12 passed.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a fourteenth time) — New-channel popover instead of a native prompt; Connections search

Two small user-reported UX gaps, same session as the authorization hardening above.

**"+ New channel" used `window.prompt()`** — a native browser dialog that appears pinned to the top of the browser chrome, not near where the user actually clicked. Replaced with a `FloatingPopover` (the same component already used for Settings' invite-link popover and Direct Messages' "New message" picker) anchored to the `+` button itself, containing a small name input + "Create channel" button, Enter-to-submit. `ChatChannelSidebar.tsx`'s `handleNewChannel` split into a `NewChannelForm` subcomponent.

**Connections had no way to find and request someone directly** — only sharing your own personal `/connect/[code]` link, plus automatic connection with coworkers sharing a workspace (both by design from the earlier Connections session). User asked for the missing other direction: search for a person and send them a request. Chose free-text name search over an "exact email only" alternative (both were offered — free text accepted, knowingly trading some privacy since any signed-in user can now find any other user by partial name, capped at 20 results).

Refactored first: the connect-link accept route's create-or-mutual-accept logic (idempotent if already connected, instant mutual-accept if they'd already requested you back, otherwise a normal pending request) moved into a shared `sendConnectionRequest(fromUserId, toUserId)` in `lib/auth/connections.ts`, so the new direct-request path doesn't duplicate it. New `GET /api/connections/search?q=` (name `contains` match, excludes self, capped at 20, annotates each result with `connected`/`requested-by-me`/`requested-by-them`/`none` so the UI can show the right state instead of a bare list) and `POST /api/connections/requests` (the search result's "Add" button hits this with `{ toUserId }`). New store actions `searchUsersToConnect`/`sendConnectionRequestTo` in `useChatStore.ts`; new `PeopleSearch` component in `DirectMessagesPage.tsx`, debounced 300ms, sitting above the existing connect-link section in the Connections tab.

Verified with a 14-check scripted pass (two users sharing no workspace, so not auto-connected as coworkers): search finds a partial-name match with the correct initial `none` status; a 1-character query returns nothing (below the server's own 2-char minimum) and a logged-out search returns an empty array; sending a request flips both sides' displayed status correctly (`requested-by-me`/`requested-by-them`); accepting flips both to `connected`; re-requesting an already-connected person is idempotent, not an error; requesting yourself is rejected; and the existing mutual-simultaneous-request auto-accept (both people request each other before either accepts) still works unchanged through the shared helper. 14/14 passed.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a fifteenth time) — Two "My tasks" regressions from the personal-workspace rework

User-reported: creating a new *List* under "My tasks" didn't show up without a manual page refresh (initially described as "tasks," corrected once actually reproduced in conversation — it's list creation, not task creation), and clicking the icon-rail "Tasks" tab stopped doing anything while inside "My tasks."

**Tasks tab click, straightforward**: its `onClick` was just `setActiveView('board')` — a no-op while already inside "My tasks" (`activeView` is already `'board'` there, just for the personal workspace), and nothing ever switched `activeWorkspaceId` back to a real team workspace. Fixed: clicking Tasks while `currentWorkspace?.isPersonal` now also switches to the first non-personal workspace first.

**New Lists needing a refresh — a real regression traced back to the "My tasks becomes a real workspace" rework itself (2026-08-17, earlier today)**: `ensurePersonalWorkspace`'s own comment said it "deliberately does NOT refetch/patch `workspaces` local state" because the personal workspace "is excluded from the switcher and never becomes 'the current workspace'" — true before that rework, false after it (the rework's whole point was making "My tasks" call `setActiveWorkspaceId` with it). On the first-ever "My tasks" click in a browser session, the workspace is created server-side by that call, but the client's already-loaded `workspaces` array (fetched once at page load, before this row existed) doesn't get it — so `setActiveWorkspaceId`'s own `workspaces.find(w => w.id === id)` silently misses, `firstSpaceId` falls back to `'everything'`, and `currentWorkspace`'s own fallback (`workspaces.find(w => w.id === activeWorkspaceId) ?? workspaces.find(w => !w.isPersonal)`) resolves to a *different, real* team workspace instead. This is exactly why *list* creation broke specifically: `createList` (and `createFolder`, same shape) update local state by finding the target Space inside the already-loaded `workspaces` tree and appending to it (`ws.spaces.map((s) => (s.id === spaceId ? { ...s, lists: [...s.lists, newList] } : s))`) — if that Space was never in local state to begin with, the `.map` silently no-ops even though the POST succeeded server-side, and it only appears after a refresh re-runs `fetchInitialData` and picks up the real thing. Tasks live in a separate flat `tasks` array unrelated to the `workspaces` tree, which is why task creation itself was never actually broken — a useful confirmation that the diagnosis was on the right track before the user corrected the specific symptom. Fixed at the root (same fix, no further change needed once precisely identified): `ensurePersonalWorkspace` now checks whether its own returned `workspaceId` is already in local `workspaces` state and calls the existing `refetchWorkspaces()` first if not, so `setActiveWorkspaceId` right after it always has correct data to find. The stale comment on `HierarchyWorkspace.isPersonal` making the same now-false claim was also corrected.

Not independently browser-verified this pass (no Playwright in this project, and the bug is inherently about first-load client cache state, awkward to script against a REST-only harness) — reasoned from tracing the exact code path rather than confirmed by clicking through it. Type-checks clean, dev log shows no errors from the real live browser session throughout. Flagged honestly to the user rather than claimed as fully verified.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a sixteenth time) — The real "new list doesn't show up" cause found: a pre-existing `select` bug, not the workspace-cache theory

User reported the list-invisible-until-refresh bug was still happening after both earlier fixes (the `fetchInitialData`/`ensurePersonalWorkspace` workspace-cache theory) *and* a full dev-server restart + hard browser refresh, which ruled out both the cache-staleness diagnosis and any hot-reload artifact from the day's very long dev session as the explanation.

**Actual root cause, found by re-reading `lib/folderTree.ts`'s `getListsIn` next to `POST /api/lists`'s response shape**: the sidebar tree filters visible lists with `l.archived === false` (default param). `POST /api/lists`'s Prisma `select` clause never included `archived` at all — so a freshly created list came back from the API with `archived: undefined`, which fails `undefined === false`, making the list silently invisible in the sidebar even though the row was created correctly server-side. A refresh "fixed" it because `GET /api/workspaces`'s own List `select` *does* include `archived`, so the next full fetch returned the field the creation route had been omitting all along. **Confirmed pre-existing, not a regression from today's chat/hardening/My-tasks work**: `git log`/`git show` on `app/api/lists/route.ts` traces this exact `select` clause back to the 2026-08-09 access-control session (which added `isPrivate`/`accessJson` to it but never added `archived`) — it just happened to surface today because that's when list creation inside "My tasks" specifically got exercised enough to notice. Not workspace-specific at all — this would have silently broken list creation the same way in *any* workspace, team or personal.

Fixed by adding `archived: true` (and `textColor: true`, also missing) to the select, matching `GET /api/workspaces`'s own List field set exactly — commented directly on the select clause that the two must stay in sync, since nothing else enforces it. Verified with a scripted check: `POST /api/lists`'s response now includes `"archived":false`, confirmed against the real running server after Turbopack's hot-reload picked up the route change (no restart needed for this one, unlike the schema-affecting fixes earlier today).

**Two real, correct, but non-explanatory fixes made earlier while chasing this down, left in place since both are genuine improvements**: `fetchInitialData` no longer excludes a personal workspace from being restored as `activeWorkspaceId` after a refresh (was incorrectly bouncing the user out of "My tasks" on reload), and `ensurePersonalWorkspace` now refetches `workspaces` local state if its own returned id isn't already cached (closes a real first-ever-visit race). Worth remembering: **when a client-side "doesn't update without a refresh" bug is reported, check whether the create/update API response is missing a field some *other* piece of code filters on, before reaching for cache-staleness or state-management theories** — the fields silently dropped by an incomplete `select` never show up as an error anywhere, they just make the affected row fail an unrelated-looking boolean check downstream.

## Today's session (2026-08-17, continued a seventeenth time) — Chat Phase 8: in-app unread badges (real push notifications deferred to post-HTTPS)

User asked about push notifications for chat. Real browser push needs a service worker + VAPID push subscriptions, and is flatly blocked without HTTPS (this app is still on plain HTTP via nip.io) — same blocker as the permanent-`AUTH_URL` deployment item. Agreed scope: build the in-app-only version now (Phase 8, already flagged optional in the roadmap), record the real-push want in "Next steps" for later.

Schema needed **zero migration** — `ChatChannelMember.lastReadMessageId`/`lastReadAt` were already added in Phase 1 specifically so this phase wouldn't need one. `GET /api/channels/[id]/messages` (shared plumbing for both channels and DMs) now marks-as-read after fetching: updates the caller's own membership row to `lastReadAt: now()`. New `lib/chatUnread.ts` (`withUnreadCounts`) computes a per-channel unread count — one `count()` query per channel, parallelized, excluding the caller's own authored messages (Slack/Discord convention) — applied to `GET /api/workspaces/[id]/channels` and `GET /api/dms`. One real correctness catch during implementation: used Prisma's top-level `NOT: { authorId: userId }` rather than the field-level `authorId: { not: userId }` — the latter silently excludes NULL rows in SQL's three-valued logic, which would have under-counted messages from since-hard-deleted authors (`authorId: null`, "history outlives the person").

Badges (small red count pill, reusing the exact style already used for the Connections incoming-request badge) added in four places: the nav-rail "Chat" icon, the Me-zone "Network"/Direct-Messages button, and per-row in both `ChatChannelSidebar.tsx` and `DirectMessagesSidebar.tsx` (the latter's "Chats" tab button too, matching "Connections" tab's existing badge). Since `channelsByWorkspace`/`dms` were previously only fetched once the user had already navigated into Chat/DMs — too late for a nav-rail badge to be useful — `PageContent` now fetches both proactively (on mount and on workspace switch) plus polls every 30s, the pragmatic no-new-infrastructure way to keep badges reasonably live without building a global "anything changed anywhere" broadcast room (today's real-time signal is per-channel, only for whatever's actively open in `ChatPanel`).

Verified with a scripted two-user pass: unread count accumulates correctly across multiple messages, resets to exactly 0 the moment the recipient opens the channel (not just visually — confirmed via a fresh `GET` after), and a message sent *after* that reopening correctly shows as 1 unread again rather than being lost or double-counted; a user's own sent messages never inflate their own unread count. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Visual badge placement/appearance not independently confirmed in a real browser (no Playwright in this project) — reasoned correct from the same store/component patterns already proven elsewhere this session, but flagged rather than claimed as seen.

**User request**: "RobUp" was always a placeholder name; rename it everywhere to **Siqt**.

Every literal `RobUp`/`robup` occurrence across the codebase (`app/`, `components/`, `lib/`, `prisma/`, `PLANNING.md`, `package.json`) replaced with `Siqt`/`siqt` — this doc's own title included, and the historical session entries above too (a pure proper-noun swap, not a rewrite of what actually happened at the time). Specific things worth calling out:
- **The browser tab title was never actually branded at all** — `app/layout.tsx`'s `metadata` was still the literal `create-next-app` scaffold default (`"Create Next App"` / `"Generated by create next app"`), a pre-existing gap this rebrand happened to catch and fix at the same time, not a regression.
- The single-letter "R" logo badge on the login/invite/connect pages (`app/login/page.tsx`, `app/invite/[code]/page.tsx`, `app/connect/[code]/page.tsx`) became "Q".
- The `.ics` calendar feed's `PRODID`, `X-WR-CALNAME`, download filename, and per-event `UID` domain suffix all updated (`app/api/calendar/[token]/route.ts`) — these are the only *user-facing-outside-the-app* strings (an imported calendar entry, a downloaded filename), worth double-checking if anyone already has an old feed subscribed somewhere.
- `package.json`'s `"name"` field changed to `"siqt"`; `package-lock.json` regenerated via a plain `npm install` (not hand-edited) so it stays internally consistent with npm's own expectations.

Not a bug, just an accepted consequence of a full rebrand — no migration written for these, matching the general "so far pre-launch, no real users on old data yet" posture this app is still in.

**Deliberately left untouched, out of scope unless asked separately**: the local folder path (`C:\dev\robup`) and the GitHub repo slug (`rhofs/robup`) — renaming either is a materially different, higher-blast-radius action (breaks the active working directory / requires updating the git remote and GitHub's own settings) than a text rebrand inside the app, and wasn't what was asked.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error; dev server restarted clean, `curl` against `/login` confirms both `<title>Siqt</title>` and the "Siqt" heading render correctly.

## Today's session (2026-08-18) — Planner/Calendar visual redesign (three feedback rounds), a real Fit-mode cropping bug, a double-border fix, and a week-numbers setting

User relayed three rounds of detailed "expert" visual feedback on the Planner/Calendar Month view (first a 10-point Norwegian list, then a precise English "visual refinement brief" with exact opacity/px targets, then a third round of smaller polish items), plus made several of their own explicit calls that overrode or refined the relayed feedback.

**Round 1** (general redesign direction): confirmed Space/List-based bar coloring already worked (`taskColorOf`/`eventColorOf` already cascade Space→Folder→List correctly — not rebuilt, just verified); made "Event" the default tab in the quick-create popover (`QuickCreatePopover.tsx`, was "Task") since the calendar context makes Event the more natural default; added a "MON 17"-style weekday header row, softened the grid, added card-style task bars with assignee avatars, a smaller "+ New task" button with an "N" keyboard shortcut, and a "Now" line refinement.

**Round 2** (precise brief, `lib/colorAlpha.ts`'s new `withAlpha()` helper): event/task bars became lighter — tinted background (18% alpha) + colored border (45% alpha) + full-opacity colored text instead of a solid fill, with a hover state bumping both up (32%/70%) via local `useState` on newly-extracted `TaskBar`/`EventBar` components (inline `.map()` callbacks can't use hooks). Bar height 24px, 4px gaps, softened grid lines (very faint vertical separators, slightly stronger horizontal week-boundaries), compact date headers, refined Today indicator (smaller, less saturated blue). Added **"Fit" mode** (`CalendarView.tsx`, default on for Month view): forces `rowHeight = containerHeight / weeks.length` so all 6 weeks fit with zero scrolling, deriving `maxVisibleLanes` from that height (lane *assignment* stays unbounded — only the display-time visible-lane cap changes) so events never shrink, just collapse into the existing "+N more" overflow.

**Round 3** (polish): Space-level calendar filter switched from a checkbox to the same dot+eye-hover pattern already used at List/Folder level (`app/page.tsx`, `FolderTree.tsx`) — an eye icon reads as "show/hide," a checkbox reads as "select/complete." Breadcrumb header's literal "Workspace" replaced with the current view's own name (Planner/Docs/Tasks/etc. — the real workspace name already shows top-left), per the user's own explicit instruction overriding the relayed feedback's narrower "say 'Multiple spaces' when several are checked" suggestion. Event-bar left padding bumped, stray horizontal scrollbar hidden (`overflow-x-hidden`), sidebar list-icon vertical alignment fixed (fixed-size centered slot), and Lists/Docs sitting directly under a Space gained a small indent so the Space boundary reads clearly — in both Planner and Tasks sidebars (`FolderTree.tsx` is shared between them, so one fix covered both, per the user's explicit scoping).

**Real bug, caught only via a user screenshot after all of the above shipped**: the Fit-mode calendar was visibly cropped — only ~4 of 6 week rows fit on screen, no scrollbar, no way to see the rest. Root cause: the calendar's wrapper in `app/page.tsx` was hardcoded `<div className="h-[75vh]">` — 75% of raw *viewport* height, with no relation to the actual remaining space below the header/breadcrumb inside a padded, independently-scrollable content pane. Fit mode's `containerHeight` measurement was therefore measuring the wrong box, computing 6 row-heights that summed taller than what was actually visible, and the outer pane's own `overflow-auto` (rather than the calendar's intended zero-scroll Fit behavior) silently absorbed the mismatch — which read as the bottom two rows just vanishing. Fixed by making the calendar's container a real `flex-1 min-h-0` all the way up its actual parent chain (the content pane itself becomes `flex flex-col` + `overflow-hidden` specifically when `activeView === 'calendar'`, everything else unchanged) so Fit mode measures the true available space. Scoped to Calendar only — Chat's own identical `h-[75vh]` pattern (line ~3764) was deliberately left alone, not requested and not broken (Chat is fine scrolling).

**Second real bug, same screenshot-driven-feedback pattern**: a visible "double line" top and bottom of the grid — the weekday header row drew its own `border-b` directly above the grid container's own top border (~4-6px apart, read as one thick doubled line), and the last week row's day cells drew their own `border-b` directly against the container's own bottom border. Fixed: removed the header row's own bottom border (container's top border is now the sole divider); added an `isLastRow` prop threaded from `CalendarView.tsx` into `WeekRow.tsx` so the very last row's cells and week-number gutter skip their own bottom border, leaving the container's own border as the sole line there too.

**New: a "Show week numbers" setting** (`hva er det neste`-adjacent request — "settings som skrur av uketall, for de som ikke ønsker det"). Same localStorage-backed, "only persist the non-default minority" pattern as the existing hidden-nav-tabs toggle (`SettingsPanel.tsx`'s `readHideWeekNumbers`/`setHideWeekNumbers`, key `siqt.hideWeekNumbers`), surfaced as a checkbox in Settings → General → new "Planner" section. Threaded as a `showWeekNumbers` prop `page.tsx` → `CalendarView.tsx` → `WeekRow.tsx` (page.tsx holds the state, refreshed via `SettingsPanel`'s existing `onChange` callback, same as `hiddenNavTabs` — `CalendarView` itself doesn't read localStorage directly since it stays mounted under the Settings modal and wouldn't otherwise notice a change). The week-number gutter column keeps its width when hidden — only the number text disappears — so no pointer-math (`GUTTER_WIDTH`-dependent drag/drop calculations) needed touching.

**Third real bug, user-reported through direct use rather than a screenshot this time**: stretching a task's *end* edge on a task that only had a `dueDate` (no `startDate` — the common "1-day task" shape, e.g. "Ulrik sjekker 360 kameraer...") relocated the whole task to the target day instead of spanning to it (Norwegian: "istedet for å gå over 2 dager, flyttes den til dagen jeg strekker den til"). Confirmed directly against the dev DB (`startDate: null`, `dueDate` set) before touching code, rather than assumed. Root cause in `CalendarView.tsx`'s `handleDragEnd`: the `resize-start` branch already correctly handles its mirror case (task has only a due date, no start — anchors a new start off the due date, leaves the existing due untouched), but the `resize-end` branch's equivalent case (task has only a due date, no start) never created a start anchor — `newStart` stayed `null`, and since `ranges`' construction falls back to using the due date for both start *and* end whenever `startDate` is missing, moving the due date alone made the single-day task appear to just relocate. Fixed by anchoring `newStart` to the task's original `due` position at the top of the `resize-end` branch when `start` was null, mirroring what `resize-start` already did. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; not yet re-confirmed by the user in the browser.

**Fourth real bug, same session: Day view misreading imported all-day tasks as timed at ~02:00** (Norwegian: "der står nå Ulrik kun på kl. 0200 typ, selv om han varer hele dagen"). Root cause traced to a real timezone-convention mismatch between two independent parts of this app, confirmed against the dev DB before touching code: this app's own `DatePickerPopover` always stores "no time set" as **local** midnight (`.setHours(0,0,0,0)` then `.toISOString()`), but the ClickUp CSV importer's `parseEpochMs` (`app/api/workspaces/[id]/import/clickup/route.ts`) passed ClickUp's raw epoch-ms straight through `new Date(n)` — and ClickUp represents its own "no time set" dates as **UTC** midnight regardless of workspace timezone. In Oslo (UTC+2 during DST, confirmed via `Intl.DateTimeFormat().resolvedOptions().timeZone`), UTC midnight reads as local `02:00` — so `DayTimeline.tsx`'s `hasTimeOfDay` check (which only ever looked at *local* hours/minutes, matching `DatePickerPopover`'s own convention) misclassified every ClickUp-imported all-day task as "timed," landing it in the hourly grid at ~02:00 instead of the "All day" strip at the top. Month/Week view were never affected — they only ever bucket by local Y/M/D (via `startOfDay`), never by hour, so the same stray hour component is invisible there. Two-part fix: (1) `DayTimeline.tsx`'s `hasTimeOfDay` now treats a date as all-day if it's midnight in *either* convention (local or UTC) — safe, since a genuinely-timed task essentially never lands exactly on UTC midnight by coincidence; (2) `parseEpochMs` now re-anchors a date that lands exactly on UTC midnight to local midnight of that same UTC calendar day, so **future** ClickUp imports match this app's own native convention instead of merely being tolerated by a defensive check. Already-imported rows (most of the "ferie"/vacation entries visible in the Planner, likely) were deliberately **not** bulk-migrated in the DB — the display-side fix already covers them; a one-time backfill script is possible later if wanted, flagged rather than run unasked since it's a real write across existing task data.

**Same request, second half — Day view's task/event blocks now visually match Month/Week's own bar style**, rather than the old solid-fill-with-white-text look: `DayTimeline.tsx`'s all-day chips and its hourly-grid task/event blocks now use the exact same tinted-background + colored-border + colored-text treatment as `WeekRow.tsx`'s `TaskBar`/`EventBar` (including the hover-saturation bump) — `BASE_BG_ALPHA`/`BASE_BORDER_ALPHA`/`HOVER_BG_ALPHA`/`HOVER_BORDER_ALPHA` were exported from `WeekRow.tsx` as the single source of truth so both views can never visually drift apart again. Assignee avatar chips' border/badge colors were also brought in line with Month view's own convention (`border-neutral-900/60`, `bg-neutral-700` for the "+N" badge, was `border-white/40`/`bg-black/30`). Inline `.map()` callbacks were extracted into three new local components (`AllDayChip`, `DayEventBlock`, `DayTaskBlock`) since the hover state needs a real `useState` hook.

**A real transient error worth remembering, not a lingering bug**: mid-edit, the dev log showed `Uncaught ReferenceError: AllDayChip is not defined` from a live browser tab — Turbopack hot-compiled an intermediate state of `DayTimeline.tsx` (the component *used* before its definition further down the file had been added yet) and served it to whatever tab was already open on Day view. Resolved by the time the edit was complete; `npx tsc --noEmit` was clean on the finished file and the dev log showed no further errors afterward. Worth remembering: a real open browser tab against a `dev` server will observe genuinely-broken intermediate states while multi-step edits are in flight, not just the final result — don't mistake a mid-edit log error for a persisting bug without checking whether it predates the last edit.

**Verification, same limitation as ever**: no browser/Playwright tool available this whole session — every change verified only via `npx tsc --noEmit` (clean throughout, only the pre-existing unrelated `prisma.config.ts` error) and dev-log inspection. Two of this session's real bugs (cropping, double border) were only caught because the user sent screenshots and pointed at the specific problem; the resize-end bug and the Day-view timezone bug were both caught by direct DB inspection once reported, not by screenshot. Not yet confirmed by the user in a real browser: the cropping/double-border fixes, the week-numbers toggle, the resize fix, and today's Day view changes (all-day tasks landing in the right section, and the bars now visually matching Month/Week).

**Rebrand #2, same day: Qvip → Siqt.** User changed their mind on the name again ("ombestemt meg, den skal hete 'Siqt'"). Same treatment as the RobUp→Qvip rebrand: every literal `Qvip`/`qvip` occurrence across the codebase (`app/`, `components/`, `lib/`, `prisma/`, `PLANNING.md`, `package.json`) replaced with `Siqt`/`siqt`, `package-lock.json` regenerated via `npm install` rather than hand-edited, the single-letter logo badge on login/invite/connect updated (`Q` → `S`), the three `qvip.*` `localStorage` key prefixes (`hiddenNavTabs`, `hideWeekNumbers`, plus the pre-existing `columnWidths`/`showActivityPanel`/`collapsedSpaces`/`collapsedFolders`/`savedColors`) renamed to `siqt.*` — same accepted consequence as last time: anyone with existing browser data under the old keys gets those specific preferences silently reset to default on next load, still fine pre-launch with no real users on old data. The main app's top-left workspace-switcher "R" avatar badge was checked and confirmed unrelated to app branding (no hardcoded "Q"/logo letter found there) — correctly left untouched, matching the same scoping the first rebrand used. Local folder path (`C:\dev\robup`) and GitHub repo slug (`rhofs/robup`) deliberately left alone again, same reasoning as before (out of scope, higher blast radius than a text rebrand). Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (same pre-existing `prisma.config.ts` error only), zero remaining `Qvip`/`qvip` occurrences confirmed via a full-codebase grep, dev server restarted clean after a `.next` cache clear, and `curl http://localhost:3000/login` confirms both `<title>Siqt</title>` and the "Siqt" heading render.

**Chat message text couldn't be selected/copied at all** (Norwegian: "Det er ikke mulig å markere teksten i chatten, det vil jeg jo gjerne kunne"). Confirmed via the user's own answer that it wasn't a partial/flaky issue — nothing highlighted at all when click-dragging over message text. Root cause: `app/page.tsx`'s app-wide root container (`<div className="... select-none">`, line ~2537) sets `user-select: none` — a CSS property that **inherits** to every descendant by default — and nothing downstream in the message-rendering path (`ChatPanel.tsx`, `ChatThreadPanel.tsx`) ever opted back in with `select-text`. The composer `<textarea>` was never affected (native form controls are exempt from inherited `user-select: none` in every browser), which is why typing/selecting your *own* draft always worked fine and made this easy to miss — only *received* message text (plain, non-editable `<div>`s) was actually blocked. Fixed by adding `select-text` to the three message-body divs that render `renderChatMessageBody(...)` output: the two in `ChatPanel.tsx` (first-message-of-run and subsequent runs) and the two in `ChatThreadPanel.tsx` (thread root + replies). Left a comment at the root `select-none` explaining the inheritance gotcha and pointing at this fix as the pattern to follow, since the same root cause almost certainly also affects task comments and Doc comments (`DocCommentsPanel.tsx` — checked, also has no `select-text` anywhere) — not fixed there since it wasn't what was asked, but worth revisiting if the user wants copyable text elsewhere too. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; dev log clean after hot reload; not yet confirmed by the user in a real browser.

## Today's session (2026-08-18, continued) — Deployment: siqt.no purchased, production build was actually broken, now fixed and verified end-to-end locally

User bought the `siqt.no` domain and asked to actually try going live. Confirmed current infra state directly rather than assuming: the Pebblehost VPS + Pterodactyl panel are already provisioned and accessible; DNS for `siqt.no` is **not** pointed anywhere yet; the only existing Pterodactyl egg on hand is a "custom Python generic" one built for a different app ("AuTONO") — not a Node.js egg, so almost certainly the wrong Docker image/runtime for this app as-is.

**Real, previously-undiscovered blocker found by actually running `npm run build`** (never run this whole session — only `next dev` and `tsc --noEmit`): production build failed outright on the exact `prisma.config.ts` type error that had been dismissed all session as a harmless pre-existing `tsc --noEmit`-only issue. `next build` runs its own stricter TypeScript pass and does not tolerate it — this would have hard-blocked every deploy attempt. Fixed with a one-line fallback (`process.env["DATABASE_URL"] ?? "file:./dev.db"`, matching `schema.prisma`'s own hardcoded datasource url exactly, which is what the generated client actually uses at runtime regardless of this config file). `npm run build` now succeeds cleanly, and `npx tsc --noEmit` is now genuinely 100% clean for the first time all session (previously always had this one residual error).

**A second real gap, caught before it could bite in production**: `package.json` had `"start": "next start"` (Next.js only) with no script running the Hocuspocus collab sidecar in production at all, and — worse — `concurrently` and `tsx`, both genuinely required at runtime for a combined production start, were sitting in `devDependencies`, which a production-mode `npm install`/`npm ci` typically skips entirely. Fixed: added `"start:prod": "concurrently -n next,collab -c blue,magenta \"npm:start\" \"npm:collab:start\""` (mirrors the existing `dev` script's shape exactly, reusing the pre-existing `collab:start` script rather than duplicating it), and moved `concurrently`/`tsx` into real `dependencies`. `package-lock.json` regenerated via `npm install`, not hand-edited.

**Verified the entire production path end-to-end locally, not just "should work"**: stopped the dev server to free the ports, ran the freshly-built app via `npm run start:prod`, confirmed both processes came up clean (`next start` on :3000, Hocuspocus on :1234, no errors), then `curl`'d `/login` and got a real `200` with `<title>Siqt</title>` rendering — i.e. the actual command a Pterodactyl "Startup Command" would run was proven to work, not assumed. Stopped it and restarted the normal dev server afterward.

**Deployment checklist, split by who can act on it** (nothing below this point has been done yet — infra actions need the user, since this assistant has no Pterodactyl/DNS/registrar access):
- User: point `siqt.no` (and `www`) DNS A-record(s) at the VPS's allocated IP (found in the Pterodactyl panel).
- User: since the only existing egg is Python-based, either (a) check whether that egg's Docker image is swappable per-server in Pterodactyl, or (b) import/create a proper Node.js egg — a fixed Python runtime container can't run this Next.js app as-is.
- Once a Node-capable egg/server exists, the **install command** should run `npm install && npm run build`, and the **startup command** should be `npm run start:prod` (now verified working locally).
- User: expose a *second* port allocation in Pterodactyl for the Hocuspocus sidecar (1234) alongside the main app port (3000) — collab/real-time chat needs both reachable.
- User: set real production environment variables on the server — `AUTH_URL=https://siqt.no` (removes the nip.io/LAN workaround entirely), `AUTH_SECRET`, `CHAT_BROADCAST_SECRET`, and `GOOGLE_CLIENT_ID`/`GOOGLE_CLIENT_SECRET` if Google sign-in should work in production (still unset even locally as of this writing).
- User: HTTPS — Pterodactyl doesn't terminate TLS for a generic app egg on its own; simplest path is Cloudflare in front of `siqt.no` (free, proxied/orange-cloud mode terminates TLS at Cloudflare's edge) rather than standing up a separate reverse proxy — real push notifications for Chat and Google sign-in both require this.
- Open question, not yet raised with the user: whether the SQLite file living at the literal path `dev.db` (hardcoded in `schema.prisma`, not env-configurable today) is fine to use as-is for production data as long as it sits on a persistent volume in Pterodactyl, or whether it's worth renaming/relocating for clarity before real user data accumulates in a file named "dev.db" — flagged, not decided.

**The `dev.db` question, resolved**: user asked for a naming recommendation, had no opinion of their own. Recommended `siqt.db` (matches the app name, environment-agnostic, doesn't misleadingly imply throwaway data once real users are on it) — but the actual fix needed is making the datasource path *configurable per environment* in the first place, not just picking a new hardcoded string, since the same literal `file:./dev.db` was used everywhere regardless of dev/prod. `prisma/schema.prisma`'s `datasource db { url = ... }` changed from the hardcoded string to `env("DATABASE_URL")`; `.env.local` gained `DATABASE_URL="file:./dev.db"` (preserves the exact current local file/data, zero risk — this was never going to touch the actual production database anyway, since a fresh deploy starts with a fresh DB regardless of what the dev file is named). `prisma.config.ts`'s own comment (written earlier this session, now stale since schema.prisma no longer ignores `DATABASE_URL`) corrected to match. Production should set its own `DATABASE_URL` (e.g. `file:./siqt.db`, or a path under a Pterodactyl persistent volume) as a real env var — no code change needed there, just server config.

**Real deployment progress**: `siqt.no` purchased; user confirmed the Pebblehost VPS + Pterodactyl are already accessible, the server's public IP is `213.170.135.134`, and shared a screenshot of the node's existing port allocations (a mix of game servers plus one existing "custom generic" egg for another app of theirs, "AuTONO," on port 8501/25575) — confirmed ports **3000** and **1234** are both free and unused on that IP, so the plan is to request those two specific ports rather than dealing with Pterodactyl's dynamic-port-templating mechanism at all.

**A second real bug caught before it could bite in production, found by actually re-testing rather than assuming the earlier fix was complete**: `start:prod`'s collab half still called the *dev* `collab:start` script, which uses `tsx --env-file=.env.local` — Node's `--env-file` flag hard-errors if the file doesn't exist, and there will be no `.env.local` file on the Pterodactyl server at all (real env vars get injected directly into the container's environment there, not via a dotenv file). Added a separate `collab:start:prod` (`tsx server/collabServer.ts`, no `--env-file`) and pointed `start:prod` at it instead. **Verified for real, not assumed**: stopped the dev server, exported `.env.local`'s values into the actual shell (`set -a; source .env.local; set +a` — simulating exactly how Pterodactyl injects real env vars with no dotenv file present), ran `npm run start:prod` cold, and confirmed both processes started clean and `/login` served a real `200` with the correct title. (Hit an unrelated cosmetic `JWTSessionError` from the browser's already-open dev-session cookie being sent to the fresh prod process — expected noise from testing against a live browser tab mid-session, not a config problem; the page itself rendered correctly regardless.) `npx tsc --noEmit` fully clean.

**Egg configuration handed to the user** (Docker image, install script, startup command, environment variables, port allocations) — see the chat transcript for the full writeup rather than duplicating it here verbatim. Confirmed `github.com/rhofs/robup` is public (unauthenticated `GET api.github.com/repos/rhofs/robup` returned `200`, `"private": false`) — no token needed for cloning.

**Two real, wrong recommendations caught before the user acted on them, by actually verifying against the live repos instead of trusting recalled package names**: `ghcr.io/pterodactyl/yolks:nodejs_22` doesn't exist (yolks only publishes up to `nodejs_20`, confirmed via `WebFetch` against the actual `pterodactyl/yolks` repo listing); `ghcr.io/parkervcp/installers:debian` (the install-image path originally given) 404s — that project's installer images moved to `ghcr.io/pelican-eggs/installers:debian`, confirmed via the actual CI workflow file that publishes them, not just a plausible-sounding org name. Both corrected before the user typed anything based on the wrong values into the panel.

**Live egg setup, walked through interactively over several screenshots — two more real deploy bugs found this way, neither guessable from reading code alone**:
1. Install script's `Script Container` field defaulted to Pterodactyl's own placeholder (`alpine:3.4`/`ash`) rather than picking up what was typed into the script body — caught from the screenshot before Save, corrected to the intended installer image/`bash` entrypoint.
2. First real install attempt: `sh: concurrently: not found` at startup despite `package.json` clearly being read correctly (npm echoed the resolved script) — traced to `ghcr.io/pelican-eggs/installers:debian` being a **bare** Debian image (git/bash/curl only, no Node.js at all) once the user confirmed via the Files tab that `node_modules`/`.next` never got created even though source files (`package.json`, `prisma/`, `app/`) were present. Fixed by adding an explicit NodeSource install (`curl ... | bash -`, `apt-get install -y nodejs`) at the top of the install script.
3. Second real install attempt (after the Node.js fix): install completed for real this time (`npm ci` actually ran), but the **runtime** crashed with two platform-mismatch errors simultaneously — `PrismaClientInitializationError` ("generated for debian-openssl-3.0.x, but the actual deployment required linux-musl-openssl-3.0.x") and Next.js's SWC compiler failing to load both its gnu and musl native variants, ending in "Could not find a production build in the '.next' directory." Root cause, diagnosed from the two errors together rather than chased separately: the install container (Debian/glibc) and the actual runtime container (`ghcr.io/pterodactyl/yolks:nodejs_20`, confirmed Alpine/musl-based by the swc error's own fallback-attempt order) are different C-library platforms, so every platform-specific native binary resolved at install time (Prisma's query engine, Next's swc) was built for the wrong target and couldn't load at runtime — npm's optional-dependency platform resolution and Prisma's engine download both happen based on *whichever platform is running the install step*, not the eventual runtime. Fixed in two complementary ways: (a) `prisma/schema.prisma`'s `generator client` block gained `binaryTargets = ["native", "linux-musl-openssl-3.0.x"]` (matches Prisma's own suggested fix exactly, and is a real robustness improvement regardless of which install image is ever used again — verified locally: `npx prisma generate` now bundles `libquery_engine-linux-musl-openssl-3.0.x.so.node` alongside the Windows one); (b) told the user to switch the install container to the *same* image as the runtime (`ghcr.io/pterodactyl/yolks:nodejs_20`) instead of a separate Debian installer, with `apk add --no-cache git` added to the script (Alpine's package manager, not `apt-get`) and the entrypoint switched from `bash` to `sh` (the install script itself is plain POSIX `[ -d ... ]`/`if`/`else`, no bashisms, so this was always safe) — eliminates the platform mismatch at the source rather than only patching around Prisma's half of it.

4. That fix (switching the *install* container to `ghcr.io/pterodactyl/yolks:nodejs_20`) turned out to be wrong in a different way — **the install process stopped executing at all**: no console output whatsoever (not even Wings' own "Server marked as..." lines, which normally appear regardless of script content), and disk usage / file timestamps stayed completely unchanged across multiple Reinstall attempts. Ruled out one hypothesis by testing it directly rather than assuming: deleted the server entirely and created a fresh one from the same (corrected) egg, on the theory that the existing server had some stale cached link to an older egg config — the new server showed the exact same symptom (zero files beyond a `.npm` directory Node itself leaves behind, zero console activity), ruling out per-server staleness as the cause. Concluded the yolks *runtime* image likely isn't reliable when used as Wings' *install* container specifically (plausible: it's built to be the long-running app container, not necessarily to satisfy whatever Wings' install-container contract expects) — unlike `ghcr.io/pelican-eggs/installers:debian`, which is purpose-built for exactly this role and had already proven itself working (produced real output and real files in attempt 2 above). Also tried, once, adding `exec > /mnt/server/install.log 2>&1` + `set -x` to the script to capture a persistent transcript regardless of console reliability — moot once the real cause (wrong image entirely) was found, but the technique (redirect all install-script output to a file on the persistent volume, since Wings' live console proved unreliable to capture/reconnect to more than once in this session) is worth reusing if a similarly silent failure shows up again.

**Switching the install container to `ghcr.io/pelican-eggs/installers:alpine`** (same purpose-built installer family as the Debian one already proven to execute reliably, Alpine-based to match the runtime's musl platform, `apk add --no-cache git nodejs npm`) turned out to be necessary but not sufficient — it got past the platform-mismatch errors, but exposed several more real bugs before the app actually came up, each found via an actual failing log rather than guessed:

- **A whole server instance got stuck in Pterodactyl's own "Installing" status for ~24 hours** with zero disk usage and zero console output — not a script bug, a panel-level stuck state (Console/Settings both blocked by a persistent "Running Installer" splash that never resolved). Fixed by an admin-level "toggle install status" action on the server (found under Admin → Servers → the server's own management page, not the normal user-facing Settings tab) — plain Reinstall clicks did nothing while stuck, confirming this was a panel/Wings-level desync, not the script hanging.
- **`git config`'s "detected dubious ownership" safety check silently broke every git sync** (`git fetch`/`git reset --hard`/`git log` all failed with this error, install script continued past them anyway since there's no `set -e`) — meant the "latest code" being deployed was actually whatever stale checkout already existed, invisible until package counts (279 vs the expected 579) gave it away. Fixed with `git config --global --add safe.directory /mnt/server` at the very top of the install script, before any git command.
- **Setting `NODE_ENV=production` as a deploy env var (done for correctness) broke the build**: `npm ci` respects `NODE_ENV=production` and skips `devDependencies` entirely, but `next build` genuinely needs several of them at build time in this deploy-by-building-on-the-server workflow — `@tailwindcss/postcss` specifically (`Cannot find module '@tailwindcss/postcss'`), and by the same logic `tailwindcss`/`typescript`/`@types/*` were equally at risk. Fixed in code: moved the entire `devDependencies` block into real `dependencies` in `package.json` (committed, not a per-deploy workaround) — same root-cause class as the earlier `concurrently`/`tsx` fix, just a second wave of packages that turned out to be build-time-required rather than truly dev-only.
- **New servers created from this egg didn't reliably inherit the egg's current Startup Command or Variables** — seen twice, on two separately-created servers. Root cause never fully pinned down (a panel-level quirk, not something fixable from the app side); the reliable workaround was editing the *server's own* Startup Command directly (Admin → Servers → the server → Startup Command Modification — a real, editable override field distinct from the read-only "Default Service Start Command" reference below it) and manually re-adding the six Variables at the egg level (they had silently never saved the first time — confirmed by checking the egg's own Variables tab and finding it genuinely empty, not a display bug).
- **`prisma db push` failing during install with "Environment variable not found: DATABASE_URL"** despite the variable being correctly defined at the egg level — Pterodactyl doesn't appear to pass a server's configured environment variables into the *install* container, only the *runtime* one (`prisma generate`, run just before, succeeded without complaint, consistent with generate not needing to resolve the actual URL value the way `db push` does). Fixed by moving `db push` out of the install script entirely into a new `deploy:prod` npm script (`npx prisma db push && npm run start:prod`) that runs at actual container startup instead, where the env vars are confirmed present — same fix pattern as the earlier `collab:start:prod`/`--env-file` split.
- **A genuinely separate, parallel fix landed on `origin/main` from another machine/session mid-arc**: `prisma.config.ts`'s bare `dotenv/config` import only ever auto-loads a file literally named `.env`, never this project's actual `.env.local` — meaning a bare Prisma CLI command (`db push`/`generate`/`studio`) on a *fresh clone* would hit the same "DATABASE_URL not found" class of error locally too, independent of the Pterodactyl-specific install/runtime env-var gap above. Merged in cleanly (`git pull`, no conflicts) rather than overwritten.

**Result, confirmed by actually loading the page, not just a clean install log**: the app is live and serving real pages at `http://213.170.135.134:3000/login` — full Siqt login page (logo, Sign in/Create account tabs, Google button, email/password form) rendering correctly, screenshotted and confirmed by the user. `siqt.no` itself still 404s/refuses (no DNS yet) — hitting the bare domain in a browser correctly redirects to `AUTH_URL`'s configured value (`https://siqt.no/login?...`) even when the request came in via the raw IP, which is expected NextAuth behavior (it builds absolute URLs from the configured `AUTH_URL`, not the request's actual host) and was itself a useful signal that the app *was* responding correctly, not a new bug.

## Today's session (2026-08-18, continued) — Resuming on this machine: `DATABASE_URL`/`prisma.config.ts` gap found and fixed

Pulled the deployment session's work (rebrand to Siqt, Planner/Calendar redesign, `schema.prisma`'s datasource moving to `env("DATABASE_URL")`, the deployment-in-progress state — see the checklist above). This machine's own `.env.local` predated that change and had no `DATABASE_URL`, so `next dev`/the collab sidecar still worked fine (both load `.env.local` directly, unaffected), but the bare Prisma CLI (`npx prisma db push`) failed outright: `Environment variable not found: DATABASE_URL`.

**Root cause, a real gap not specific to this machine**: `prisma.config.ts`'s `import "dotenv/config"` only auto-loads a file literally named `.env` — this project has never had one, only `.env.local` (Next.js's own convention, loaded automatically by `next dev` and explicitly via `tsx --env-file=.env.local` for the sidecar). The CLI's own schema validation reads `process.env.DATABASE_URL` directly regardless of `prisma.config.ts`'s separate `datasource.url` fallback, so nothing there rescued it. Fixed by pointing the config's `dotenv` import at `.env.local` explicitly (`config({ path: ".env.local" })`) — a real, reusable fix, not a workaround specific to this machine; would have hit the exact same error on any other fresh clone running a plain `prisma` CLI command. Added this machine's own `DATABASE_URL="file:./dev.db"` to its (gitignored, per-machine) `.env.local`, ran `npm install` (picks up `concurrently`/`tsx` moving to real `dependencies`), `prisma db push` (schema already in sync, only the client needed regenerating for the new `binaryTargets`), and a full dev-server restart. Verified: `npx tsc --noEmit` fully clean (zero output, matching the deployment session's own milestone), `<title>Siqt</title>` renders correctly.

## Today's session (2026-08-21) — Backup layer 1: rotating on-disk SQLite snapshots (backlog #10)

Started on the 2026-08-19 backlog, picking backup first per the user's explicit request — sequenced ahead of the other 15 items specifically because production is about to hold real data and a `git reset --hard` Reinstall was already flagged as a workflow the DB file needs to survive.

**Built**: `scripts/backupDb.ts`, run via a new `npm run backup:db` (local, loads `.env.local` — mirrors the existing `collab:start`/`collab:start:prod` split) / `npm run backup:db:prod` (production — env vars already live in the container, no `--env-file` needed, would otherwise hard-fail like `dev:collab` did on a missing `.env.local` historically). Uses SQLite's own `VACUUM INTO '<path>'` (via `prisma.$executeRawUnsafe`) rather than a plain file copy — a raw copy can land mid-write and produce a torn/corrupt snapshot even under normal operation; `VACUUM INTO` is SQLite's documented safe way to snapshot a live, in-use database. Writes timestamped files (`backups/siqt-backup-<ISO timestamp>.db`) and prunes down to the newest `BACKUP_RETENTION_COUNT` (env-overridable, default 14) on every run — verified locally: ran it 3x with retention forced to 2, confirmed exactly the 2 newest files survived each time and the correct oldest one was named in the "Pruned" log line each round. Also confirmed the written file is a real, valid SQLite database (header bytes `SQLite format 3`, opens fine), not just "a file got created." `backups/` is gitignored (machine-local data, same treatment as `dev.db` itself).

**What this does and doesn't cover — important, read before assuming backups are "done":**
- **Covers**: a bad migration, an accidental delete, app-level data corruption — any of these can be recovered by restoring the most recent `.db` snapshot, since each one is a separate consistent file.
- **Does NOT cover**: losing the whole VPS/disk/volume, since today these snapshots sit right next to the live database on the exact same persistent volume. A destroyed server destroys the live db and every one of these snapshots identically. This is explicitly **layer 1 only**.

**Not yet done, panel-side (needs the user, no code left to write for this part):**
1. **Actually schedule it.** Nothing runs this automatically yet. Pterodactyl has a **Schedules** tab per-server (separate from its Backups feature) that can run an arbitrary command inside the running container on a cron-like interval — set one up to run `npm run backup:db:prod` daily (or more often once real usage starts).
2. **Get a copy off the box** — the real gap layer 1 doesn't close. Two options, either is fine, doing both is better:
   - Check whether the server's own **Backups** tab in Pterodactyl is enabled/configured for this instance — Pterodactyl's native backup system snapshots the whole server volume on its own schedule and, depending on how Pebblehost has the node configured, can store it off the VPS (S3-compatible remote) rather than only locally. Worth just looking at what's actually available/already configured before building anything custom.
   - Alternative/addition: periodically pull the newest file in `backups/` off the server entirely (SFTP/manual download, or a small remote-upload step added to the script later) — flagged here rather than built now since it needs a decision on where "off-box" actually means (own PC, cloud storage, etc.), a real user choice, not an engineering default to just pick.

**Verified**: ran `npm run backup:db` locally end-to-end, confirmed valid SQLite output and correct retention/pruning behavior (see above). **Not yet verified in production** — next step once this is pushed is running `npm run backup:db:prod` once by hand on the actual server (via Pterodactyl's console) to confirm it behaves the same way against the real `DATABASE_URL`/persistent volume, before relying on a schedule to run it unattended.
