## Today's session (2026-08-21, continued a third time) — Task/Event Planner parity: backlog #12, #14, #15

User picked this bundle next off the 2026-08-19 backlog. Ended up touching schema, five API routes, three calendar components, and the store — the largest of today's three chunks.

**Schema (`prisma/schema.prisma`)**: `Comment.taskId` is now optional (`String?`/`Task?`, was required), and `Comment.eventId` (`String?`/`Event?`, `onDelete: Cascade`) was added alongside it — exactly one of the two is ever set in practice, enforced in the API routes rather than a DB-level `CHECK` (this schema has no migration history to add one via raw SQL, only `prisma db push`). `Event.comments Comment[]` back-relation added. Pushed clean, no backfill needed (existing Task comments keep their non-null `taskId`, nothing about them changes).

**New: `ensureEventAccess` moved from being defined inline in `app/api/events/[id]/route.ts` into the shared `lib/auth/resourceAccess.ts`** (same file as `ensureTaskAccess` etc.), so the new Event comments routes below could reuse it instead of a third copy.

**New API routes** (mirror `app/api/tasks/[id]/comments/(route|[commentId]/route).ts` almost exactly): `GET`/`POST /api/events/[id]/comments`, `DELETE /api/events/[id]/comments/[commentId]`.

**`PATCH /api/events/[id]/route.ts` gained real activity logging** — diffs old vs. new (title, startDate/endDate, assigneeIds) and writes `Comment`s with `type: 'activity'`, same shape as Task's own route. **This was previously missing entirely for Event** (the whole point of #12), and checking the equivalent Task code path along the way turned up a real, separate finding: **`PATCH /api/tasks/[id]/route.ts` never logged a date-change activity either** — dragging/resizing a Task's own Planner bar has apparently never logged to Activity & Comments, contrary to what the user (reasonably) assumed was already true for Task and only missing for Event. Fixed both routes with the same "Dato endret til <date>" entry (new `datesChanged` activityKind, icon added to `ACTIVITY_ICONS` in `app/page.tsx`), not just Event's.

**Real security finding, fixed in passing**: `POST /api/tasks/[id]/comments` (and `PATCH /api/tasks/[id]`'s various `activities.push(...)` calls) set `authorId: body.authorId` straight from the client request body — the exact same spoofable-identity class of bug as the Google Doc export bug fixed earlier this project (any direct/malformed API call, not the normal UI, could attribute a comment to any user id it liked). The route already calls `getCurrentUserId()` for its own auth check, so the fix was using that instead: `authorId: userId`. Applied to the Task comments POST route and used from the start in the new Event one. ~~**Not fixed**: the same `body.authorId ?? null` pattern still exists in `app/api/tasks/route.ts`, `app/api/tasks/[id]/docs/route.ts`, and `app/api/docs/[id]/route.ts`~~ — closed in the dedicated security pass later this same day (2026-08-21, continued a fifth time), which also turned up 3 more instances a partial grep here missed, including inside this exact file's own PATCH handler.

**Store (`store/useTaskStore.ts`)**: new `eventComments: Record<string, TaskComment[]>` state + `fetchEventComments`/`addEventComment`/`deleteEventComment` actions, same shape as the existing Task comment actions but — like `docComments`'s own precedent — no "Comment as..." authorId param at all (that picker is a pre-auth legacy artifact on the Task panel specifically, not worth replicating for a new feature, per that file's own existing comment on `addDocComment`).

**New `components/calendar/EventActivityPanel.tsx`** — the Event equivalent of the Task modal's Activity & Comments panel, its own small component (matching how Doc comments are their own file too) rather than inlined in `EventDetailModal.tsx`. Small local `EVENT_ACTIVITY_ICONS` map (just the 3 kinds Event's route actually generates) rather than reusing Task's much larger `ACTIVITY_ICONS`. Wired into `EventDetailModal.tsx` right above the "Delete event" button; that modal's body gained `max-h-[80vh] overflow-y-auto` since it's now taller.

**#14 — Event resize, Month/Week (`WeekRow.tsx`) and Day (`DayTimeline.tsx`) alike.** Both files' three interaction-handler functions (`startInteraction`/`moveInteraction`/`endInteraction`) were generalized from taking a whole `Task` object to taking a plain `id: string` — `ClippedSegment`/`DragState`'s own `taskId` field was already generic enough in practice (WeekRow already looked up either `tasksById` or `eventsById` by that same id for rendering), so this was a signature change, not a new mechanism. `EventBar`/`DayEventBlock` gained the same resize-edge `<div>`s `TaskBar`/`DayTaskBlock` already had — **deliberately no move handler on the body** (an Event's date range is meant to be a deliberate edit via the resize handle, not something that drifts from an accidental drag on the whole bar, unlike a Task's due-date-driven bar). `CalendarView.tsx`'s `handleDragEnd(id, mode)` (was `handleDragEnd(task, mode)`) now looks up `tasksById`/`eventsById` itself and delegates to a new `handleEventDragEnd` for the Event case (resize-only, no lane-pin — Event has no `calendarLane` field); the drag-ghost preview generalized from `draggedTask` to `draggedTask ?? draggedEvent`. All-day Events/Tasks (`AllDayChip` in Day view) are unaffected either way — resize has no meaning for a day-wide chip, exactly like all-day Tasks already work.

**#15 — modest visual differentiator, not a deeper redesign.** Every Event bar/block/chip, in every Planner granularity (`EventBar`, `DayEventBlock`, `AllDayChip` when passed `isEvent`), now gets a dashed border (Tasks stay solid) plus a small `CalendarClock` icon before the title — a plain color difference alone isn't reliable since either a Task or an Event can be given any color, so this is the actual at-a-glance tell.

**Verified end-to-end against the real dev server + real SQLite DB** (scripted, logged-in sessions — no Playwright in this project): created a real Task and a real Event, PATCHed each one's dates (simulating what a resize-drag does), confirmed both now produce a `datesChanged` activity comment with the correct human-readable date range; posted a real comment on an Event and confirmed its `authorId` matches the actual signed-in session, not anything client-supplied; confirmed an unauthenticated request gets an empty array, and — more importantly — that a second real logged-in user who is **not** a member of the event's workspace gets a real `403` on `GET`/`POST /api/events/[id]/comments` and `PATCH /api/events/[id]` (not just "unauthenticated is blocked" — a genuine cross-account authorization check, matching this project's own established access-control precedent). `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout. **Not yet visually confirmed in a real browser** — the actual pointer-drag resize gesture, the dashed-border/icon treatment, and the new Activity & Comments panel's layout inside `EventDetailModal.tsx` all still need a real look; the dev server was left running locally for that.

## Today's session (2026-08-21, continued a fifth time) — Security & privacy pass (backlog #11)

**Closed the remaining authorId-spoofing gap, everywhere it existed.** Last session's Task/Event work fixed two instances of this in passing (Task/Event comment POST routes) and flagged three more as "found but not fixed yet." Ran a full `grep -rn "authorId\s*:"` sweep across `app/api` this time instead of relying on that earlier partial list — found **6 real instances total**, not 3: `app/api/tasks/route.ts` (task-created + subtask-added activity), `app/api/tasks/[id]/docs/route.ts` (doc-created activity), `app/api/docs/[id]/route.ts` (doc-deleted activity, via a `?authorId=` query param rather than body — same bug, different transport), `app/api/docs/[id]/comments/route.ts` (standalone Doc comments), and **`app/api/tasks/[id]/route.ts`'s own PATCH handler** — the exact route whose `activities` array gained date-change logging last session, which still had 3 of its own `body.authorId ?? null` writes untouched at the time. All six now use the already-verified `userId` from `getCurrentUserId()` instead. Verified end-to-end: a real logged-in user tried to attribute a task-created activity, a task comment, and a doc-created activity to a second real user's id via the request body — all three came back correctly attributed to the actual caller, never the target id.

**Verified the session-cookie posture is already correct — no code needed.** Checked `node_modules/@auth/core`'s own source rather than assuming: `useSecureCookies` (which drives `secure`, the `__Secure-`/`__Host-` name prefixes, and is paired with `sameSite: 'lax'`/`httpOnly: true` on every Auth.js cookie) defaults to `url.protocol === 'https:'`, and this project's own `AUTH_URL` override (confirmed set to `https://siqt.no` in production per the 2026-08-18 deployment sessions) makes that `url` resolve to HTTPS regardless of the raw request. So cookies are already `Secure`/`HttpOnly`/`SameSite=Lax` in production, contingent on that one env var — **worth a quick manual double-check that `AUTH_URL` is still actually set on the live server**, since it can't be verified from here.

**Fixed a stale, actively misleading code comment**: `auth.ts`'s `trustHost: true` comment said "safe here since this app isn't yet deployed behind a public-facing proxy... revisit once it is" — the app *is* now deployed behind Cloudflare, and the "once it is" case already happened and was already handled (the `AUTH_URL` override above). Comment rewritten to state the current, correct reasoning instead of the pre-deployment one.

**New: rate limiting on login and signup — neither existed at all before.** `lib/rateLimit.ts`, a plain in-memory sliding-window limiter (no Redis — matches this app's own "skip infra a single instance doesn't need" precedent, and this is genuinely a single Pterodactyl process). Keyed by `cf-connecting-ip`/`x-forwarded-for` (Cloudflare's own header takes priority, matching how this app is actually deployed). `POST /api/auth/signup`: 10 attempts/15min per IP. `POST /api/auth/[...nextauth]` (NextAuth's own catch-all handler) now wraps its `POST` export to gate only requests whose path ends `/callback/credentials` — 20 attempts/15min per IP — session/csrf/OAuth-callback traffic through the same catch-all handler is untouched.

**Verified**: a real login (correct password) still succeeds normally; 25 rapid wrong-password attempts against the same account got a real `429` starting at attempt #20 (limit working exactly as configured), while the legitimate first attempt still went through; 12 rapid signups got `429` starting at #10. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean throughout.

**Not addressed this pass, explicitly out of scope**: Google OAuth env vars (needs the user, real third-party credentials, not a code fix); encryption-at-rest for the SQLite file or chat message content (an infra/architecture decision, not a quick pass item — flag separately if wanted).

## Today's session (2026-08-21, continued a sixth time) — Logged-out landing page (backlog #7)

**Design decision made, not just executed**: considered adding a genuinely separate public marketing route (e.g. `/welcome`) with the bare `/` redirecting there when logged out, matching how ClickUp itself splits a marketing domain from the app. Went the other way instead — `proxy.ts`'s auth gating (`/` → `/login` via Auth.js's own `authorized()`/`pages.signIn`) is a real security boundary already working correctly, and `app/page.tsx` is a single giant client component that starts fetching protected data on mount with no built-in "logged-out" render path of its own; carving out a new always-public route at `/` (or teaching `page.tsx` to conditionally skip its own data-fetching) would have meant touching that boundary for a purely cosmetic ask. `/login` was already public, already the first thing every logged-out visitor sees, and had nothing in it beyond a bare centered auth card — enriching that one page satisfies the actual request ("the landing page... should explain the product") with zero risk to the auth boundary.

**Built**: `app/login/page.tsx` restructured into a two-column layout (stacks on mobile) — marketing pitch on the left (headline, one-paragraph pitch leaning on the app's own self-hosted/local-SQLite identity, "your data stays on infrastructure you control"), the exact same functional sign-in/signup card on the right, completely untouched (no logic, state, or handler changed — only the outer JSX wrapper). Five feature blurbs (Tasks/Planner/Docs/Chat/Office) use the *same* icons as the real app's own nav rail (`List`/`Calendar`/`FileText`/`MessageSquare`/`Building2`), so a new user recognizes them once actually inside.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; `curl http://localhost:3000/login` (unauthenticated) returns a real `200` with the new headline text present in the server-rendered HTML, confirming it's not just client-side-only content. **Not yet visually confirmed in a real browser** — the responsive stacking on mobile and general look need a real look (dev server left running locally).
