## Today's session (2026-08-21, continued a ninth time) — Real browser push notifications for Chat/DMs

Deferred since 2026-08-17 (flatly blocked without HTTPS, per that session's own note) — unblocked now that `siqt.no` is actually live. User asked to build it directly, then separately asked about mobile strategy (answered inline, not a PLANNING.md item: build the responsive web app / PWA route first, since the existing UI — dense sidebars, drag-and-drop, hover states — doesn't port to native without a full separate rewrite; this push feature is itself the first real step toward a PWA).

**New dependency**: `web-push` (+ `@types/web-push` dev-only). **VAPID keys generated once** via `npx web-push generate-vapid-keys`, stored in `.env.local` (`VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY`/`VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY`/`VAPID_SUBJECT`) — same "missing env var degrades to a silent no-op, not a crash" precedent `CHAT_BROADCAST_SECRET` already established (`lib/push.ts`'s `ensureConfigured()`).

**Schema**: new `PushSubscription` (`userId`, `endpoint` unique, `p256dh`, `auth`) — one row per subscribed browser/device, a user can have several (phone + laptop). A dead/revoked endpoint (the push service itself returns `404`/`410`) is deleted by `lib/push.ts`'s `sendPushToUser` as it's discovered, not swept proactively.

**API**: `GET /api/push/vapid-public-key` (serves the public key from the server's own env var rather than baking it into the client bundle — no rebuild needed if it ever rotates), `POST`/`DELETE /api/push/subscribe` (upsert/remove the caller's own subscription by endpoint).

**Server-side send**: `lib/push.ts`'s `sendPushToUser(userId, {title, body, url})` — never throws, so a route sending a push can't have its own response broken by a slow/down push provider or a malformed subscription. Wired into `POST /api/channels/[id]/messages` (the one route both real channels and DMs post through) right after the existing `broadcastChatSignal` call: every *other* member of that channel/DM gets a push attempt, title is the channel name (`#general`) or the sender's own name for a DM. **Scope decision**: every message triggers a push attempt, not just @mentions or DMs specifically — matches how the existing in-app unread-badge system already treats channels and DMs uniformly, and this app's scale (a small team, not Discord-at-scale) makes "every message" a reasonable v1 rather than needing a mentions-detection system first. Click-through currently just opens `/` (the app root) — deep-linking straight to the specific channel would need matching `lib/navUrl.ts`'s NavState shape server-side, flagged as a possible follow-up, not done now.

**Client**: `public/sw.js` (push-only service worker — no offline/caching strategy, just a `push` handler that shows the notification and a `notificationclick` handler that focuses an already-open tab on this origin or opens a new one). `lib/pushClient.ts` (`enablePush`/`disablePush`/`getPushStatus` — permission request, service-worker registration, `PushManager.subscribe()`, POSTs the subscription to the server). New "Notifications" section in `SettingsPanel.tsx`'s General tab — a single Enable/Disable button reflecting the real current per-browser subscription state (not a stored preference; this is inherently per-device).

**Verified end-to-end** against the real dev server: `GET /api/push/vapid-public-key` returns the real generated key; subscribing (with a well-formed but fake browser subscription, since a real one needs an actual browser) stores correctly and is rejected unauthenticated (`401`); sending a real chat message between two users **correctly attempts the push** (confirmed via the dev log: `web-push` itself rejected the fake test key's malformed `p256dh` value, proving the send path actually ran) **without breaking the message POST's own `200` response** — the whole point of the fire-and-forget design; unsubscribe works. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. **Not yet tested with a real browser subscription** (needs an actual Chrome/Firefox granting permission, can't be scripted) — worth a real click-through next: enable notifications in Settings, send a message from a second account, confirm a real system notification appears and clicking it focuses/opens the app.

## Today's session (2026-08-21, continued a tenth time) — Production deploy broken by a real dependency-placement bug: `@types/web-push` in devDependencies

User pasted a real production deploy log (Pterodactyl): `next build`'s TypeScript check failed on `lib/push.ts`'s `import webpush from 'web-push'` ("Could not find a declaration file for module 'web-push'"), so `.next` never got created, so the runtime container's `next start` failed outright ("Could not find a production build in the '.next' directory"). Exact same root cause class as the pre-existing, already-documented "move build-time devDependencies to real dependencies" fix (commit `a50675c`) — the install step's `npm install` doesn't install devDependencies in this production environment, but `next build`'s own type-check needs every `@types/*` package regardless. `@types/web-push` landed in `devDependencies` when it was added (2026-08-21, push-notifications session) instead of `dependencies` like every other `@types/*` package already sitting there — a straightforward oversight, not a new class of bug.

**Fixed**: moved `@types/web-push` into `dependencies` in `package.json`, ran `npm install` locally to sync `package-lock.json` (confirmed via diff: the `"dev": true` flag is gone). **Verified for real, not just assumed**: ran an actual `npm run build` locally after the fix — full production build completed successfully (`.next` created, every route listed, zero errors) — this is exactly the command Pterodactyl's own install step runs, so this is the same fix already proven to work, not a guess.

## Today's session (2026-08-21, continued an eleventh time) — Full two-way Google Calendar sync (backlog #13)

User explicitly chose the "build full two-way sync now" option over starting smaller (one-way export only, or just the location field alone) — asked directly, this was their own scoping call, not assumed. Also asked about Google OAuth for login and mobile-app strategy in the same message; both answered inline in chat (OAuth is pure Google Cloud Console + Pterodactyl-panel configuration the user has to do, walked through the concrete steps; mobile: build the responsive web/PWA route before ever considering a native app, since the existing dense/hover/drag-heavy UI doesn't port to native without a full separate rewrite — Capacitor, wrapping the existing web app, is the realistic path *to* an app store later if that's ever wanted, cheaper than React Native or true native Swift+Kotlin).

**Two deliberate scope cuts, both documented at the top of `lib/google/calendarSync.ts` itself, not just here:**
1. **Polling, not real-time push webhooks.** Google Calendar's `events.watch` push channels need a public HTTPS callback (have one now) but also expire (max 7 days) and need active renewal, plus signature verification — real infrastructure beyond what a polling script needs. `pullChangesFromGoogle` uses Google's own incremental-sync cursor (`syncToken`) so a poll only costs fetching what actually changed, not the whole calendar — just not instant.
2. **A brand-new event created directly on the Google side is never auto-imported into Siqt.** Only Events that already have a `googleEventId` (i.e., originated in Siqt) get pulled back and updated/deleted; a foreign Google event with no matching id is skipped outright, since importing it would need picking a target workspace/Space with no natural answer. "Two-way" here means edits to a Siqt-created Event flow both ways, not "anything on your Google Calendar shows up in Siqt."

**Schema**: `Event.location` (plain text — the "maps/hvor" part of #13, mapped directly to/from Google's own `location` field, no real geocoding), `Event.googleEventId` (unique — the join key both sync directions look events up by), `Event.googleSyncOwnerId` (fixed at creation to whoever made the Event in Siqt — **only that person's Google connection ever reads/writes this event**; other assignees are added as Calendar *attendees* by email, an ordinary invite, so they don't each need their own OAuth connection for the event to appear on their own calendar). `User.googleCalendarId` (a dedicated secondary "Siqt" calendar, created lazily on first sync — never the user's primary calendar, keeps everything cleanly separable) + `User.googleCalendarSyncToken` (the incremental-sync cursor).

**OAuth**: `lib/google/oauthClient.ts`'s scope list gained `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/calendar` (the full scope, not the narrower `calendar.events` — creating the dedicated Siqt calendar itself, `calendars.insert`, is a Calendars-resource operation that only the full scope covers). Same shared "Connect Google" flow/refresh-token already backing Docs export — one consent now covers both; anyone previously connected needs to reconnect once (Google re-prompts automatically when the requested scope set changes, and the existing `oauth/start` route already forces `prompt: 'consent'`).

**Sync engine** (`lib/google/calendarSync.ts`): `pushEventToGoogle` (create-or-update, silent no-op if the sync owner isn't connected — never an error state, an Event just stays Siqt-only), `deleteEventFromGoogle`, `pullChangesFromGoogle` (last-write-wins conflict policy, compared by `updated` timestamp — a plain, documented rule, not real merge resolution). All-day date conversion handles Google's exclusive end-date convention explicitly both directions (Siqt's own ranges are inclusive, matching `ganttLayout.ts`'s existing convention) — reasoned through by hand against concrete before/after examples since a live round-trip couldn't be tested here.

**Wired into** `POST /api/events`, `PATCH /api/events/[id]` (including the `restore` branch — best-effort re-create, self-heals on the next edit if the old Google id had gone stale), and `DELETE /api/events/[id]` (both soft and permanent — a trashed Event shouldn't linger on the calendar). New `POST /api/google/calendar/sync` (on-demand pull, called once when `CalendarView.tsx` mounts, so a Google-side edit doesn't wait for the next scheduled run before showing up) and `scripts/syncGoogleCalendar.ts` (the background path — same "runnable script + Pterodactyl Schedule" precedent as `scripts/backupDb.ts`, new `npm run sync:calendar`/`sync:calendar:prod`). `EventDetailModal.tsx` gained a Location field (with a map-pin icon).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean. Ran real API calls confirming **zero regression** for a user with no Google connected — creating/updating/deleting an Event with a `location` set all still work exactly as before, `googleEventId` correctly stays `null`, and the new on-demand sync endpoint returns a clean `{updated: 0, deleted: 0}` rather than erroring. The all-day exclusive-end-date conversion logic was checked by hand against concrete multi-day examples rather than a live script (not exported for testing, and no real Google event to round-trip against anyway). **Not verified**: an actual real Google account's OAuth consent + real Calendar API round-trip — no browser available here to grant consent. Next session should do a real click-through once Google OAuth env vars exist in production: connect Google, create an Event in Siqt with a location, confirm it appears on the "Siqt" calendar in real Google Calendar with the location set, edit it on the Google side, and confirm (after either the on-demand Planner-open pull or the scheduled script) it updates back in Siqt.

## Today's session (2026-08-21, continued a twelfth time) — Corrected the Calendar sync design after direct user feedback: real primary-calendar import, not a dedicated secondary one

User pushed back on the previous entry's scope cut #2 immediately, with a ClickUp reference screenshot showing a Google-native event tagged with the Google logo right inside ClickUp's own quick-create UI ("Event, in rhofseth@gmail.com") — making the point directly: a Google-side event should just show up, not be skipped. Correct call — the original design (a lazily-created dedicated secondary "Siqt" calendar, foreign events never imported) was a real, load-bearing scope decision made without checking what the actual reference behavior looks like, not a minor detail.

**Design corrected**: sync now operates directly on the user's real **primary** Google Calendar (`calendarId: 'primary'`), not a separate one — `ensureSiqtCalendarId`/`calendars.insert` and `User.googleCalendarId` removed entirely (added and pushed in the immediately-preceding commit, then ripped back out same day once the design changed — cheaper to just remove than carry unused dead weight forward). `pullChangesFromGoogle` now actually **creates a new Siqt Event** for any Google event it doesn't already recognize (previously just skipped), landing it in the user's personal ("My tasks") workspace — the one place that's unambiguously theirs alone, avoiding the "which shared team workspace" question a foreign event has no natural answer to. New `Event.importedFromGoogle` boolean marks these specifically (true only for events that started on the Google side), separate from `googleEventId` (which every synced event has, regardless of which side originated it).

**First-sync bounding**: pulling in someone's *entire* Google Calendar history on first connect would be a bad surprise (years of unrelated past events) — the very first sync (no `syncToken` yet) is bounded to a rolling window (30 days back, 365 days forward) via `timeMin`/`timeMax`; every later incremental poll just uses the syncToken as-is (Google's API doesn't accept time bounds alongside one — the token already encodes the scope the first call established).

**New `components/icons/GoogleIcon.tsx`** — a small inline SVG of Google's own "G" mark (lucide-react has no brand logos) — shown in place of the usual Event calendar-clock icon on `WeekRow.tsx`'s `EventBar`, `DayTimeline.tsx`'s `DayEventBlock`/`AllDayChip`, and as a "Google Calendar event · in <email>" line in `EventDetailModal.tsx`'s header (matching the reference screenshot's own "Event, in rhofseth@gmail.com" treatment) — shown for *any* Google-synced event, not just imported ones, distinguishing "this is a Google-native import" from "this is a Siqt event that also happens to sync."

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; re-ran the earlier no-Google-connected regression check after the rewrite — still passes, and the on-demand sync endpoint now correctly returns the new `{created, updated, deleted}` shape. Ran a real `npm run build` before pushing (now a standing habit after the `@types/web-push` deploy break). **Still not verified against a real Google account** — same limitation as before, no browser/OAuth flow available here; the next real click-through should specifically confirm an event created directly in Google Calendar shows up in Siqt's "My tasks" Planner with the Google icon, not just that Siqt→Google pushes work.

## Today's session (2026-08-21, continued a thirteenth time) — Google-colors dashed border on imported events

User asked specifically: a Google-imported Event's dashed border should be in Google's own 4-color "rainbow" (blue/red/yellow/green), not the usual single-color one every other Event gets.

A real multi-color dashed CSS border isn't achievable with `border-color` (one flat color) or `border-image` (ignores both `border-style` and `border-radius` in every browser — tried reasoning through it, not worth the inconsistency). New `components/icons/GoogleDashedBorder.tsx` instead: four separate absolutely-positioned strips (top/right/bottom/left), each its own Google color, each drawn via a `repeating-linear-gradient` along its own axis to get an actual dash pattern — genuinely reliable across browsers, unlike the border-image approach. `rounded-[inherit]` + `overflow-hidden` on the wrapper clips it to whatever radius the parent bar already has, so it doesn't need to know which corners are actually rounded.

Wired into the exact same three surfaces the Google icon already uses (`WeekRow.tsx`'s `EventBar`, `DayTimeline.tsx`'s `DayEventBlock` and `AllDayChip`) — only for `importedFromGoogle` events; the underlying element's own real border is set to `transparent` (not removed — keeps the same border-width reserved so nothing else shifts) so the two don't double up. `npx tsc --noEmit` clean; hit the app afterward with no compile/runtime error. **Not yet seen in a real browser** (no Google-imported event exists to look at without a real connected account either) — worth a visual check once that's possible.

## Today's session (2026-08-21, continued a fourteenth time) — A third Office avatar surface had been missed: the sidebar's own "Team" list

User reported right-click/DM "doesn't work anywhere in Office, neither in the sidebar nor in the office [floor plan]" after being told it was already built. Re-verified the actual wiring end-to-end first (still fully intact, `app/page.tsx` → `OfficePage.tsx` → `OfficeRooms.tsx`/`RoomDetail.tsx` → `ManageableAvatar.tsx`, nothing regressed) — the code genuinely wasn't broken, it just didn't cover everywhere the user meant. Found it by grepping: `app/page.tsx`'s own main sidebar renders a completely separate "Team" member list when `activeView === 'office'` (a plain `<button>` per person, colored-initials circle, no `ManageableAvatar` at all) — a third place Office shows user avatars that the backlog #9 work never touched, since it isn't part of `OfficeRooms.tsx`'s own component tree.

**Fixed**: that sidebar list now renders each person through `ManageableAvatar` too (same right-click/badge/DM/role popover as the floor plan and room-detail views). Had to change the row from a `<button>` to a `<div onClick=...>` first — `ManageableAvatar`'s own avatar is itself a `<button>` (via `PersonAvatar`), and nesting a button inside a button is invalid HTML that breaks click handling; the row's own click (for "navigate to this person's page") now lives on the wrapping `<div>` and is also passed as `ManageableAvatar`'s `onClick`, so clicking anywhere in the row (not just the tiny avatar) still navigates, matching the original behavior.

**Real, separate finding along the way, not fixed**: this sidebar "Team" list renders the *global* `users` array (`GET /api/users`, every registered account on the whole instance) with zero workspace filtering — unlike `OfficePage.tsx`'s own floor-plan view, which correctly scopes to `workspace.members`. Means this list currently shows people who aren't even in the current workspace. Pre-existing, unrelated to today's fix, and out of scope for "make right-click work" — flagged here rather than silently expanded into or ignored.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, hit the app afterward with no compile/runtime error. **Not yet visually confirmed in a real browser** (no Playwright in this project) — worth a real click-through: right-click a name in this sidebar list specifically (not just the floor plan) and confirm the same popover with "Send DM" opens.

## Today's session (2026-08-22) — Mobile-responsive redesign, Phase 0 + Phase 1 (nav shell)

User asked for the app to become genuinely usable on a phone, web-first (a Capacitor native wrapper is the agreed later step, not part of this work). Gave a concrete spec off two ClickUp mobile-app reference screenshots: a bottom nav (Tasks/Planner/Chat/Menu) with a tappable "Menu" opening a fuller app-launcher grid, a Task list where the name is primary and swiping left reveals the other fields, and a Planner/Calendar styled like a native Google Calendar widget (month view with Gantt bars, tap a day to drill in). Hard constraint from the user: none of this may change desktop/PC appearance or behavior — planned and built entirely as additive Tailwind `md:` breakpoint gating (768px cutoff) plus one new `useIsMobile()` hook for the few places behavior (not just layout) needs to differ, never a rewrite of an existing branch. Full plan written via Plan Mode (Explore + Plan sub-agents) before any code — see the plan file this produced for the full phased design (nav shell → task list swipe → calendar → optional PWA/drag-reorder polish).

**Phase 0 (foundation, no visible change)**: `hooks/useIsMobile.ts` (`matchMedia('(max-width: 767px), (pointer: coarse)')`, SSR-safe default `false`); `viewport` export added to `app/layout.tsx` (`viewportFit: 'cover'`, for `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` on notched iOS); extracted the desktop rail's inline Tasks-click workspace-fallback logic and Office-click reset logic into `handleTasksNavClick`/`handleOfficeNavClick`, and the whole rail's tab list into one `visibleNavTabs` `useMemo` (`app/page.tsx`) — single source of truth for `hiddenNavTabs`/`hasRealWorkspace` gating, shared by both the desktop rail and the new mobile surfaces below rather than re-derived twice. Desktop rail's own JSX rewritten to just `.map()` over `visibleNavTabs` — same rendered output, now backed by shared state instead of five near-identical inline blocks.

**Phase 1 (mobile nav shell)**: desktop icon rail and `<aside>` sidebar both gained `hidden md:flex` (invisible below 768px, pixel-identical at `md`+). New `components/mobile/MobileBottomNav.tsx` (`flex md:hidden`, fixed to the bottom of the root flex column): Tasks/Planner/Chat pulled from `visibleNavTabs` by id, plus a 4th "Menu" button. New `components/mobile/AppLauncherGrid.tsx`: a bottom-sheet grid (framer-motion `AnimatePresence`, matches this app's existing sheet-transition convention) covering every tab `visibleNavTabs` exposes plus Settings/Trash — every tile calls a setter the desktop rail already calls, no new state introduced. New `components/mobile/MobileSpacesSheet.tsx`: since the Spaces/Lists tree (`FolderTree.tsx`) lives in the now-hidden sidebar, added a small `md:hidden` "Lists" button in the Tasks-view header that opens a lighter-weight flat space→list picker (ordered via the existing `getOrderedListIds` helper, calling the existing `handleListClick` handler with synthetic no-modifier-key event data for a plain single-select tap) — deliberately not a full re-wire of `FolderTree.tsx` itself, which carries drag-and-drop/context-menu/rename wiring that a tap-to-navigate mobile sheet doesn't need. New shared `components/mobile/navTypes.ts` (`NavTab` type) so `app/page.tsx` and the three new mobile components agree on one shape without duplicating it.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds (standing habit since the `@types/web-push` deploy break), scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet visually confirmed** — everything here is real touch/gesture/layout work with zero Playwright coverage in this project; needs a real phone or Chrome DevTools' device-emulation toolbar to confirm the bottom nav appears/works below 768px, the desktop rail/sidebar are unchanged above it, and the app-launcher/spaces sheets open and navigate correctly. Task list swipe-to-reveal (Phase 2) and the mobile Planner/Calendar (Phase 3) are not yet built — next up.

## Today's session (2026-08-22, continued) — Mobile-responsive redesign, Phase 2 (Task list swipe-to-reveal)

Continuing the mobile redesign plan (Phase 0/1 above): the Task list's mobile layout, where the task name is primary and swiping a row left reveals status/assignee/dates/custom fields.

**The dnd-kit conflict flagged in the plan was real** — `TaskRow.tsx`'s whole row root is a `useDraggable` drag source (task reordering/moving, `PointerSensor` at 5px activation), which would race against a horizontal swipe gesture on the same element. Rather than trying to directionally disambiguate one pointer stream between two gesture systems (fragile — dnd-kit has no built-in axis lock), `TaskRow.tsx` now renders two entirely separate row layouts gated by the new `useIsMobile()` hook: the desktop grid row (byte-identical to before, still the sole `useDraggable`/`useDroppable` consumer) and a new mobile row with no drag-and-drop wiring at all — mobile task reordering is out of scope for this pass, matching the plan.

Chose the JS-hook gate over a CSS-only `hidden md:...` toggle specifically for this file (unlike the nav shell in Phase 1) because the column cells themselves carry real interactive state — `FloatingPopover`-based status/assignee pickers, `DatePickerPopover` — that would double-mount and share one `statusOpen`/`assigneeOpen` boolean across two simultaneously-rendered instances (one visibly on mobile, one invisibly `display:none` on desktop) if only hidden via CSS; a real risk of a hidden popover's portal trying to position itself off a collapsed 0×0 anchor rect. Extracted the column-cell switch (status/assignee/date/custom) into one shared `renderColumnCell(col)` function used by both layouts, so there's exactly one `FloatingPopover`/`DatePickerPopover` instance mounted per column at any time, never two.

**Mobile row structure**: a `framer-motion` `drag="x"` foreground bar (checkbox, done-toggle, task name, inline rename) sits over an absolutely-positioned reveal panel sized to `columns.length` (same `activeColumns`/`visibleColumns` source of truth desktop's grid uses, so what swipe reveals never drifts from what desktop shows), snapping open/closed on drag-end past a half-width threshold. A tap while revealed closes the panel instead of opening the task (consumes the tap); a plain tap otherwise opens the task detail modal, same as desktop. The reveal panel also holds a "more options" button (opens the same context menu as desktop's right-click), since touch has no right-click equivalent. Both `TaskRow` call sites (main list, subtask list inside the task modal) picked this up automatically — no changes needed at either, plus their column-header bars (`rowGridTemplate`-based) got `hidden md:grid` so they don't show above the mobile row layout.

No new dependencies — `framer-motion`'s `drag`/`dragConstraints`/`dragElastic` were already available (the row was already a `motion.div` for enter/exit animation).

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet visually confirmed** — swipe/gesture feel needs a real phone or DevTools' touch emulation, same caveat as Phase 1. Next up: Phase 3, the mobile Planner/Calendar.

## Today's session (2026-08-22, continued a second time) — Mobile-responsive redesign, Phase 3 (Planner/Calendar)

Continuing the mobile redesign plan: the mobile Planner/Calendar, styled like a native Google Calendar widget (month view with Gantt bars, tap a day to drill into Day view).

**Reused `CalendarView.tsx`/`WeekRow.tsx`/`DayTimeline.tsx` wholesale rather than building a new calendar** — the month-view Gantt bars and the tap-a-day-to-drill-into-Day-view interaction the spec asked for already existed on desktop (`drillToDay`/`onDrillDay`, already wired). The only real gaps were: Week granularity has no place on a phone-width screen (redundant with Month, and not touch-sized), and every bar's resize/move-drag pointer handlers are mouse-cursor-sized hit targets that actively fight a touch tap.

**Changes**: `CalendarView.tsx` now filters its granularity switcher to `['month', 'day']` under `useIsMobile()` (Week stays desktop-only), with an effect that redirects an active Week view back to Month if the viewport crosses into mobile mid-session (e.g. a foldable rotating). `isMobile` threads down as a plain prop into `WeekRow.tsx` → `TaskBar`/`EventBar` and `DayTimeline.tsx` → `DayTaskBlock`/`DayEventBlock`: on mobile, every resize-edge strip (2px/6px-wide `onPointerDown` divs, meant for a mouse cursor) is skipped entirely, and `TaskBar`/`DayTaskBlock`'s move-drag pointer handlers are replaced with a plain `onClick={() => onOpenTask(id)}` — same "tap to open, no drag" fallback used for the Task list row in Phase 2. `EventBar`/`DayEventBlock` needed no click-handler change (they already open via a real button, independent of the resize handles) — just the resize strips removed. The calendar's own container padding (`app/page.tsx`) drops from `p-6` to `p-2 md:p-6` so the month grid isn't squeezed on a narrow screen.

Chose prop-threading (`isMobile: boolean` passed down through `WeekRow`/`DayTimeline`) over each sub-component calling `useIsMobile()` itself — these are already deep, heavily-prop-drilled component trees (`WeekRow` → `TaskBar`/`EventBar`, `DayTimeline` → `DayTaskBlock`/`DayEventBlock`), and a single value computed once at `CalendarView`'s top level is simpler to reason about than five independent hook calls.

**Deliberately not done this pass** (flagged in the plan as lower-priority): touch-sizing `WeekRow.tsx`'s pixel constants (`BAR_H`/`BAR_GAP`/`DAY_NUM_H`/`GUTTER_WIDTH`) for larger tap targets — the day cell's own tap-to-drill button and the bars' new tap-to-open behavior are already reasonably sized (24-26px), and parameterizing these into mobile-specific values would touch `CalendarView.tsx`'s row-height/lane-cap/drag-math computations for a marginal touch-comfort gain not explicitly asked for. Worth a follow-up if real device testing shows it's actually cramped.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet visually confirmed** — same caveat as every mobile phase this session, needs a real phone or DevTools' touch emulation to confirm month view renders correctly at phone width, tapping a day drills into Day view, and tapping a task/event bar opens it without triggering a stray drag. This closes out the three core asks from the original mobile spec (nav shell, Task list swipe, Planner/Calendar) — remaining optional polish (mobile drag-to-reorder tasks, full PWA installability) is explicitly deferred per the plan, not started.

## Today's session (2026-08-22, continued a third time) — Mobile drag-to-reorder + full PWA installability

User asked for the two optional Phase-4 items explicitly, after the three core mobile-redesign asks (nav shell, Task list swipe, Planner/Calendar) shipped: mobile drag-and-drop on the Task list, and full PWA installability (manifest, icons, install prompt) — building on the `public/sw.js` that already existed for push notifications.

### Mobile drag-to-reorder

Worth naming precisely what dragging a task actually does in this app, since "reorder" undersells it: dropping a task onto another task's `task:${id}` droppable calls `optimisticSetParent` (nests it as a subtask), dropping onto a `list:`/`space:`/`folder-drop:` target moves it there — the same shared `DndContext` in `app/page.tsx` already handles Space/Folder/List/Doc/Room/Person drag-and-drop too, dispatched by the dragged id's string prefix. Mobile only gets the task-onto-task and task-onto-visible-target cases for free, scoped to whatever's actually on-screen in the flat list (the sidebar with List/Space targets is hidden below `md`, per Phase 1) — that's still a real, useful capability, not a fake "reorder."

**The conflict**: desktop's whole `TaskRow` root is a `useDraggable` source (`PointerSensor`, 5px activation) — spreading that onto the mobile row would race the swipe-to-reveal gesture (Phase 2) on the same element. Fixed with dnd-kit's own documented "drag handle" pattern: added a small `GripVertical` handle to the mobile row's foreground bar (`components/TaskRow.tsx`), with `{...attributes} {...listeners}` spread only onto that handle, never the row root — the row root (`ref={setNodeRef}`) is still what gets dragged/measured, just not what *starts* the drag. The handle's own `onPointerDown` calls `e.stopPropagation()` before delegating to dnd-kit's own handler, so the swipe `motion.div`'s ancestor drag-gesture recognizer never sees the pointerdown that originated on the handle. `touchAction: 'none'` on the handle stops the browser's native touch-scroll from competing with dnd-kit's pointer capture (dnd-kit's own recommendation for touch dragging).

**No `TouchSensor` needed** — dnd-kit's `PointerSensor` (already registered as `taskSensors`) handles touch input natively via the Pointer Events API; mixing in a separate `TouchSensor` alongside it is what dnd-kit's own docs warn against (both would try to claim the same touch input). The existing `isDragging`/`isOver` visual feedback (dim while dragging, ring while hovered-over) and the shared `DragOverlay` floating preview now also apply to the mobile row — same source of truth as desktop, no new state.

### Full PWA installability

Checked `node_modules/next/dist/docs/.../manifest.md` and `app-icons.md` first per this repo's own "not the Next.js you know" instruction (Next 16's file-based conventions: `app/manifest.ts`, `app/icon.tsx`, `app/apple-icon.tsx`, generated via `next/og`'s `ImageResponse` — no binary image asset needed since there's no existing logo file).

- **`lib/pwaIcon.tsx`**: one shared `siqtIconElement(sizePx, maskable?)` — dark background (`#0a0a0a`, matches the app's own neutral-950 theme) with a bold blue-400 "S", reused by every icon file below so they all agree on look.
- **`app/icon.tsx`** (32×32, browser tab favicon) and **`app/apple-icon.tsx`** (180×180, iOS home-screen icon) — Next's special file conventions, auto-wired into `<head>`.
- **`app/pwa-icon-192/route.tsx`**, **`app/pwa-icon-512/route.tsx`**, **`app/pwa-icon-512-maskable/route.tsx`** — plain Route Handlers (not the special icon-file convention) at fixed, predictable paths, since the manifest needs a literal stable `src` URL to reference and the special `icon.tsx` convention's own URL includes a build-generated query string outside this app's control. The maskable variant renders the "S" smaller/centered (a `maskable` icon's background must fill the entire canvas edge-to-edge since a launcher may crop it into a circle/squircle — the glyph has to survive that crop, hence the extra safe-zone padding).
- **`app/manifest.ts`**: name/short_name "Siqt", `display: 'standalone'`, `background_color`/`theme_color` matching the dark theme, all three icon routes (`purpose: 'any'` ×2, `purpose: 'maskable'` ×1).
- **`app/layout.tsx`**: added `viewport.themeColor` (same dark color, so the OS status bar/task-switcher chrome reads as a continuation of the app rather than a mismatched bar) and `metadata.appleWebApp` (`title`, `statusBarStyle: 'black-translucent'`) — iOS ignores the Web App Manifest entirely for "Add to Home Screen" launch behavior; these Apple-specific meta tags are what actually make a Home-Screen-launched Siqt open standalone instead of just opening Safari.
- **`components/ServiceWorkerRegistrar.tsx`** (new, mounted in `app/layout.tsx`): registers `/sw.js` unconditionally on every load, not gated behind push opt-in like `lib/pushClient.ts`'s own registration call — a controlling service worker registration is part of what Chrome/Edge require before treating a site as installable at all. Registering the same script/scope twice is a harmless no-op per spec, so this doesn't conflict with the push-specific registration later.
- **`hooks/useInstallPrompt.ts`** (new): wraps `beforeinstallprompt` (Chrome/Edge desktop+Android — `preventDefault()`'d so the browser's own mini-infobar stays suppressed and this app can offer its own button instead), `appinstalled`, and a `display-mode: standalone` / iOS's legacy `navigator.standalone` check for "already installed." Wired into two places: an "Install Siqt as an app" tile in the mobile `AppLauncherGrid` (only shown when `canInstall`), and a fuller row in `AccountSettingsPanel.tsx` that falls back to a text hint ("tap Share, then Add to Home Screen") on iOS, since Safari never fires the programmatic prompt at all.

**A real, separate bug this surfaced and fixed**: `proxy.ts`'s auth matcher protects every page-like route by default (redirecting an unauthenticated request to `/login`) and only explicitly excluded `api`/`login`/`invite`/`connect`/`_next/*`/`favicon.ico` — none of the new manifest/icon/service-worker paths were in that exclusion list, so an unauthenticated request to any of them (confirmed via `curl` with no cookies) got a 307 to `/login` instead of the actual asset. That's not just cosmetic: a redirected (HTML) response in place of the real `/sw.js` script fails service worker registration outright, and the browser's own installability check + the tab favicon both need to load on an unauthenticated `/login` visit too. Added `manifest.webmanifest|icon|apple-icon|pwa-icon-192|pwa-icon-512|pwa-icon-512-maskable|sw.js` to the matcher's exclusion list — confirmed via `curl` after a dev-server restart (matcher changes need one, same as any `next.config.ts`-level change) that every one of these now returns 200 with the correct content-type with zero cookies attached.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds (all new routes show up in the route list — `/manifest.webmanifest`, `/icon`, `/apple-icon`, `/pwa-icon-192`, `/pwa-icon-512`, `/pwa-icon-512-maskable`), scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker, and a direct unauthenticated `curl` against each new route confirms 200 + the right `Content-Type` (`application/manifest+json`, `image/png` ×5, `application/javascript` for `sw.js`) rather than the pre-fix 307. **Not yet verified**: the actual `beforeinstallprompt` flow and a real install (needs a real Chrome/Edge on Android or desktop — a scripted `curl` can't fire real browser install-eligibility heuristics), the mobile grip-handle drag gesture itself (same no-Playwright/no-real-touch-device caveat as every other mobile-gesture change this session), and iOS's Add-to-Home-Screen behavior end to end.

## Today's session (2026-08-22, continued a fourth time) — Mobile UX feedback pass, seven items after real phone testing

User tried the mobile redesign on an actual phone (first real device feedback this whole mobile effort — everything before this was DevTools emulation + code review) and came back with seven concrete, mobile-only issues. All fixed, all scoped so desktop is untouched.

1. **Breadcrumb bar looked unpolished on mobile** — `app/page.tsx`'s main-area header breadcrumb text (`Space / List`, `Office / Person`, etc.) is now `hidden md:flex`; the right-side action buttons (Lists/Archive/Channels/Chats) that used to sit in the same `justify-between` row got their own wrapper with `ml-auto` so they stay pinned to the right once the breadcrumb div (their former "other half") stops participating in layout on mobile.

2. **Task modal's Comments panel was cropped** — it's a fixed `w-[420px]` side-by-side panel, wider than most phones. On mobile it now fully replaces the task-fields pane instead of squeezing beside it: the main pane gets `hidden` while `showActivityPanel` is on, and the panel itself goes `w-full` instead of animating to a fixed 420px. The existing header toggle button (the `MessageSquare` icon, already wired to flip `showActivityPanel`) doubles as "back to task" with zero new UI — pressing it again just un-hides the main pane. Needed the `useIsMobile()` hook here specifically (added to `app/page.tsx` for the first time) rather than pure CSS, since a CSS-only toggle would still *mount* the fixed-width panel's layout math (the `animate={{width:420}}` target) regardless of viewport.

3. **Add Task / Clear overdue / +Column were too small to comfortably tap** — bumped padding/text size on mobile only (`py-3.5 text-sm` for Add Task, `px-3 py-2` for the other two, both falling back to the original smaller desktop sizing via `md:` overrides) — no logic changes, just touch target size.

4. **Planner**:
   - **(i)** Tapping a day now always drills into Day view; the old hover-revealed "+" quick-add corner button was a real bug on mobile, not just a nice-to-have — `opacity-0 group-hover` never resolves without a mouse, so it sat there fully invisible yet still tappable, silently eating taps meant for the day cell underneath it. Replaced with a long-press (500ms, cancelled if the finger drags more than 8px — reads as a scroll, not a hold) on the day cell itself calling the same quick-add action; the corner button is now `hidden md:flex` (desktop-only, unchanged there).
   - **(ii)** The header row (`CalendarView.tsx`) now stacks into two rows on mobile (`flex-col` below `md`, `flex-row` at `md`+) instead of squeezing the date-nav and the New Task/granularity-switcher groups onto one line — the fix the user asked for ("more air without shrinking the button") is structural (two rows) rather than cosmetic. Also dropped the "N" keyboard-shortcut hint on mobile (meaningless without a keyboard, frees a little more room).
   - **(iii)** `QuickCreatePopover.tsx` (used for both new Task and new Event, tab-switched) was also a fixed `w-[420px]` — genuinely too wide for a phone screen on top of the keyboard problem. Now `w-full max-w-[420px]` with `items-start` alignment on mobile (vertically centering a modal fights the keyboard: centering happens against the full layout viewport, but the keyboard only shrinks the *visual* one, so a centered modal's lower fields end up hidden behind it) plus `max-h-[85vh] overflow-y-auto` so the card scrolls internally if needed. Title (the first field) now stays visible near the top regardless of keyboard state.

5. **Chat had no way to start a new channel/DM on mobile** — `ChatChannelSidebar`/`DirectMessagesSidebar` (which own the "+ New channel"/"Start a DM" flows) live inside the desktop-only `<aside>`, same as the Spaces/Lists tree was before Phase 1's `MobileSpacesSheet`. New `components/mobile/MobileChatSheet.tsx` reuses those two components unmodified inside a mobile bottom sheet (same pattern as `MobileSpacesSheet`), triggered by a new "Channels"/"Chats" button in the header's mobile button row. Neither sidebar component exposes an "onSelect" callback (they call `setActiveChannelId` directly, same as desktop), so the sheet closes itself by watching that store field for changes instead of threading a new prop through two existing, shared components.

6. **Top search bar was cropped** — the header is `[workspace switcher, shrink-0 w-64] + [search, flex-1] + [balancing spacer, shrink-0 w-64]`; two 256px `shrink-0` blocks alone exceed a phone's viewport width, squeezing the middle search button to near-zero. Fixed: workspace-switcher block narrows to `w-32` on mobile (`md:w-64` restores it), the trailing spacer (purely a desktop centering trick) is `hidden md:block`, and the "Ctrl+K" hint text is `hidden md:inline` (meaningless without a keyboard, frees more room).

7. **No way to reach "Personal stuff" (My Tasks/Assigned/Network/Profile) on mobile** — these four live in the desktop sidebar's "Me zone", inside the same hidden-below-md `<aside>`, and (unlike Spaces/Lists or Chat) had no mobile counterpart built yet since they aren't nav-rail tabs (`visibleNavTabs`/`hiddenNavTabs` never covered them). New `meNavItems` array (`app/page.tsx`, same shape as `visibleNavTabs` but not gated by `hiddenNavTabs`) surfaced as a new "Me" tile section in the mobile `AppLauncherGrid`, reusing the exact same click handlers the desktop Me-zone buttons already call (`ensurePersonalWorkspace`+`setActiveView('board')`, `setActiveView('mytasks'/'directMessages'/'profile')`). Added a shared `MenuTile` type (`components/mobile/navTypes.ts`) alongside the existing `NavTab` type since these four aren't restricted to `NavTabId`'s closed union of rail-tab ids. `AppLauncherGrid.tsx` also got a small internal `Tile` sub-component to avoid the tile markup being duplicated a fourth and fifth time.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet re-confirmed on a real device** — this whole batch is itself a response to real-phone feedback, but the fixes themselves have only been checked by code review + the same build/compile checks as every other mobile change this session; the user will need to check these seven again on their phone before they're considered actually done.

## Today's session (2026-08-22, continued a fifth time) — Planner bug: stale grid-height tracking across Day↔Month transitions

User reported (Norwegian): drilling into a Day view on mobile and then swiping back, visible tasks/events disappear from the Planner.

**Found and fixed a real, related bug**, though not yet confirmed as the full explanation (see below): `CalendarView.tsx`'s month-view grid `<div ref={gridContainerRef}>` genuinely unmounts and remounts every time `granularity` flips to `'day'` and back — the JSX is `{granularity === 'day' ? <DayTimeline/> : <div ref={gridContainerRef}>...}`, two different element types sharing one ternary slot, so React tears the whole div down going into Day view and mounts a fresh one coming back. The `ResizeObserver` that measures this div for `containerHeight` (which Fit-mode's row-height math divides by) was set up in a `useEffect(..., [])` — empty deps, so it only ever ran once, on `CalendarView`'s own first mount, and stayed bound to that *original* div forever. Once that div was torn down, the observer never re-attached to the fresh one created on returning to Month view, so `containerHeight` silently stopped tracking real layout changes — stale forever after the first Day-view visit.

**Fix**: switched to a callback ref (`gridContainerCallbackRef`) that mirrors the node into a small piece of state (`gridContainerNode`), and the `ResizeObserver` effect now depends on `[gridContainerNode]` instead of `[]` — it tears down and re-attaches to whichever div is actually mounted, every time. `gridContainerRef.current` itself is preserved unchanged for the two places that read it directly and synchronously (`dayOffsetFromPoint`/`laneOffsetFromPoint`'s pointer-to-cell math), so this only changes *when the observer's `useEffect` re-runs*, not any of the pointer-math code paths.

**Not yet confirmed this is the full explanation**: the existing `rowHeight` fallback already guards `containerHeight <= 0` by falling back to a fixed `contentHeight` (never actually renders at 0 height), so a stale-but-nonzero `containerHeight` alone wouldn't fully explain a real *disappearance* of tasks/events (as opposed to a subtly wrong row height) — and this bug would apply to *any* Day→Month return (tapping the "Month" pill included), not swipe-back specifically. Asked the user to confirm whether tapping "Month" directly (not swiping back) shows the same symptom, to help isolate whether this is the full story or whether the browser's native back-gesture is *also* interacting with the app's own state<->URL history sync (`app/page.tsx`'s two-effect `nav state <-> URL query string` pair) in some way that a swipe-back-specific repro would be needed to chase further.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet visually confirmed** — same caveat as every mobile-gesture fix this session; needs the user to retest on their phone, specifically: (a) does the fix alone resolve it, (b) if not, does tapping "Month" directly (bypassing swipe) also show the bug, which would point at the render/layout path rather than history/URL sync specifically.

## Today's session (2026-08-22, continued a sixth time) — Mobile browser chrome was eating into the app's own height (`h-screen` → `h-dvh`)

User reported having to scroll down to see the mobile bottom nav, correctly guessing it was the browser's own address bar taking up space, and asked whether this is fixable for regular web users too (not just a future installed app).

**Root cause**: the app's root layout div (`app/page.tsx`) used `h-screen` (`height: 100vh`). On mobile Safari/Chrome, `100vh` is sized against the *largest possible* viewport — the one you'd get with the address bar hidden — not the currently-visible one. With the address bar showing (the default, until the user scrolls a scrollable area enough to auto-hide it), the real visible viewport is shorter than `100vh` by exactly the address bar's height, so a `100vh`-tall flex column pushes its last child (the mobile bottom nav) below the actual visible fold.

**Fix**: swapped `h-screen` for `h-dvh` (`height: 100dvh`, Tailwind's dynamic-viewport-height utility, built in since v3.4 and still present in this project's v4) on the root wrapper and the loading-state fallback screen. `dvh` tracks the *actual* visible viewport and updates live as the browser chrome shows/hides — this is a plain CSS fix that works identically for a regular browser tab and an installed PWA, not something that needs the app to be installed to benefit from. Confirmed the utility actually compiled (`h-dvh{height:100dvh}` present in the built CSS bundle) rather than just trusting Tailwind's JIT scanner picked it up.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker, confirmed the compiled CSS actually contains the `dvh` rule. **Not yet visually confirmed** — needs the user to check on their phone (after deploying) that the bottom nav is now visible without scrolling, both with the address bar showing and after it auto-hides on scroll.

## Today's session (2026-08-22, continued a seventh time) — Second round of real-phone mobile feedback (6 items)

User tested more of the mobile redesign on their phone and came back with six more concrete issues, spanning Tasks, Chat, and Planner.

1. **No way to see Task columns on mobile** — swipe-to-reveal (Phase 2) has no visual affordance at all, so it's a gesture nobody would think to try, and on some devices a horizontal swipe can lose out to the list's own vertical scroll gesture recognition. Added an always-visible chevron button (`components/TaskRow.tsx`) next to the grip handle that plainly taps to toggle the same reveal panel — swipe still works as a bonus, but tapping is now the reliable primary path, not gesture-dependent.

2. **Assignee picker checkboxes too small to tap accurately** — the per-user checkbox in the assignee dropdown (`renderColumnCell`'s `'assignee'` branch) was `w-3.5 h-3.5` regardless of screen size. Bumped to `w-5 h-5` + taller row padding + a wider popover panel on mobile only (`isMobile` conditional classes), desktop untouched.

3. **Chat: no way to reply, open a thread, or react on mobile; wanted a "hold" gesture** — `MessageActions` (reply/thread/react/delete) was `hidden group-hover:flex`, permanently unreachable on touch (no `:hover` at all) — same class of bug as every other hover-only affordance found and fixed earlier this session (the calendar's old "+" button, TaskRow's rename pencil). Added a shared long-press pattern: new `hooks/useLongPress.ts` (extracted from the day-cell long-press already built in `WeekRow.tsx` — same 500ms-hold/8px-cancel-tolerance shape, factored out now that a third call site needs it) plus a per-row-shared-ref long-press implementation directly in `ChatPanel.tsx`/`ChatThreadPanel.tsx` (can't use the hook per-message since the number of message rows changes — one hook call per list item isn't valid). `MessageActions` gained a `forceVisible` prop so a held message's actions show regardless of hover state; tapping anywhere else in the message list clears it. Also gave `TaskRow.tsx`'s mobile row a long-press → opens the same context menu desktop gets from right-click (Open/Rename/Mark done/Delete) — parity with desktop, no touch equivalent existed before.

   **Also found and fixed along the way**: `ChatThreadPanel.tsx` is a fixed `w-80` side panel — the exact same "cropped on a phone screen" shape the task modal's Comments panel had before that got a full-screen-replace treatment a few sessions ago. Gave it the identical fix: a new `fullWidth` prop, applied whenever `useIsMobile()` is true at both of its two mount sites (`app/page.tsx`'s direct Chat view, `DirectMessagesPage.tsx`'s DM view) — the main message list hides via the same `hidden`-when-thread-open pattern, the thread panel goes `w-full` instead of `w-80`.

4. **"#Channels" button too subtle** — the mobile "Channels"/"Chats" header button (added in the previous session's item 5 fix) was a plain outlined-neutral button, visually identical to secondary actions like "Archive." Since it's the *only* way into the channel/DM list on mobile (the sidebar it replaces is hidden below `md`), restyled it as a filled blue button matching how other primary actions (e.g. "New task") already look in this app.

5. **Network tab "doesn't work" on mobile** — traced to `ChatPanel.tsx`'s own empty state: `if (!activeChannelId) return <div>Pick a channel from the sidebar, or create a new one.</div>` — pointing mobile users at "the sidebar," which is hidden below `md` and was never visible to them in the first place. Added an `onOpenMobilePicker` prop (threaded from `app/page.tsx` through `DirectMessagesPage.tsx` into `ChatPanel`) that, on mobile, replaces the dead-end text with a real "Choose a conversation" button opening the same `MobileChatSheet` the header button does — this was never actually a functional crash, just a message pointing at UI that doesn't exist on that screen size, with no visible alternative.

6. **No way to filter the Planner by Space/List on mobile** — the desktop calendar-visibility checkboxes live inside the sidebar's `FolderTree.tsx` (hidden below `md`), with no mobile counterpart at all. New `components/mobile/MobileCalendarFilterSheet.tsx` (same flat, folder-nesting-skipped shape as `MobileSpacesSheet.tsx` — a multi-select filter doesn't need the full folder tree either), reusing the exact same `calendarVisibleListIds`/`toggleCalendarList`/`toggleCalendarSpace` state and setters `app/page.tsx` already has for the desktop version — no new filter state, just a new mobile-reachable UI for the existing one. Triggered by a new "Filter" button in the Planner's mobile header row.

Chose *not* to refactor `WeekRow.tsx`'s existing (already-working, already-verified) inline long-press implementation to use the new `useLongPress` hook — the hook is for new call sites only; retrofitting proven code for DRY-ness alone risks a regression for no behavior change.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet visually confirmed** — same caveat as every mobile-gesture/layout fix this session; all six need the user to retest on their phone after deploying, especially the long-press timing (reply/thread in Chat, task context menu) and whether the chevron reveal button and swipe now agree with each other without fighting.

## Today's session (2026-08-22, continued an eighth time) — Fixed: checked-off subtasks never left the subtask list

User reported (both PC and mobile): marking a subtask done just leaves it sitting there, green, instead of disappearing into the archive the way checking off a normal top-level task does.

**Root cause**: `currentSubtasks` (`app/page.tsx`) — the list rendered inside a task's detail modal — was `tasks.filter((t) => t.parentId === activeModalTask.id)`, no `archived` filter at all. The main Task list's own equivalent (`filteredTasks`) has always filtered by `!!task.archived === showArchived`; this parallel list for subtasks never got the same treatment, so "mark as done" (which is genuinely just `optimisticArchiveTask`, the identical action a normal task's done-toggle calls) visually succeeded (the row does turn green — `TaskRow.tsx`'s `showAsDone = task.archived` styling) but the row itself never left the list, since nothing was filtering it out.

**Fix**: one-line filter added — `tasks.filter((t) => t.parentId === activeModalTask.id && !t.archived)`. Checked that nothing else reads `currentSubtasks` in a way this would break (only the "Subtasks (N)" count header and the row map itself use it; the separate entrance-animation-tracking effect for newly-added subtasks recomputes its own id set directly from `tasks`, independent of this list, so it's unaffected).

Scope note: this makes archived subtasks disappear by default, matching the user's ask exactly — it does *not* add a "view archived subtasks" toggle (the main list's own "Archive" pill has no subtask-scoped equivalent). Worth building later if the user ever wants to browse completed subtasks, but not asked for here.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. This is a pure client-side filter (no API/schema change), so the usual desktop-browser click-through is the real verification — not yet done by the user.

## Today's session (2026-08-22, continued a ninth time) — Mobile Task rows: redesigned from horizontal swipe to vertical expand, bigger, and a smoother done animation

User feedback after the chevron-toggle fix: columns on mobile were "still messed up, really clumsy," tasks felt cramped and could be bigger, and the subtask-checked-off animation "doesn't look too good."

**Columns redesign**: the previous approach (a horizontal swipe-reveal panel, ~90px-wide chips in a side-scrolling strip, opened either by swipe or the chevron button added last round) was fundamentally the wrong shape for touch — squeezing a status pill, date pickers, and dropdown/text custom fields into narrow horizontal chips is cramped no matter how it's triggered. Replaced entirely with a **vertical accordion**: tapping the chevron now expands a full-width stacked list below the row — one `label: control` line per column, each control getting the same room it has on desktop instead of a squeezed chip. This also simplified the gesture model considerably: dropped `drag="x"`/`dragConstraints`/the whole `revealWidth`/`REVEAL_ITEM_WIDTH` sizing math entirely, since there's no swipe anymore — tapping the row opens the task, tapping the chevron expands/collapses, long-press opens the context menu, the grip handle drags. Four clearly separated triggers instead of several gestures competing on the same element. Animated open/close via `framer-motion`'s `height: 0 → 'auto'` (`AnimatePresence`), matching the app's existing collapsible-panel convention elsewhere.

**Bigger rows**: mobile-only size bump on the shared `selectCheckbox`/`doneToggle` (now `isMobile`-conditional sizing so desktop stays byte-identical) — checkbox 3.5→4 units, done-toggle 4→5 units, row padding `py-3`→`py-4`, base text `text-xs`→`text-sm`, grip/chevron icons and their tap-padding all bumped too. Also noticed and fixed in passing: the bulk-select checkbox's `opacity-0 group-hover:opacity-100` visibility rule (same hover-only-on-touch bug class as everything else this session) meant it was invisible until selected on mobile — now `opacity-100` unconditionally on mobile, since group-hover never fires there anyway.

**Smoother "mark done" animation**: removed the `justToggled`/`done-glow` box-shadow pulse entirely (state, className, and the now-unused `@keyframes done-glow` in `app/globals.css`). Root cause of it looking bad: the glow is a 600ms animation, but the row that owns it starts its own exit transition (fade/scale/blur, 280ms) essentially immediately after — since completing a task always removes it from whichever filtered view you're looking at (subtasks after last session's archive-filter fix; top-level tasks already worked this way in both the normal and "Archive" views), the glow can *never* actually finish before the whole row starts blurring away out from under it. It was cut short and looked glitchy on every single completion, not just subtasks — this was just the first time it became visible enough to notice. The checkbox's own instant solid-green fill plus the row's clean fade-out is enough "yes, that's done" feedback without the interrupted flourish.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet visually confirmed** — needs the user's phone, same as every mobile layout/gesture change this session; this one in particular changes the interaction model enough (no more swipe at all) that it's worth a fresh look rather than assuming the previous round's feedback fully carries over.

## Today's session (2026-08-23) — Expert design review: mobile Task cards and Task modal cleanup

User relayed detailed feedback from an outside design expert reviewing the mobile redesign, with reference screenshots. Confirmed with the user first that this whole pass (including the general visual-polish principles in the expert's point 3 — fewer hard borders, more rounded corners, elevation instead of borders) is **mobile-only**, matching the rule the entire mobile-redesign effort has followed all session — desktop is untouched throughout.

### Task list → cards (expert's point 1)

`TaskRow.tsx`'s mobile layout changed again — not just restyled, the interaction model itself changed. The previous pass (last session) already replaced horizontal swipe with a tap-to-expand chevron, but the expert flagged that the underlying idea (fields hidden behind a toggle at all) was still the wrong shape: task apps built for touch show status/assignee/date as compact, always-visible pills, not something you have to reveal first.

- **Metadata is now always visible**, not hidden behind any toggle — a `flex-wrap` row of compact pills sits directly under the title, reusing the exact same `renderColumnCell` popovers/pickers already built (one instance each, not duplicated). Due/start dates get a small calendar icon prefix; status and assignee already render as compact colored pills/avatar circles, so they needed no extra wrapping.
- **Card, not row**: `rounded-xl`, elevated `bg-neutral-900` background (against the page's darker background) instead of a bordered/square-cornered table row. Cards are gap-separated (`flex flex-col gap-2` at the list-container level in `app/page.tsx`) instead of `divide-y` hairlines.
- **Title wraps** (no `truncate`) instead of cutting off long titles.
- **Grip (drag) + More (⋯) both moved to the card's top-right corner**, always visible without needing to reveal anything first — the More button used to live inside the now-removed expand panel.
- **Rename dropped from an always-visible pencil icon** — it's still reachable via the long-press context menu (which already has "Rename"), one less element competing for space on the card.
- Removed the whole `columnsExpanded`/`AnimatePresence`-height-animation mechanism from last session — genuinely unnecessary now that nothing needs revealing.

**A real bug found along the way**: even after TaskRow's own mobile layout stopped needing horizontal space, the *list container* in `app/page.tsx` still applied `style={{ minWidth: tableMinWidth }}` — an inline width computed from the desktop grid's column widths — regardless of which TaskRow layout was actually rendering inside it. This alone was still forcing horizontal scroll on mobile even though the visible cards no longer needed it. Fixed: `minWidth` is `undefined` on mobile (both the main list and the subtask list inside the task modal use the same `tableMinWidth`, both fixed), and the outer "table shell" wrapper drops its border/shared-background/`overflow-x-auto` on mobile entirely, since each card now carries its own elevated surface instead of a shared one.

### Task modal cleanup (expert's point 2)

- **UUID hidden from the main view on mobile** (`hidden md:block` on the `ID: {id}` line) — reachable instead via a new "Copy task ID" button in the modal header (`md:hidden`, copies via `navigator.clipboard` + a toast).
- **Status/Timeframe/Assignees** went from bordered boxes with headers to slim elevated rows (`bg-neutral-900/60 rounded-lg`, no border) on mobile — Timeframe's separate "Start:"/"Due:" sub-rows collapsed into one `19 Aug – 21 Aug` line with a calendar icon; Assignees got a person-icon-led single row. Both reuse the *exact same* `DatePickerPopover`/assignee `FloatingPopover` instances as desktop (shared via a ternary / IIFE returning the same JSX, not duplicated popover state) — only the surrounding container differs between the two breakpoints.
- **Subtask add input** now starts collapsed to a plain text link ("+ Legg til underoppgave") on mobile, matching the same pattern the main Task list's own "+ Add Task" already uses — expands to the real input + blue "Add" button on tap, collapses back on blur *only* if nothing was typed (so tapping "Add" itself, which blurs the input first, can never race the collapse).
- **Breadcrumb** ("Space / Task") shrunk and dropped to lower contrast on mobile (`text-[11px] text-neutral-500` vs desktop's `text-xs text-neutral-400`) so the real task title reads as the primary heading.
- Modal's own corners bumped to `rounded-xl` on mobile (`rounded` on desktop, unchanged) — small, contained instance of the expert's "softer corners" principle rather than a separate global pass.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. Hit one real syntax bug while building this (a `{/* JSX comment */}` placed directly inside a ternary's parenthesized branch rather than as an actual JSX child — only plain `/* */` is valid in that position, not the `{/* */}` JSX-comment form; the parser error cascaded into several unrelated-looking errors further down the file, worth remembering next time a JSX-comment edit produces confusing downstream syntax errors). **Not yet visually confirmed** — this is the biggest single mobile visual change this session; needs the user's phone (and ideally the same design-review eye that flagged the original issues) before considering it settled.

## Today's session (2026-08-23, continued) — Four fixes: cropped task menu, diagonal dates, subtask un-nesting, Event location

1. **Task "⋯" menu cropped off-screen** — `taskMenu` (and the "Move to..." picker added below) positioned themselves at raw `e.clientX`/`e.clientY` with no viewport-edge check. Now the most likely trigger, on mobile, since the "more" button lives in a task card's top-right corner — often close to the screen's own right edge. Added `clampMenuPosition(x, y, width, height)` (`app/page.tsx`) and applied it to `openTaskMenu` (192×230 estimate) and the new list picker (224×288). Not retrofitted to every other context menu in the app (Space/Folder/List/Doc menus have the same latent issue) — scoped to what was actually reported plus the new feature that shares its positioning code.

2. **Start/due dates looked diagonal on the mobile Task card** — `TaskRow.tsx`'s metadata row rendered `startDate`/`dueDate` as two independent `flex-wrap` items, which could each land on a different line depending on how the row wrapped. Now grouped into one combined pill (`19 Aug – 21 Aug`, one calendar icon, a dash between) that wraps as a single unit — same fix shape as the Task modal's Timeframe row from the previous session.

3. **Accidentally dragging a task onto another (nesting it) was hard to reverse, wanted an easier way to pull a subtask back out** — added a **"Move to..."** item to the task "⋯" menu (`app/page.tsx`), reusing the exact picker UI (`taskListPicker`) the drag-and-drop-onto-a-Folder/Space flow already has, now populated with every List across every Space (`Space / Folder / List` labels via the existing `listPathLabel` helper) rather than only the specific Folder/Space that was dropped on. New `moveTaskToListAndUnparent` wraps `optimisticSetParent(id, null)` (only when the task actually has a parent) + the existing `moveTaskToList` in one `useHistoryStore` `transaction()`, so Ctrl+Z undoes both together — picking an explicit destination List now always means "this is an independent item from here on," which directly solves the "drag a subtask out to the list" ask and, as a side effect, gives a reliable manual fix for an accidental nest without needing to find/use Ctrl+Z at all (which doesn't exist as a gesture on mobile anyway). The same helper now also backs the pre-existing drag-and-drop picker path, for consistency.

4. **Event location field, like Google Calendar** — turned out `Event.location` already existed end-to-end on the *edit* side (schema, Google Calendar sync, `EventDetailModal.tsx`'s existing text field) from earlier Google Calendar sync work — it just was never exposed on the *create* flow (`QuickCreatePopover.tsx`'s Event tab had no Location field at all), which is almost certainly the only place the user had actually looked for it. Added the same Location input there (state, wired through `onCreateEvent`'s params, `optimisticCreateEvent`'s param type, and the create route call in `app/page.tsx` — `POST /api/events` itself already accepted `body.location`, no server change needed). Also added a **"View on Google Maps"** link (new tiny shared `lib/googleMapsUrl.ts` — a plain `google.com/maps/search` URL, no API key needed for free-text queries) to both the new create field and the existing edit field, since a plain text input alone doesn't really match "like we see in Google Calendar" — the clickable map link is what makes a typed address actually *feel* like a location, not just a label. Applies identically on desktop and mobile — it's the same single popover/modal on both.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. Item 4's backend wiring specifically confirmed via a scripted `POST /api/events` with a `location` field followed by a `GET` round-trip — genuinely verified, not just compiled. Items 1–3 are layout/interaction fixes with no API surface to script-test — **not yet visually confirmed**, need the user's phone (item 1 especially, since it's about viewport-edge behavior that's hard to eyeball from a description alone).

## Today's session (2026-08-23, continued a third time) — Bottom nav pill animation, "Spaces" replaces "Tasks", Connections fix

User sent ClickUp mobile screenshots as a direct visual reference for the bottom nav and the Spaces list.

1. **Floating pill bottom nav with a sliding active indicator** — `MobileBottomNav.tsx` was a flush-edge bar with plain color-only active state; redesigned into a rounded floating pill (`rounded-full` nav strip, inset from the screen edges, not stretched full-width) with a `framer-motion` `layoutId="mobileNavPill"` background that's rendered inside whichever tab is currently active — since only one tab ever renders it at a time, switching tabs makes it FLIP-animate (spring transition) from the old position to the new one instead of just appearing, matching the "bubble slides between tabs" reference.

2. **"Tasks" → "Spaces", now a real Spaces list** — the bottom nav's board tab used to jump straight into the last-viewed board. Now labeled "Spaces" (icon swapped to `LayoutGrid`, mobile-only — the desktop rail's own "Tasks" tab/icon/behavior is completely untouched, since `MobileBottomNav` just special-cases how *it* renders that one shared tab rather than changing `visibleNavTabs` itself) and opens a redesigned `MobileSpacesSheet.tsx`: "All Tasks – *workspace name*" pinned at top, then every Space in the current workspace as its own row with a colored icon badge (reusing `FOLDER_ICON_MAP`, the same icon lookup `FolderTree.tsx`/`SpaceHome.tsx` already use, falling back to the Space's own color + first-letter initial when no icon is set) and a chevron. Tapping a Space navigates straight to it (`setNavigation(spaceId, [])` + `setActiveView('board')`), landing on the existing `SpaceHome.tsx` (a Space's own browsable list of Lists) — deliberately dropped the previous version's inline List-drilling, since Space Home already covers "show me this Space's Lists" without duplicating that UI here. The board view's own header trigger (previously "Lists") now says "Spaces" too, opening the same sheet, for consistent terminology. Noted but didn't try to replicate: the reference's "Robin Hofset 🔒" row is ClickUp's per-user personal *Space* shown inline — Siqt's "My tasks" is architecturally a separate personal *workspace*, not a Space inside the current one, so it stays reachable via its own existing "Me" tile rather than being merged into this list.

3. **"Network" → "Connections", and a real fix, not just a rename** — user's own reasoning: "Chats" (the bottom-nav tab) already covers both DMs and rooms, so "Network" landing on `DirectMessagesPage`'s default `'chats'` sub-tab was pure redundancy — and that's also *why* it "didn't work" on mobile: it opened onto the same empty-conversation state Chat itself has (fixed last round with a button, but still a detour to get to what this entry point is actually *for*). Now the Me-section tile explicitly calls `setActiveDmTab('connections')` before switching views, jumping straight to the Connections sub-view (search people, connect link, incoming/outgoing requests) — a self-contained page that never depended on an `activeChannelId` being set in the first place, so the old empty-state dead-end never applied to it at all. Icon swapped `MessageCircle` → `Users`, dropped the DM-unread badge (no longer relevant once this tile isn't showing DMs).

**Deferred, not implemented — asked the user instead of building it**: real Google Places-style address autocomplete for the Event Location field (item 4 of this round's feedback). The current field is a plain text input + a "View on Google Maps" search link (free, no API key). Real autocomplete-as-you-type needs Google's Places API specifically, which requires an API key and billing enabled on a Google Cloud project (usage-based cost beyond a small free tier) — a real setup/cost decision, not something to silently build in.

## Today's session (2026-08-23, continued a fourth time) — Event location autocomplete (Google Places API), gated behind an unset key

Following up on the Location field question: user asked about real address autocomplete, then about capping Google Cloud spend, then decided to proceed — built the whole integration now, matching the VAPID-keys precedent (ships fully wired, silently does nothing until the production env var is actually set).

**Architecture**: new `app/api/places/autocomplete/route.ts` proxies Google's **Places API (New) Autocomplete** endpoint — never called directly from the browser, both to keep `GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY` server-only (same handling as every other secret in this app) and to require a real session (`getCurrentUserId()`, 401 otherwise) so a stranger can't hit this URL directly and burn through the account's free quota with zero login. Deliberately **only** calls Autocomplete, never Place Details — the field just wants the suggested address text, not coordinates/place metadata — so this stays entirely on the cheaper Essentials-tier SKU (10K free calls/month) and never touches Pro (5K/month). New shared `components/LocationAutocompleteInput.tsx` (debounced 300ms, `AbortController`-cancelled on each new keystroke, generates a random session-token UUID per search per Google's own cost-optimization convention) replaces the plain `<input>` in both `QuickCreatePopover.tsx` (new Event) and `EventDetailModal.tsx` (existing Event) — same `value`/`onChange` contract as a normal controlled input, so swapping it in was a one-line change at each call site.

**Graceful no-op by design**: whenever `GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY` isn't set, the route always returns `{ configured: false, suggestions: [] }` and the component never shows a dropdown — behaves as a completely plain text input, no error state, no "not configured" message anywhere. Verified this exact path with a scripted authenticated request against the (currently unset) local key. `.env.local` got a new blank `GOOGLE_PLACES_API_KEY=` line with a comment explaining the Essentials-only scoping and pointing at the Google Cloud checklist (enable Places API (New), billing on, a **daily quota cap set directly on that API in Cloud Console** — confirmed with the user this is a real hard stop, unlike a Billing budget alert which only emails and never actually cuts off usage — key restricted to just this API). **Production needs the same env var set separately** once the user finishes the Google Cloud side — same "local .env.local ≠ production" gap as every other secret in this app.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds (confirmed `/api/places/autocomplete` in the route list), scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. Also scripted-tested the route directly: unauthenticated request → 401; authenticated request with no key configured → 200 with `{configured: false, suggestions: []}` exactly as designed. **Not yet tested against the real Google API** — needs the user to finish the Google Cloud setup (enable the API, billing, quota cap, generate+set the key) before the actual suggestion-fetching path can be exercised for real.

## Today's session (2026-08-23, continued a fifth time) — Spaces as a real screen, not a popup; deduped the Menu grid

User feedback after trying the new "Spaces" nav on their phone: tapping it opened "yet another menu" popping up, when they just wanted to land directly on the Spaces list and navigate onward from there. Also asked to drop Tasks/Planner/Chat from the "Menu" grid, since those three already have their own dedicated slots in the bottom pill.

**`MobileSpacesSheet.tsx` redesigned from a partial-height bottom sheet into a full-height page**: opaque background (no dimmed backdrop — nothing to see through anymore), fills the screen from the top down to just above the bottom nav, slides in with a subtle fade instead of sliding up from the bottom edge. Deliberately stops *just above* the nav rather than covering it — `MobileBottomNav.tsx` got `relative z-40` (bumped above this one screen's `z-30`; every other mobile overlay, which are all meant to feel like real modals, stays at their existing `z-50`) so the pill nav stays visible and tappable the whole time this screen is showing — you can jump straight to Planner/Chat/Menu without closing Spaces first, which is what "navigate onward from there" actually needed. Selecting a Space (or "All Tasks") still closes the screen and lands you on that Space's board/Space Home, same as before — only the *browsing* experience changed, not the destination.

**`AppLauncherGrid.tsx`** ("Menu" grid) now filters out `board`/`calendar`/`chat` from the tab tiles it renders — those three are already one tap away via the bottom pill, so showing them a second time here was pure redundancy. Only `docs`/`office` (whichever aren't hidden by workspace admin settings) plus Settings/Trash/Install remain.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet visually confirmed** — z-index stacking across two independently-positioned fixed/relative elements is exactly the kind of thing that can look right in theory and still surprise in a real mobile browser (address-bar show/hide, safe-area insets varying by device) — needs the user's phone before calling this settled.

## Today's session (2026-08-23, continued a sixth time) — The bottom nav's 4th slot is now a ClickUp-style dynamic shortcut/switcher, with a real container-transform morph into the grid
