User pointed at ClickUp's own mobile nav behavior in detail (with reference screenshots): its menu button remembers the last thing you picked from the popup grid, shows *that* item's icon/label plus a small chevron, and does double duty — tap it to jump straight to that destination when you're elsewhere, tap it again (now that you're there) to reopen the picker. Also asked for the currently-pinned tile to show a visible "selected" marker in the grid, and gave a detailed animation spec: the button should visibly grow into the full menu (container transform / shared-element transition) rather than the grid just sliding up as an unrelated bottom sheet — spring/ease-out, ~250-300ms, content crossfading+scaling in as the container expands.

**State**: `app/page.tsx` now tracks `pinnedMenuTileId` (persisted to `localStorage` under `siqt:pinnedMobileMenuTile`, read via a lazy `useState` initializer guarded for SSR). `mobileGridTabs` (everything in `visibleNavTabs` not already pinned to the 3 fixed bottom-nav slots — the fixed-slot id list moved to a new shared `PRIMARY_NAV_TAB_IDS` export in `navTypes.ts` so it's defined once, not copy-pasted across `MobileBottomNav`/`AppLauncherGrid`/`page.tsx`) and `meNavItems` (My Tasks/Assigned/Connections/Profile) are merged into one `pinnableMobileTiles` list — both are pinnable now, which is what makes "select Connections from the grid, it becomes the shortcut" work. `pinnedMobileTile` resolves the id against that list, falling back to its first entry so the button never shows a bare, meaningless icon.

**`MobileBottomNav.tsx`**: the 4th slot renders `pinnedTile.icon`/`pinnedTile.label` (falling back to a plain `Menu` icon/label pre-first-pick) plus a `ChevronUp`/`ChevronDown` that flips with `menuOpen` — the "this is also a switcher" cue from the reference. Tap logic: grid open → close it; grid closed and pinned tile isn't the active view → navigate straight there; grid closed and pinned tile *is* the active view (or nothing's pinned yet) → open the grid. `onToggleMenu` was split into explicit `onOpenMenu`/`onCloseMenu` since the button's behavior is no longer a plain toggle.

**Container-transform morph**: the button's own background pill and `AppLauncherGrid`'s panel now share one framer-motion `layoutId` (`"mobileMenuMorph"`), with only one of the two ever mounted at a time (the button's pill only renders while the grid is *closed*; the panel owns the id while open) — framer-motion FLIPs between a small rounded-full pill and a large rounded-2xl panel automatically, growing out from the button's own position/center rather than sliding up as a disconnected sheet. Kept fully separate from the existing `"mobileNavPill"` id the 3 primary tabs already share for their own sliding active-indicator — mixing the two would have broken both animations.

**`AppLauncherGrid.tsx`** reworked to match the reference's shape: a floating rounded-2xl card positioned just above the nav (not a full-width bottom sheet), 3-column icon-grid instead of 4, each tile now a colored/neutral rounded-square icon badge with the label below rather than a filled rectangular row — the currently-pinned tile gets a `ring-2 ring-blue-500` highlight (this app's own accent color standing in for the reference's black border). Picking any pinnable tile now also calls the new `onSelectTile` prop before closing, pinning it for next time. Settings/Trash/Install stay as non-pinnable utility tiles below a divider, unchanged in behavior.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet visually confirmed** — this is the highest-risk change of the whole mobile pass so far purely on the animation side: framer-motion's border-radius interpolation during a `layoutId` shape morph (round pill → rounded rectangle) is exactly the kind of thing that can look subtly wrong (a brief squash/stretch, a flash) only once actually watched on a real device, not from reading the code. Needs the user's phone before calling this settled.

## Today's session (2026-08-23, continued a seventh time) — The cross-component `layoutId` morph was the bug, not just unpolished: real device testing (confirmed on an already-redeployed, freshly-reloaded production build, ruling out stale-deploy/PWA-cache as the cause) reported no animation *and*, worse, tapping different nav/menu items stopped switching views at all

The previous round's flagged risk turned out real. Sharing one framer-motion `layoutId` (`"mobileMenuMorph"`) between `MobileBottomNav.tsx`'s button pill and `AppLauncherGrid.tsx`'s panel — two elements living in entirely separate component trees, only one of which was ever wrapped in an `AnimatePresence` — asked framer-motion to FLIP-track a shared element across a lifecycle boundary it isn't built for. The most likely failure mode: a render-phase error inside framer-motion's projection/measurement code when the two instances' mount/unmount timing didn't line up as expected, which — with no error boundary anywhere in this tree — unmounts the *entire* React root per React 18's default behavior. That would explain both symptoms as one bug: no animation the first time it misfired, and every tap after that doing visibly nothing, because the app had silently gone down.

**Fix**: dropped the shared `layoutId` entirely. `AppLauncherGrid.tsx`'s panel is now a single self-contained `motion.div` with plain `initial`/`animate`/`exit` (`opacity` + `scale`, `transformOrigin: 'bottom center'` so it still visually reads as growing up from the nav, ~140ms `easeOut`) — the same proven recipe every other mobile overlay in this app already uses (`MobileSpacesSheet`, `MobileCalendarFilterSheet`). `MobileBottomNav.tsx`'s 4th-slot button pill is now a plain `AnimatePresence`-wrapped opacity fade, independent of the grid panel. All the *functional* pinning/shortcut logic from last round (remembering the last-picked tile, tap-to-navigate vs tap-to-open-picker, the selected-tile ring) is unchanged — only the animation mechanism was replaced.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Still not visually confirmed on-device** — needs the user's phone, this time after a genuine redeploy + a full PWA close/reopen (or reinstall) to rule out any lingering service-worker-cached bundle.

## Today's session (2026-08-23, continued an eighth time) — The real cause of "nothing in the nav responds to taps": a mixed-content WebSocket retry storm, not the nav code at all

Previous round's fix (dropping the shared `layoutId` morph) didn't help — the user reported the *primary* Spaces/Planner/Chat tabs, untouched by any of today's nav work, were equally unresponsive. Asked the user to open siqt.no in desktop Chrome with DevTools' mobile emulation and check the console. The actual answer: a wall of repeating `Mixed Content: ... attempted to connect to the insecure WebSocket endpoint 'ws://siqt.no:1234/' ... blocked` errors, with a stack trace of `Promise.catch → o → t` chains nesting deeper on every single line — i.e. each blocked attempt's reconnect logic re-firing again immediately, not a slow real-network retry.

**Root cause**: `usePresenceConnection.ts`, `useChatChannelConnection.ts`, and `CollabDocEditor.tsx` all hardcoded `url: \`ws://${window.location.hostname}:1234\`` for their `HocuspocusProvider` connections. `usePresenceConnection` is mounted once for the whole app session (not per-view) — so this fires on every page load regardless of which view is active, matching the report that it happened on the base view *and* on `view=chat`. Production (`siqt.no`) is served over HTTPS (via Cloudflare, per the DNS/HTTPS work earlier this project) — a browser refuses a plain `ws://` connection from an `https://` page outright as mixed content, before any real network I/O happens at all. Because the failure is a synchronous browser-policy block rather than a real network timeout, Hocuspocus's reconnect logic re-fired near-instantly on every failure with no effective backoff, which is consistent with pegging the main thread hard enough that click handlers never got a turn to run — a full explanation for "no animation" *and* "doesn't switch at all," independent of anything in `MobileBottomNav.tsx`/`AppLauncherGrid.tsx`. **The previous round's suspicion that the cross-component `layoutId` morph itself crashed the React root was very likely a misdiagnosis** — there's no direct evidence it was ever the actual cause, just a plausible-sounding theory that happened to be wrong; it's noted here so a future session doesn't treat that "known bugs" entry as more certain than it is.

**Fix**: new `lib/collab/collabWsUrl.ts` — one shared helper computing `wss://` vs `ws://` from `window.location.protocol`, used by all 3 call sites (`CollabDocEditor.tsx`, `usePresenceConnection.ts`, `useChatChannelConnection.ts`). This does **not** make realtime collab/chat/presence actually work over HTTPS in production yet — the collab sidecar (`server/collabServer.ts`, port 1234) has no TLS termination in front of it, so a `wss://` attempt will very likely still fail. What it *does* fix: failing this way is a normal, slower-backoff WebSocket connection failure instead of an instant, tight-loop, main-thread-choking mixed-content block — the actual reported symptom. Getting realtime collab genuinely working over HTTPS in production is a separate, not-yet-done infra task (a TLS-terminating reverse proxy or tunnel in front of port 1234, or routing it through the same Cloudflare-fronted origin as the main app) — worth revisiting deliberately, not bundled into this fix.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet confirmed this actually restores nav responsiveness on-device** — needs a real redeploy + retest; if taps still don't register after this, the mixed-content storm wasn't the whole story and the earlier "nav-specific" investigation needs to resume.

## Today's session (2026-08-23, continued a ninth time) — The `wss://` protocol fix wasn't enough on its own: disabled realtime presence/chat-push in production until the collab port actually has TLS in front of it

User retested after the `wss://` fix and reported the exact same thing: nav taps work briefly, then stop after a couple of taps. That's consistent with the mixed-content instant-block being only *one* symptom of a deeper problem — a real `wss://` handshake against a port with no TLS listener (production's port 1234 situation, unchanged) also fails fast, and `usePresenceConnection`/`useChatChannelConnection` each spin up a fresh `HocuspocusProvider` on every mount (chat channel connection in particular re-mounts each time the Chat view is entered), so a few taps in and out of Chat can leave several independently-retrying failed connections piling up — worse the more you tap, matching the report precisely.

**Fix**: `lib/collab/collabWsUrl.ts` gained `isCollabRealtimeEnabled()` — true unconditionally on `http:` (local dev is untouched, was never the problem), false on `https:` unless `NEXT_PUBLIC_COLLAB_WS_ENABLED=true` is explicitly set. `usePresenceConnection.ts` and `useChatChannelConnection.ts` now skip creating a provider at all when this returns false — both are pure "nice to have live" signals (who's online, an instant chat-refresh nudge), never required for the app to function, so skipping them entirely in production is a clean, safe default until the collab port actually has a TLS-terminating reverse proxy in front of it (still a separate, not-yet-done infra task). **`CollabDocEditor.tsx` (the Docs editor) was deliberately left alone** — its `HocuspocusProvider` isn't optional the same way, the editor's extensions are gated behind `provider` even existing at all (it's the actual Yjs document backing the editor, not just a live-sync nicety), so simply disabling it would break Docs editing outright. Its retry behavior is a known follow-up (bounding `maxAttempts` requires constructing a custom `HocuspocusProviderWebsocket` instead of the `url` shorthand, since those retry-tuning fields aren't accepted alongside a plain `url`) — not fixed here since Docs wasn't implicated in this specific nav-taps-stop-working report.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet confirmed on-device** — needs a real redeploy + retest.

## Today's session (2026-08-23, continued a tenth time) — Still broken after disabling presence/chat realtime too. Stopping here for the day — status for tomorrow

User retested the presence/chat-disable fix (`5ec47fd`) and reported it made no difference — bottom nav taps still stop working after a few interactions. **One new, important detail this round**: a fresh page refresh temporarily restores it — the nav works for "a couple of times" right after a reload, then degrades again. Session paused here at the user's request to write up status before stopping for the day; **nothing below this point has been tried yet**, this is a handoff, not a resolved bug.

**What this new detail changes**: the "refresh temporarily fixes it, degrades with more taps/interactions" pattern was already consistent with the WebSocket-retry-storm theory *before* today's presence/chat disable — but that fix has now shipped and the symptom is unchanged. That's a real, meaningful data point: **it means the presence/chat Hocuspocus connections were likely never the (sole) actual cause**, or at least not the whole story. Two most-likely remaining explanations, neither yet investigated:

1. **`CollabDocEditor.tsx` (the Docs editor's own collab connection) was deliberately left untouched last round** (its `HocuspocusProvider` is required for the editor to function at all, not just a nicety — see the previous entry) — if this component (or something that mounts it) is active/mounted more broadly than just "the user has a Doc open" — e.g. mounted persistently somewhere, or re-created on some state change that happens to correlate with nav taps — it would still be hammering the same unreachable `wss://…:1234` endpoint via the exact same retry mechanism, fully unaffected by this round's fix. **First thing to check tomorrow**: is `CollabDocEditor` mounted anywhere outside of actually viewing/editing a Doc? Grep every place it's rendered (`<CollabDocEditor` in `app/page.tsx` and any Task-modal "Documents" panel) and confirm it's fully unmounted (not just visually hidden) whenever no Doc is actually open.
2. **The bug may not be collab/WebSocket-related at all.** Given disabling 2 of the 3 known Hocuspocus connections didn't help, it's entirely possible the real cause is something unrelated that just happens to *also* fit "degrades with interaction count, resets on refresh" — e.g. an event listener or interval leaking on every render/mount somewhere in the mobile nav's own code (or elsewhere in `app/page.tsx`, a ~6000+ line component), a `framer-motion` `AnimatePresence`/`layout` issue unrelated to the one already ruled out, or a dnd-kit sensor/listener not being cleaned up. This needs actual profiling, not more guessing from code review alone.

**Concrete next steps for tomorrow** (none attempted yet):
- Repeat the DevTools console check that found the WebSocket errors last time, but this time also open the **Network tab** (filter: WS) and watch whether *any* WebSocket connection attempts are still happening after this round's fix — if yes, that confirms `CollabDocEditor` (or something else) is still the live culprit; if the Network tab is clean of WS attempts and it *still* degrades, that rules out collab entirely and points at something else in the app.
- Open Chrome DevTools' **Performance** tab, hit Record, tap the bottom nav a handful of times until it stops responding, then stop recording — a growing/sawtooth memory graph or a long task pegging the main thread will show up directly, far more conclusive than reading code.
- Alternatively/additionally, Chrome's **Task Manager** (Shift+Esc) can show this tab's memory climbing in real time while tapping around, without needing to interpret a full performance trace.
- If the Network tab shows no WS activity and profiling shows no obvious leak, the next suspect is React re-render cost itself — `app/page.tsx` is a single giant component; React DevTools' Profiler tab (record a tap, see what re-rendered and how long it took) would show if some unrelated state update is forcing an expensive full-tree re-render on every nav tap that compounds somehow.
- Worth trying as a pure bisection step regardless of the above: temporarily comment out the `<CollabDocEditor>` mount (or short-circuit `usePresenceConnection`/`useChatChannelConnection` even harder, e.g. force `isCollabRealtimeEnabled()` to always return `false` with no HTTPS check at all) and confirm whether the nav degradation still happens with *zero* Hocuspocus code running client-side — this single test would definitively confirm or rule out collab as a factor before spending more time on it either way.

**Everything shipped so far this session remains in place** (safe, non-regressive even if insufficient): the dynamic pinned-tile nav behavior, the simplified (non-`layoutId`-morph) grid animation, the `wss://` protocol fix, and the presence/chat disable-on-HTTPS default. None of these need to be reverted — they're each independently reasonable, just not (yet) the complete fix for the unresponsive-nav bug.

## Today's session (2026-08-24) — Found the actual bug: the Spaces screen (and every other mobile overlay) never closed itself on navigating away. The whole WebSocket investigation was a red herring

User sent a screenshot of desktop Chrome's mobile-emulated DevTools: Network tab showed a clean request log (no WebSocket connections at all, confirming yesterday's collab-disable fix worked and also that collab was never actually the live culprit at this point), Console was completely empty (no errors), and the URL bar/nav pill both clearly showed `?view=office` with the **Office** tab highlighted as active — yet the visible screen was still the Spaces list. The user's own read of it was exactly right: "it does show in the side that it changes view... but it's still stuck on spaces."

**Real root cause**: `mobileSpacesOpen` (and `mobileMenuOpen`/`mobileChatSheetOpen`/`mobileCalendarFilterOpen`) had no relationship to `activeView` at all. `MobileSpacesSheet` only ever closed via its own explicit `onClose` (picking a Space, or its X button) — tapping a *different* bottom-nav destination (Planner, Chat, or anything in the Menu grid) called `setActiveView(...)` correctly, the URL and the nav's active-tab highlight updated correctly, but nothing ever told the Spaces screen (sitting at `z-30`, deliberately kept *below* the nav so the nav stays tappable — see the "Spaces as a real screen" entry two days ago) to close. So it just sat there permanently once opened, visually masking the real (correctly switching) view underneath — every tap after that first "open Spaces" looked exactly like "nothing happens," because visually nothing did, even though the app was working perfectly underneath the whole time. This explains every single symptom from this saga in one shot: works fine until the first stray tap on Spaces, "stops after a couple of taps" (however many it took to first open Spaces), and "refresh fixes it temporarily" (a fresh load starts with `mobileSpacesOpen` false, until it's opened once more).

**None of yesterday's WebSocket work was the actual cause.** The mixed-content errors were real (confirmed directly in a console at the time) and worth fixing regardless — a browser-blocked `ws://`-from-`https://` retry storm is a genuine problem on its own merits — but they were coincidental to this specific bug, not its cause. Worth being honest about in case a future session needs to revisit any of that reasoning: it was a plausible-sounding theory that happened to be wrong, arrived at by code review alone with no way to see the actual browser state until this screenshot.

**Fix**: one `useEffect(() => { setMobileSpacesOpen(false); setMobileMenuOpen(false); setMobileChatSheetOpen(false); setMobileCalendarFilterOpen(false); }, [activeView])` in `app/page.tsx`, right where those 4 states are declared — any real navigation away closes every mobile-only overlay screen, not just the one the user happens to be looking at. Simpler and more centralized than threading an explicit close call into every single nav button/tile's own `onClick` across `MobileBottomNav.tsx`/`AppLauncherGrid.tsx`.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet confirmed on-device** — but this time the failure mode is fully understood and reproduced (not guessed at from code alone), so confidence is much higher than the last two attempts. Still worth a real retest before calling it closed.

## Today's session (2026-08-24, continued) — Nav taps confirmed working; the Spaces button's own highlight was the next real gap

With the overlay-close bug fixed, the user confirmed taps work again — but noticed the "Spaces" nav button never lights up (blue/highlighted) at all, even while actively browsing the Spaces screen. Root cause: `MobileBottomNav.tsx`'s Spaces slot calls `onOpenSpaces` (just opens `MobileSpacesSheet`) instead of `tab.onClick` (`setActiveView('board')`) — so the tab's own `active` flag, derived from `activeView === 'board'`, never becomes true just from opening the sheet; it only ever did once a specific Space was actually picked. Fixed by passing `mobileSpacesOpen` down as a new `spacesOpen` prop and OR-ing it into that one tab's active/highlight state (`tab.active || spacesOpen`) — every other tab's logic is untouched.

Also hit a real environment issue on resuming after a PC crash: `npm run build` failed with `Debug Failure. Expected .../.tsbuildinfo === ...\.tsbuildinfo` (a path-casing mismatch) — TypeScript's incremental build cache left in a corrupted/partial state by the crash. Fixed by deleting `.next/cache` and rebuilding clean; worth remembering as the first thing to try if a build fails with a `.tsbuildinfo`/incremental-cache-shaped error right after an unclean shutdown.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds (after the cache clear), scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet visually confirmed on-device.**

## Today's session (2026-08-24, continued again) — Re-implemented the container-transform morph, now that the real nav bug is fixed and confirmed

With nav taps confirmed working, the user asked to pick the pill-to-panel morph animation back up — it had been abandoned two rounds ago on the (wrong) theory that sharing a `layoutId` across `MobileBottomNav.tsx`'s button and `AppLauncherGrid.tsx`'s panel had crashed the app. Since the actual cause (the Spaces screen never closing on navigation) is now understood and fixed, that theory no longer holds, so the morph is back.

**`AppLauncherGrid.tsx`**: the panel `motion.div` now carries `layoutId="mobileMenuMorph"` again, with a `transition` that separates `layout` (the shape/position FLIP — `type: 'spring', stiffness: 380, damping: 30, mass: 0.9`) from `opacity` (a plain quick fade) so both animate on their own appropriate curves. The tile grid itself is now a nested `motion.div` with its own `initial`/`animate` (`opacity` + `scale: 0.8 → 1`, ~160ms, `delay: 0.1` so it settles in just after the container's own growth is mostly done) — matching the spec's "container expands, then content fades/scales in right after," rather than everything animating simultaneously.

**`MobileBottomNav.tsx`**: the 4th slot's background pill now also carries `layoutId="mobileMenuMorph"`, gated on `!menuOpen && pinnedActive` (previously just `pinnedActive`) — the extra `!menuOpen` check is what makes this work at all: only one of {this pill, the grid panel} is ever mounted at a time, so framer-motion always has exactly one clear "owner" of the shared id to FLIP from/to, rather than two simultaneously-mounted elements fighting over it. Both sides are wrapped in their own `AnimatePresence` for a clean opacity fade on top of the shared layout FLIP — this two-independent-`AnimatePresence`-instances-sharing-one-`layoutId` shape is exactly what framer-motion's own documented shared-element-transition examples use (a thumbnail and a detail view in different components, connected only by a matching id), so this isn't actually the risky pattern the earlier (wrong) diagnosis assumed it was.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet visually confirmed on-device** — this is a genuinely new attempt at real shared-layout animation in this app (distinct from the already-working `mobileNavPill` bubble slide, which only ever slides a same-shaped pill between siblings, never changes shape/border-radius) — needs the user's phone to confirm the corner-radius interpolation and overall feel actually reads as smooth "growing from the button," not just that it doesn't crash.

## Today's session (2026-08-24, continued a third time) — Two Spaces/Planner tabs lighting up at once, and the morph animation reported as not visible at all

User reported two things after the morph re-add: (1) the "grow from the button" animation doesn't visibly happen at all, and (2) going from Planner to Spaces leaves **both** tabs highlighted blue at once (Chat still behaves correctly — only Chat lights up when active).

**(2) is a real, understood regression from earlier today's Spaces-highlight fix**: opening Spaces only sets `mobileSpacesOpen`, never `activeView` — so whatever tab was genuinely active before (e.g. Planner) stays "true" underneath the whole time you're just browsing Spaces, and now Spaces *also* lights up via the `spacesOpen` OR-condition added earlier today. Fixed with a `!spacesOpen` guard on every *other* tab's active state (`components/mobile/MobileBottomNav.tsx`) — the Spaces screen visually covers everything while open, so nothing else should read as active at the same time.

**(1) is still unresolved and needs more diagnosis before a 4th blind attempt.** Two attempts at a shared-`layoutId` container-transform between the nav button's pill and the grid panel have now both failed to produce a visible animation on a real device, despite the second attempt following framer-motion's own documented pattern for this (two independently-`AnimatePresence`-wrapped instances sharing one id, gated so only one is ever mounted at a time). Rather than guess a third implementation blind, next step is to ask the user a narrower diagnostic question: does the grid panel appear with *any* animation at all (a fade, a pop), or does it snap into existence with literally zero transition? That distinguishes "the shared-layout FLIP specifically isn't triggering" (opacity/scale still plays) from "something more fundamental is preventing any animation on this element" — worth checking directly rather than continuing to reason about it from code alone, since the last two rounds of guessing from code both turned out to explain the wrong bug.

**Verified** (the tab-highlight fix only): `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker.

## Today's session (2026-08-24, continued a fourth time) — Clarified: the popup morph is fine, what needed animating was the active-tab pill highlight itself

Follow-up clarified the previous report entirely: the grid popup's own grow/fade animation is working well and shouldn't be touched. What the user actually wants animated (matching the ClickUp reference) is the **active-tab indicator** on the 3 primary nav tabs — instead of just the label text hard-cutting to blue, a pill-shaped background should visibly mark "this tab is active" and slide/pop in. That mechanism (`layoutId="mobileNavPill"`) was already built, but its fill was `bg-neutral-800` against a `bg-neutral-900` nav bar — a ~1-shade difference in dark mode, easy to read as "nothing happened, just the text changed color" even if the pill and its slide animation were technically working the whole time.

**Fix**: `components/mobile/MobileBottomNav.tsx`'s 3 primary tabs now use `bg-blue-500/15 ring-1 ring-inset ring-blue-500/30` for the active pill instead of `bg-neutral-800` — a clearly visible tinted pill matching the existing blue active-text accent, rather than a low-contrast gray that blended into the bar. The 4th (Menu) button's own pill was deliberately left untouched (still `bg-neutral-800`) since it's the same element that morphs into the grid panel — the user explicitly asked not to touch that animation, and changing its resting color risks an inconsistent color jump partway through the morph.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet visually confirmed on-device.**

## Today's session (2026-08-24, continued a fifth time) — Found the real reason the pill "never" showed: navigating away from Spaces silently failed whenever the destination was already the active view underneath

User reported the pill still didn't appear at all after the contrast fix, plus a genuinely new, inconsistent bug: going from Spaces to Planner sometimes just doesn't work, but a few extra taps elsewhere (Chat → Spaces → Planner) can suddenly make it work again — and going from "My Tasks" (a Menu-grid tile) to Spaces could leave both lit blue. All symptoms specifically involved Spaces, never the other 3 destinations directly.

**Root cause**: the auto-close effect added earlier today (`useEffect(() => { setMobileSpacesOpen(false); ... }, [activeView])`) only fires when `activeView`'s *value* actually changes. Opening Spaces never touches `activeView` at all — so if you were already on Planner, opened Spaces to browse, then tapped Planner again to go back, `activeView` was `'calendar'` both before and after that tap: no value change, so the effect never re-ran, and the Spaces sheet stayed stuck open — reading as "the tap did nothing." Going through Chat first works because that *does* change `activeView` (`'calendar'` → `'chat'` → `'calendar'`), so the effect correctly fires and closes Spaces. This also explains the "invisible pill" report from the previous round: if a tap sometimes silently fails to actually switch `activeView`, there's nothing for the active-tab pill to animate to — no bug in the pill itself, just nothing ever triggering it.

**Fix**: added an explicit `closeMobileOverlays` callback in `app/page.tsx`, threaded into `MobileBottomNav.tsx` and `AppLauncherGrid.tsx` as a new `onNavigate` prop, called directly at the *start* of every nav-triggering tap (primary tabs, the pinned 4th-slot button, every grid tile) — not left to the reactive effect alone, which is kept only as a backstop for `activeView` changes that don't go through one of these tap handlers (e.g. a deep link). This guarantees a clean overlay-closed state on every deliberate navigation regardless of whether the destination happens to match what's already active underneath.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker. **Not yet visually confirmed on-device** — this is now the third fix in this saga aimed at the same family of "mobile overlay state vs. activeView" bugs; worth a thorough real-device pass (rapid taps in varied orders, specifically starting and ending on Spaces) before considering this settled.

## Today's session (2026-08-24, continued a sixth time) — Found why the active-tab pill was truly invisible: a negative z-index escaping to the wrong stacking context, plus LAN dev testing set up for faster iteration

User set up local LAN testing this round (`npm run dev` + `http://192.168.1.51.nip.io:3000` on their phone, already configured earlier in the project via `AUTH_URL`) to iterate faster than a full Pterodactyl redeploy each time — hit PowerShell's default script-execution restriction (`npm.ps1 cannot be loaded because running scripts is disabled`) first, fixed globally with `Set-ExecutionPolicy -Scope CurrentUser -ExecutionPolicy RemoteSigned` (persists across terminal sessions, no admin rights needed).

**Real root cause of the "invisible pill" found**: `-z-10` on the pill (used to tuck it behind its button's own icon/label) doesn't mean "one layer behind this element" — a negative z-index escapes to the *nearest ancestor that actually establishes a stacking context*. The button itself (`position: relative`, no explicit z-index) does **not** establish one on its own, so the negative z-index escaped all the way up to the outer wrapper (`relative z-40`) several levels up — meaning the pill was rendering **behind the entire nav bar's own opaque background**, correctly present in the DOM the whole time but completely invisible. Fixed by adding `z-0` to each button (giving it, combined with its existing `position: relative`, its own local stacking context), scoping the `-z-10` pill correctly to just that button's own content. Also dropped the pill's border/ring per request, keeping just the tinted fill.

**Also surfaced a real design tradeoff, not yet resolved**: switching between one of the 3 primary tabs and the 4th (pinned/dynamic) slot is a hard cut, not a slide — because the pinned slot's pill deliberately uses a *separate* `layoutId` (shared with the grid panel for the morph animation, which the user explicitly confirmed works well and asked not to touch) rather than joining the primary tabs' shared sliding group. Unifying all 4 into one continuous slide would mean one element trying to serve two different shared-layout roles at once (slide-among-tabs vs. grow-into-panel), risking visual conflicts between the two. Recommended keeping this as-is (fade only for the 4th slot) rather than risk the already-approved morph; **awaiting the user's decision**.

**Also clarified a false alarm**: user reported the fix worked locally but reverted after a production redeploy — turned out to simply be that the z-index fix hadn't been pushed to git yet at that point, so Pterodactyl's pull picked up the previous (still-broken) commit. Not a real dev-vs-production discrepancy.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` succeeds, scripted login + `GET /` returns 200 with no error-overlay marker.

## Today's session (2026-08-24, continued a seventh time) — Unified the active-tab pill across all 4 nav slots, confirmed working via local LAN testing

User confirmed the pill worked on the 3 primary tabs but hard-cut (no slide) to/from the 4th (dynamic) slot — asked if it could join the same animation. Resolved by splitting the 4th slot's background into two separate, stacked `layoutId`-tracked elements instead of one dual-purpose one: an **invisible** anchor (`layoutId="mobileMenuMorph"`, `opacity: 0`) that exists purely so the grid panel (`AppLauncherGrid.tsx`) still has this button's exact position/size to grow from when it opens, and a **visible** pill (`layoutId="mobileNavPill"`, same blue tint/spring as the other 3) that now joins their shared sliding group. One element can't serve two different shared-layout identities at once (slide-with-tabs vs. grow-into-panel), but two elements occupying the same spot — one purely for geometry, one purely for color — can each do their own job independently without conflicting.

**Confirmed working** via the LAN dev-testing setup from earlier this session (`npm run dev` + phone on `http://192.168.1.51.nip.io:3000`) before pushing — first genuinely on-device-confirmed fix in this whole multi-round mobile-nav saga, rather than shipped on faith. `npx tsc --noEmit` / `npm run build` / scripted smoke check all clean as usual.

## Today's session (2026-08-25) — Resumed on this machine; a friend couldn't reach the site, then a 6-item feedback batch

Pulled all the work from the sessions above (rebrand, mobile redesign, deployment). Local environment was stale (Prisma client generated against the old schema, `web-push` not installed) — `npm install` + `prisma generate` + `prisma db push` brought it in sync; `npx tsc --noEmit` clean afterward.

**"Kompis can't log in to siqt.no" turned out not to be a login bug at all.** Walked through it live with the user: the friend actually saw `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` ("Dette nettstedet er ikke tilgjengelig"), which then also started happening on the user's own PC (previously fine) while their phone kept working. A direct `nslookup`/`curl` from this machine confirmed `siqt.no` resolves to real Cloudflare IPs and returns a real `307` over HTTPS — the deployment itself is healthy, this was never a server-side or DNS-propagation problem. Landed on IPv6 as the most likely remaining explanation (the DNS answer lists AAAA records before A records; a wired connection with a half-working IPv6 route is a known way to get exactly this symptom while WiFi/cellular on the same or a different device sail through) and handed the user a `Test-NetConnection` v4-vs-v6 comparison to run — **not yet confirmed**, the conversation moved on to the feedback batch below before this was resolved. Worth picking back up if it's still happening.

**Six items relayed in one message, two of them real bugs affecting a friend's ability to use the app right now:**

1. **Sent a targeted workspace invite via Network — the recipient saw nothing to accept.** Root-caused, not guessed: the invite *was* created successfully server-side (`POST /api/workspaces/[id]/member-invites` has no silent-failure path, and the store action correctly surfaces `{ok:false, error}` to the UI, which does display it) — the actual gap is that a pending invite only ever rendered *inside* the workspace-switcher popover's own contents, with no badge or signal anywhere telling the recipient to open it. Same "built correctly, zero discoverability" shape this project already hit once with role assignment (2026-08-21). **Fixed**: a red count badge next to the workspace-switcher's chevron whenever `memberInvitesIncoming.length > 0`, same convention as the existing Chat-unread/Network-DM badges.
2. **"Only sees the Chat tab" turned out to be a direct consequence of #1, not a separate bug.** `visibleNavTabs`' `hasRealWorkspace` gate (added 2026-08-21, "no workspace should ever look like a real default") correctly hides Tasks/Planner/Docs/Office until the viewer has a real (non-personal) workspace — Chat is the one nav-rail tab deliberately left ungated. A friend who was never able to *see* his pending invite has zero real workspaces, so this is the gate working exactly as designed, not a bug to relax. Fixing #1's visibility should resolve this on its own once he can actually accept.
3. **Right-click "Send DM" + click-avatar-for-profile on the Network/Connections list** — scoped, not yet built. `ManageableAvatar.tsx` already does exactly this pattern but is workspace/role-shaped (needs a `HierarchyWorkspace`, drives promote/demote/remove) — doesn't fit a Connection who may share no workspace at all with the viewer. Needs a smaller, dedicated version for this list: DM is easy (the same `createOrOpenDM` + navigate-to-chat call every other DM entry point already uses), but "view profile" has no existing target to link to — `ProfilePage.tsx` is self-only (`currentUser`/`onUpdate`), and Office's own per-person page is workspace-role-scoped. A genuine small gap: **this app has no read-only "view someone else's profile" surface at all yet** — worth building as its own small modal (name/avatar/bio/title, whatever `AppUser` already carries) rather than forcing either existing surface to serve double duty.
4. **Real email invites (not just in-app)** — explicitly deferred by the user again, same as the 2026-08-21 scoping call: needs a real email service (SMTP/Resend/etc.) chosen first, a user decision, not an engineering default.
5. **Design ask: unify Chat's left sidebar to show both Channels and DMs, rename Network → Connections, reachable from inside that same unified Chat surface.** User's own words: "confusing that there are two different chats in two completely different places." Concrete proposal below — not yet built, needs the user's sign-off on the shape before touching code.
6. **A live "someone is writing to me" signal in Chat, plus channel muting.** Checked first rather than assumed: per-conversation unread badges *already exist* in both `ChatChannelSidebar.tsx` and `DirectMessagesSidebar.tsx` (`c.unreadCount`/`dm.unreadCount`), not just the aggregate nav-rail count — so if "a sign when someone writes to me" means an unread indicator, that part may already be satisfied and just wasn't noticed. Ambiguous whether the actual want is a live "typing…" presence indicator instead (a materially bigger, real-time feature, nothing like it exists today) — flagged back to the user rather than guessed at. **Muting** is a real, small, unbuilt feature: needs a `muted: Boolean` (or `mutedUntil: DateTime?`) on `ChatChannelMember`, a mute toggle in each channel/DM's context menu, and skipping `sendPushToUser`/badge-counting for muted rows.

**Design proposal for #5, given for the user to react to, not yet built:**
- **Today's actual shape** (confirmed by reading both components, not assumed): `ChatChannelSidebar.tsx` (workspace channels) and `DirectMessagesSidebar.tsx` (DM list) are already two *separate*, independently reusable components — they just currently mount in two unrelated top-level places (the plain `'chat'` view vs. `DirectMessagesPage`'s own "Chats" tab reached through the Me-zone's "Network" button). That separation is *why* it reads as two different chats living in two different places — the underlying data/store already treats channels and DMs uniformly (same `ChatChannel` model, same unread-count shape), only the UI entry points diverged.
- **Proposed shape**: one Chat sidebar with two stacked, always-both-visible sections — "Channels" (existing `ChatChannelSidebar` content, unchanged) above "Direct Messages" (existing `DirectMessagesSidebar` content, unchanged) — collapsible independently if the list ever gets long, matching the pattern `FolderTree.tsx`'s own Space-collapse already established. Both sections keep their own existing "+ New channel" / "Start a DM" affordances. The Me-zone's "Network" button is renamed to **"Connections"** and its component shrinks to *only* what doesn't fit in a chat sidebar row — the connect-link, incoming/outgoing connection requests, and the plain connections list (with #3's new right-click DM / view-profile actions) — its current "Chats" tab is deleted outright, since that list now lives permanently in the unified Chat sidebar instead of behind a second click.
- **Why not go further and merge Connections into the Chat sidebar too**: a Connection you haven't messaged yet has no `ChatChannel` row at all (DMs are created lazily, on first message) — showing every Connection as a phantom "conversation" would mean inventing fake rows for people you've never actually chatted with, and conflates "people I'm connected to" with "conversations I'm having." Keeping Connections as its own smaller, purpose-built list (reachable via the renamed Me-zone button, with a one-click "Message" action that opens/creates the real DM and drops the user straight into the unified Chat view) avoids that, while still solving the actual complaint — there is genuinely one Chat surface now, Connections is just where you go to *start* a new one, not a second inbox.
- **Mobile**: `MobileChatSheet.tsx` already reuses both sidebar components inside one bottom sheet — this proposal is really just bringing desktop in line with what mobile already does by necessity, not a new pattern.

Nothing in this session was committed except the #1 badge fix — items 3/5/6 are scoped and awaiting the user's go-ahead on the design before any more code changes.

## Today's session (2026-08-25, continued) — Critical finding: Docs edits are never actually saved in production; ties together the "must refresh everywhere" complaint too

User reported two things in one message that turned out to be the *same* root cause: "must refresh to see what others do — chat, docs, lists, everything" and, far more seriously, "type in a Doc, navigate away and back, and it's gone — never saved for anyone, no real-time either."

**Root cause, already half-diagnosed in this file (see the `wss://`/mixed-content Known Bugs entry below) but not fully connected until now**: production has no TLS termination in front of the Hocuspocus collab sidecar (port `1234`) at all — only the main app's port `3000` got a working Cloudflare route during deployment. `lib/collab/collabWsUrl.ts`'s own `isCollabRealtimeEnabled()` already knows this and gates *presence* and *chat's live-push signal* behind an explicit `NEXT_PUBLIC_COLLAB_WS_ENABLED` opt-in (currently unset/off) specifically because a `wss://` handshake against a cert-less port just fails outright on HTTPS — which is exactly why chat/lists/etc. only ever update on a manual refetch (REST APIs work fine over the already-proxied port 3000; only the *live push* signal is disabled). That part was a deliberate, documented stopgap, not a surprise.

**What's new and much worse**: `components/collab/CollabDocEditor.tsx` never checks that same gate at all — it unconditionally opens a `HocuspocusProvider` pointed at the broken `wss://…:1234` URL regardless. Docs' Tiptap editor uses the Y.js `Collaboration` extension bound directly to `provider.document` with no separate plain-text save path — the document's *entire* content model lives inside the Y.Doc that only the (currently always-failing) WebSocket connection can ever persist to the database. Since the connection never succeeds in production, **every keystroke in every Doc has only ever existed in that browser tab's local memory** — nothing has been saved server-side since this went live, for anyone, confirmed by reading the editor's own extension config rather than guessed.

**Immediate fix shipped**: `CollabDocEditor.tsx` now tracks real connection state via the provider's own `onStatus` callback (`WebSocketStatus.Disconnected`, not the normal brief `Connecting` state on first load) and shows a visible amber warning banner — "Not connected — changes here aren't being saved right now. Copy your text somewhere safe before leaving this page." — rather than silently losing work with zero indication anything was wrong. This does **not** fix persistence itself, only stops it from being invisible.

**Verified**: `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, a real `npm run build` succeeds. The warning banner itself hasn't been seen in a real browser — same standing caveat as every other UI change this whole deployment arc, no Playwright available here.

## Today's session (2026-08-25, continued a third time) — The real fix: route collab WebSocket traffic through the app's own already-working port instead of a second Cloudflare route

User chose the "proxy through port 3000" path over fighting Cloudflare's dashboard for a second Origin Rule for port 1234 — sound call given how hard Origin Rules were to even locate last time (see the earlier deployment sessions above).

**New `server/customServer.ts`** — a standard Next.js custom server (`createServer` + `next()`'s programmatic API, confirmed still supported in Next 16 by reading `node_modules/next/dist/docs/01-app/02-guides/custom-server.md` directly rather than assuming; confirmed compatible with this app's config since `output: 'standalone'`, which the docs explicitly say can't be combined with a custom server, was never set here). Wraps Next's own request handler for normal HTTP traffic unchanged, and additionally listens for `upgrade` events: any request whose path starts with `/collab` gets handed to a new `http-proxy` instance (`ws: true`, `xfwd: true` so the original client headers — including the auth session cookie Hocuspocus's own `onAuthenticate` reads — reach the real Hocuspocus process) pointed at `127.0.0.1:${COLLAB_PORT}`. Everything else falls through unhandled rather than being destroyed, in case Next ever registers its own upgrade listener on the same server (moot in production, since Turbopack's HMR websocket — the only other thing that would want one — only exists under `next dev`, which this file never runs under).

**`lib/collab/collabWsUrl.ts`** now branches: on `https:` (production), the client connects to `wss://<host>/collab` — same origin, no port, so it rides through the exact Cloudflare route port 3000 already has working, no new DNS/Origin Rule/allocation needed at all. On plain `http:` (local dev), it's untouched — still a direct `ws://<host>:1234` connection, since local dev runs the two processes separately with no mixed-content concern to route around.

**A real question resolved by reading the actual client/server source rather than assuming**: does a single shared `/collab` URL correctly disambiguate between different documents/channels/presence rooms? Checked both `@hocuspocus/provider` and `@hocuspocus/server`'s bundled `dist` output directly — the document name is never part of the connection URL at all (the client's own `url` getter just returns the configured URL verbatim); it's embedded inside the Yjs sync-protocol messages exchanged *after* the connection opens. A raw byte-level proxy (which `http-proxy`'s `.ws()` is — it never parses WebSocket frames, just pipes the underlying TCP stream) passes this through completely transparently, so one shared `/collab` path is correct and sufficient regardless of how many different docs/channels ever connect through it.

**Dependency-placement lesson applied preemptively this time, not learned the hard way again**: `http-proxy` went into real `dependencies` (needed at runtime); `@types/http-proxy` initially landed in `devDependencies` via plain `npm install --save-dev` and was moved to `dependencies` immediately, before it could repeat the exact `@types/web-push` production-build break from two sessions ago (`NODE_ENV=production` strips `devDependencies`, but `next build`'s type-check needs every `@types/*` package regardless of environment).

**Verified for real, not just "the code looks right"**: started the actual `start:prod` process chain locally (`server/customServer.ts` + `server/collabServer.ts` together, real env vars exported into the shell — same simulation technique used throughout this deployment arc), confirmed the app itself still serves correctly through the custom server (`curl /login` → real `200`, correct title), then wrote a throwaway script driving a real `HocuspocusProvider` (Node `WebSocketPolyfill` via a temporary `npm install ws --no-save`, same exact verification pattern already used once before for the original Hocuspocus auth fix) against `ws://localhost:3000/collab`. The connection reached `connected` status and — critically — got a real `onAuthenticationFailed` callback with reason `permission-denied` for the deliberately-unauthenticated test connection, proving the proxy correctly carries a live connection all the way through to Hocuspocus's own real `onAuthenticate` business logic and back, not just that a raw TCP handshake succeeds. Test script and the temporary `ws` install were both removed afterward (`git status` confirmed neither `package.json` nor `package-lock.json` picked up the temporary dependency). `npx tsc --noEmit` and a real `npm run build` both clean.

**Not yet done, needs the user, infra not code**: deploy this (push already done; the Pterodactyl server needs a Reinstall/restart to pick it up), then set `NEXT_PUBLIC_COLLAB_WS_ENABLED=true` as a new environment variable and rebuild once the live deploy is confirmed working (check by opening a Doc — the new amber "Not connected" warning banner should no longer appear) — that flag still deliberately gates presence/chat's live-push signal behind a manual step even after this fix, so a real deploy can be confirmed working before flipping it; Docs itself was never behind that gate and should just start persisting correctly the moment this ships, no flag needed for that part specifically.

## Known bugs / things to remember

- **dnd-kit gotcha**: `useDraggable`/`useDroppable` bind to the *nearest ancestor* `DndContext` by React-tree position, not by id-naming intent. Fix: one shared `DndContext` for all sidebar/task dragging, dispatch in `onDragEnd` by inspecting the dragged id's prefix (`task id`, `list-drag:`, `folder-drag:`, `space-drag:`).
- **A second droppable on the exact same DOM rect ties in collision detection** — dnd-kit's `closestCenter` can't meaningfully prefer one over the other when their centers are identical, so the "winner" is effectively arbitrary. Hit this twice: once (correctly avoided) by reusing List's existing `list:` task-drop target for List-reorder instead of adding a second droppable there; and initially got it wrong for Space reordering in the same way before applying the same fix. When something is draggable AND needs a reorder-drop-target at the same location, reuse an existing droppable id and disambiguate by the *dragged* id's prefix instead of registering a second `useDroppable` there.
- **Interval/cluster boundary math needs `<=`, not `<`, for single-point-in-time ranges.** A same-day task has `start === end`; a merge-overlapping-intervals boundary check using strict `<` will treat two such tasks as non-overlapping neighbors instead of a real conflict. Hit this in `assignLanes` (calendar lanes) — watch for it again if any other interval-clustering code gets added.
- **Async store actions in drag-end handlers are fire-and-forget.** `reorderSpace`/`reorderList`/`updateFolder` etc. return promises but callers don't await them — fine for the app itself (optimistic local state updates synchronously), but when verifying via a direct API fetch right after a script-driven drag, give the network request a moment (a few hundred ms to ~1s) before checking, or the check can race ahead of the PATCH actually landing and look like a failure that isn't one.
- **Framer Motion `layoutId` gotcha**: matches globally by default (no `LayoutGroup` in this app) — scope the id string to whatever makes two instances *actually* the same logical element (see `TaskRow`'s `navScope` prop).
- **~~Don't share a `layoutId` across two elements in separate component trees~~ — retracted, this was a misdiagnosis.** Originally written after real device testing showed the mobile nav going totally unresponsive right after adding a `layoutId` morph between `MobileBottomNav.tsx`'s button pill and `AppLauncherGrid.tsx`'s panel, and guessed the shared id itself was the cause. It wasn't: the actual bug (found two sessions later, from a user screenshot showing the URL/active-tab updating correctly *underneath* a stuck-open screen) was that `MobileSpacesSheet` never closed itself on navigating away, unrelated to this animation entirely. The morph was re-added (2026-08-24) once that was fixed, using two independent `AnimatePresence`-wrapped instances sharing one `layoutId` — exactly framer-motion's own documented shared-element-transition shape, and it works fine. **Actual lesson**: don't attribute a hard-to-explain bug to the most recently-added or most-technically-exotic piece of code just because it's the most plausible-*sounding* culprit — get a screenshot/console/network trace of the real broken state before committing to a theory, especially when no in-session browser tool can see it directly.
- **Playwright drag scripts: grab the source element at its own center, not an arbitrary offset like "5px from the left edge."** dnd-kit's collision detection tracks the *dragged element's* translated rect (original rect shifted by however far the pointer has moved from its mouse-down point), not the raw cursor position. Grabbing off-center means the effective drop point is offset from where the mouse visually ends up — with tightly-packed sidebar rows (e.g. a folder immediately followed by its own nested child), that offset is enough to land the drop one row off from the intended target. Cost an hour of confused debugging (looked like a real move-into-child-folder bug) during Docs drag-and-drop verification before realizing it was the test script's grab point, not the app.
- Tailwind's `slate-*` palette has a real blue undertone — use `neutral-*` for "neutral gray."
- This machine reliably needs a dev-server restart after any `prisma db push`/schema change: `prisma generate` fails with `EPERM` on the query-engine DLL while the dev server holds it open. Find the node processes via `Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "Name='node.exe'"` (look for `next dev`/`start-server.js` under the project path), stop them, `prisma generate`, then restart `npm run dev`. Hit this twice today (once for `List.color`/`icon`, once for `Space.order`) — budget for it any time a Prisma schema field is added.
  **Update (2026-08-07, since the collab sidecar):** `npm run dev` now spawns the `concurrently` wrapper plus *two* children (`next dev` and `tsx watch server/collabServer.ts`) — the sidecar imports the same generated Prisma client and can hold the exact same DLL lock, so it must be stopped too, not just the old single `next dev` process. There's no longer one process to look for by name; stop every `node.exe` under the project path (`Get-CimInstance Win32_Process -Filter "Name='node.exe'"`, filter `CommandLine` for anything under `C:\dev\siqt`), `prisma generate`, then `npm run dev` restarts both together in one shot.
- Turbopack dev-mode cold-compile quirk: the very first hit to a changed API route can occasionally return a truncated/empty response, throwing "Unexpected end of JSON input" client-side. Harmless, resolves on the next request.
- Seed/dev data currently has "Ideer" and "IDeer irl" test folders, plus a "Marketing Super" empty folder under Marketing — cosmetic leftovers from verification passes, feel free to reset.
- **dnd-kit's `onDragOver` only fires when the closest droppable *changes*, not on every pointer move.** Learned this building the Space drag-reorder insertion indicator: comparing the dragged element's rect to the target's rect *inside* the `onDragOver` callback gives a stale, essentially arbitrary snapshot near a boundary, since the callback simply doesn't re-fire while the pointer moves within the same closest-droppable's territory. Fix: track *which* droppable is closest via `onDragOver` (event-driven), but compute fine-grained "where within it" continuously from a raw `window.addEventListener('pointermove', ...)` instead.
- **Undo/redo: push the history entry only *after* the mutating fetch has actually resolved, not before.** Pushing first (so it "feels" symmetric with the code order) creates a real race: a fast Ctrl+Z can fire the inverse action before the original write has committed server-side — hit this exactly with delete+undo, where undo's re-create raced an in-flight DELETE and got a unique-constraint error because the row it was "restoring" hadn't actually been removed yet.
- **A "coalesce a burst of calls into one undo step" helper must keep the *first* call's `undo` and only ever advance `redo`.** Overwriting the whole stored entry on each call (as a first draft of `pushCoalesced` did) means Ctrl+Z only reverts the most recent call in the burst, not the whole session — silently defeats the entire point of coalescing.
- **A new required column with a `@default` on an existing table can't go straight to required via `prisma db push`** — SQLite has no way to backfill existing rows with a *client-side* Prisma default in one step, so `db push` refuses. Two-step it: add the column as optional (`String?`, still `@unique` if needed) and push, backfill existing rows with a script, then flip it to required with the real `@default(...)` and push again. Hit this adding `User.calendarToken`.
- **Any nested `include: { someRelation: true }` on a model that carries a secret-ish field ships that field to every caller of the outer route, not just the ones that "should" see it.** Adding `User.calendarToken` (a bearer secret for the `.ics` feed) immediately leaked through `Task.assignees` and `Comment.author` on routes that had `include: { assignees: true }` / `include: { author: true }` — neither of those routes has anything to do with calendar feeds, they just happened to embed a `User`. Fix: a shared `publicUserSelect` (`lib/prisma.ts`) used everywhere a `User` is nested inside another model's response; only `GET /api/users/[id]` returns the real thing. Worth grepping for `include: { <relation>: true }` on any model any time a new sensitive field gets added to it.
- **Restoring a deleted entity from a snapshot needs a way to tell its own field-setters "don't log this as a fresh event."** The snapshot already contains the original activity-log comments, replayed verbatim — if the same `optimisticArchiveTask`/`optimisticSetAssignees`-style calls used to reconstruct the entity *also* trigger their normal "log this change" side effect, you get duplicates. Solved with a module-level `isRestoringSnapshot` flag in `useTaskStore.ts`, true only for the duration of a snapshot-restore call, threaded into the relevant request bodies as `skipActivityLog`.
- **A two-way state<->URL sync effect pair only needs a mutual "already equal, so no-op" check on both sides — not a manual reentrancy flag.** But two adjacent gotchas bit this in `page.tsx`'s back/forward sync: (1) don't special-case "the first push after hydration" as a `router.replace` meant to silently normalize a messy URL — that effect only runs when something *changes*, so "first push" is in practice almost always the user's first real click, and demoting it to `replace` collapses two navigations into one back-step. Just always `push` once hydrated. (2) a bare URL segment meaning "no opinion, don't touch" is only correct on the very *first* hydration pass (so a fresh `/` keeps an app default like auto-selecting the first Space) — on every later parse (a real back-navigation landing on that same bare URL), it must resolve to the concrete default instead, or backing past the user's first-ever choice does nothing visible.
- **Optional chaining (`a?.b.c()`) only protects against `a` itself being nullish — it does nothing if `a` is real but `.b` is `undefined` for some other reason (like a field-name mismatch between an API response and its TypeScript type).** `currentSpace?.spaceDocs.find(...)` crashed on every render once `currentSpace` became a real object, because the actual JSON field was named `docs` (matching the Prisma relation), not `spaceDocs` (the frontend type). TypeScript didn't catch it either, since the API response is untyped JSON at the fetch boundary. When a `select`/`include` field name and a frontend type's field name need to differ (to avoid colliding with another field, as here), rename explicitly in the API route's response mapping — don't rely on chained optional-access to paper over the mismatch, and don't assume TS will catch a field that only ever flows through an untyped `fetch().json()`.
- **Don't couple "navigate into X" with "toggle X's expand/collapse" in one click handler.** `DocFolderRow`'s row click did both — clicking an already-expanded folder to browse into it also collapsed it, so its own "+ New doc" row (rendered only while expanded) vanished out from under a click that was about to use it. The chevron already has its own dedicated toggle with `stopPropagation`; the row's own click should always *navigate and expand*, never toggle closed.
- **A "fix every leak" pass on a sensitive field needs to check a route's own un-`select`ed return value, not just its `include`s of other models.** The `calendarToken` leak fix earlier this session only grepped for `include: { <relation>: true }` (the `Task.assignees`/`Comment.author` case) — it missed that `POST /api/users` and `PATCH /api/users/[id]` *return the row they just wrote*, `prisma.user.create()`/`.update()` with no `select`, which is the exact same class of leak but doesn't match an `include` grep at all. When auditing a model for a newly-added sensitive field, check every `create`/`update`/`upsert` on that model too, not just places it's nested inside something else.
- **An optimistic mutation that removes/changes local state before the fetch resolves needs to check the response and roll back on failure — "fire and forget" silently loses data.** Every delete action in `useTaskStore.ts` updated state first, then `await fetch(...)` with no `.ok` check and no catch; if the request failed (dropped connection, dev-server restart mid-request — a real risk on this machine, see the `db push`/EPERM entry above), the item stayed hidden in the UI forever with no server-side effect and no trash entry, and nothing told the user anything had gone wrong. Fixed across all seven delete actions: check `res.ok`/catch the rejected promise, and on failure resync from the server (`refetchWorkspaces`/`refetchTasks`/`fetchDocs`) plus an `alert()` so it's never silent. Worth the same check any time an optimistic update's own confirmation request is added.
- **Playwright auto-dismisses native `window.confirm()`/`window.alert()` dialogs unless a `page.on('dialog', ...)` handler is registered.** Cost real debugging time verifying task delete: the click on "Delete" appeared to succeed (no thrown error) but the task was never actually removed, because `handleDeleteTask`'s `window.confirm(...)` guard was silently getting auto-cancelled. Not an app bug — the app already correctly uses a native confirm here (unlike the `ConfirmDialog` component used for space/list/folder/trash-purge deletes). Any Playwright script driving a flow that hits `window.confirm`/`alert`/`prompt` needs the dialog handler wired up first, or the action underneath it never happens.
- **`transaction(label, fn)` only groups pushes that happen synchronously (or before an awaited `fn` resolves) — a plain `.forEach` calling an un-awaited async action doesn't qualify, even though it *looks* like it's "inside" the transaction.** `transaction()`'s `finish()` runs the instant `fn()` returns; if `fn` is a sync function whose body fires off async calls without awaiting them (e.g. `arrayMove(...).forEach((x, i) => updateThing(x.id, {order: i}))`), the group closes and (if empty) gets discarded before any of those calls' own `push()` — which for most `updateX` actions in `useTaskStore.ts` only happens *after* their own `await fetch(...)` resolves — has landed. Each update then pushes its own entry directly onto the bare stack instead of being bundled, so Ctrl+Z only reverts whichever one happened to push last. Silent and easy to miss: a 2-item swap often only changes one side's `order` in practice (the other's old value already equals its new index), so only one update fires and there's nothing to split apart — this hid the bug through every single/double-item reorder test done earlier in the project. Only surfaced once a 3-way tie forced a genuine 2-item simultaneous change during the Office Rooms reorder work. Fix: make `fn` itself `async` and `Promise.all` (or sequentially `await`) every update inside it — `transaction()` already special-cases a `Promise`-returning `fn` and waits for it before closing the group. Applies to every `reorder*Siblings`-shaped helper; doesn't apply to actions (like `optimisticArchiveTask`) whose `push()` happens synchronously before the fetch is even awaited.
- **Creating a resource with a real side effect (opening a WebSocket, starting a timer, subscribing to something) inside `useMemo` is unsafe — React's dev-mode Strict Mode double-invokes `useMemo` factories specifically to catch this, and only "keeps" one of the two results, silently orphaning whatever the other one created.** `components/collab/CollabDocEditor.tsx` built its `HocuspocusProvider` (whose constructor opens a live WebSocket) inside `useMemo(() => new HocuspocusProvider(...), [docId])` — two real connections opened, React kept one, and typed edits went into whichever Y.Doc ended up bound to the *discarded* connection roughly as often as the kept one, so they silently never reached the server. On-screen everything looked correct (the character appeared as typed); only a reload or a second client revealed nothing had actually synced. Fix: move any side-effecting resource's create/destroy into `useEffect` + `useState` (create on mount, destroy in the cleanup, recreate when deps change) — the correct Strict-Mode-safe lifecycle — and reserve `useMemo` for genuinely pure derived values, per React's own stated contract for the hook.
- **Hocuspocus's `unloadImmediately` defaults to `true`: a document unloads from server memory the instant its last client disconnects, even if a debounced `onStoreDocument` write is still pending.** Closing a tab shortly after typing (comfortably inside the configured `debounce` window) silently lost that edit — the pending write never got the chance to run, and the next `onLoadDocument` re-read the old, pre-edit row from the database with no error anywhere. Set `unloadImmediately: false` (`server/collabServer.ts`) so the server waits out the pending debounced store before actually unloading; `flushPendingStores()` is the equivalent explicit escape hatch for a clean server-shutdown path if one gets built later.
- **A "sort by `order`" view can't tell a freshly created row apart from an old untouched one if the create route never actually sets `order`** — it just inherits the schema's `@default(0)` and ties with everything else that's never been manually reordered. `POST /api/rooms` was exactly this: the new HQ Building view sorts floors by descending `order` to put the newest on top, but new rooms silently landed at the bottom because they tied at 0 with existing rooms and a stable sort preserves creation-order for ties. Any "append at the end/top" UI built on an `order` field needs the creation route to actually compute `(current max) + 1` — don't assume a field exists just because the type does.
- **A positional Playwright locator (`.first()`/`.last()`) is unsafe for a *destructive* click while the exact ordering it's relying on is still being fixed in the same session.** Verifying the fix above, an early test script clicked "the newest room's delete button" via `.first()` — written before the ordering bug was actually fixed, so at that moment the newest test room was tied with a real pre-existing room ("Robs kontor") and, thanks to sort stability, the *real* room rendered first and got deleted instead. Caught by cross-checking the database, not by trusting the screenshot. Once list order itself is what's under test, scope destructive actions to an element matched by name/id, never by position.
- **`proxy.ts` gates every non-API page route, including static-looking paths like `/uploads/*` — a bare Node-side `fetch()` with no session cookie gets silently redirected to `/login` (200 `text/html`), not a 404 or 401.** Verifying the local image-upload feature, a first check of the uploaded file's URL via plain `fetch()` looked like a serving bug (wrong content-type) until re-checked through `page.request.get(...)` inside the already-authenticated Playwright browser context, which returned the real `200 image/png`. Any script verifying a URL this app serves under `/` (not `/api/*`) needs a real session cookie attached — either drive it through an authenticated `page`/`page.request`, or don't bother with a bare `fetch()` at all.
- **`auth.ts`'s Credentials `authorize()` lowercases the submitted email before querying, but a seed script that creates a test user must lowercase it too — creating the row with any uppercase character and then logging in with that same value fails with "Invalid email or password", indistinguishable from a real bad-credentials bug.** Cost real debugging time verifying per-Doc presence: two test users seeded as `presenceA-...@test.local`/`presenceB-...@test.local` (capital A/B) failed to log in even though a direct `bcrypt.compare` against the stored hash succeeded — the row genuinely existed with the correct password, just under a differently-cased email than what the lowercased lookup queried for. Every other test script this session happened to use all-lowercase email prefixes and never hit this. Always lowercase a script-generated test email, matching what the real signup/login flow already normalizes to.
- **A `has-text("X")` locator can accidentally match a test fixture's own name, not the UI element the script meant.** Verifying the Event/Task popover, `button:has-text("Event")` matched the workspace-switcher button instead of the Event tab — because the test script had itself created a workspace literally named "EventTest WS", and `has-text` does substring matching against anything in the subtree, including a completely unrelated ancestor's label. Fixed with `getByRole('button', {name: 'Event', exact: true})` scoped to a specific container. Worth remembering when naming throwaway test fixtures too: avoid embedding words the same script's own locators search for.
- **Same lesson, non-destructive flavor: a generic `text=X` locator with `.first()`/`.last()` silently reads a completely different real DOM element when a page renders the same string in more than one place (a plain-styled breadcrumb/card *and* the actual colored/interactive row).** Verifying independent icon/text color, `page.locator('span:has-text("TCList")').last()` grabbed a `SpaceHome.tsx` browse-card's name (always plain `text-neutral-200`, by design) instead of the sidebar row's own span — its `getComputedStyle().color` never changed after setting a new `textColor`, which looked exactly like a real bug (a silent no-op) until the database was checked directly and showed the write had actually succeeded. Resolved by enumerating *every* match (`page.locator('text=X').all()`) and inspecting each one's own tag/class/computed style, rather than guessing which index is "the" element. Worth reaching for that enumeration step early any time a locator's result contradicts a direct DB/API check — the mismatch is at least as likely to be the test's own targeting as the app.
- **`components/collab/PresenceBar.tsx` renders as a sibling of `.collab-doc-editor` in `CollabDocEditor.tsx`'s JSX, not inside it.** A verification script querying `.collab-doc-editor`'s own `innerHTML` for a peer's name will always come back empty, even when presence is working correctly — query the shared parent instead. Cost a chunk of debugging time chasing a phantom "presence broken" regression (even disabling the change under test didn't fix it) before realizing the test's DOM scope, not the app, was wrong.
- **A component that only reads its "who am I" identity once at construction time won't pick up a later identity switch without remounting.** `CollabDocEditor.tsx`'s `useEditor` dependency array is `[docId, provider]` — it deliberately doesn't include the current user, so `CollaborationCaret`'s displayed name is fixed at whatever "You are: ..." was set to when the editor first mounted; switching identity after a doc is already open doesn't retroactively relabel that tab's cursor until the doc is reopened. Predates this session, not a regression from anything built here — but worth knowing before writing a test (or filing a bug) that selects identity *after* opening a doc and expects the collaboration caret to reflect it.
- **A package that loads its own asset files (fonts, binaries, data) via `__dirname`-relative paths at runtime will break under Turbopack/webpack's default route-handler bundling, and the resulting error path is misleading.** `pdfkit` (added for Doc → PDF export) threw `ENOENT ... C:\ROOT\node_modules\pdfkit\js\data\Helvetica.afm` — a path that doesn't even resemble this project's real location (`C:\dev\siqt\...`), because bundling rewrites/virtualizes `__dirname` inside the compiled route handler. Fixed with `serverExternalPackages: ["pdfkit"]` in `next.config.ts`, which tells Next to `require()` it natively from `node_modules` instead of bundling it — the same treatment Next's own defaults already give `sharp`/`better-sqlite3`/`canvas` for exactly this class of package (see the full auto-external list in `node_modules/next/dist/docs/.../serverExternalPackages.md` — checked directly rather than assumed, per this repo's own "not the Next.js you know, read the docs" instruction in AGENTS.md, since the option was renamed from `serverComponentsExternalPackages` in older Next versions). A `next.config.ts` change needs a dev-server restart to take effect — saving the file alone doesn't trigger Turbopack to pick it up.
- **A "find-or-create" route needs a real DB-level unique constraint plus an atomic `upsert`, not a `findFirst`-then-`create` pair — no amount of careful sequencing on the write side closes the gap between the read and the write.** `POST /api/workspaces/personal`'s first draft checked for an existing personal workspace, then created one if missing — two near-simultaneous requests (the sidebar's "My tasks" click called it, and `PersonalTasksPage.tsx`'s own mount effect called it again for deep-linking, then both doubled again by React Strict Mode's dev double-invoke) could each see "nothing exists yet" before either had committed, creating multiple personal workspaces for the same user. Fixed with a unique `Workspace.personalOwnerId` column and a single `prisma.workspace.upsert({ where: { personalOwnerId } })` — verified by firing 10 genuinely concurrent requests via `Promise.all` and confirming they all converged on one row. React Strict Mode's double-invocation is worth treating as a free, built-in race detector for exactly this class of bug during dev, not just an annoyance to work around.
- **When adding an auth check to a `[id]` route, grep the actual call sites before assuming "the caller can only act on their own id" is the intended rule.** Locking down `/api/users/[id]` PATCH/DELETE, a naive `callerId === id` check looked obviously correct — until grepping turned up the Office directory editing *other* teammates' phone/title/status/DND (`onUpdatePhone`/`onUpdateUserField`/`onToggleDnd`) and the Team panel's "remove a team member" both legitimately calling the same endpoints with someone *else's* id. A self-only check would have silently broken both real features while looking like a clean security fix. Landed on "must be authenticated, doesn't have to be the same id" instead — this app has no admin/owner role concept yet to check against for real, so that's the honest, narrowest fix rather than inventing a permissions model on the spot.
